| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | With Use Custom Naming on, a typed character and the code points of a range are named uniXXXX or uXXXXX, as typed code-point names are kept. Settled by observing a font with the option on. |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | New glyphs that land between the same two existing glyphs are in sort-key order; the glyphs already in the font are never moved. Settled by observation (05 question 2). |
| `glyph.addGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | Headless calls default to Keep Existing (05-font-view.md § 3.1); the dialog first calls with ask, which changes nothing when a name exists and returns the existing names. Our design; nothing to settle. |
| `glyph.newGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | When newGlyph is taken the new glyph is named newGlyph.001, newGlyph.002 and so on, the rule documented for duplicated and pasted glyphs. Settled by observation (05 question 6). |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | Each copy is placed directly after its original in the glyph order. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | A copy of a suffixed glyph gets one more suffix (A.ss01 gives A.ss01.001). Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | The copy keeps everything but the code points, so the original stays the glyph for its characters. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.duplicateGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | The `suffix` parameter names the copy A.<suffix> (A.<suffix>.001 when taken), for scripts that want a feature suffix in one step. Our addition; nothing to settle. |
| `glyph.removeGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | Decompose replaces each component of a removed glyph with that glyph's shapes from the matching layer; components inside it that point at glyphs that stay remain components. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.removeGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | A metrics key points at a glyph when its formula starts with that glyph's name (=H, =\|H, =O-4); the longest leading name that is a glyph wins, since names may contain hyphens. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.removeGlyph` | M4 | handbook | yes | A removed glyph's bitmaps leave every bitmap strike with it, so a new glyph with the same name starts without one. Settled by observing whether the reference app keeps a deleted glyph's bitmap for a new glyph of that name. |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | Stored overrides of category, subcategory, script, production name, case and direction are dropped, so those properties follow the database again; the sort name, tags, colour labels, note and export flag stay. Settled by observing a glyph with a custom category before and after the command (05 question 5). |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | With Use Custom Naming on, names are kept and only the other properties are updated. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | A rename the command makes goes through glyph.rename, so references follow it; a new name that is already taken (by any glyph of the font before the command, or by an earlier glyph of the batch) leaves the glyph's name and Unicode values unchanged and is reported. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.updateGlyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | The Unicode values follow the name whether or not it changed: the glyph data's code point for it, none for a name it knows without one (A.alt), and the stored values for a name it does not know. Settled by observation. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | The table adds a Description column (ours). Settled by observing the window. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | With an empty search field the table lists the whole database in the sorted order, up to the search's limit of 10,000 rows (the CJK ideographs past it are reached by searching). Settled by observing the window when it opens. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | Sorting is ascending only; a second click on the sorted header keeps the order. Settled by observing a second header click. |
| `window.glyphInfo` | M4 | handbook | yes | Shift+click selects the rows from the last clicked one; Cmd+click adds or removes one row. Settled by observing the window. |
| `glyphInfo.addToFont` | M4 | handbook | yes | Names the font already has are left as they are and listed in `existing`; no Replace question is asked. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.listMissingGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | A Categories entry expects the glyphs that the languages of the listed scripts need and that the glyph data puts in that category, subcategory or case group; without language data it has no expected set. Settled by observing the badge of a category in a font with one language list shown. |
| `fontView.generateMissingGlyphs` | M4 | handbook | yes | Generated glyphs are empty, as wide as the Default Layer Width parameter or the built-in width makes a new glyph (as New Glyph), take the code point the entry gives or the glyph data gives their name, and are placed by the default sort key; all of them are one undo step. Settled by observing Generate on a list filter. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | Selected Glyphs pairs the copied glyphs, in copy order, with the selected targets in glyph order. Settled by observation (05 question 18). |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | Content of Active Layer writes into the target's layer for the master Font View shows. Settled by observation (05 question 18). |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | Copied masters are matched to the target's by id, then by name, then by position; layers of an unmatched master are not pasted. Settled by pasting between fonts with different masters. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | All Data keeps the target's name and code points. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | In Edit View's text mode Paste Special (menu item or the paste key with Option) is refused with a message instead of typing the clipboard's text; the Edit menu of the running reference app in text mode settles it. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | LSB moves the outline and keeps the right side bearing; RSB sets the width from the outline's right edge. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.pasteSpecial` | M4 | handbook | yes | As Components adds each component at the origin, after the shapes already there, and leaves out a component that would make a glyph refer to itself. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.infoForSelection` | M4 | handbook | yes | The menu item opens the window and does not toggle it; `open: false` closes it. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.infoForSelection` | M4 | handbook | yes | In Edit View the window describes the edited glyph. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.getInfo` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; there is no reference behaviour to settle. |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | When the base name changes, the Unicode values become the glyph data's for the new name whenever the data knows the name, directly or through the suffix, ligature or code-point rules, so renaming B to A.alt clears them; a name the data does not know keeps the stored values. Settled by observation (05 question 4). |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | Rename updates the font's references by name: components (also in backgrounds), kerning pairs, UFO groups, the glyphOrder parameter of the font and its instances, and metrics keys of the glyph and its layers. Feature code, kerning contexts, other custom parameters, hints and display strings are not rewritten; the result lists where the old name is still used. Settled by observation (05 question 9). |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | A typed production, alternative or code-point name becomes the nice name (as for a new glyph), unless the font uses custom naming. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | A name that breaks the naming rules is refused, as Find and Replace refuses one. Settled by observing whether the reference accepts an illegal name typed in Font View. |
| `glyph.rename` | M4 | handbook | yes | When a locked glyph uses the renamed glyph as a component, the rename is refused, because a locked glyph refuses changes to its layers. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setCase` | M4 | inferred | yes | Case is edited in the Selection Info window and in List View's Case column; the handbook names no control for it. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `glyph.setDirection` | M4 | inferred | yes | Writing direction is edited in the Selection Info window and in List View's Direction column; the handbook names no control for it. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `glyph.setDirection` | M4 | inferred | yes | The vertical directions VTR and VTL, which source files may store, are accepted too. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setColorLabel` | M4 | handbook | yes | Label names are matched without case and spaces, and grey is accepted for gray (lightGreen, Light Green and lightgreen are the same). |
| `glyph.setLayerColorLabel` | M4 | handbook | yes | Locked glyphs, and glyphs without the layer, are skipped and listed, because a locked glyph refuses changes to its layers. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setTags` | M4 | handbook | yes | A tag is trimmed, and an empty tag or a repeated one is dropped. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.removeTag` | M4 | inferred | yes | Deleting a capsule in the Tags field removes that tag from every selected glyph. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setSortName` | M4 | handbook | yes | Null clears both stored sort names: the one used with Keep Alternates Next to Base Glyph on and the one used with it off. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.setComponents` | M6 | handbook | yes | The cell lists component names separated by a comma and a space; a name added to the list appends a component at offset zero (aligned automatically when the layer allows it), a name removed deletes the last component with that name, and only the layer of the master shown in Font View changes; editing the cell in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.setComponents` | M6 | handbook | yes | Removing a locked component through the list is refused with a message naming it, and nothing changes; the layer's hints follow their shapes to their new places (PostScript hints and TrueType instructions count paths, so removing a component leaves them as they are) and a hint on a removed component goes with it; removing a locked component's name from the cell in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.copyGlyphNames` | M4 | handbook | yes | Names come in glyph order (the order Font View shows), not in the order they were selected. Settled by observation. |
| `glyph.copyGlyphNames` | M4 | handbook | yes | One per Line and Python List put no line break after the last line; code points are written in uppercase hex. Settled by pasting the reference's output into a text editor. |
| `glyphData.lookup` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; its answers must equal what the reference shows in Glyph Info and the inspector for the same name, which the oracles' copies of the data (fontc, glyphsLib) check. |
| `glyphData.lookup` | M4 | inferred | yes | A ligature without a record takes the script that all its known parts share, and no case. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.search` | M4 | inferred | yes | A query matches as a case-insensitive substring of the name, the description, the hex code point, or equals the character. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.search` | M4 | inferred | yes | Results are sorted by name unless another field is asked for, and at most 10,000 are returned. An empty query matches every record. |
| `glyphData.productionName` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; its answers must equal the names the oracles write (fontc and fontmake with production names on). |
| `font.validateGlyphNames` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; it applies the handbook's naming rules and also lists names used by more than one glyph. There is no reference behaviour to settle. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | A batch that would give two glyphs the same name, or any glyph an invalid name, is refused as a whole and the clashes are listed. Each rename follows glyph.rename. Settled by observation (05 question 9). |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | The results are not converted to nice names: Find and Replace gives the names it computes. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | In Regex mode a group reference is a backslash and one digit (`\0` is the whole match, `\10` is group 1 followed by 0), `\\` is a backslash, and `$` is plain text. Settled by renaming with a two-digit group reference and a `$` in the reference app. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | Matching is case-sensitive unless `matchCase` is false, in plain and Regex mode alike; with it false, plain Find is still plain text. Settled by observation. |
| `edit.findAndReplace` | M4 | handbook | yes | An empty Find appends Replace in Regex mode as in plain mode. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.setSearch` | M4 | handbook | yes | Glyphs that a new search hides are deselected, so batch commands never act on glyphs the view does not show. Settled by observing whether the reference keeps hidden glyphs selected after a search. |
| `fontView.setSearch` | M4 | handbook | yes | A term the matcher refuses (a regular expression that does not compile yet) is refused and the view stays as it was; the field keeps the typed text while it has the keyboard. Settled by typing an unfinished regular expression in the reference's field. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | Shift-click selects every entry listed between the last entry selected in the clicked entry's section and the clicked one, in any section (the handbook names filters only), and keeps the other sections' entries. Settled by Shift-clicking categories and languages in the reference. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | Without a selected entry in its section, a Shift-clicked entry is added as Cmd-click adds it. Settled by Shift-clicking an entry of an unused section in the reference. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | Glyphs that a new sidebar selection hides are deselected. Settled by observing whether the reference keeps hidden glyphs selected after choosing a sidebar entry. |
| `fontView.selectSidebarEntries` | M4 | handbook | yes | A selected entry that disappears (a removed or renamed filter, a category with no glyph left) is deselected. Settled by removing a selected filter in the reference. |
| `fontView.queryGlyphs` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own query for agents; it must give the glyphs the sidebar and search would show for the same entries and text. |
| `fontView.addSmartFilter` | M4 | handbook | yes | Headless, a filter added without rules gets one rule every glyph matches (Glyph Name contains an empty text), standing for the editor's single empty rule. Settled by observing what an untouched new smart filter shows. |
| `path.roundCoordinates` | M3 | handbook | yes | A font whose grid is off (spacing 0) rounds to whole units. |
| `path.roundCoordinates` | M3 | handbook | yes | With a selection, the handles next to a selected on-curve node are rounded with it. |
| `path.roundCoordinates` | M3 | handbook | yes | With a selection, anchors and components stay where they are, even when selected. |
| `path.roundCoordinatesForAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | The label "… for All Masters" is ours; the handbook describes the Option key but not the item's label (03a question 18). |
| `path.removeOverlapForAllMasters` | M3 | handbook | yes | The label "… for All Masters" is ours; the handbook describes the Option key but not the item's label (03a question 18). |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyphForAllMasters` | M6 | handbook | yes | The shortcut is Cmd+Alt+Shift+D, Decompose with Option added as for the other Option variants; the Glyph menu of the reference app with Option held settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyphForAllMasters` | M6 | handbook | yes | Only master layers are decomposed; intermediate and alternate layers keep their components. A composite with a brace layer in the reference app settles it. |
| `path.convertToQuadratic` | M3 | handbook | yes | New off-curve points are rounded to the grid. |
| `path.convertToQuadratic` | M3 | handbook | yes | With allMasters, master layers with the same node structure are converted together (one spline length for all), so they stay compatible. |
| `fontView.keyboard` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Each key above behaves as in a file browser's icon view. Settled by pressing them in the reference's Font View. |
| `fontView.keyboard` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Plus comes before minus at the bottom left. Settled by looking at the reference's Font View. |
| `fontView.keyboard` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Minus is greyed out with no selection. Settled by looking at the reference with nothing selected. |
| `fontView.cell.followsMaster` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Font View's master is its own: choosing a master in a tab does not change it, and a tab opened from Font View starts in it. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.followsMaster` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Each document window keeps its own selected master, as each font has its own window in the reference app; switching between two open fonts there would settle it. |
| `fontView.cell.contentBySize` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Names and Unicode markers show from a cell size of 64 points up. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.unicodeIndicator` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | CJK glyphs are those whose first code point is in the Han, Hiragana, Katakana, Hangul or Bopomofo script; the character is drawn with the interface font, and the marker is drawn when that font lacks it. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.unicodeIndicator` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The marker is a small grey square. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeMetricsOutOfSync` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Any layer the out-of-sync scan covers counts, not only the layer of the master Font View shows; a font whose second master alone is out of sync, viewed on the first master in the reference app, would settle it. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeMetricsOutOfSync` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Unlinked keys and keys that cannot be evaluated give no triangle; a key naming a missing glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeAnnotation` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Drawn in the top-left corner; the handbook gives no position. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `fontView.cell.badgeCustomInfo` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Drawn in the bottom-left corner; the handbook gives no position. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `fontView.cell.badgeCustomInfo` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A glyph counts as custom when it stores a category, subcategory, script, production name, case, direction or sort name that differs from the database's, or Unicode values that differ from the database's for its name. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.badgeNonExporting` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Drawn at the top centre of the cell; the handbook gives no position. Settled by observation (05 question 15). |
| `fontView.cell.colorLayerOnly` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The cut-out is a triangle over 30% of the side at the top-left corner. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.cell.imageDisplay` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The image is drawn at its own opacity (50 % when the file states none), as Edit View draws it. Settled by a Font View cell of a glyph with an image in the reference. |
| `fontView.cell.imageDisplay` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A missing or unreadable file draws the missing-image marker in the cell. Settled by moving an image file away and looking at Font View in the reference. |
| `fontView.column.char` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A character the interface font lacks is drawn with the glyph's own outline from the document, at row height in the text colour, instead of a system font's. Settled by observing the reference with a code point that the system fonts lack. |
| `fontView.column.components` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The cell shows and edits the layer of the master Font View shows, not the first master's; editing the cell with a second master selected in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontView.column.otherProperties` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Every column is shown by default, in this order: ID, Char, Name, Unicode, Production Name, Script, Category, Subcategory, Case, Direction, Exports, Color, Tags, Sort Name, Note, Components, Last Changed, LSB, RSB, Width, Left Group, Right Group. Settled by observation (05 question 14). |
| `fontView.column.otherProperties` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Besides the four cells the handbook calls editable (Name, Unicode, Note, Components), cells of properties that have a set command (Exports, colour, tags, production name, script, category, subcategory, case, direction, sort name) are editable too. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.mixedExport` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A click on the mixed box makes every selected glyph export. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A field shows the metrics key that governs it (the layer's own key first, written with ==), else the measured value with up to two decimals; a screenshot of the reference inspector on a glyph with a key would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The orientation icon sits after the first field; the vertical set puts TSB and BSB on the first row and vertical origin and vertical width on a second row; a screenshot of the reference inspector would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A field left empty commits nothing; trying it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.metricsFields` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The vertical origin shows 0 and the vertical width shows ascender minus descender when the layer stores none; the reference inspector on a new glyph would settle it. |
| `fontView.inspector.tagsField` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Leaving the Tags field also commits the typed text as a tag; Escape drops it. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.tagsField` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Each capsule has a cross that removes the tag from every selected glyph (glyph.removeTag). Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector.tagsField` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Several tags pasted at once with commas are added one command each. Settled by observation. |
| `addGlyphs.recipe` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A recipe that names a glyph the font does not have adds nothing and is listed in `skipped` with the missing names; trying a recipe with a missing part in the reference app settles it. |
| `addGlyphs.recipe` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A recipe for a glyph the font already has follows the same Keep, Replace and Cancel answers as a plain name, and Replace rebuilds the glyph from the recipe, keeping its code points; a recipe for an existing glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `addGlyphs.recipe` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The name after `=` is kept as typed, without turning a legacy name into a nice name, and gets the code point the glyph data gives it unless another glyph has it; a recipe ending in `=uni00E9` in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontView.sidebar.missingBadge` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The badge counts expected glyphs present by name or by code point (a glyph with the character counts even under another name). Settled by observing a badge for a font that names a glyph differently. |
| `fontView.paste.glyphs` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Pasted glyphs take their place in the default sort order, like added glyphs. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.paste.nameClashSuffix` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A renamed pasted glyph has no code points, so the existing glyph stays the one for its characters. Settled by observation. |
| `removeGlyph.usedAsComponent` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Decompose reverses each path that a mirroring component transform (negative determinant) places, as export decomposition does, so the outline keeps its direction. Settled by decomposing a mirrored component in the reference app and checking the path direction. |
| `glyphData.caseAndDirectionEncoding` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | A script is right to left when most of its letters with a code point are strong right-to-left characters in Unicode 17.0: adlam, arabic, avestan, chorasmian, elymaic, hebrew, mandaic, nko, rovas, samaritan, syriac, thaana and yezidi. A glyph with neither a stated direction nor a script is right to left when the character its name stands for is. Settled by observing the reference's directions. |
| `glyphData.legacyUnicode` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | Adding by a legacy character (typing U+FB04) finds the record through unicodeLegacy and creates f_f_l without a code point. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.lookupOrder` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | When two built-in records share a code point or a production name, the first by name wins; the pinned data has no such pair. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.customFile` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A custom record replaces the built-in record of the same name as a whole: fields it leaves out are empty, not taken from the built-in record. Settled by observation. |
| `glyphData.customFile` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A file over a limit (16 MB, 200,000 records, 1,024 bytes per attribute value, two levels of elements) or with an entity declaration or a malformed record is rejected as a whole; attributes the format does not define are skipped with a warning. |
| `glyphData.customFile` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Beside a source file and in the Info folder beside it, every file named GlyphData*.xml is read, in name order, those beside the file first; at most 32 files of the user's and 32 of a document's. Settled by observation. |
| `export.productionNameLigature` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | A ligature part whose production name has a dot is joined as fontc joins it (bh_a-telugu → uni0C2D.half0C05), not replaced by the cleaned glyph name as glyphsLib does. Settled by exporting such a ligature from the reference app. |
| `export.cidMapping` (behavior) | M12 | handbook | – | A glyph named cid followed by a decimal number takes that CID, as CID-keyed fonts and AFDKO's tools name CID glyphs; exporting such a glyph from the reference app with the ROS parameter would settle it. |
| `export.cidMapping` (behavior) | M12 | handbook | – | Without bundled collection data a nice-named glyph gets its CID from the Unicode CMap file the user gives, through its first code point the file maps; the reference app maps nice names from its own data, which exporting a nice-named glyph would show. |
| `import.keepGlyphNames` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | The glyphOrder parameter is added whatever the setting says, following the Opening Font Files page rather than the Glyph Order page, which ties it to the setting; opening a TTF with the setting off would settle it. |
| `glyphName.firstCharacter` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | .null is exempt like .notdef. |
| `glyphName.invalidBlocksExport` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Only glyphs that export are checked: a glyph that is not written to the font cannot block its export. Settled by exporting a font whose non-exporting glyph has an invalid name. |
| `glyphName.invalidBlocksExport` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The glyph's name is checked, not a production name it stores; a stored production name with bad characters is cleaned at export like any other. Settled by exporting a font with such a production name. |
| `glyphName.clickToEdit` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A click on the name of the glyph that is already the only one selected starts editing; a click on another glyph's name selects it. Return or a click elsewhere commits through glyph.rename, Escape cancels. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.invertSelection` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Invert Selection works among the visible glyphs, as Select All does. Settled by observation (05 question 12). |
| `fontView.batch.appliesToSelection` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | With no glyph shown and nothing selected, a glyph command refuses with a status message instead of acting on the font's first glyph or on every glyph; whether the reference greys such menu items out would settle it. |
| `fontView.batch.addComponent` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | When the component cannot go into one selected glyph (it would contain itself), the whole batch is refused and no glyph changes; adding a glyph to a selection that includes it in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontView.batch.palette` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Fit Curve applies each glyph's remembered node indices to its layer of Font View's master; glyphs never edited, or whose nodes are gone, are skipped; the run is one undo step. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.batch.pluginsAndScripts` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | A filter module run from Font View acts on the selected glyphs' layers of Font View's master only, not on every master; observing a filter plug-in run on a Font View selection in a font with two masters would settle it. |
| `fontView.search.fields` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A plain term matches names and notes by substring; it matches a code point when it is four to six hexadecimal digits (optionally after U+ or 0x) equal to it, and a character when it is that one character. Shorter hexadecimal terms such as FE only match names, so a term like a does not also find every code point containing A. Settled by observing a search for 00FE and for FE. |
| `fontView.search.regex` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | With Regex on, the pattern must match a whole field (name, hex code point or note) among the fields the magnifier menu selects; the handbook's examples only show names. Settled by observation (05 question 13). |
| `fontView.sidebar.categoryFilter` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A category lists case groups (Uppercase, Lowercase, Smallcaps, Minor: the glyphs with that case, or with the subcategory older files store under the same name) and then its subcategories, each only when the font has such glyphs or the shown languages expect some. Settled by observing the expanded Letter entry. |
| `fontView.sidebar.scriptAutoShown` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The default short list is latin, greek and cyrillic. Settled by observing the Languages section of a new user. |
| `smartFilter.rule.glyphName` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | Text rules (Glyph Name, Tags, Script) offer is, is not, contains, does not contain, begins with, ends with and matches (a regular expression); number rules offer =, ≠, <, ≤, >, ≥; yes-or-no and choice rules (Case, Category & Subcategory, the colour labels, Custom) offer is and is not. The reference's stored predicates use ==, !=, <, >, CONTAINS, IN, BEGINSWITH and ENDSWITH (tutorial "Custom sidebar entries in Font View"), which this set covers. Settled by observing the rule editor (05 question 10). |
| `smartFilter.rule.categoryAndSubcategory` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The rule is a choice (is, is not) of a category or a category and subcategory, written Letter/Uppercase; the case groups Uppercase, Lowercase, Smallcaps and Minor also match glyphs by case, as data that stores case instead of those subcategories does. Settled by observing the rule's value menu. |
| `smartFilter.rule.case` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The rule offers all five case values, minor included, as the Case property has five; the rule's own description lists four. Settled by observation (05 question 10). |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasTrueTypeHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A TrueType hint left without its points (its nodes were deleted, so it is never compiled) does not count; a reference font with such a hint, filtered by this rule, would show whether it does. |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasAnchors` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Only the layer's own anchors count, not those it gets from its components; filtering a composite without anchors of its own in the reference app settles it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.containsComponent` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | The glyph name is compared whole, not as a substring; trying a partial name in the reference app settles it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasMetricsKeys` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A metrics key stored on one layer only (a local key) makes the rule true for the glyph; a glyph with a layer-local key in the reference app would settle it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.metricsKeysOutOfSync` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Keys that name a missing glyph or cannot be evaluated do not count as out of sync, and a key out of sync on any master counts; the reference app on a glyph with an unlinked key and on one out of sync in one master only would settle it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.hasKerningGroups` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A vertical kerning group (top or bottom) alone makes the rule true, as a left or right group does; the reference app on a glyph with only a vertical group would settle it. |
| `smartFilter.rule.colorLabel` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A glyph whose label is an RGBA colour, not one of the twelve, counts as set but equals none of the twelve. Settled by observing a file with an RGBA label. |
| `smartFilter.rule.custom` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The language adds matches (a regular expression over the whole value), which fea-rs refuses, because the Glyph Name rule offers matches and compiles to this language; && and \|\| stand for AND and OR, and a yes-or-no attribute alone means == true. A comparison on tags holds when it holds for one tag. Settled by trying these forms in the reference's Custom rule. |
| `smartFilter.live` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Results are recomputed from the current font whenever the filter is shown and after every edit, for the master Font View shows. Settled by observation (05 question 11). |
| `listFilter.order` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | When several filters are selected and one is a list filter, the result follows the first selected list filter; a folder shows glyph order even when it holds list filters. Settled by observing a list filter combined with a smart filter and with a folder. |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | An opened file keeps its stored glyph order, which is the order the oracles compile; only glyphs added later, renamed, or given a sort name move to the place the sort key gives. Settled by observing where the reference puts a new glyph (05 question 2). |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | The published data has no sort names, so the key is ours: category in the order Letter, Number, Punctuation, Symbol, Separator, Mark, then glyphs without one; within a category the base glyph's first code point, then the name; suffixed glyphs after the run of base glyphs, or right after their base with Keep Alternates Next to Base Glyph on. Settled by observation (05 question 2). |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | A sort name (from custom glyph data or the glyph itself) places the glyph after the others of its category, ordered by the sort name. |
| `glyphOrder.default` (behavior) | M4 | handbook | – | In a stored order that is not sorted by the key, a new glyph goes right after the last glyph whose key is not greater than its own (first when there is none), so it follows the glyphs it would follow in a sorted font. Settled by observing where the reference puts a new glyph in a hand-ordered font. |
| `fontView.image.dropOnCell` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The active layer of a glyph in Font View is its layer of Font View's master. Settled by dropping an image on a cell while Font View shows the second master in the reference. |
| `fontView.image.dropOnCell` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Several files dropped on Font View are matched to glyph names, whatever cell they land on, as Add Image does with several files. Settled by dropping two files on one cell in the reference. |
| `fontView.image.batchByFileName` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Files with no glyph of their name are skipped and listed in the result; no glyph is created. Settled by Add Image with an unmatched file in the reference. |
| `fontView.image.caseSensitivity` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | File names are matched to glyph names exactly, case included, on every platform. Settled by Add Image with `a.png` in a font with `A` and `a` in the reference. |
| `fontView.setSidebarEntryOpen` | M4 | inferred | no | The sections start open and every other entry with children starts closed. Settled by opening a new document in the reference. |
| `glyphDataEditor.show` | M14 | inferred | no | The editor opens from the Window menu with no shortcut; the companion tool is a separate application, so nothing settles a menu place. |
| `fontView.grid` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Shift+click adds every visible glyph from the last clicked glyph to the clicked one; Cmd+click adds or removes one glyph; a click on empty space clears the selection. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.grid` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Dragging across the grid draws a rectangle that selects the cells it touches, added to the selection while Shift or Cmd is held. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.grid` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Section headers of the glyphOrder parameter show only while the view lists every glyph in glyph order (no filter, search or sort). Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.list` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A click on an editable cell of the only selected row, or a double-click on it, starts editing; a click on an Exports cell flips the flag; a double-click on a cell that cannot be edited opens the glyph. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.list` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Values that come from the glyph data rather than from the glyph are drawn dimmed. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | With several glyphs selected the Name field is read-only and shows Multiple Values, since glyph names are unique. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The metric and kerning group fields are shown read-only until their commands exist (M5). Settled by M5. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A Unicode value typed with several glyphs selected goes to all of them, as for any other field. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.inspector` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The orientation icon between the metric fields is left out until M5 (fontView.toggleMetricsOrientation). Settled by M5. |
| `window.selectionInfo` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Turning on the sort name check box only enables the field; the field's value is stored when it is committed. Turning it off removes the stored sort name (glyph.setSortName with null). Settled by observation. |
| `window.selectionInfo` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The window is not modal and follows the selection while it is open. Settled by observation. |
| `window.selectionInfo` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The fields are in the order name, exports, glyph colour, layer colour, tags, Unicode, production name, script, category, subcategory, case, direction, sort name, note, the three metrics keys and the two kerning groups. Settled by opening Info for Selection in the reference. |
| `window.selectionInfo` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The layer colour field edits the layer of Font View's master. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A right-click on a glyph that is not selected selects it alone first; on a selected glyph the menu acts on the whole selection. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The items come in this order: the colour swatches under a caption (Glyph Color, or Layer Color with Alt), then Tags, then Copy Glyph Names. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | With Alt held the swatches set the layer colour of Font View's master. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The swatches sit in a Glyph Color submenu, Exports follows them, then Tags and Copy Glyph Names. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Exports shows no mark when only some of the glyphs export, and choosing it then makes them all export; with all exporting it turns export off. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Exports is in this menu as the Glyphs 3 video shows it in the canvas menu. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.glyphContextMenu` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | More Tags… shows the sidebar with the glyph inspector, whose Tags field lists every tag. Settled by observation. |
| `fontView.sidebar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The sections start open; categories, scripts, folders and groups start closed. Settled by opening a new document in the reference. |
| `fontView.sidebar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The actions menu sits at the foot of the sidebar, below the entries. Settled by a screenshot of the reference's Font View. |
| `fontView.sidebar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A script entry shows the Unicode Script property value its glyph-data id stands for (`Latin`, `Tibetan`, `Canadian Aboriginal`), not the id; paths and parameters keep the id. Settled by a screenshot of the reference's Languages section. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A language requires its CLDR main exemplar characters only; the auxiliary, index, numbers and punctuation sets are not counted. Settled by comparing a language's badge total in the reference with its CLDR sets. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | CLDR lists letters in lowercase, so each character's single-character uppercase form is added (ß gets none; Georgian gets no Mtavruli capitals). Settled by observing whether the reference's badge for a Latin-script language counts capitals. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A language is listed under the glyph database's script that most of its exemplar characters belong to, ignoring Common and Inherited characters (Japanese falls under han, Korean under hangul). Settled by observing where the reference lists Japanese and other mixed-script languages. |
| `fontView.sidebar.languages` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Every CLDR locale with its own main exemplar set is a language entry, regional and script variants included (de-CH, sr-Latn); those that inherit their parent's set are left out. Settled by observing which entries the reference lists under one script. |
| `fontView.sidebar.filters` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A double-click anywhere on a filter's row except its icon and disclosure triangle renames it, the empty space after the name included. Settled by double-clicking right of a short filter name in the reference. |
| `fontView.sidebar.filters` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Return renames only while the sidebar has the keyboard, which it takes when a row is pressed and gives up when anything else is pressed. Settled by selecting a filter, clicking a glyph cell and pressing Return in the reference. |
| `fontView.sidebar.filters` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Over a folder's middle half a dragged filter goes into the folder, at its end; over the top or bottom quarter it goes before or after the folder, and below an open folder with entries it goes first inside it. Settled by observing the reference's drop feedback. |
| `fontView.sidebar.filters` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | An unchanged or empty name typed into the field runs no command. Settled by renaming a filter to its own name in the reference. |
| `fontView.sidebar.filters` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Escape or a click away from the name field ends the edit and renames nothing; macOS lists often keep the typed name on a click away. Settled by typing a new name and clicking a glyph cell in the reference. |
| `fontView.searchField` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The magnifier menu offers All Fields, Name Only, Unicode Value Only and Note Only as one choice, then Match Case and Regex. Settled by opening the reference's magnifier menu. |
| `fontView.statusBar` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The counts read "N selected · N shown · N in font". Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.addGlyphsExisting` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The buttons sit Cancel, Replace, Keep Existing from left to right, Keep Existing the default (Return). Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.missingGlyphs` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Every missing glyph starts chosen, so Generate right away adds them all. Settled by opening the reference's list and pressing Generate. |
| `dialog.pasteSpecial` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The dialog opens on Glyphs With Same Name with All Data ticked. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.removeGlyph` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Decompose is the default button because it keeps other glyphs' outlines; observing the reference dialog would settle it. |
| `dialog.removeGlyph` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | With components to answer, the buttons sit Cancel, Remove, Decompose from left to right. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.removeGlyph` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The metrics-key question is a second pop-up menu in the same dialog (Dissolve first) instead of a second dialog. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.findAndReplaceGlyphNames` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A Match Case checkbox, on by default, as names are case-sensitive; the handbook names only Find, Replace and Regex. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.findAndReplaceGlyphNames` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Nothing is renamed until Replace; there is no live preview. Settled by observation. |
| `dialog.smartFilterEditor` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | Besides Alt-click on `+`, every row keeps a separate `…` button that adds a group without Alt. Settled by observing whether the reference's editor shows a dots button while Option is up. |
| `dialog.smartFilterEditor` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A new smart filter is named New Filter, or New Filter 2 and so on when the name is taken. Settled by adding a smart filter in the reference. |
| `dialog.listFilterEditor` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | A new list filter is named New List, or New List 2 and so on when the name is taken. Settled by adding a list filter in the reference. |
| `dialog.languagesList` (panel) | M4 | handbook | – | The checklist offers every script the language data lists and the default ones, alphabetically. Settled by opening the reference's list. |

## `06-font-info.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `font.info.selectTab` | M7 | inferred | yes | Our own command for scripts; with the window closed it opens Font Info on that tab. Nothing to observe: the reference app switches tabs only by clicking them. |
| `font.info.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Setting a field the scope offers but the target lacks adds it, and a text sets a localizable field's default language; typing into an empty field row in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.listFields` | M7 | inferred | yes | The Font tab offers familyNames, designers, designerURL, manufacturers, manufacturerURL, copyrights, versionString, vendorID, uniqueID, licenses, licenseURL, trademarks, descriptions, sampleTexts, postscriptFontName, postscriptFullNames, compatibleFullNames, WWSFamilyName, WWSSubfamilyName and variationsPostScriptNamePrefix; the Exports tab the same plus styleNames, styleMapFamilyNames, styleMapStyleNames, preferredFamilyNames, preferredSubfamilyNames and variableStyleNames; the Masters tab offers none and keeps master properties as read. The pickers of the reference app settle the three lists. |
| `font.info.addField` | M7 | handbook | yes | Adding a field that is already present on a target is refused with a message pointing to Set Localized Value; the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.addField` | M7 | handbook | yes | A new localizable field gets one entry in the default language (dflt) holding the given value, or else the built-in value it overrides (familyNames takes the Family Name, styleNames the Style Name), else empty text; adding the field in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.removeField` | M7 | handbook | yes | Built-in fields (Family Name, Units per Em, Version, Creation Date, Style Name, Weight Class, Width Class) cannot be removed; only added fields and custom parameters can; the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.setLocalizedValue` | M7 | handbook | yes | A language tag the name table cannot map to a Windows language id is refused, except dflt (the default); the language menu of the reference app settles which tags it offers. |
| `font.info.removeLocalizedValue` | M7 | handbook | yes | Removing the last language entry of a field removes the field; removing its default (dflt) entry while others remain is allowed, and export then uses the English entry as the default name; the reference app settles both. |
| `font.info.copyRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | Rows are copied as our own JSON (scope, kind, key, value, enabled) and, for custom parameters and fields, also as property-list text in the file format's spelling, so a text editor or another document can read them; copying rows in the reference app and pasting into a text editor settles the format. |
| `font.info.pasteRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | The mode append adds parameters after the others and, since a field cannot repeat, replaces a field the target has; skip leaves out every row the target already has; pasting in the reference app settles both. |
| `font.info.pasteRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | A pasted row whose field or single-valued parameter is already present replaces its value, a repeatable parameter is appended, a row not allowed in the target scope is skipped and listed, and pasting between tabs and between documents is allowed; pasting in the reference app settles all four. |
| `font.info.pasteRows` | M7 | handbook | yes | A pasted row whose value its check refuses (one stored before the check existed, say) is skipped and listed with the reason, and the other rows are pasted; pasting such a row in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | Scaling by new/old covers every layer of every glyph (paths, component offsets but not component scales, anchors, guides, hints, backgrounds, special layers), the advance widths and vertical metrics, master metric positions and zone sizes, stems, kerning, global guides, and the custom parameters whose catalogue entry is measured in units (typoAscender, hheaAscender, winAscent, underlinePosition and the like); metrics keys, italic angles and percentages are kept; the reference app on a font holding all of these settles the list. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | Scaled coordinates are rounded to the font's grid (none when the grid spacing is 0), and a scale that would put a coordinate beyond ±32768 is refused; the reference app settles the rounding. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | A placed image keeps its place over the scaled outline (its position is scaled and its own scale multiplied by the factor), stroke widths and the positions and widths of hints not attached to nodes are scaled, and named number values are kept; the reference app on a font holding these settles it. |
| `font.info.scaleToUnitsPerEm` | M7 | handbook | yes | Shapes the model keeps as stored (shape groups, paths with node kinds it cannot read) are not scaled, and a font with a locked glyph is refused naming the glyph, as every other edit of a locked glyph is; the reference app settles whether it scales locked glyphs. |
| `font.info.stepVersion` | M7 | handbook | yes | One step is 1 on the minor version; a step above 999 or below 0 is refused with a message and nothing changes, and the major part has no stepper; the stepper of the reference app settles both ends. |
| `font.info.updateDate` | M7 | handbook | yes | A given date with a time-zone offset is converted to UTC before it is stored; the reference app has no such parameter, so nothing there settles it. |
| `font.info.updateDate` | M7 | handbook | yes | The update button stores the current time in UTC (+0000); the date the reference app saves after the button settles the time zone. |
| `font.axes.list` | M9 | inferred | yes | A query of ours: the reply lists each axis's index, name, tag and hidden flag in Font Info order; the reference app shows the same facts only in its Axes list. |
| `font.axes.list` | M9 | inferred | yes | An axis whose tag another axis shares (possible in a file opened from disk) gets a warning that the font exports only with every tag different, and stays editable by index; opening such a file in the reference app settles what it shows. |
| `font.axes.add` | M9 | handbook | yes | A new axis is named Weight with tag wght when the font has no such axis, else a private axis `Axis N` (N its place in the list) with the first free tag of AXSA, AXSB…; settle by pressing the plus button in the reference app. |
| `font.axes.add` | M9 | handbook | yes | Existing masters, instances and virtual masters get coordinate 0 on the new axis, and intermediate layers inherit it from their master; settle in the reference app's Masters tab after adding an axis. |
| `font.axes.add` | M9 | handbook | yes | A name or a tag (compared as exported, padded to four characters) that another axis already has is refused; settle by giving two axes the same tag or name in the reference app's Font Info. |
| `font.axes.set` | M9 | handbook | yes | A tag must be one to four printable ASCII characters not starting with a space; a longer one is refused by the command, while a source's longer tag is kept and exports its first four characters. Settle by typing a five-character tag in the reference app's axis tag field. |
| `font.axes.set` | M9 | handbook | yes | Renaming an axis renames its entries in every Axis Location and Virtual Master parameter, and a new tag renames its Axis Mappings entry; settle by renaming an axis in a font with those parameters in the reference app and saving. |
| `font.axes.remove` | M9 | handbook | yes | Removing an axis drops its coordinate from every master, instance, virtual master, intermediate layer and alternate range; an alternate range that becomes empty on every axis stays an alternate layer `[]`. Settle in the reference app. |
| `font.axes.remove` | M9 | handbook | yes | The removed axis's entries in Axis Location, Virtual Master and Axis Mappings are dropped, and a parameter left without entries is removed; settle by removing an axis in a font with those parameters in the reference app and saving. |
| `font.axes.listPredefined` | M9 | inferred | yes | The list is the five registered axes (Weight, Width, Italic, Slant, Optical Size) followed by Grade and the eight parametric axes proposed for registration (XOPQ, YOPQ, XTRA, YTUC, YTLC, YTAS, YTDE, YTFI) with the names the public axis registry gives them; settle by opening the list in the reference app. |
| `font.customParameters.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | The result lists rows in stored order with `enabled` and `known` flags and the values as stored; the reference app's scripting access to custom parameters would settle the shape. |
| `font.customParameters.describe` | M7 | inferred | yes | The search matches the query against names, aliases and summaries, case ignored; the Font Info parameter picker's filter in the running app would settle it. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | One scope may hold the same parameter more than once; for a parameter that takes one value the first enabled row counts and the list marks the others; adding a duplicate in the reference app settles whether it is refused. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | Without a value the new row holds the kind's empty value (off, 0, empty text, empty list); the new rows of the reference app settle it. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A parameter whose appendix entry names its scopes is refused in other scopes; one whose entry names none is allowed in all three. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A name the catalogue does not know is added as given, its value checked only against the size limits, and listed with the unknown mark; whether the reference app's picker accepts a typed free name settles it. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A name property (designers, versionString and the others the format keeps as properties) added here is written to the General fields, refused on masters and refused where the field is already set; adding one from the reference app's picker settles where it goes. |
| `font.customParameters.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A value naming a missing master warns and keeps the value; typing an unknown master name in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.customParameters.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | A value is checked against the catalogue's kind before it is stored, and an unknown name is refused rather than stored as is; editing a row in the running app's Font Info would settle it. |
| `font.master.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | The list gives id, name, icon and axis coordinates only, our choice of the fields a script needs to pick a master; the reference app's scripting access to masters would settle it. |
| `font.master.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | Several masters report null for a value they do not share, as the Font Info window shows multiple values; the window with two masters selected would settle it. |
| `font.master.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | The new master is called Regular, takes the first master's axis coordinates and the file format's default metric values scaled to the UPM (ascender 800, cap height 700, x-height 500, descender −200 at 1000 UPM, zones 16 and −16 at 1000 UPM), has no stem values, number values or parameters, and goes to the end of the list; every glyph gets an empty layer for it; adding a master in the reference app settles name, coordinates, values and position. |
| `font.master.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | Each glyph's empty layer for the new master takes the advance width of its layer in the first master (0 without one); the width of a new master's layers in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.master.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A font with a locked glyph refuses adding, duplicating and removing masters until the glyph is unlocked, since those change the glyph's layers; doing so with a locked glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.master.addFromOtherFont` | M9 | handbook | yes | Metrics are matched by kind and name, stems and numbers by name; a value the other font lacks takes the active font's first master's value. Settle with two fonts whose metrics differ in the reference app. |
| `font.master.addFromOtherFont` | M9 | handbook | yes | Only each glyph's master layer comes along, not the other font's special layers of that master; settle with F7 in the reference app. |
| `font.master.addFromOtherFont` | M9 | handbook | yes | Glyphs missing in the imported master get an empty layer for it; glyphs only the other font has are not added; axis coordinates come from the other font's master by axis tag, 0 for tags it lacks. Settle by adding a master from a font with different glyphs in the reference app. |
| `font.master.duplicate` | M7 | handbook | yes | Each copy goes right after its original with a new id and the same name; the original's master layers, their backgrounds, the special layers tied to it and its kerning are copied and tied to the copy; duplicating in the reference app settles position and special layers. |
| `font.master.remove` | M7 | handbook | yes | Removing a master removes its layers in every glyph, the special layers tied to it and its kerning; instances stay; parameters that name it (Link Metrics With Master, Get Hints From Master, Variable Font Origin) stay and the command's result names them in a warning; removing such a master in the reference app settles both. |
| `font.master.remove` | M7 | handbook | yes | An instance's manual interpolation weight for a removed master is dropped with it; removing a master an instance with manual interpolation uses in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.master.setIcon` | M7 | handbook | yes | A glyph icon is stored in the master's iconName, as a built-in icon is; the command refuses an icon name outside the 25 and a glyph the font lacks; the iconName the reference app saves after typing a glyph name in the picker settles the first part. |
| `font.metrics.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | Metrics are listed in the font's order with category, name and scope, plus one master's positions when asked; the Masters tab in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.metrics.add` | M7 | inferred | yes | Add Metric appends a definition of the given category and gives every master position 0 and zone 0; the reference app settles whether the Metrics heading has a plus button and what it adds. |
| `font.metrics.add` | M7 | inferred | yes | A definition equal to an existing one (same category, name and scope) is refused, and a metric without a category needs a name; adding a second plain x-height in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.metrics.set` | M7 | handbook | yes | A category change that the masters' values do not fit (a baseline off 0, an italic angle beyond ±89°) is refused rather than changing the values; changing a metric with a value of 800 to Baseline in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.metrics.remove` | M7 | inferred | yes | Every metric can be removed, Baseline and Italic Angle included, and its values go from every master; the reference app settles whether the built-in metrics can be deleted. |
| `font.master.setMetric` | M7 | handbook | yes | Zone sizes may be negative (zones below a line, such as at the baseline and the descender); Italic Angle takes a position only, between −89 and 89 degrees; typing a negative zone in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.stems.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | Stems are listed in the font's order with name, orientation and scope, plus one master's values when asked; the Masters tab in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.stems.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | A stem added without a name is called `Stem` with `.1`, `.2` appended while taken, names are unique, and every master gets 0 unless `values` gives its value; adding a stem in the reference app settles the default name and value. |
| `font.numbers.list` | M8 | inferred | yes | Numbers are listed in the font's order, plus one master's values when asked; the Masters tab in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.numbers.add` | M8 | handbook | yes | A number value name is letters, digits and `_`, not starting with a digit, so that a `$name` token can read all of it; a leading `.` is allowed for settings such as `.monospace`, which the handbook names. A name the font has is refused. Naming a number value with a space, a hyphen or an inner dot in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.numbers.add` | M8 | handbook | yes | The plus button adds a number value called `value` (`value1`, `value2`… while taken) for the user to rename, because the handbook does not say what a new one is called; clicking the plus button twice in the reference app settles it. |
| `font.numbers.rename` | M8 | inferred | yes | Renaming a number value leaves tokens in feature code unchanged, so they then name an unknown value and the next compile reports them; renaming one used in feature code in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.instance.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | An instance whose source states no class reports the format's defaults (400 and 5); the Exports tab showing such an instance in the running app would settle it. |
| `font.instance.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | The result includes every General field the entry has and its custom parameters in stored order; the Exports tab in the running app would settle which fields are shown. |
| `font.instance.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Several entries change in one undo step, and a value one of them cannot take refuses the whole call; editing several exports at once in the running app would settle it. |
| `font.instance.add` | M7 | handbook | yes | The new instance has Weight Class 400, Width Class 5, the first master's axis coordinates, Active on, no style linking, and goes to the end of the list; adding an instance in the reference app settles the defaults and the position. |
| `font.instance.addForEachMaster` | M9 | handbook | yes | Every chosen master gets an instance even when one already sits at its location, with no weight or width class of its own; settle by running the command twice on F1 in the reference app. |
| `font.instance.toMaster` | M9 | handbook | yes | The new master goes to the end of the master list; settle with F1 in the reference app. |
| `font.instance.toMaster` | M9 | handbook | yes | An instance on a master's location is refused, since two masters at one location cannot interpolate; settle by trying it in the reference app. |
| `font.instance.toMaster` | M9 | handbook | yes | An intermediate layer at the instance's location is removed, the new master layer holding the same design; settle with a brace layer at the instance's location in the reference app. |
| `font.instance.toMaster` | M9 | handbook | yes | The new master gets the main design of glyphs with alternate layers and no alternate layers of its own; settle with F1's alternate glyph in the reference app. |
| `font.instance.addVariableFontSetting` | M9 | handbook | yes | A new setting is called Regular and stores no coordinates; settle by adding one in the reference app. |
| `font.instance.placement` | M9 | inferred | yes | A query of ours: the reference app shows extrapolation only in the Exports tab and in the export; the reply's shape is ours. |
| `font.instance.setStyleLinking` | M7 | handbook | yes | An empty base field with Bold or Italic checked links to Regular; exporting such an instance from the reference app settles it. |
| `font.instance.setWidthClass` | M7 | handbook | yes | Values outside 1–9 are refused, typed or not; typing 10 into the field of the reference app settles it. |
| `font.features.list` | M8 | inferred | yes | Entries are listed prefixes, classes, then features, each in stored order, with a flag for current compile errors; the Features tab sidebar in the running app would settle the order. |
| `font.features.get` | M8 | inferred | yes | Stylistic set names are returned as language and value pairs in stored order; the Features tab with a named stylistic set would settle it. |
| `font.features.listAvailable` | M8 | inferred | yes | A tag is offered as generatable when one of our generators can write an entry of that name in this font; the Add Feature picker in the running app would settle which tags it marks. |
| `font.features.addPrefix` | M8 | handbook | yes | A new prefix without a name is called Prefix, then Prefix 2, Prefix 3 and so on, and a prefix name another prefix has is refused; adding two prefixes in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.addFeature` | M8 | handbook | yes | Adding a tag the font already has is refused, although files may hold a tag twice; adding a present feature again from the picker in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.addFeature` | M8 | handbook | yes | A feature added without `automatic` is a manual entry with empty code, and the picker asks for automatic code when Update could generate the feature; adding smcp from the picker to a font with .sc glyphs in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.rename` | M8 | inferred | yes | A double-click on a sidebar entry's name renames it; a feature tag must be four characters and a name may not be taken in its group, else the command is refused; the running app would settle it. |
| `font.features.setCode` | M8 | inferred | yes | Set Feature Code refuses an automatic entry instead of switching it to manual; typing into an automatic entry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.update` | M8 | handbook | yes | An automatic entry whose generator no longer writes it (its glyphs are gone) is removed, as the handbook says automatic classes are at export; Update after deleting every .sc glyph would settle it. |
| `font.features.update` | M8 | handbook | yes | A new feature goes before the first feature that sorts after it in this order, else last: aalt, ccmp, locl, the Indic features, subs, sinf, sups, numr, dnom, frac, afrc, ordn, lnum, pnum, tnum, onum, case, the positional forms, dlig, liga, hlig, rlig, c2sc, smcp, c2pc, pcap, hist, titl, swsh, ornm, salt, ss01 to ss20, cv01 to cv99, the width and kana features, vrt2, cpsp; aalt and the Languagesystems prefix go first. Oswald and Markow store their features in this order; Update on F1 (Latin features) would settle it. |
| `font.features.update` | M8 | handbook | yes | salt is added beside ss01, as the handbook says, although none of the five corpus sources with an automatic ss01 has a salt feature; Update on a font with .ss01 glyphs would settle it. |
| `font.features.updateEntry` | M8 | handbook | yes | An entry whose glyphs are gone keeps its place with empty code rather than being removed; pressing the entry's update button after deleting its glyphs would settle it. |
| `font.features.setDisabled` | M8 | inferred | yes | A Disable and Enable item in the sidebar's context menu sets the flag; opening the context menu on a sidebar entry in the reference app would settle whether it is there. |
| `font.features.setDisabled` | M8 | inferred | yes | Disabling a class that an enabled feature uses is allowed and gives a compile error; disabling such a class in the reference app would settle whether it warns first. |
| `font.features.copyEntries` | M8 | inferred | yes | Entries travel as feature-file text with `# entry:` marker lines, so they paste into a text editor too; copying a feature in the reference app and pasting it into a text editor would settle the format it uses. |
| `font.features.pasteEntries` | M8 | inferred | yes | Pasted entries go to the end of their groups rather than after the selected entry; pasting into a sidebar with a selection in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.pasteEntries` | M8 | inferred | yes | A pasted feature whose tag the font has is added as a second entry with that tag rather than renamed or merged; pasting liga into a font with liga in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.features.compile` | M8 | handbook | yes | Compile reads the master the Features tab passes (the active Edit View tab's), else the first master; compiling in a two-master font whose masters differ in a number value used by the code, with the second master showing, would settle it. |
| `font.features.compile` | M8 | handbook | yes | Compile checks the code without the generated kerning and mark features; an error the reference app reports only when the generated code is added would show the difference. |
| `font.features.listErrors` | M8 | inferred | yes | The error list is the one of the last Compile and is marked out of date after an edit rather than recomputed; editing code after a compile in the reference app and watching whether the error markers move or vanish would settle it. |
| `font.features.applyFix` | M8 | handbook | yes | Four fixes of ours carry a wrench: a glyph name that matches an existing one apart from case, a class name that matches a defined one apart from case, a missing semicolon at the end of a statement (inserted before any comment), and a class used before its entry (moving the class entry up); the reference app's error list on a font with these mistakes would settle its own set. |
| `font.features.listSnippets` | M8 | inferred | yes | The built-in snippets come before the user's groups, and the groups follow file-name order; opening the snippets menu with two snippet files in the folder would settle the order. |
| `font.features.insertSnippet` | M8 | handbook | yes | A title without a group finds the built-in snippet first, then the first user snippet of that title in menu order; only the menu names snippets in the reference app, so nothing there settles it. |
| `font.features.setLabel` | M8 | handbook | yes | The language of a name is `dflt` or one of the OpenType language tags the name table can hold, and an empty name is refused; the language picker of the reference app would settle the list. |
| `font.features.complete` | M8 | inferred | yes | Completion offers glyphs in rules, classes after @, lookups after `lookup`, number values after $ and inside ${…}, registered feature tags after `feature` and statement keywords (then glyphs) at the start of a statement, exact-case matches first; typing in each of these places in the reference app would settle its lists. |
| `font.settings.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | keyboardIncrementShift is the format's keyboardIncrementBig and keyboardIncrementCmd its keyboardIncrementHuge, with 10 and 100 when the font sets none; saving after changing the two fields in the reference app settles the keys. |
| `font.settings.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Grid spacing is accepted from 0 to 10000, subdivision from 1 to 1000 and the keyboard increments above 0 up to 10000, and a value equal to the current one (stated or default) is not stored; typing the ends of each range in the reference app settles them. |
| `font.note.get` | M7 | inferred | yes | The note is returned as plain text, as stored; the Notes tab in the running app would settle whether formatting is kept. |
| `font.note.set` | M7 | inferred | yes | Setting the note replaces it whole in one undo step; editing the Notes tab in the running app would settle how undo groups typing. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Shift-click adds every row between the last row clicked and the clicked one; Shift-clicking in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Copy puts the rows' JSON on the system clipboard, and a selected row of a parameter whose name repeats copies every row of that name; pasting copied rows into a text editor from the reference app settles the format. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Paste replaces the rows with the same key in the shown font, masters or exports and appends repeatable parameters; pasting a row the target has in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.rows.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Cmd+Delete removes the selected fields in one undo step and each selected parameter in one of its own, and leaves built-in fields; undoing after Cmd+Delete in the reference app settles the steps. |
| `fontInfo.rows.localizable` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The plus button adds the first language the field lacks, in the name table's tag order after the default language, with empty text; picking another language in a row's menu moves its text there; clicking the plus in the reference app settles both. |
| `fontInfo.familyName.allowedCharacters` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Other characters are accepted with a warning, not refused; typing an ampersand or an accented letter in the reference app settles whether it warns, refuses or neither. |
| `fontInfo.unitsPerEm.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | font.info.set refuses values outside 16–16384 and returns a warning above 3000, above 4096 (glyph widths of CFF-flavoured fonts, given whatever the outline flavour) and above 5000; the reference app settles whether it warns at all. |
| `fontInfo.unitsPerEm.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The Font tab shows each units-per-em warning under the Units per Em field while the value stands, not only right after an edit; typing 6000 in the reference app and reopening Font Info settles it. |
| `fontInfo.version.headAndNameId5` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The suffix is `;FontCraft <version>`, without a build number while FontCraft has none; an export from the reference app shows only its own form. |
| `fontInfo.axes.predefinedNameSetsTag` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A predefined name is recognised without regard to case (`weight` sets wght); settle by typing a lowercase predefined name in the reference app's axis name field. |
| `fontInfo.customParameters.masterInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A parameter interpolates when its value is a number, or a list of numbers of the same length, on every master; other values are taken from the origin master. Settle with a parameter that is text on one master in the reference app. |
| `fontInfo.customParameters.masterInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | A parameter that is a whole number on every weighted master stays whole, rounded half up as fontmake rounds font info; the origin master's parameters are the ones the instance has, so one missing from the origin is left out. Settle with a parameter set only on the second master in the reference app. |
| `fontInfo.metrics.defaultsByScript` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A new font whose chosen scripts include Arabic and not Latin gets the metrics Ascender 800, Alef Height 700, Joining Line 150, Baseline 0, Descender −200 and Meem Depth −250 at 1000 UPM; a new Arabic font in the reference app settles names, order and values. |
| `fontInfo.metrics.defaultsByScript` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Alef Height, Joining Line and Meem Depth are stored as named metrics without a category, with zones of 16 units (−16 below the baseline, none on the Joining Line); the file a new Arabic font saves in the reference app settles their categories and zones. |
| `fontInfo.metrics.scopeHidesLine` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A hidden metric hides its alignment zone too, and in text mode the glyph being edited decides for the whole line of text; scoping x-height to Greek and typing A and alpha side by side in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.exports.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | With nothing selected the tab acts on the first entry, and Cmd-click on the only selected entry leaves the first entry shown; the reference app settles what an empty selection shows. |
| `fontInfo.exports.selection` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Dropping a dragged entry onto another moves it to that entry's place, and the moved entry becomes the selection; dragging in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.exports.inactiveGrayIcon` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Entries are drawn with a square icon in the accent colour, a variable font setting with an outlined square; inactive ones grey; the reference app's sidebar settles the icons. |
