| `fontInfo.styleName.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | font.instance.set and the Exports tab warn, without refusing, when the style name has characters outside ASCII letters, digits, spaces and hyphens, or when family and style name together exceed 20 characters without a postscriptFontName; the reference app settles whether it warns. |
| `fontInfo.styleName.limits` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | An empty or all-space style name is refused by font.instance.set and font.instance.add; clearing the Style Name field in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.weightClass.range` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The commands accept every value from 1 to 1000 and warn about any outside 100, 200, … 900, 1000 included; a class an older source writes as a name, or none, gets no warning. Typing 350 and 1000 as a weight class in the reference app settles whether it warns. |
| `fontInfo.instance.extrapolation` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Font Info › Exports shows the extrapolation warning, in the warning colour, under the Axes Coordinates of each selected instance beyond the masters, and the list entry is not marked; selecting an extrapolated instance in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.styleLinking.sameFamily` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | font.instance.setStyleLinking and font.instance.set link anyway but warn when the base instance named in the same file exports under another family name (its familyNames field); a base in another file is not checked. Linking two instances with different familyNames in the reference app settles whether it warns. |
| `fontInfo.features.contextMenu` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | The context menu's items and their order are ours; right-clicking a sidebar entry in the reference app would settle them. |
| `fontInfo.features.contextMenu` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A disabled entry is drawn struck through; disabling an entry in the reference app would settle how it is shown. |
| `fontInfo.features.sidebarFilter` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | The filter matches code as well as names; typing a glyph name into the reference app's sidebar search, if it has one, would settle it. |
| `fontInfo.features.inlineErrors` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Error ranges are underlined in the code and each error is listed under the code with its line and an icon, not drawn between the code lines; a screenshot of an error in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.features.notesDivider` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The notes field starts four rows tall and the divider keeps it between two rows and half the tab's height; dragging the divider in the reference app settles the limits. |
| `fontInfo.features.stylisticSetNames` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A name in a language without a Windows language id is left out, as fontc does (glyphsLib stops with an error), and text tokens in names are not expanded yet; a name in such a language exported from the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.features.stylisticSetNames` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Choosing another language for a name row removes the name and adds it in the new language, as two undo steps; changing a row's language in the reference app settles whether it is one step. |
| `fontInfo.fontType` (behavior) | M9 | handbook | – | Default and Variable both use the variable-font model (fontTools' VariationModel); they agree on one axis and on full grids, and the Multiple Master model of the reference is not documented for other setups. Settle by comparing an instance of an irregular two-axis setup under both settings in the reference app (docs/plans/M9.md § 8). |
| `fontInfo.notes.markdownHighlighting` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Headings of every level take the section heading size and the strong text colour, bold takes the strong text colour, italic slants, links take the accent colour, and the Markdown syntax itself is dimmed; the reference app's Notes tab settles the styles. |
| `fontInfo.notes.markdownHighlighting` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Underscore emphasis needs a word boundary on both sides and asterisk emphasis does not, emphasis and links stay within one line, and an autolink needs an http, https or mailto address; typing the edge cases in the reference app settles them. |
| `fontInfo.notes.markdownHighlighting` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A note longer than 256 KiB is shown without highlighting so typing stays fast; a long note in the reference app settles whether it stops highlighting. |
| `fontInfo.exports.instancePreview` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The preview sits above the instance's fields, one line high, and draws the first selected instance; an instance between masters is drawn interpolated, and a variable font setting shows none; the Exports tab of the reference app settles placement. |
| `fontInfo.exports.instancePreview` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The default `Aang126` is read as characters and drawn with the glyphs mapped to them, skipping characters the font lacks; the reference app settles whether it names glyphs instead. |
| `font.info.features.select` | M8 | inferred | no | Our own command for scripts and the window's clicks; nothing to observe, the reference app selects sidebar entries only by clicking them. |
| `font.info.exports.select` | M7 | inferred | no | Our own command for scripts and the window's clicks; nothing to observe, the reference app selects exports only by clicking them. |
| `fontInfo` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Font Info opens on the tab it last showed for that document, and on the Font tab the first time; opening it twice in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | One Font Info window shows the active document, and switching documents shows the other document's information in it; opening Font Info in two documents of the reference app settles whether each keeps a window of its own. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The double-arrow button opens a prompt under the field that asks for the new units per em, starting at the current value, with Scale and Cancel buttons; clicking the button in the reference app settles the prompt's form. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The minor version shows three digits, and its stepper is a plus and a minus button after the field; the reference app's Version field settles the look. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The Creation Date is shown and typed as the file stores it (`2026-10-11 12:00:00 +0000`), next to an Update button; the reference app's date field settles the display. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The Version row stands for both version parts, so selecting it selects and copies versionMajor and versionMinor together; copying the Version row in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Each axis row starts with a drag handle; dropping it on another axis row moves the axis there (font.axes.reorder). Dragging axes in the reference app, if it allows it, settles it. |
| `fontInfo.font` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The predefined menu lists the registered axes, a separator, then the proposed ones, each as `Name (tag)`; opening the menu in the reference app settles the look. |
| `fontInfo.fieldPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The picker lists only the fields not yet present on the selected font, masters or exports, in the order of font.info.listFields, and allows several to be selected; the picker of each tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.fieldPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A click selects one field and Cmd-click or Shift-click adds or removes one more; Add adds them in list order, each as an undo step of its own; using the picker in the reference app settles both. |
| `fontInfo.customParameterPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The list shows every catalogue parameter allowed in the tab's scope, in alphabetical order, including those already present; the search matches any part of the name, ignoring case; the picker in the reference app settles order and matching. |
| `fontInfo.customParameterPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A parameter whose appendix entry names no scope is offered in all three tabs; the picker of each tab in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.customParameterPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The search also matches the descriptions and the other spellings of a name, and Add gives each parameter its kind's empty value; searching for a word of a description in the reference app settles the first, adding Filter there the second. |
| `dialog.os2Bits` (panel) | M7 | inferred | – | fsType, codePageRanges and unicodeRanges each open a dialog of check boxes with a search field; opening each row's editor in the reference app settles it. |
| `dialog.os2Bits` (panel) | M7 | inferred | – | codePageRanges stores code page numbers, and a stored bit below 64 that a code page stands for shows as that page; the file the reference app writes after ticking a box settles it. |
| `dialog.panose` (panel) | M7 | inferred | – | The menus name the values only for family kind 2 (Latin Text); other family kinds show plain numbers. The dialog of the reference app with family kind 3 settles it. |
| `dialog.panose` (panel) | M7 | inferred | – | Variable Font Origin, Get Hints From Master and Link Metrics With Master are menus of the font's masters that store the master's id; the reference app's row and the file it writes settle it. |
| `dialog.panose` (panel) | M7 | inferred | – | A refused value's message stays under its row until the next edit; the reference app with vendorID ABCDE settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masters` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A master made with Add Master becomes the selection, and a dragged master stays selected at its new place; the list's selection in the reference app after each action settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masters` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The minus button is disabled while every master is selected, because a font keeps at least one master; whether the reference app disables it or refuses with a message settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masters` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The General section has the name and icon only, without a plus button, because masters take no added General fields; the masters tab of the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.addOtherFont` (panel) | M9 | handbook | – | The list includes the masters of the active font as well as the other open fonts, as the handbook's wording says; opening the sheet in the reference app would show whether the active font is left out. |
| `fontInfo.masterIconPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A click on a built-in icon applies it to the selected masters and closes the picker; the glyph field applies on Use Glyph or Enter; how the reference app's popover closes settles it. |
| `fontInfo.masterIconPicker` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The 25 built-in icons are five weights (Light, Regular, Medium, SemiBold, Bold) by five widths (Condensed, SemiCondensed, normal, SemiExtended, Extended), named `Weight` or `Weight_Width` as the corpus files spell them; the names the reference app writes for each icon of its picker settle the set. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The category menu offers the file format's metric types (ascender, cap height, slant height, x-height, midHeight, bodyHeight, descender, baseline, italic angle) with readable labels, plus an empty category for a metric known only by its name; the menu of the reference app settles the list and the labels. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The scope is typed as a glyph predicate in a text field rather than built with the smart-filter rule editor; the scope editor of the reference app settles whether it shows rules. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The plus beside the Metrics heading opens this editor to add a metric, starting at the first category the font has no plain metric of; what the reference app's plus adds settles it. |
| `fontInfo.metricEditor` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A stem's actions button opens the same editor with only the scope field; the reference app's stem scope popover settles its fields. |
| `fontInfo.exports` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Active, Style Name, Weight Class, Width Class, the Axes Coordinates and Style Linking sit under the General heading, above the added fields; the Exports tab of the reference app settles the order. |
| `fontInfo.exports` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A variable font setting shows Active, its name and its custom parameters, without classes, axes or style linking; selecting a variable font setting in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.exports` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The class menus list each standard value with its OpenType name (400 Regular, 5 Medium (normal)); the disclosure menus of the reference app settle the labels. |
| `fontInfo.features` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | With no entry selected, the tab selects the first entry of the sidebar; the reference app's tab opened on a font with features settles it. |
| `fontInfo.features` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | The plus button beside Classes adds a class named NewClass (NewClass1, NewClass2… when taken), renamed by double-clicking it; adding a class in the reference app settles the name and whether it asks for one. |
| `fontInfo.features` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | Entries are renamed by double-clicking them and reordered by dragging them within their group; trying both in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.featurePicker` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | A feature added from the picker is generated automatically when Update could write its code for the font, else it starts empty and manual; adding smcp to a font with and without .sc glyphs in the reference app settles it. |
| `fontInfo.other` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Each setting is written when its field loses the keyboard or its box or menu changes, as one undo step per change; changing a setting and choosing Undo in the reference app settles whether settings are undoable. |
| `fontInfo.notes` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The note is written when the text box loses the keyboard, as one undo step for everything typed meanwhile; typing in the Notes tab and choosing Undo in the reference app settles the undo steps. |
| `fontInfo.addMetric` (panel) | M7 | inferred | – | Adding a metric opens the same editor as editing one, starting on the first category the font lacks; adding a metric in the reference app would show whether it adds a row at once instead. |
| `instance.manualInterpolation` (parameter) | M9 | docs | – | With manual interpolation on, the instance is the stored factors applied to the master layers; intermediate layers get no weight, alternate layers still switch by the instance's location, and the factors also weight metrics, kerning and parameters; settle with an instance using manual factors and a brace glyph in the reference app. |
| `instance.instanceInterpolations` (parameter) | M9 | docs | – | The model keeps one number per master; an x/y pair keeps its first number, so horizontal and vertical factors are the same; settle with an instance whose factors are pairs in the reference app. |

## `07-import-export.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `file.exportAll` | M2 | handbook | yes | The reference's settings for Export All are not documented; using each document's last-used export settings is our assumption, and until FontCraft remembers settings the command takes them as parameters. |
| `file.exportVariable` | M9 | handbook | yes | A glyph that has no layer for the origin master does not vary and keeps the origin's empty outline, with a warning; exporting such a font from the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.exportVariable` | M9 | handbook | yes | An intermediate layer that lies outside the axes of a variable font setting (one that disables masters) is left out of that font, with a note; exporting such a setting from the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.exportMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | The `metrics` format is our kerning-text format (`kerning-text` in 30-formats.toml), because the reference does not document its own metrics file; files in the reference format are neither read nor written. |
| `file.exportMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A metrics file holds the pairs of one direction (left to right by default, `direction` for the others), because kerning-text has one direction line; an export of a master with right-to-left or vertical pairs needs one file per direction. A reference metrics file with such pairs would settle it. |
| `file.exportMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | The metrics file's extension is .tsv; the reference's own extension is not documented. |
| `file.open` | M1 | handbook | yes | The menu label and place are our assumption, and the shortcut is the platform convention; the chapter states neither. |
| `file.open` | M1 | handbook | yes | File › Open… without a path asks the system chooser for files with one filter, Fonts, that holds every extension file.open reads (gz standing for .pcf.gz and .bdf.gz); the reference app's chooser filter is not documented. |
| `file.open` | M1 | handbook | yes | Several files chosen at once each open, the last one shown; when some fail, the status bar lists the failures. |
| `file.open` | M1 | handbook | yes | A .fontcraftproject file given to file.open opens as a project, as project.open does, since the reference app's File › Open opens project files. |
| `file.open` | M1 | handbook | yes | A font file dropped on the window opens as with File › Open…, recognised by its extension; an SVG file is left to the image drop, which places it on a glyph, so a dropped SVG font does not open. |
| `file.openFolder` | M1 | inferred | yes | The item has no shortcut, since the reference app has no such item to copy one from. |
| `file.importOutlines` | M3 | handbook | yes | An SVG reads as one font unit per SVG unit with SVG y = 0 at the ascender; the handbook does not say how a drawing is scaled. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | The import goes to the master given, else the first master, and `sourceMaster` defaults to the first master of the source file. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Importing kerning replaces the master's pairs in each imported direction, so export then import gives back the same table; the handbook does not say whether it merges. Importing into a master with pairs in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Import Kerning and Import Kerning Classes default to on (Import Kerning off for a Glyphs file, which cannot give kerning values), and Import Metrics to off; the handbook states no defaults. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A metrics file also sets the left, right and width keys as it gives them, an empty key field removing the key the field shows; an AFM leaves the keys as they are. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Width, Outline Centred rounds the new LSB half up, so the outline stays on whole units; the handbook does not say. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Selected Glyphs limits glyph metrics and kerning classes; kerning values are imported whole. |
| `file.importMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Selected Glyphs means the glyphs selected in Font View, also while an Edit View tab shows; the handbook does not say which selection. Ticking it with a tab open in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | A class or prefix whose name the font already has gets the first free numeric suffix from 2 (`Upper2`), and the imported code's references to a renamed class follow it; importing into a font with clashing names in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | A feature whose tag the font already has is added as a second entry with that tag, because blocks of one tag add up in the feature-file language and a suffixed tag is not a tag; the same check would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | Each run of top-level statements other than class definitions and feature blocks becomes a prefix named Prefix, and a `# Prefix: name` comment names the prefix after it; importing a file with lookups and language systems in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importFeatures` | M8 | handbook | yes | Statements the split cannot place (a `useExtension` feature, an `anon` block, a class whose value is not a list) stay in a prefix as written rather than failing the import. |
| `file.exportFeatures` | M8 | inferred | yes | The reference app documents no feature-file export; the command exists for the command line and automation. |
| `file.exportFeatures` | M8 | inferred | yes | The fea export assembles the stored entries without running Update first, so automatic entries are written as they were last updated; running Update before the export gives the regenerated code. |
| `file.exportFeatures` | M8 | inferred | yes | The fea-kern export names group classes as the reference's feature code does (`@MMK_L_` for a right-side group, `@MMK_R_` for a left-side group) and leaves out pairs whose glyphs are missing or whose names feature code cannot spell, counting them in the warnings. |
| `file.newProject` | M7 | handbook | yes | A new project is saved as a native FontCraft project file (`.fontcraftproject`, JSON: the linked font's path relative to the project, the instances in the native format, the export destination); reading and writing `.glyphsproject` waits for a sample file from the reference app. |
| `file.newProject` | M7 | handbook | yes | Project edits are not undo steps: Edit › Undo acts on font documents only; undoing in a project window of the reference app settles it. |
| `file.newProject` | M7 | handbook | yes | An unsaved project is called Untitled Project (then Untitled Project 2, …); the reference app's window title settles it. |
| `project.linkFont` | M7 | handbook | yes | The link is stored relative to the project file when both share a folder tree, else absolute; linking copies the font's instances into the project only when the project has none; saving a project in the reference app settles the path form. |
| `project.linkFont` | M7 | handbook | yes | A link that no longer resolves is kept when the project opens and reported as a warning; only export and revert refuse it; opening a project whose font was moved in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.addInstance` | M7 | handbook | yes | A new project instance has the defaults of font.instance.add; a variable font setting waits for M9 and is refused until then. |
| `project.addInstance` | M7 | handbook | yes | `at` places the new instance in the list (default: the end); the plus button in the reference app adds at the end or after the selection, which observing it settles. |
| `project.revertInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | Reverting reads the font file on disk, not an open copy with unsaved changes; reverting with the font open and edited in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.moveInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | Several moved instances keep their order and land together, the first at `to` of the list without them; dragging several rows in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.duplicateInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | A duplicate keeps the original's name and goes right after it unless `to` is given. |
| `project.duplicateInstances` | M7 | handbook | yes | With `to`, the copies go together at that position; a copy gets no id of its own. |
| `project.editInstance` | M7 | handbook | yes | Edits are checked as Font Info › Exports checks an export of the linked font (its axes and masters), or of a new empty font while no font is linked. |
| `project.setExportDestination` | M7 | handbook | yes | The destination is stored relative to the project file like the font link, need not exist, and is created by export; saving a project in the reference app settles the stored form. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export refuses a project with unsaved changes as well as one never saved; exporting in the reference app with an unsaved change settles it. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export reads the font file on disk even when the font is open with unsaved changes; exporting with the font open and edited settles it. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export builds TrueType outlines in plain files unless asked otherwise, and a project without an active static instance is refused rather than exporting the font's first master; the reference app's export from a project settles both. |
| `project.export` | M7 | handbook | yes | Export is refused, writing nothing, when one of its files would replace the linked font (a compiled font in the destination folder); exporting such a project in the reference app settles it. |
| `project.open` | M7 | inferred | yes | A project already open is made active rather than opened twice, as for fonts; a font link that no longer resolves is kept and reported; opening one in the reference app settles both. |
| `project.save` | M7 | handbook | yes | Saving writes only the project file, atomically, and refuses a path that is the linked font; File › Save in a project window runs this command. |
| `project.close` | M7 | inferred | yes | Closing a project with unsaved changes refuses unless `discard` is true, as closing a font does; closing a changed project window in the reference app would settle what it asks. |
| `project.list` | M7 | inferred | yes | A query of ours listing each open project's font link, destination, instances and saved state; the reference app shows the same facts only in each project window. |
| `export.fileNaming` (behavior) | M2 | memory | – | The PostScript-name stem is recalled from the reference, not read in a source; dropping characters a file name cannot hold and falling back to the source file's name are ours. |
| `export.fileNaming` (behavior) | M2 | memory | – | The fileName parameter keeps its text tokens as written, as fontmake keeps them; an export of an instance whose fileName holds {{{name}}} in the reference app would settle it. |
| `export.flavourSubfolders` (behavior) | M2 | inferred | – | An export of both flavours at once puts them into ttf and otf subfolders; how the reference lays out such an export is not documented. |
| `export.autohintCff` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Zones or stems the autohinter cannot use (overlapping zones, fewer than two BlueValues zones, a direction without stems) make the export autohint without them, with a warning, where otfautohint refuses the font; a reference export of a font with overlapping zones would show what it does. |
| `export.instanceExportFolder` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The sub-path is used as written, `..` segments included, as the handbook says; an absolute path, an empty segment, a folder name with a character such as `:` or `\`, or a path of more than 32 segments makes export skip that instance with the reason, one trailing `/` is ignored, and the warnings name every file written outside the export destination; an Export Folder of `../x` and one of `/tmp/x` in the reference app settle it. |
| `export.instanceExportFolder` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Exports from the command line, MCP and the control channel skip an instance whose Export Folder resolves outside the destination folder; the reference app has no such callers, so nothing there settles it. |
| `export.ufoProductionNames` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | A glyph whose name feature code uses is written under its own name, because the feature code is written unchanged; exporting a UFO with features from the reference app with the option on would settle it. |
| `export.ufoProductionNames` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | The option renames glyphs even when the font's Use Custom Naming option or the Don't use Production Names parameter is on, since the user asked for it. |
| `export.afmIsLossy` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Group pairs are expanded to glyph pairs with the exceptions applied, so the AFM file holds the kerning that applies to every covered glyph pair; the handbook does not say. |
| `export.afmIsLossy` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | AFM values stay in the font's units and are not scaled to 1,000 units per em, so a round trip loses nothing; Adobe's note assumes 1,000. An AFM exported by the reference from a 2,048-unit font would settle it. |
| `source.ufoUnsupportedFeatureWarning` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | The warning does not block the command: the edit is made and the reply carries the warning; the handbook says only that turning such a feature on raises a warning. Settled by adding a corner component to a UFO in the reference. |
| `source.ufoUnsupportedFeatureWarning` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | The warnings are worked out by comparing the save loss report before and after each edit, plus a count of corner components, caps, brushes, segments and smart components, since the handbook lists no features; settled by trying each feature on a UFO in the reference and noting which warn. |
| `open.appliesNamingScheme` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | Only names the glyph data knows are renamed (production, alternative, legacy and code-point names, with their suffixes); names it does not know stay as they are. Opening a TTF with such names in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.appliesNamingScheme` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | A glyph whose name occurs in feature code, a custom parameter, a hint or a display string keeps it, with a warning, because those texts are not rewritten and the font would stop compiling. Opening a UFO whose features.fea names a uni-named glyph would settle it. |
| `open.appliesNamingScheme` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | A nice name that is already a glyph's name, or that an earlier glyph takes, is not used, and the glyph keeps its name without a warning. |
| `open.appliesNamingScheme` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | Renamed glyphs keep their places in the glyph order; they are not sorted by the default order. |
| `open.appliesNamingScheme` (behavior) | M1 | handbook | – | The user setting Keep Glyph Names from Imported Files does not switch on the font's Use Custom Naming option, so names typed later still become nice names. |
| `open.alignsCompositeMetrics` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A UFO counts as an imported file and is aligned on opening, because the Source Formats page points a UFO to the importing behaviours and gives it its own Disable Automatic Alignment option in Font Info; the running app opening a UFO with an out-of-date composite would settle it. |
| `open.alignsCompositeMetrics` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Aligning on opening is not an undo step and does not mark the document as edited; the reference app's edited mark after opening a UFO with an out-of-date composite settles it. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Lookups that must come before a shared lookup to keep the lookup order go into Prefix as well; opening a font with a shared lookup in the middle of its lookup list in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The languagesystem statements go into a prefix named Languagesystems; the handbook names only the Prefix entry. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A lookup only one feature uses is written inside that feature, where the feature first uses it; the handbook says only where shared lookups go. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | When the document's kerning cannot give back every kern pair lookup (other values, device tables, other lookup flags, kerning read in part), the kerning is cleared and the kern feature stays code, so export does not kern twice; opening such a font in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A font whose glyph names feature syntax cannot hold opens with every reconstructed entry turned off and a warning; no source says what the reference does. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A font whose layout rules use glyph ids past its glyph count opens with every reconstructed entry turned off, a warning, and the kerning read kept; no source says what the reference does. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | When the reconstructed entries open turned off, the kerning read stays and the kern entry leaves out the pair lookups it was read from, even ones whose values the kerning cannot hold (those values are lost, with a warning), because the handbook treats kerning as generated beside any kern code; opening such a font in the reference app and turning the kern feature on would settle it. |
| `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A stylistic set's name comes back as the default-language label, read from the name table's English record. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A class read from a compiled font becomes a kerning group named after its first glyph in glyph order; no source says how the reference names recovered groups. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph keeps the first class it is in on each side, and pairs of a later class with other members are stored as glyph pairs; opening a font whose subtables use overlapping classes in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A `kern` feature record used only by right-to-left scripts fills the right-to-left kerning, any other the left-to-right kerning; opening an Arabic font in the reference app would settle it. |
| `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A pair two feature records of one direction cover (for two scripts) takes the first record's value; no source says what the reference does. |
| `open.postscriptHintsPreserved` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Imported stem hints are numbers, none linked to nodes, and a stem repeated by hint replacement is kept once; opening a CFF font with hint replacement in the reference app would show whether it keeps repeats or links edges to nodes. |
| `import.outlinesSingleInEditView` (behavior) | M3 | handbook | – | A single file imported in Edit View is appended to the active layer; the handbook does not say whether existing shapes are kept or which layer receives them. |
| `import.metricsModes` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The default mode is LSB and width (the RSB follows); the handbook gives no default. |
| `import.metricsBreaksAutoAlignment` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Imported values that round to the derived ones (less than half a unit away) leave alignment on; importing a metrics file whose values differ by fractions in the reference app settles it. |
| `import.metricsBreaksAutoAlignment` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Alignment is switched off only on the layer of the master being imported, for every component of that layer; the other masters' layers stay aligned; importing into one master of a two-master font in the reference app settles it. |
| `paste.strokeAndFillMayDouble` (behavior) | M3 | inferred | – | A pasted shape with both fill and stroke may arrive as two contours; this is our reading of the handbook's advice, which gives no cause. |
| `window.project.select` | M7 | inferred | no | One Project panel shows one open project at a time, with a choice between them when several are open, instead of one window per project; opening two projects in the reference app shows its window arrangement, and the dock has no free-floating windows. |
| `window.project.select` | M7 | inferred | no | The Duplicate item sits in the list's plus menu rather than a context menu on the entry; right-clicking a project instance in the reference app settles where it lives. |
| `dialog.exportMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The format, file name and folder sit on the tab, with the folder chooser for the folder, because the dialog framework has no save-file chooser; the handbook puts them in a save dialog after Next…. A save-file chooser in the shell would settle it. |
| `dialog.exportMetrics` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The tab shows a Master pop-up that starts on the toolbar's master, so a master can be exported without changing the toolbar; the handbook names no such control. |
| `dialog.importOutlinesMissingGlyph` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | One question covers every unmatched file, with the buttons Add Glyphs and Skip; the handbook asks once per file, with Add Glyph and OK (skip). |
| `dialog.importMetricsFile` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The two dialogs are two tabs of one window opened from the menu, with the file typed as a path, because the dialog framework has no file chooser field; the handbook asks for the file first and then shows the dialog that fits it. A file field with a chooser would settle it. |
| `dialog.importMetricsFont` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The tab can import from another open document as well as from a file; the handbook names files only. |
| `dialog.importMetricsFont` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The tab offers Selected Glyphs and a Source Master field; the handbook does not say whether this dialog has them. |
| `dialog.importFeatures` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | The file is typed as a path, as in the other import dialogs, until the shell has a file chooser limited to .fea files. |
| `dialog.pastedOutlineBounds` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The correction only translates: it moves the pasted box's bottom left to the origin. |
| `dialog.pastedOutlineBounds` (panel) | M3 | handbook | – | The buttons read Move and Keep Position, with the line "The pasted outline lies outside the glyph box." above the fields. |
| `window.project` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | A Save button beside Export saves the project, and a project never saved asks the save chooser for its file, offering a name after its title; the handbook only names File › Save. Saving a project in the reference app settles where saving lives. |
| `window.project` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Closing the window closes the project, and a project with unsaved changes stays open with the reason in the status bar; the reference app's close prompt is not documented. Closing an edited project in the reference app settles it. |
| `window.project` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | The entry's context menu offers Duplicate beside copy and paste; checking the reference app's context menu on a project instance settles it. |
| `window.project` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | Paste puts the copies together after the last selected instance (at the end with none selected), and pastes only in the project the instances were copied from, leaving out a copied instance that changed since; pasting in the reference app after changing the selection, and in a second project, settles it. |
| `window.project` (panel) | M7 | handbook | – | General fields an instance carries are shown read-only, since the project commands edit only the built-in fields and the custom parameters; editing a localized name in a project in the reference app settles whether they are editable there. |
| `dialog.saveChangedOnDisk` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | The reference app asks before overwriting a file changed on disk, with Overwrite and Cancel; saving over a file another program changed in the reference app settles it. |

## `08-spacing-kerning.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A numeric left sidebearing moves the outline, components, anchors, guides, annotations, placed image and background sideways and changes the width by the same amount, so the right sidebearing stays. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key is evaluated and applied as soon as it is stored. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A glyph-level key is applied to the edited layer only; the glyph's other layers keep their numbers, out of sync, until Update Metrics runs. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key that cannot be evaluated (a missing glyph, division by zero, an empty referenced glyph, a result out of range) is refused and nothing is stored. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key typed with one `=` into a field that shows a layer key removes that layer key, so the new glyph-level key applies on that layer. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | The sidebearing setters skip a layer without an outline and report it, since such a layer has no sidebearings. |
| `spacing.setLeftSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | With `beam` (the metrics beam, M13.20) a number is measured where the outline meets the horizontal line at that height, a layer the line misses is skipped, and a metrics key is refused because keys measure at the bounds; the secondary references' pages do not say how a key behaves at the beam. |
| `spacing.setRightSidebearing` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key is evaluated and applied as soon as it is stored. |
| `spacing.setWidth` | M5 | handbook | yes | A numeric width change is absorbed by the right sidebearing; the handbook does not say how it is split. |
| `spacing.setWidth` | M5 | handbook | yes | A width key may be a glyph name, a constant or a calculation; `\|` and `@` are refused in the width field. |
| `spacing.setTopSidebearing` | M5 | inferred | yes | Setting the top sidebearing moves the vertical origin and changes the vertical width by the same amount, so the bottom sidebearing stays, as the left sidebearing does horizontally. |
| `spacing.setTopSidebearing` | M5 | inferred | yes | The top and bottom fields take the same key forms as the left and right ones, with `\|` naming the opposite vertical sidebearing; `@` is refused there. |
| `spacing.setBottomSidebearing` | M5 | inferred | yes | Setting the bottom sidebearing changes the vertical width only. |
| `spacing.setVerticalOrigin` | M5 | inferred | yes | Setting the vertical origin keeps the vertical width, so the whole vertical box moves; a glyph with a vertical origin typed into the reference Info box settles it. |
| `spacing.setVerticalOrigin` | M5 | inferred | yes | The vertical origin field takes the width's key forms (a glyph name, a constant, a calculation, `==` for the layer); `\|` and `@` are refused. |
| `spacing.setVerticalOrigin` | M5 | inferred | yes | A layer with no stored vertical origin reads 0. |
| `spacing.setVerticalWidth` | M5 | inferred | yes | A numeric vertical width change is absorbed by the bottom sidebearing, as the width's is by the right one. |
| `spacing.setVerticalWidth` | M5 | inferred | yes | A vertical width key may be a glyph name, a constant or a calculation; `\|` and `@` are refused, as in the width field. |
| `spacing.getSharedMetrics` | M5 | inferred | yes | A metric field whose glyphs share a key shows the key even when their measured values differ; several glyphs with one key, one of them out of sync, in the reference app settle it. |
| `spacing.updateMetricsKey` | M5 | handbook | yes | A metric whose value rounded half up already equals its key's value is left as it is, so the command makes no change there; watching the button on a fractional metric in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.updateMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | Keys are evaluated in dependency order (the metrics a key reads, and the left sidebearings of the glyphs a composite's components show, come first), so a glyph whose reference or base was updated in the same run gets the new value; one Update Metrics on a two-step chain and on a composite of a keyed base in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.updateMetrics` | M5 | handbook | yes | A key that cannot be evaluated (unlinked, in error, or on an empty layer) is skipped and the other keys are still updated; running it on a font with one unlinked key in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.updateMetricsForAllMasters` | M5 | handbook | yes | Besides the master layers it updates the layers with attributes (intermediate, alternate, colour, smart), the layers the out-of-sync scan lists, and leaves out masters that take their metrics from another master; a brace layer with an out-of-sync key in the reference app would settle it. |
| `spacing.listOutOfSyncMetrics` | M5 | inferred | yes | A key is out of sync when its value differs from the layer's metric rounded half up. |
| `spacing.listOutOfSyncMetrics` | M5 | inferred | yes | Master layers and layers with attributes (intermediate, alternate, colour, smart) are scanned; backup layers and the layers of a master that takes its metrics from another master are not. |
| `kerning.setMode` | M5 | handbook | yes | A new Edit View tab starts in kerning mode; no source gives the default. |
| `kerning.setPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | A group side must be the group some glyph has on the side that faces the pair, and a glyph side a glyph of the font, so a typo is refused rather than stored as a pair that never applies; storing a pair for an unknown group in the running app's Kerning window settles it. |
| `kerning.setPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | Values beyond ±32,767 are refused, the range a compiled pair value holds; typing a larger value in a kern field settles what the reference does. |
| `kerning.removePair` | M5 | handbook | yes | Removing a pair the master does not store is refused with a message rather than ignored; clearing an empty kern field in the running app shows nothing either way, so only the scripting API's behaviour would settle it. |
| `kerning.increaseLeft` | M5 | handbook | yes | Increase adds to the kerning value (more space) and decrease subtracts; the handbook names the directions without a sign, and the reference kerning tutorial says negative values tighten a pair. The Info box kern value after one keypress, in the running app or a video, settles it. |
| `kerning.setLeftGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | An empty name clears the group, as null does; emptying a Group field in the Info box of the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.setTopGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | The top and bottom group fields check names as the left and right ones do. |
| `view.showGroupMembers` | M5 | handbook | yes | Members are drawn as thin coloured outlines behind the glyphs, in text mode and while editing outlines alike; a screenshot of the option in the reference app settles the look. |
| `view.showGroupMembers` | M5 | handbook | yes | At most 200 members are drawn for each of the two glyphs, the first in glyph order; a very large group in the reference app settles whether it caps them. |
| `window.kerning` | M5 | handbook | yes | While Font View shows, the window keeps the writing direction of the last Edit View tab shown and Font View's master; opening the window from Font View in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.resetFilter` | M5 | handbook | yes | Reset leaves the search term in the field and restores only the options; choosing Reset with a term typed in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.showPairInEditView` | M5 | handbook | yes | The pair at the cursor is the current glyph and the glyph before it; with no glyph before the current one the pair is inserted at the cursor, and the cursor ends between the two glyphs. Selecting rows in the reference with the cursor at the start of a text settles it. |
| `kerning.selectNextPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | With no row selected Down selects the first row, and at the last row the selection stays; pressing Down past the end of the list in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.selectPreviousPair` | M5 | handbook | yes | With no row selected Up selects the last row, and at the first row the selection stays; pressing Up at the top of the list in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.showAllGlyphs` | M5 | handbook | yes | The pairs replace the pair at the cursor, one line per left glyph with its pairs separated by the space glyph (none when the font has no space); running Show All Glyphs on a group pair in the reference settles the layout. |
| `kerning.renameGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | With scope `pair`, only the side of the selected pair changes to the new name and the glyphs keep their group; no source says whether the reference also moves glyphs into the new group, and the Group fields after a pair-only rename settle it. |
| `kerning.renameGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | With scope `font`, a new name that is already a group of that side is refused rather than merging the two groups; renaming onto an existing group in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.renameGroup` | M5 | handbook | yes | With scope `font`, the rename reaches every master and both writing directions, since groups are shared between them; reading another master's pairs after a rename in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.copyPairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | The clipboard holds the pairs as kerning-text lines (`left<TAB>right<TAB>value`), so they also paste into a text editor; no source gives the reference clipboard format. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A pasted group side keeps its name; a group the target font lacks is pasted as it is and reported. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A glyph the target font lacks is pasted as it is and reported, like a missing group. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A pasted pair that exists with the same value is not a conflict, so it does not bring up the question; pasting an unchanged selection back into its master settles it. |
| `kerning.pastePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | When the pasted pairs name one pair twice, the later value counts. |
| `kerning.deletePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A pair the master lacks (with all masters: that no master has) refuses the whole command; the reference can only delete listed rows, so the question does not arise there. |
| `kerning.deletePairs` | M5 | handbook | yes | A linked master is refused, as for the other kerning edits. |
| `kerning.cleanUp` | M5 | handbook | yes | Clean Up acts on the given master and direction only, like the Kerning window list; running it on a font with two masters and reading the other master's pairs settles it. |
| `kerning.compress` | M5 | handbook | yes | Compress acts on the given master and direction only; running it on a font with two masters and reading the other master's pairs settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Under the descender, the left sidebearing sits at the glyph's left edge, the right one at its right edge and the width centred below them; in vertical text they run along the column's left edge; a screenshot of the reference app settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A label keeps to the part of the advance a negatively kerned neighbour does not cover, so neighbouring labels do not overlap; a tightly kerned pair in the reference app settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Glyphs less than 24 screen points wide get no labels; the reference app zoomed out settles it. |
| `spacing.metricsOverlayInTextMode` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph whose advance on screen is too narrow for its two sidebearing labels side by side shows only its width label; the labels of a narrow glyph such as `i` at a small zoom in the reference app settle it. |
| `spacing.infoBoxArrowSteps` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Up and Down in a field that holds a metrics key leave the key unchanged; no source says what they do there, and pressing Up in such a field in the reference app settles it. |
| `spacing.shortcutsNeedTextTool` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A spacing shortcut on a metric that holds a metrics key changes the number and keeps the key, which then shows as out of sync; no source says whether the reference keeps the key, removes it or ignores the keypress. |
| `spacing.shortcutModifierScheme` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In right-to-left text the shortcuts address the visual left and right sidebearings, as in left-to-right text. No source says. |
| `spacing.shortcutModifierScheme` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In top-to-bottom text the spacing shortcuts act on the top and bottom sidebearings: Ctrl on the top one, Cmd on the bottom one, both together shift the outline vertically. No source says; trying the shortcuts in top-to-bottom text settles it. |
| `metricsKey.notSyncedAutomatically` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A key whose chain of references returns to its own glyph, or runs deeper than 32 steps, is an error: the metric keeps its number and the key is reported. A forum answer by the reference developers shows the reference does not stop such cycles (thread "Possible bug with spacing formula"). Watching the reference app with such a key settles what it shows. |
| `metricsKey.notSyncedAutomatically` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Division by zero, and a result outside 0 to 65,535 units for a width or −32,768 to 32,767 for a sidebearing, are errors reported the same way; watching the reference app with such a key settles what it shows. |
| `metricsKey.calculationSyntax` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Spaces around the parts of a key are ignored. |
| `metricsKey.calculationSyntax` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A key holds at most one operation; a second operator or a parenthesis makes it invalid, and it is reported, not evaluated. |
| `metricsKey.calculationSyntax` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | `@` and its height come last, after the operation (`=H+8@200`). |
| `metricsKey.decimalOperands` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The glyph name is the longest leading part of the key that names a glyph in the font; what follows must be nothing, an operation, or `@` and a number. So `=ka-deva/1.4` reads ka-deva even when a glyph `ka` exists. |
| `metricsKey.decimalOperands` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | When no leading part names a glyph, the key is unlinked and names the shortest leading part after which the rest is valid: `=xa-deva-5` names `xa-deva`. |
| `metricsKey.resultRounded` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Exact halves round up (toward positive infinity), as both oracles round kerning values: fontc 1.0.0 and fontmake 3.12.1 write −10.5 as −10 and 10.5 as 11. |
| `metricsKey.constant` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | On a glyph that is not aligned automatically, `=-30` and `=+30` are constants; reports on the reference forum treat `=-30` as a key that should work (thread "Build 1015 metrics keys"). On a glyph aligned automatically the form is the offset of `metricsKey.autoAlignedOffset`, evaluated from M6. |
| `metricsKey.glyphLevelByDefault` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A layer that is not a master layer (intermediate, alternate, backup) reads the referenced glyph's layer with the same layer id, else the layer of its own master; no source says. |
| `metricsKey.localPrefix` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | On its layer a local key wins over the glyph-level key of the same field. |
| `metricsKey.localPrefix` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A plain number typed into a field removes the key the field showed: a local key from the layer, a glyph-level key from the glyph. |
| `metricsKey.baselineOffset` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | When the line at the given height does not cross the outline, the key uses the bounding-box sidebearing; no source says. |
| `metricsKey.baselineOffset` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In a master with an italic angle, the crossing at the given height is moved along the italic angle to half the x-height, where the bounding-box sidebearings are measured; no source says. |
| `metricsKey.autoAlignedDisplay` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | A sidebearing that holds an offset key shows the key followed by the derived value in parentheses (`=+18 (68)`) instead of the word auto; an aligned glyph with an offset in the reference app settles it. |
| `metricsKey.autoAlignedDisplay` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Emptying an aligned field that holds an offset removes the offset; the reference app with an offset deleted from the field settles it. |
| `metricsKey.autoAlignedOffset` (behavior) | M6 | handbook | – | Offsets apply to the left and right sidebearing keys only: a left offset moves every component right and widens the glyph, a right offset widens it; an offset typed into the width field of an aligned glyph is refused, and one stored there by a file is ignored; entering =+N in the width field of an aligned glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.highlightColors` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The tint fills the gap between the two advances, from the descender to the ascender, behind the glyphs; a screenshot of the reference app in text mode settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A typed 0 is stored as a pair with value 0, which overrides a group value; both oracles keep such a pair (a 0 exception against a −60 group pair compiles to 0). Typing 0 in the reference app and reading the Kerning window settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A typed value with a fraction is rounded to a whole unit; values read from files keep their fractions. Typing a fraction in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Emptying the field removes the stored pair that applies, which can be a group pair that other glyph pairs share; emptying a kern field kerned through groups in the running app and reading the Kerning window settles it. |
| `kerning.infoBoxFieldEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Up and Down in an empty field step from zero and create the pair; pressing Up in a field showing the placeholder in the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.shortcutsNeedTextTool` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A keypress changes the kerning that applies by the step and stores the result on the sides the locks choose, creating that pair when it does not exist; no source says, and the Kerning window after the first keypress on an unkerned pair settles it. |
| `kerning.shortcutModifierScheme` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | In right-to-left text Ctrl picks the pair with the glyph that is visually on the left and Cmd the pair with the glyph visually on the right; the handbook does not mention right-to-left text here, and watching the shortcuts on right-to-left text settles it. |
| `kerning.verticalGroups` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | M5 stores and edits vertical pairs and shows them in top-to-bottom text; export writes none, as neither oracle does. |
| `kerning.verticalGroups` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A vertical group side is written `@MMK_T_` with the upper glyph's bottom group and `@MMK_B_` with the lower glyph's top group, by analogy with the left-to-right prefixes; a vertical group pair saved by the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.groupMembersDisplayScope` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Each member is placed so its facing side lines up with the glyph it stands in for (left sides at the glyph's origin, right sides at the end of its advance); vertical members share the glyph's origin. Comparing members of different widths in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.groupMembersDisplayScope` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The current glyph at the start of a line has no predecessor, so only its own group shows; the reference app with the cursor at a line start settles it. |
| `kerning.lockStates` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A lock is open when the pair that applies names that glyph itself on that side while the glyph has a group there; opening a lock stores such a pair at the value that applied, so the kerning does not change. The kern field and the Kerning window right after a lock is opened, in the running app or a video, settle it. |
| `kerning.closingLockRemovesException` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Closing a lock deletes the exception pair in the current master only; the all-masters variant exists only for deletion in the Kerning window. Closing a lock in a font with two masters and reading the other master's pairs settles it. |
| `kerning.closingLockRemovesException` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Closing one lock while both are open keeps the other lock open: the glyph–glyph pair is replaced by the closed side's group against the open side's glyph, at the value that applied, unless that pair is already stored. Closing one lock of such a pair in the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.closingLockRemovesException` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Closing the left lock alone of a glyph–glyph pair is refused when the left glyph's pair with the right glyph's group exists, because that pair would apply before the group–glyph pair and the left lock would stay open. The owner chose the refusal over deleting that exception. Closing the left lock in that situation in the running app settles it. |
| `kerning.pairLookupOrder` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | When two stored pairs name the same sides in different spellings (the reference's group prefix and the UFO's), the first in stored order applies; a source with both, opened in the reference app, settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The defaults are contains, every entry kind, both sides and not limited to the Font View selection; Reset returns to them. The options menu of a newly opened Kerning window settles them. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A pair is listed when one of the searched sides matches the term, has the chosen entry kind and, with the selection option, is a selected glyph or a group one of them belongs to; a search on a running Kerning window with mixed pairs settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Does not contain keeps a pair only when no searched side contains the term; a search for a letter that occurs on one side of a pair settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The term is compared with the bare name of a group, and with its @ spelling when the term starts with @; searching for @ in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSearchOptions` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The search is case-sensitive, since glyph names are; searching for t in a font with T settles it. |
| `kerning.windowEntryColors` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A selected row draws its entries in the selection's text colour instead of blue and gold; a screenshot of a selected row in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSorting` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Before a header is clicked the list keeps the stored order of the pairs. |
| `kerning.windowSorting` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Names sort byte by byte in their @ spelling and equal entries keep the stored order in both directions; sorting a list with repeated values in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.windowSelectionDrivesEditView` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A group side is shown by its first member in glyph order. |
| `kerning.windowInPlaceEditing` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Return or leaving the cell confirms and Escape cancels, as in the Info box fields; a glyph name cell is not editable. Editing cells in the reference settles both. |
| `kerning.copyPasteBetweenMasters` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A paste goes into the writing direction the window shows, whatever direction the copied text names; copying from a left-to-right list and pasting into a right-to-left one in the reference settles it. |
| `kerning.copyPasteBetweenMasters` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The list keeps Copy and Paste from the Edit menu until another field takes the keyboard or a click lands outside the window and the menus; choosing Edit › Copy in the reference after clicking the list, then after clicking the canvas, settles it. |
| `kerning.compressRules` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Within one run the pairs are taken in stored order and each sees the lifts made before it, so when two pairs would lift to the same group pair the first is lifted and the second meets it (absorbed with an equal value, kept as an exception with another); running Compress on such a pair list in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.compressRules` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A glyph–group pair is not lifted or absorbed while group–glyph pairs stored for members of its group would take over those glyphs once it is gone; a pair list with T–@n and @T–m in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.compressRules` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A later pair with the same sides as an earlier one (one group in two spellings, or two entries for one master) never applies, so it is removed together with the earlier pair when that one is lifted or absorbed; compressing such a pair list in the reference app settles it. |
| `kerning.compressOneStepPerRun` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | A run lifts the left side of every pair that can lift it, else its right side; one run on a pair list where both sides can lift, in the reference app, settles it. |
| `kerning.implicitKernFeature` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The generated lookups follow fontc's kern writer (split by script and direction, glyph pairs before class pairs, values rounded half up); the handbook gives no rules, and the GPOS of a font the reference app exports from a test source settles it. |
| `kerning.implicitKernFeature` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | Until feature code is compiled (M8), a top-level include in enabled feature code leaves the generated kerning out with a warning naming the file, because the file may hold a kern feature; following the include would settle it, and an export from the reference app of a source whose included file has no kern feature shows what it writes. |
| `kerning.groupClassNames` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Only the `@MMK_L_`/`@MMK_R_` classes the code refers to and no class entry defines are written, before the first entry, with the glyphs the target exports in font order, and a group without such glyphs stays undefined so the compiler names the reference; the expanded code of a reference export settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | With several glyphs selected, a field whose values differ is empty with the grey Multiple Values placeholder the handbook gives the Font View inspector; the reference app with several glyphs selected settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The glyph box hangs under the current glyph's descender, centred on its advance, separate from the selection box at the foot of the canvas; a screenshot of the reference app with a selection settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Emptying a field that holds a key stores the metric's current number, so the key goes and nothing moves; emptying such a field in the reference app settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | A key that cannot be evaluated (a cycle, a division by zero, a result out of range) is drawn red like an out-of-sync key, without the update button; a cyclic key in the reference app settles it. |
| `infoBox.spacing` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | A master that takes its metrics from another master shows that master's values read only, with a line naming it; a linked master in the reference app settles it. |
| `editView.kerningModeButton` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The button sits between the writing direction buttons and the zoom controls; the reference app's bottom bar settles it. |
| `infoBox.kerning` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | With several glyphs selected, a Group field whose glyphs differ is empty with the grey Multiple Values placeholder, and a kern field acts on the pair at the cursor only; the reference app with several glyphs selected settles it. |
| `infoBox.kerning` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | A side whose glyph has no kerning group there shows its lock disabled, since opening it has nothing to make an exception from; the Info box on an ungrouped glyph in the running app settles it. |
| `infoBox.kerning` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | In right-to-left text the kern field, lock and group at the left edge belong to the pair with the glyph drawn on the left, the next glyph in logical order; a right-to-left tab in the running app settles it. |
| `infoBox.precedingGlyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The group in the preceding glyph's box is an editable field that sets the group facing the current glyph (the right group in left-to-right text); clicking it in the running app settles it. |
| `infoBox.precedingGlyph` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The box is not shown when no glyph comes before the current one on its line; placing the cursor at the start of a line in the running app settles it. |
| `dialog.renameKerningGroup` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The buttons read All Pairs, This Pair and Cancel; the handbook gives no captions, and a screenshot or video of the dialog settles them. |
| `dialog.renameKerningGroup` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | All Pairs (scope font) is the default button that Return presses, since editing a group name most often means renaming the group; the dialog in the reference app settles it. |
| `dialog.pasteKerningConflict` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The warning has a Cancel button besides Overwrite and Keep Existing. |
| `dialog.pasteKerningConflict` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | Overwrite is the default button that Return presses; the warning in the reference app settles it. |
| `dialog.pasteKerningConflict` (panel) | M5 | handbook | – | The warning lists the conflicting pairs with the pasted and the current value; the handbook names no list, and a screenshot of the warning settles it. |

## `09-reusing-shapes.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | The components are the full Unicode canonical decomposition of the glyph's code point, each code point mapped to its glyph name through the glyph database (U+0301 to acutecomb), suffixes carried over and the preferred-mark rules applied, plus our own table for glyphs Unicode does not decompose (i, j and ligatures by their name parts); the composites stored in corpus files saved by the reference app measure it (M6 plan § 5). |
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | A glyph the database cannot decompose, or one whose parts are missing from the font, is refused with a message naming the missing parts and nothing changes; running it on such a glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | The ends of PostScript hints on the replaced paths become numbers at their places; TrueType instructions and corner components on them are removed and reported, other hints on the replaced shapes (cap components…) are removed with them, and hints without node references stay; running it on a hinted glyph with corner components in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.createComposite` | M6 | handbook | yes | Create Composite keeps the layer's anchors and the glyph's metrics keys; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.addComponent` | M6 | handbook | yes | The component is appended at offset zero, after every other shape, and the layer's automatic alignment places it; a component that would make the glyph contain itself is refused; adding a component in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | The suggested name is `_part.` plus the current glyph's name, with `.001`, `.002` appended while the name is taken; the dialog of the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | A name that is already a glyph is refused with a message; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | The new glyph keeps the shapes at their coordinates and the component sits at offset zero; anchors stay on the original layer; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | On each other master the shapes with the same indices move into the new glyph's layer of that master, and a master whose layer lacks them gets an empty layer; a two-master font in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | Without a name the command takes the suggested one; each layer of the new glyph takes the width of the original glyph's layer of its master; the reference app's new glyph settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | Only master layers are split: a non-master layer is refused, and images in the selection are refused; the reference app with a brace layer or an image selected settles it. |
| `glyph.componentFromSelection` | M6 | handbook | yes | Hints move with the paths they point at; a PostScript hint that links a moved and a kept path stays with its end on the moved path turned into a number, a TrueType instruction or corner component that does is removed and reported, and any other hint that does is removed with a warning; a hinted glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `component.move` | M6 | handbook | yes | A move of an aligned or locked component is refused with a message rather than silently left without effect (the font stays the same either way); a component aligned horizontally only takes the vertical part, and a purely horizontal move of it is refused; dragging such components in the reference app settles whether it shows a message. |
| `component.setAnchor` | M6 | handbook | yes | The anchor menu lists the anchors of the attachment target whose name is the mark anchor's name without its underscore or that name followed by an underscore and a suffix; when the stored name no longer exists, the component attaches to the plain name and keeps the stored value; the reference app settles it. |
| `component.setAnchor` | M6 | handbook | yes | The command accepts only names the component can attach with: one of its own underscore anchors without the underscore (`top` for `_top`), or that name followed by an underscore and a suffix (`top_viet`, `top_2`); an empty name clears the choice; the anchor menu of the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.showGlyphsUsingAsComponent` | M6 | handbook | yes | The glyphs open in a new Edit View tab, one per line slot in glyph order; the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.listComposites` | M6 | inferred | yes | `recursive` adds glyphs that use the composites, to the component depth limit; it has no counterpart in the reference app. |
| `component.enableAutomaticAlignment` | M6 | handbook | yes | Enable stores 0 (decided by the layer) on a layer of components only and 1 (forced) on a layer that has paths, so the item always aligns the component; enabling alignment on a component next to a path in the reference app settles it. |
| `component.lock` | M6 | handbook | yes | Lock Component is offered only for components that are not aligned automatically, and the command refuses an aligned component with a message; the context menu of an aligned component in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyph` | M6 | handbook | yes | The anchors that decomposition brings in are the propagated anchors the layer lacks, so mark attachment is unchanged; decomposing a composite with propagated anchors in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyph` | M6 | handbook | yes | Decomposed paths take the component's place in the shape order and the layer's hints are renumbered to keep their nodes; a hinted composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.decomposeGlyph` | M6 | handbook | yes | A base glyph whose corner components cannot be fitted is decomposed without them, as the editor draws it, with a warning; the reference app with a broken corner settles it. |
| `component.decompose` | M6 | handbook | yes | Decomposing one level adds no anchors to the layer; a smart component becomes its interpolated outline; corner, cap, segment and brush components inside the base glyph are applied before its paths are copied. Decomposing such components in the reference app settles it. |
| `component.decompose` | M6 | handbook | yes | A smart component that cannot be interpolated is decomposed from its base layer, as the editor draws it, with a warning; the reference app with incompatible pole layers settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.showSettings` | M6 | handbook | yes | With no glyph given, the window shows the edited glyph, else the first glyph selected in Font View; using Info for Selection in the reference app with nothing selected in a smart glyph settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.showSettings` | M6 | handbook | yes | CJK radical and Korean base glyphs, which the handbook also counts as smart glyphs, open the Selection Info window until they have a property; Info for Selection in such a glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.addProperty` | M6 | handbook | yes | A name another property of the glyph already has is refused; adding a second property with the same name in the reference app's Properties tab settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.setProperty` | M6 | handbook | yes | A rename carries the layers' poles and the values stored on every component of the glyph to the new name, and anchors named after the property keep their names; renaming a property in the reference app's settings window and saving settles it. |
| `smartGlyph.removeProperty` | M6 | inferred | yes | Removing a property drops its pole on every layer and leaves values stored on components in place and ignored; a minus button in the Properties tab of the reference app settles it. |
| `smartComponent.showSettings` | M6 | handbook | yes | With smart components of different smart glyphs selected, the window lists the properties of all their glyphs by name and sets each on the components whose glyph has it; selecting two such components in the reference app settles it. |
| `smartComponent.setValue` | M6 | handbook | yes | A new smart component stores no values and draws with the poles of its master layer, as glyphsLib does with a missing value; a property removed from the glyph leaves the stored value in place and ignored; adding a smart component in the reference app and saving settles it. |
| `smartComponent.setValue` | M6 | handbook | yes | A locked component refuses value changes; changing a value on a locked smart component in the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.add` | M6 | handbook | yes | A node that already holds a corner gets the new corner in its place, unscaled and aligned left, rather than a second corner; adding a corner to a node with a corner in the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.add` | M6 | handbook | yes | An off-curve node and the first or last node of an open path are refused, because the fitting needs a segment on each side; the context menu on an open path's end node in the reference app settles it. |
| `cornerComponent.add` | M6 | handbook | yes | A corner follows its node through outline edits as a linked hint does (crates/hinting/src/remap.rs), including a background's corners; an edit that deletes its node removes the corner and reports it, as a TrueType instruction on that node is removed; Remove Overlap moves a corner on a removed node or an extra node to the nearest node the removal made. Inserting a node before a corner's node and deleting a corner's node in the reference app would settle both. |
