| `token.glyphProperty.layerChoice` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Only the documented properties (width, LSB, RSB, TSB, BSB, anchors.<name>.x and .y) are accepted and any other is an error naming it; Option-clicking a token with another layer property in the reference app would show what else exists. |
| `token.predicate.boolean` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | isAligned holds when every master layer has a component placed automatically (a forced or horizontal component, or one left to the usual rule on a layer without paths), with the font setting off; a composite with one master unaligned in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.boolean` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | hasHints counts stem and TrueType hints on any layer and not corner components, caps, brushes or segments; a glyph with only a corner component in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.boolean` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | isColorGlyph holds for glyphs with colour palette layers only, and isAnyColorGlyph for every kind of colour layer; an SVG colour glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.logic` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | NOT binds tighter than AND, and AND tighter than OR, as in Apple's predicate format that the reference's language follows; this row's observe step would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.classFunction` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | In feature code a class name the font has no enabled class for is an error naming it, and in the Custom smart-filter rule it matches nothing; writing `class(Missing)` in the reference app would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.classFunction` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Predicate tokens inside a class count non-exporting glyphs as members; only the token's own result leaves them out. A class built from a predicate in a font with a non-exporting match would settle it. |
| `token.predicate.resultOrder` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | The Edit View preview target also leaves out non-exporting glyphs, as export targets do; Option-clicking a predicate token in a font with a non-exporting match would settle it. |
| `featureCode.ifdefOtherSymbols` (behavior) | M9 | inferred | – | Only `#ifdef VARIABLE` and `#ifndef VARIABLE` blocks are read, without nesting or `#else`; an unterminated `#ifndef VARIABLE` runs to the end of the code, as glyphsLib reads it. Settle with nested blocks in the reference app. |
| `featureCode.conditionScope` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | The scope and coordinate rules follow glyphsLib's converter; settle with a feature holding two conditions and a bare `condition;` in the reference app. |
| `featureCode.conditionScope` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A static export keeps the rules of a condition that holds at its location (lower limit inclusive, upper strict) instead of refusing the statement; the handbook places conditions only in variable-only blocks. Settle with a condition outside `#ifdef VARIABLE` exported as a static instance. |
| `featureCode.variablePosLowering` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A static instance gets the value the variable font has at its location; a reference export of `pos a a 8 (wght:700) 14;` as an instance between the default and 700 would settle it. |
| `featureCode.variablePosLowering` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A number that ends a glyph name (`a1 (wght:700) 5`) is not read as a value, where glyphsLib reads it; a reference export would settle which the reference does. |
| `featureCode.standaloneLowering` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | The split-block lowering keeps the lookup's place in lookup order and leaves it out of the feature; a reference export of a feature with a standalone lookup, read back with a font tool, would settle it. |
| `featureCode.standaloneLowering` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A standalone lookup starts with the lookup flags in effect in its feature, as a named lookup inside a feature does in fea-rs, so the split lookup states them; a reference export of a standalone lookup after `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` would settle it. |
| `featureCode.multipleSubstitutionSingletons` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A single glyph or one-member class as the target of a rule whose replacements are larger classes is passed to the compiler, which refuses it: repeating the target gives several rules for one glyph. A reference export of `sub a by @B @C;` would settle what the reference does. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | All four classes are optional: Update refreshes them when the font has them and never adds them, because Oswald has lowercase letters and no Lowercase class, and Markow and Oswald Italic have capitals and no Uppercase class; Update on a font with capitals and no classes would settle it. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Uppercase and Lowercase hold the letters whose subcategory is Uppercase or Lowercase, and a glyph without a subcategory takes it from its case; Inter's stored Lowercase also holds 11 small capitals that store the category Letter and nothing else, which this rule leaves out. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | All leaves out .notdef and .null, as Inter's stored All does. |
| `autoClass.general` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Which other classes Update offers would be settled in the running app. |
| `autoFeature.generalRule` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature name goes to liga when it is on the handbook's list or has `.liga`, to dlig with `.dlig`, to hlig when it contains longs or has `.hlig` or `.hist`, to rlig with `.rlig` or on the lam-alef list, and to dlig otherwise, the first match winning in that order; Update on a font with f_f_i.dlig and longs_t would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | Apart from the Indic scripts, only the scripts the locl code names get lines, as in every corpus source (Andika Mini, with Latin glyphs and no locl, has the DFLT line alone); Update on a font with Latin, Cyrillic and Greek glyphs and no locl would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | With several scripts, each script's group follows a blank line, in the order locl first names the scripts; Update on a font with .loclBGR and .loclTRK glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A language that locl names before any `script` statement gets a `languagesystem DFLT <language>;` line after the DFLT line. |
| `autoFeature.languageSystems` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A font with glyphs of one of the nine Indic scripts the Indic generators serve (by the glyph's effective script, else its name's script suffix) gets that script's group after the locl scripts, in the order its first glyph comes: the newer tag with its locl languages, then `languagesystem <older tag> dflt;`; Update on a font with Devanagari and Tamil glyphs and no locl would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.aalt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated as one `feature xxxx;` line per other feature with substitutions, in sidebar order, leaving the grouping to the compiler, as the corpus shows; cv features and features listed after aalt included. |
| `autoFeature.aalt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Left out: ccmp and all four ligature features (hlig and rlig by analogy with liga and dlig), and disabled features; Update on a font with hlig and a disabled ss01 would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated code starts with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;`, as the newer corpus files have it, and lists longer ligatures first; Update on a font with f_f_i, fi and a mark would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature goes to the first feature that claims it: its routing suffix, then the lam-alef set (rlig), then a longs component (hlig), then the default list (liga), else dlig; Update on a font with longs_t.liga would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature with another suffix joins components carrying that suffix (`sub f.sc i.sc by f_i.sc;`) and gets no rule when one is missing; Update on a font with f_i.sc would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.liga` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A second ligature with the same component sequence in one feature gets no rule, and ligatures whose components are all marks get none; Update on a font with lam_alef-ar and lam_alef-ar.fina but no positional forms would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.dlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated like liga, with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` and longer ligatures first; Update on a font with f_odieresis would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated like liga, with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` and longer ligatures first; Update on a font with such ligatures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated like liga, with `lookupflag IgnoreMarks;` and longer ligatures first; Update on a font with such ligatures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rlig` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Ligatures of joining scripts take positional components where the font has them: `sub lam-ar.init alef-ar.fina by lam_alef-ar;` and `sub lam-ar.medi alef-ar.fina by lam_alef-ar.fina;`; Update on F2 would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2sc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | With a shared `.sc` set each capital maps to the small cap named after its lowercase letter (`sub A by a.sc;`), with a `.c2sc` set to its own suffix; Update on a font with A, a and a.sc would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2sc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A capital takes its own `.c2sc` form first, then a shared-set glyph on its own base (A.sc), then the shared-set glyph of its lowercase letter (a.sc); the case pair is found by code point, else by the name with its first letter changed. Update on a font with A.c2sc, A.sc and a.sc would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.smcp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.smcp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A `.sc` or `.smcp` glyph on an uppercase base (A.sc) serves smcp from the lowercase letter of the same character, after any glyph on a lowercase base; Update on a font with A.sc only would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2pc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.c2pc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Capitals are paired with petite capitals as c2sc pairs them with small capitals: `.c2pc` first, then `.pc` or `.pcap` on the capital, then on its lowercase letter. |
| `autoFeature.pcap` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.sups` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The `superior` ending is accepted on any base glyph, not only figures, since the handbook names letter forms such as ainferior too; a `.sups` glyph wins over a `superior` name for the same base. Update on a font with asuperior would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.subs` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `.subs` comes before `.sinf` and both before an `inferior` name when one glyph has several forms (sinf: `.sinf` first); Update on a font with a.subs and a.sinf would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.afrc` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One ligature rule per nut fraction from the figures, slash and figures it names, in the handbook's list order; Update on a font with oneovertwo and oneoverten would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.frac` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | With `.numr` and `.dnom` figures and a fraction glyph, frac gets three lookups: slash between figures to fraction, figures to numerators, numerators after fraction or a denominator to denominators; Update on a font with both sets would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.frac` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The fallback takes the Unicode vulgar fractions in code point order, then the nut fractions in the handbook's order, one rule per input sequence; Update on a font with onehalf and oneovertwo would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.onum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Default and `.tf` figures map to `.osf` and `.tosf`, keeping proportional or tabular width; Update on a font with lf, tf, osf and tosf figures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.tnum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The unsuffixed glyph of a figure set takes the first style no suffixed form claims, in the order lf, osf, tf, tosf, decided per base name; every form maps to the form of the other width with the same height. Update on a font with lf, tf and osf figures would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pnum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | pnum maps each tabular form to the proportional form of the same height, with the default figure placed as tnum describes; lines follow the glyph order of the suffixed glyph of each pair, as the corpus shows. |
| `autoFeature.lnum` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `.osf` and `.tosf` figures map back to the default and `.tf` figures, keeping width; Update on a font with all four figure sets would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ordn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Letters missing from the font are left out of the `[A a]` and `[O o]` classes, and the numero rule needs N, o and period; Update on a font without capital A would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ornm` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `sub bullet from [A.ornm …];` with the ornaments in glyph order, then `sub A by A.ornm;` per letter whose base glyph exists; Update on a font with bullet and A.ornm would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hist` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.case` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Default figures that take an oldstyle style (by the tnum rule, for example when `.lf` forms exist and `.osf` forms do not) map to the lining figures of their width; the `.case` rule wins for a glyph that has both. Update on a font with one and one.lf would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.cpsp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The amount is 5 units per side, written `pos @Uppercase <5 0 10 0>;`; adding Capital Spacing in the reference app would settle the amount. |
| `autoFeature.cpsp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Without an Uppercase class entry the rule lists the capitals inline (`pos [A B] <5 0 10 0>;`); a capital is a glyph whose subcategory is Uppercase, or without category data one whose code point is an uppercase letter; adding Capital Spacing to a font without the class would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The code takes the form of the version 4 file in the corpus (Markow): one `script` section per script, one named lookup per rule set (idotaccent, commaaccent, puntvolat) after a `language` statement listing every language that shares it; Update on a font with idotaccent and Scommaaccent would settle the current form. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | When several Turkish i glyphs exist, idotaccent is used before i.TRK and i.loclTRK, the order the handbook lists them; Update on a font with idotaccent and i.loclTRK would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Catalan and Dutch rules use, in order of preference, the `.loclCAT` middle dot, the `.loclCAT` or `.loclNLD` ligature, then the legacy Ldot, ldot or Jacute, jacute in context, lowercase rule first; the ligature and Jacute forms have no corpus example and Update on fonts with each would settle them. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Dutch lookup is named ijacute and the Urdu lookup urdu; Urdu writes `sub X by X.urdu;` under `script arab; language URD;` for each `.urdu` glyph whose base exists, and six-ar alone writes nothing; Update on a font with Jacute and on one with four-persian.urdu would settle them. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Other `.loclXXX` glyphs (XXX three or four capitals or digits) get `sub base by base.loclXXX;` in a lookup named locl_<script>_<XXX>, under the script of the base glyph's code point, else of the stored script, else DFLT, after the built-in sets; Update on a font with be-cy.loclSRB would settle the shape. |
| `autoFeature.locl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Suffixed forms of the built-in glyphs (i.sc and idotaccent.sc) get no built-in rule; Update on a font with small caps would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.cvXX` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.salt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | salt copies the rules of ss01 only, as the handbook says; Update on a font with ss01 and ss02 would settle whether other sets join. |
| `autoFeature.swsh` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.titl` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.init` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |

| `autoFeature.fin3` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fin3` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The suffix is accepted on glyphs of any script, since the handbook only says it is used in Syriac; Update on a Latin glyph with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.fwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Single substitutions to the suffixed forms plus the space substitution, one line each in glyph order; Update in the reference app would settle it. |

| `autoFeature.hkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base counts as kana by its code point (Hiragana, Katakana, their extensions and the halfwidth forms) or its script; Update on a font with a kanji and a kana with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hojo` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.hwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Single substitutions to the suffixed forms plus the space substitution, one line each in glyph order; Update in the reference app would settle it. |

| `autoFeature.pkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base counts as kana by its code point (Hiragana, Katakana, their extensions and the halfwidth forms) or its script; Update on a font with a kanji and a kana with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pwid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |

| `autoFeature.ruby` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.twid` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.vkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.vkna` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base counts as kana by its code point (Hiragana, Katakana, their extensions and the halfwidth forms) or its script; Update on a font with a kanji and a kana with the suffix would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.vrt2` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | One `sub base by base.suffix;` line per glyph, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag, as the corpus shows for case, sups and tnum; Update in the reference app on a font with such glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.abvs` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature belongs in abvs when it has no code point of its own, its production name `uniXXXXYYYY` spells a letter and a sign the font encodes, and the sign has a `_top` anchor; Update in the reference app on a Kannada font with ki-kannada would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.abvs` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The halant and nukta are never the sign, since rphf, half and nukt form those pairs; the same Update would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.akhn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The akhands are k_ssa and j_nya in every Indic script, written as consonant, halant, consonant; Update on a Devanagari font with those glyphs would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.blwf` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The rakar mark (`rakar-deva`) is formed in blwf from halant and ra, as Indic shaping expects, besides the `.below` glyphs the handbook names; Update on a Devanagari font with rakar-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.blws` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A ligature belongs in blws when its production name spells a letter and a sign with a `_bottom` anchor, as for abvs with `_top`; Update in the reference app on a Devanagari font with ru-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.blws` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A Gujarati `X-gujarati.straight` replaces `X-gujarati` before the script's below-base forms (its rakar and its `.below` glyphs), as no source says what the straight form is for; Update on a Gujarati font with a .straight glyph would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.blws` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Oriya halant combinations the handbook mentions are not written, as no source names their glyphs; Update on an Oriya font would settle them. |
| `autoFeature.cjct` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Conjunct rules read the half forms the half feature has made, falling back to consonant and halant; Update on a Devanagari font with k_ya-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.half` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Half forms are recognised by the name (the full form with a final `a` exists) rather than by the Halfform subcategory alone; Update on a Devanagari font would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.nukt` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The rule is `sub base nukta by baseNukta;` in glyph order; Update in the reference app on a Devanagari font with kaNukta-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pref` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | pref forms a rakar whose glyph database subcategory is Spacing Combining (rakar-malayalam), and blwf forms the other rakars; Update in the reference app on a Malayalam font with rakar-malayalam would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pref` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Scripts outside the nine Indic scripts of the glyph names (Khmer, Myanmar, Javanese) get no pref; Update on a Khmer font with ro-khmer.pre would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pres` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The variant chosen for a base is the one whose `imatra` anchor, with the matra set before the base, lands nearest the base's `top` anchor; a tie goes to the earlier glyph, the plain matra first. Update in the reference app on a font with iMatra-deva.01 to .05 would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pres` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A base without a `top` anchor is measured at its right edge (its advance width); a variant without an `imatra` anchor is not a candidate. The same Update would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pres` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Only the vowel sign i of Devanagari, Bengali, Gurmukhi and Gujarati (the scripts where it stands before the base) gets variants; iiMatra and its `iimatra` anchor are not used. Update on a font with iiMatra variants would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.pres` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Each rule looks at the one glyph after the matra, so a half form between the matra and the base is not measured; Update on a font with s-deva and iMatra variants would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.psts` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `X-kannada.base` replaces `X-kannada` before halant-kannada and eMatra-kannada, and `X-kannada.base.e`, where it exists, takes the e sign instead; Update in the reference app on a Kannada font with ka-kannada.base and ma-kannada.base.e would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.psts` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Without halant-kannada nothing is written, as the handbook names the halant as the trigger; the same Update without the halant would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.psts` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Oriya `.base` glyphs of the glyph database get no psts rules, as the handbook names Kannada only; Update on an Oriya font with ka-oriya.base would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rkrf` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Rakar ligatures are recognised by the names `X_ra-S` and `Xa_rakar-S`, as the handbook gives no naming; Update on a Devanagari font with k_ra-deva would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.rphf` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Generated from the glyph names alone, in glyph order, with no lookup or flag; Update in the reference app on a Devanagari font would settle the form. |
| `autoFeature.vatu` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | vatu calls every lookup the rkrf entry defines or calls (`lookup NAME;` each), so the rakar forms also apply where a shaper runs vatu; an rkrf of plain rules, as Update writes it, gives no vatu. Update in the reference app on a font whose rkrf holds a lookup would settle which lookups count. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Latin ccmp is generated in the corpus's form: mark-ligature lookup first, then the dotless lookups with the top-accent filtering set; scripts other than Latin get no ccmp until a source shows their form. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | Lookups are numbered `ccmp_DFLT_1`, `ccmp_DFLT_2` in that order, as the newest corpus sources name a lone mark-ligature or dotless lookup `ccmp_DFLT_1`; Update in the reference app on a font with both would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | `@CombiningTopAccents` holds the encoded marks of canonical combining class 230, sorted by name, which matches every stored list in the corpus; a font with an unencoded top mark in the reference app would settle whether anchors count too. |
| `autoFeature.ccmp` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Latin split lookup `ccmp_latn_1` (fi, fl, the lj, nj and dž digraphs into their letters, in a fixed order) is kept from a version 3 source, as no newer source has those glyphs; Update in the reference app on a font with fi would settle it. |
| `autoFeature.kern` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The kern lookups are split by script and direction as fontc splits them; the GPOS of a font with Latin and Cyrillic kerning exported from the reference app settles it. |
| `autoFeature.dist` (behavior) | M5 | handbook | – | The scripts whose kerning goes to dist are fontc's (Indic, Universal Shaping Engine, Khmer, Myanmar); a font with Devanagari kerning exported from the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.syntax` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A token FontCraft cannot resolve stays in the text as written, and the compile report names it, as glyphsLib leaves it; a misspelt token in the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.versionString` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The token gives FontCraft's full release number (`0.0.1`), where the handbook's example shows two parts (`3.4`); the reference app's output for a release number of three parts would settle the form. |
| `textToken.keyPath` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The supported key paths are the font's familyName, versionMajor, versionMinor, unitsPerEm (and upm), date (as the source writes it), copyright, designer, designerURL, manufacturer, manufacturerURL, license, licenseURL, trademark, description, sampleText, vendorID, uniqueID, and the instance's name, familyName, fullName and postscriptFullName (family and style name with a space), postscriptFontName and fontName (family without spaces, a hyphen, the style name), preferredFamilyName, preferredSubfamilyName, weightClass (400 when unset) and widthClass (5 when unset); tokens tried in the reference app settle more. |
| `textToken.keyPath` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | In an instance context a key the instance does not have (designer, copyright…) reads the font's value, where glyphsLib keeps such a token as written; the same token typed into an instance's field in the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.numberValue` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A master that stores no value for a defined number value reads 0, as a missing metric value does; the reference app with a number value added after the master settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | In a font or instance context the token reads the first master's layer (an instance does not interpolate the value yet); the reference app with `{{{H:width}}}` in an instance's name settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | LSB and RSB of an empty layer name nothing and stay as written; the reference app with `{{{space:LSB}}}` settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | Numbers are written without a fraction when whole and with at most three decimals otherwise; a glyph with a fractional width in the reference app settles it. |
| `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | The bounds are spelled as the reference's scripting object names them (`bounds.origin.x`, `bounds.size.width`); the reference app with those tokens settles it. |
| `regex.engine` (behavior) | M4 | inferred | – | Patterns use the Rust regex crate, which has no look-around and no back-references inside a pattern; the reference's engine may accept them. Settled by observation. |
| `url.show.path` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A path that is empty, relative, a network share, not a file or folder, or that cannot be opened is skipped with a warning together with its glyphs, and the rest of the URL is still handled; the observe step below settles it. |
| `url.show.production` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A glyph without a production name of its own is found by its glyph name; a show URL with production=A in the reference app would settle it. |
| `url.show.defaultLayer` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | The main master is the font's first master; a font whose main master is not the first, opened by a show URL in the reference app, would settle it. |
| `url.show.encoding` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Query values are percent-decoded as UTF-8 and unknown glyphs or layer ids are skipped with a warning; the reference does not say. |
| `url.show.encoding` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A `+` stays a plus sign rather than a space, and a URL holding raw spaces, control characters or non-ASCII characters is refused; a show URL with `+` in a glyph name in the reference app would settle the first. |
| `url.show.encoding` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A `%` not followed by two hexadecimal digits, bytes that are not UTF-8 and encoded control characters refuse the whole URL; unknown parameters are skipped with a warning. |
| `url.safety` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | URLs only open and show; any other path after `fontcraft://` is refused, so a web page cannot run commands; the reference documents only `show`. |
| `url.safety` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A URL with a fragment, user information or anything after `show/` other than the query is refused; device files and network shares are not opened. |
| `snippet.builtIn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The built-in snippets are Automatic Code, Lookup, Contextual Substitution, Language System and Stylistic Set Name (a featureNames block with a substitution); opening the snippets menu in the reference app would settle the list and the titles. |
| `snippet.builtIn` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The Automatic Code snippet inserts `# Automatic Code` followed by a line break, so code after the caret is not commented out; inserting it in the middle of a line in the reference app would settle it. |
| `snippet.fileNaming` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | The extension is matched without regard to case and the `FeatureSnippets` prefix with it; a file whose NAME is empty is skipped with a warning; a snippet folder with such files in the reference app would settle both. |
| `snippet.fileFormat` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | An entry without a text `title` or `code` is skipped and counted in one warning for its file while the file's other entries are kept; a file with such an entry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `snippet.fileFormat` (behavior) | M8 | handbook | – | A binary property list is refused with a message; whether the reference app reads one would settle it. |
| `snippet.insertion` (behavior) | M8 | inferred | – | A snippet is inserted at the caret and replaces the selection; inserting one over a selection in the reference app would settle it. |
| `fontInfo.featureAutocomplete` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | The list opens after a name character, @, $ or { is typed, follows the keyboard only (Up, Down, Return or Tab to insert, Escape to close) and closes on a click; using it in the reference app settles mouse clicks and Tab. |
| `fontInfo.featureNamePreview` (panel) | M8 | handbook | – | The glyph is drawn as it is in the master of the active Edit View tab, else the master Font View shows; Option-clicking a glyph name with another master active in the reference app settles it. |

## `13-postscript-hinting.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `hint.ps.renderPreview` | M10 | inferred | yes | The preview rasterizes with skrifa's PostScript hinter (a port of FreeType's), not with an Adobe rasterizer; comparing with the Adobe application preview the handbook suggests would show how far they differ. |
| `hint.ps.autohintGlyph` | M10 | handbook | yes | Autohint replaces any hints the layer already has and acts on every glyph selected in Edit View or Font View; the reference app would settle both. |
| `hint.ps.autohintGlyph` | M10 | handbook | yes | Only the stems are stored (an export rebuilds the hint masks from them; counter masks and flex stay export-only), each edge linked to the layer's first on-curve node at that position, else stored as a number; a reference glyph autohinted from the context menu and read back would show which nodes it links. |
| `hint.ps.addHorizontalHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | With no node selected the new hint sits at the vertical centre of the bounding box of the layer's on-curve nodes with the master's first horizontal stem as its size (50 units without stems), and on an empty layer it starts at the baseline; doing it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.addHorizontalHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | Two selected nodes make one hint even when they lie on different paths (the stem of an O); selecting the outer and inner nodes of an O's stem in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.addVerticalHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | With no node selected the new hint sits at the horizontal centre of the bounding box of the layer's on-curve nodes with the master's first vertical stem as its size (50 units without stems), and on an empty layer it is centred on the advance width; doing it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.makeGhostHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | Make Ghost Hint keeps the hint's origin edge with its node link and makes a top ghost when that edge lies in or nearer to a top zone than to a bottom zone (the baseline zone counts as a bottom zone), else a bottom ghost; a ghost hint becomes a stem hint again when hint.ps.setHintValues gives it a size; the reference app would settle both. |
| `hint.ps.setHintValues` | M10 | handbook | yes | Typing a value for a linked end unlinks that end and keeps the typed number; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.setHintValues` | M10 | handbook | yes | A typed origin leaves a linked other end on its node and, when the other end is a number, keeps the hint's size; typing an origin for a hint linked at one end in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.moveHints` | M10 | inferred | yes | Moving several hints with one arrow key press is one undo step, as moving several nodes is; pressing an arrow key with two hints selected in the reference app and then Undo once would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.attachHintToNode` | M10 | inferred | yes | Dropping a marker on a node links that end, and dragging it off onto empty space unlinks it, keeping the current number; the reference app would settle the unlinking gesture. |
| `hint.ps.selectNextHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | Tab wraps from the last hint to the first; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.selectNextHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | With several hints selected, Tab moves on from the one latest in list order and selects only the next hint; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.selectPreviousHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | Shift+Tab wraps from the first hint to the last and, with several hints selected, moves back from the one earliest in list order; the reference app would settle it. |
| `autohint.ps.run` | M10 | docs | yes | The autohinter is a port of otfautohint with its defaults; which autohinter the reference app runs is not documented, and a reference export compared with otfautohint's output on the same font would show whether they agree. |
| `autohint.ps.run` | M10 | docs | yes | The masters of one glyph are hinted together so their stems match, and one by one when their outlines are not compatible; a reference export of a two-master font would show whether its instances' hints come from one master. |
| `autohint.ps.runFont` | M10 | inferred | yes | The hinted CFF table is subroutinised again by FontCraft's subroutiniser, where otfautohint writes the new charstrings without subroutine calls and keeps the old subroutines; the file sizes of both outputs on one font would show whether this matters. |
| `autohint.ps.runFont` | M10 | inferred | yes | The outline is written back with its coordinates as they were, fractions included, and only the stems are rounded unless decimal, where otfautohint without --decimal writes the whole charstring with rounded coordinates; a font with fractional coordinates hinted by both would show the difference. |
| `autohint.ps.runFont` | M10 | inferred | yes | A glyph selected for overlap handling is hinted against the overlap-free outline only when removing the overlaps changes the number of contours or the area they cover, as in autohint.ps.run; otfautohint checks the flattened outline point by point (guess on autohint.ps.overlapCorrection). |
| `autohint.ps.runFont` | M10 | inferred | yes | In a CFF2 font the masters hinted together are the default location and the peak of each region of the glyph's region list, in list order, and each master's stems are rounded before they are blended; otfautohint's own choice of master locations for a built CFF2 font is not documented, and hinting one CFF2 font with both would show whether the stems agree at each master. |
| `autohint.ps.runFont` | M10 | inferred | yes | In a CFF2 font, which has no FontBBox, the font's vertical extent for fontinfo override sets is the head table's yMin and yMax. |
| `autohint.ps.runFont` | M10 | inferred | yes | In a CFF2 font flex is never written, as in the variable CFF2 export, since one hint structure serves every master. |
| `hinting.ps.standardStems.privateDictMapping` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | More than twelve stems in one direction: the export writes the first twelve in list order and warns, and hint.ps.validateFontHints reports it; an export from the reference app with thirteen stems would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.alignmentZones.limits` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Overlapping zones and values that are not positive are errors and every other broken rule a warning, and the export still writes what fits the CFF limits; the reference app's font-info check would show how it ranks them. |
| `hinting.ps.alignmentZones.gridCompensation` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The check flags a zone whose size is a whole number of grid steps when the grid is coarser than one unit (it was not widened), and says nothing for finer or fractional grids, where a widened zone cannot be told apart; whether the reference app checks this at all would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.alignmentZones.blueValuesMapping` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | Too many zones (more than seven BlueValues pairs or five OtherBlues pairs): the export keeps the first pairs in ascending order and warns, and hint.ps.validateFontHints reports it; an export from the reference app with eight top zones would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.blueScale` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | As in fontmake, BlueScale, BlueFuzz and BlueShift are written only when the master has alignment zones; a reference export of a font with no zone settings would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.blueScale` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When BlueScale times the tallest zone reaches 1, the export keeps the BlueScale and warns rather than lowering it; a reference export of a font with a 30-unit zone and no blueScale parameter would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.blueShift` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | blueShift, blueScale, blueFuzz and the other hinting parameters whose scope the handbook does not give are read from the instance, else the master, else the font, as FontCraft reads every other parameter; a reference export with a master-level and a font-level blueShift that differ would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.familyAlignmentZones` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Family Alignment Zones is read where it is most specific (instance, then master, then font) and written as FamilyBlues and FamilyOtherBlues with the same rule as the master zones (a zone at 0 or of positive size to FamilyBlues); an export from the reference app would confirm the scope and the split. |
| `hinting.ps.export.autohintParameter` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The Autohint parameter is read on the instance only, and covers both outline flavours; when the instance has none the export dialog's checkbox (default off) decides; a reference export would show whether a font-level parameter counts. |
| `hinting.ps.export.hintReplacement` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Manual hints that overlap get masks from FontCraft's own rule: the first mask applies from the start, a new one starts at the first node on an edge of a stem that overlaps a stem the current mask holds, the first of two overlapping stems at one node wins, and a stem no node touches joins every mask it fits; the reference's masks for a hand-hinted 8 would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.flex.automatic` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Flex is added to every exported glyph that qualifies, hand-hinted glyphs included, with otfautohint's flex test (the handbook's conditions plus its length ratio); an export from the reference app of a cupped serif under 20 units, hinted by hand, would settle both points. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.hintKinds` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A stem with one edge on a node and the other a number (left when a linked node is deleted) is stored with the number as place = (number, 0); a file saved by the reference app after deleting one linked node of a stem hint would show its form. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.display` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | PostScript hints are shown and edited with the Select tool while View › Show Hints is on; whether another tool is needed would be settled in the reference app. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.display` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Badges and markers of horizontal hints stand in a column left of the glyph's origin, those of vertical hints in a row below the descender; a reference-app screenshot of n with hints would settle where they sit. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.display` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A marker is drawn filled when its end is linked to a node and hollow when it is a number; the reference app would settle how a link shows. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.display` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The hint items join the canvas context menu only while Show Hints is on; the reference app would settle whether they show with hints hidden. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.select` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The arrow keys move selected hints as they move selected nodes, the whole hint at once, so an end linked to a node becomes a number; an arrow along a hint's edges leaves it alone; with several hints selected they move in one undo step. The handbook says nothing about arrow keys and hints; pressing an arrow key with a linked hint selected in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.select` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Tab after the last hint wraps to the first and Shift+Tab before the first wraps to the last; with several hints selected Tab moves on from the one latest in list order; trying it in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.select` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A click on a hint's marker selects the hint as a click on its badge does; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.dragMarkers` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Dragging the origin marker moves only that edge, and a dragged edge lands on whole units; the reference app would settle both. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.linkOnDrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An edit that deletes the node a hint end is linked to turns that end into a number at the node's last position; deleting a linked node in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.linkOnDrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | After an edit, a linked node is found again by index, then by place and on- or off-curve class within half a unit, then by index when its path keeps the same node classes; reversing a contour and changing its start point with linked hints in the reference app would settle whether links follow the node. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.linkOnDrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Hints in a layer's background keep their links through background edits as the layer's own do; inserting a node before a linked node in the background of a hinted glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.linkToExtremes` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A linked node counts as an extreme when it is on-curve and its two neighbouring points (handles included) both lie on one side of it along the hint's axis, within half a unit; the reference app shows no such warning, so its Add Extremes on a test outline, which adds a node wherever it finds an extreme missing, is the nearest check. |
| `hinting.ps.manual.ghostFromSingleNode` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A ghost hint made from one node is a top ghost when the node lies in a top zone or nearer to a top zone than to a bottom zone (the baseline zone counts as a bottom zone), else a bottom ghost; a master without zones makes a top ghost above the middle of the layer's outline; doing it in the reference app on a node at the cap height and one at the baseline would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.masters.transferByNodeLink` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An unlinked hint end keeps its main-master number in every master and instance, and a link whose node no longer exists in a master falls back to that number; an export of an interpolated instance with one unlinked hint would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.masters.transferByNodeLink` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When a glyph's interpolated layer has paths whose node counts differ from its main-master layer's, the instance keeps the hints interpolation gave it instead of the main master's; exporting an instance of such a glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.masters.fallbackToOtherMasters` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When the main master has no hints for a glyph and several other masters do, the first of them in master order is used; a reference export of such a glyph would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.cff2.sharedHintStructure` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | In variable CFF2 output the hints come from the main master's manual hints resolved through node links in every source, or else from the autohinter run on all sources together; unlinked manual hints keep one value in every master; a variable export from the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.cff2.sharedHintStructure` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | A glyph whose sources' hints differ in structure (fontTools refuses the merge with VarLibCFFHintTypeMergeError or a hint mask error) is written without hints and with a warning, so the rest of the font still exports. |
| `hinting.ps.cff2.sharedHintStructure` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | An intermediate layer without PostScript hints of its own takes the hints its master uses through node links, as master layers do, so a variable font keeps the glyph's hints; a variable export from the reference app of a glyph with an unhinted intermediate layer would settle it. |
| `hinting.ps.cff2.sharedHintStructure` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Autohinted CFF2 glyphs are hinted without flex, as the merge draws flex as two curves; static exports keep the autohinter's flex. Running otfautohint on a CFF2 font would show whether it adds flex there. |
| `autohint.ps.inputs` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | Identical zones are merged before the autohinter reads them: glyphsLib's mapping (and FontCraft's Private dict) keeps duplicates, which otfautohint rejects as overlapping zones (Oswald Italic, ascender equal to cap height); the export still writes the Private dict as fontmake does. |
| `autohint.ps.overlapCorrection` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | FontCraft treats a glyph as overlapping when its path operations change the outline, where otfautohint asks whether any point of its flattened outline disappeared; the two differ on a cross of two bars whose corners all lie outside the other bar (overlapping here, not there), and comparing both autohinters on such glyphs would settle which to follow. |
| `autohint.ps.output` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | autohint on a compiled font writes INPUT.autohinted.otf, as for source input, where otfautohint writes over the input; --in-place gives otfautohint's behaviour. |
| `autohint.ps.writeToDefaultLayer` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | The autohint CLI writes hints to the default layer without -w, where otfautohint writes them to com.adobe.type.processedglyphs; a source keeps its hints with its outlines, and a user who needs the processed layer would show whether it is worth building. |
| `autohint.ps.allowChanges` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | With allowChanges the changed outline only guides the autohinter: the stems it finds are stored and the layers' outlines stay as they were, since a source stores stems and an export builds the hint masks from them; otfautohint writes the reordered contours into the font. Comparing contour order in a font hinted by otfautohint -c with FontCraft's output would show what that changes. |
| `autohint.ps.variable.keepConflicts` (behavior) | M10 | docs | – | The search that picks which conflicting stems to remove tries both choices for each conflict, as otfautohint does, but only for its first 4,096 steps; after that it keeps the first choice, so a glyph with that many order conflicts across masters can lose different stems than otfautohint would. Hinting such a glyph with otfautohint would show whether the limit is ever reached in practice. |
| `infoBox.psHint` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | With several hints selected the box shows the first of them in list order, with the count; the reference app would settle which one it shows. |

## `14-truetype-hinting.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `autohint.tt.run` | M10 | inferred | yes | The built-in TrueType autohinter is FontCraft's own (decided in M10 § 7, question 2): Snap and Stem instructions from the PostScript autohinter's zone and stem analysis, compiled with the manual-instruction compiler; ttfautohint runs instead when the TTFAutohint binary path parameter names one; the reference app's built-in autohinter is ttfautohint. |
| `hint.tt.getPsZones` | M10 | handbook | yes | Get PS zones replaces the TT zone list with the master's alignment zones, named after their metrics (xHeight for the x-height metric so that it gets the x-height rounding, else the metric's name, else zone 1, zone 2…); the reference app would settle replace or append and the names. |
| `hint.tt.getPsZones` | M10 | handbook | yes | On the main master, Snap instructions keep their zone by name after Get PS zones, and those whose zone is gone turn to Auto; the reference app would settle it with a Snap on a custom zone. |
| `hint.tt.addZone` | M10 | handbook | yes | Adding, removing or renaming a TT zone or stem writes the TTFZones or TTFStems parameter of every master, so a master that used its alignment zones or standard stems gets an explicit list; the reference app would show whether such a master gets the parameter. |
| `hint.tt.removeZone` | M10 | handbook | yes | Snap instructions that name a removed zone fall back to Auto, and zones aligned to it lose their alignment; a rename updates both; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.setZone` | M10 | inferred | yes | One command edits any property of a TT zone, as the sheet's row does, and refuses deltas and filters outside the main master; the reference app's sheet would settle whether other masters show those buttons at all. |
| `hint.tt.getPsStems` | M10 | handbook | yes | Get PS stems replaces the TT stem list, keeping the stems' names; the reference app would settle replace or append. |
| `hint.tt.removeStemDefinition` | M10 | handbook | yes | Stem instructions that name a removed stem fall back to Auto; a rename updates them; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.setStemDefinition` | M10 | inferred | yes | One command edits any property of a TT stem, as the sheet's row does, and refuses deltas and filters outside the main master; the reference app's sheet would settle whether other masters show those buttons at all. |
| `hint.tt.setStemDefinition` | M10 | inferred | yes | Turning a stem's orientation turns the Stem instructions it no longer suits to Auto; the reference app would settle it by switching a stem that a Stem instruction names. |
| `hint.tt.removeImportedHinting` | M10 | inferred | yes | The gasp Table parameter stays unless gasp is true, since an unhinted font may still want its rendering ranges; whether the owner wants it removed by default would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.showPixelPreview` | M10 | handbook | yes | The toggle hides only the blue and red outlines, as the handbook says; the pixel backdrop stays while the Instructor is active. Choosing an instance turns the preview on. The reference app would settle whether the backdrop goes with the outlines. |
| `hint.tt.setRenderingIntent` | M10 | inferred | yes | The rendering intent is a pop-up in the TrueType Instructor's Info box beside the pixel size; Grayscale maps to skrifa's smooth normal target, ClearType to its LCD target without symmetric rendering (no vertical oversampling), DirectWrite to LCD with symmetric rendering; the reference app would settle the control and the comparison of outlines. |
| `hint.tt.setPixelSize` | M10 | handbook | yes | The pixel size field accepts whole numbers from 4 to 400 PPM; the reference app would settle the limits. |
| `hint.tt.increasePixelSize` | M10 | handbook | yes | The pixel size starts at 12 PPM, the handbook example; the reference app would show its starting value. |
| `hint.tt.showPointIndexes` | M10 | handbook | yes | The point numbers show with every tool, not only the TrueType Instructor, since the handbook puts the toggle in the Edit View context menu; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.showPointIndexes` | M10 | handbook | yes | The canvas context menu offers the toggle only while the TrueType Instructor is active, so other tools' menus stay short; the reference app would settle whether every tool's menu has it. |
| `hint.tt.setPreviewScale` | M10 | handbook | yes | The waterfall starts at 1x and the scale is one setting for the Preview Area and the Preview Panel; the reference app would settle the default and whether the two keep separate scales. |
| `hint.tt.showPreviewAddress` | M10 | handbook | yes | The server listens on a free port of the local network interfaces, serves only the preview page and the font, and stops when the dialog closes (decided in M10 § 7, question 5); the reference app would settle lifetime and port. |
| `hint.tt.showPreviewAddress` | M10 | handbook | yes | The page and font live under a random 32-digit path in the address, so a device that only finds the port cannot read the font; the reference app's address format would settle whether it does the same. |
| `hint.tt.showPreviewAddress` | M10 | handbook | yes | The served instance is the first exported static instance (or the one named by `instance`), else the first master, since the export path builds instances, not the master the tab draws in; the reference app would settle which instance its page shows. |
| `hint.tt.showPreviewAddress` | M10 | handbook | yes | Glyphs of the tab without a Unicode value are left out of the page, which can only set characters; the reference app's page would settle how it shows them. |
| `hint.tt.showPreviewAddress` | M10 | handbook | yes | The canvas context menu offers the item only while the TrueType Instructor is active, so other tools' menus stay short; the reference app would settle whether every tool's menu has it. |
| `hint.tt.autohintGlyph` | M10 | handbook | yes | The Instructor's Autohint writes vertical Snap on every extreme in a TT zone and vertical Stem for each horizontal stem the PostScript stem analysis finds, and nothing horizontal, as ttfautohint hints heights only; a glyph hinted by the reference app would show which points and directions it picks. |
| `hint.tt.removeHints` | M10 | handbook | yes | Remove Hints in the TrueType Instructor removes TrueType instructions only and leaves PostScript hints, since the handbook names it in the TrueType section; running it on a glyph with both in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.deleteInstructions` | M10 | inferred | yes | The Delete key removes the selected instructions, as for PostScript hints; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.deleteInstructions` | M10 | inferred | yes | Deleting an instruction that later ones build on deletes it and reports those later ones, which stay in the list; the reference app would settle whether it refuses, deletes them too or keeps them silently. |
| `hint.tt.deleteInstructions` | M10 | inferred | yes | Deleting one member of a triple hint dissolves the triple hint and keeps the other two Stem instructions; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.moveInstruction` | M10 | handbook | yes | Repositioning an instruction moves the point it acts on, not only its badge; a drop away from any node changes nothing; dragging a Snap badge onto another node in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.moveInstruction` | M10 | handbook | yes | A move that leaves a later instruction without a touched origin or reference is refused rather than reported; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.setSnapZone` | M10 | handbook | yes | A named zone on a horizontal Snap instruction is refused, since zones are vertical; the reference app would show whether its pop-up offers zone names in horizontal mode. |
| `hint.tt.addStemHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | The context-menu label of the Stem instruction is Stem, as the key list names it; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.addStemHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | With four or more points selected, the points pair in the order given (first with second, third with fourth); the reference app would settle how a larger selection pairs. |
| `hint.tt.addStemHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | In a pair of one touched and one untouched point the touched one becomes the origin, and of two untouched points the first one does; the reference app would settle which end gets the arrowhead. |
| `hint.tt.setStemReference` | M10 | handbook | yes | A named stem whose orientation does not suit the instruction (a vertical stem on a vertical instruction) is refused; the reference app would show whether its pop-up lists stems of both orientations. |
| `hint.tt.makeTripleHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | Deleting or reversing one member of a triple hint dissolves the group and the other two become plain Stem instructions; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.makeTripleHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | Three instructions that would interleave with another triple hint in list order are refused, since the stored form keeps one flag per instruction and groups the flagged ones by threes; a reference file with two interleaved triple hints would settle how groups are stored. |
| `hint.tt.makeTripleHint` | M10 | handbook | yes | A triple hint keeps its pixel gaps by moving the middle and then the upper stem, never the lower one; the reference app would settle it on an E with crowded bars at 8 or 9 PPM. |
| `hint.tt.addDelta` | M10 | handbook | yes | Delta columns accept masters as well as static instances, both stored as the location key; the reference app's Delta table for an instruction would show which columns it offers. |
| `hint.tt.setDeltas` | M10 | handbook | yes | Set Delta Cells changes only the cells it names and keeps the rest, as clicking single cells of the table does; the reference app's table would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.cubicSourceQuadraticOutput` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An instruction on a cubic handle moves the off-curve point of the converted segment nearest to the handle, and an on-curve node that the conversion could leave implied is written as a point; exporting a glyph instructed on a handle from the reference app and reading its glyf would settle both. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Rounding only in GDI ClearType is stored as options 3, which no source shows; a file saved by the reference app with that rounding mode would show the value. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A Snap stores its zone in the stem key (−2 automatic, −1 none, else the index), as a Stem stores its stem; a file with a Snap set to a named zone would show the key. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Stem instructions flagged as triple form groups of three in list order; a file with two triple hints in one glyph would show how they are kept apart. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A delta cell is stored as 8 (up) or −8 (down) under its PPM, in a column keyed by the instance's axis values in braces padded to six, as the one format sample shows; a file with deltas set for two instances would confirm both. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An instruction of a TrueType type that lacks a point its type needs (the corpus has place-only Snap and Stem hints and a Stem without target) is detached: kept as stored, never listed or compiled; the reference app's export of such a glyph would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.mainMasterOnly` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Special layers' own TrueType instructions: an intermediate (brace) layer's are never exported, and an alternate (bracket) layer's are exported for its alternate glyph in a variable font when the layer belongs to the main master, since it is then that glyph's main-master layer; exporting a glyph whose special layers carry their own instructions from the reference app would settle both. |
| `autohint.tt.option.increaseXHeight` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Under the built-in autohinter the zones round to the nearest row after the x-height scaling, as ttfautohint's output does, not by TTFZoneRoundingThreshold; zone and stem deltas still apply on top. The reference app autohinting a font with that parameter would settle it. |
| `autohint.tt.option.increaseXHeight` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The built-in autohinter scales the family zones it uses up to TTFFamilyZonesThreshold like the zones they replace; ttfautohint has no family zones. |
| `autohint.tt.option.increaseXHeight` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An autohinted export leaves out the xHeight zone, and with it the x-height scaling, when the font draws none of u, v, x, z, o, e, s, c, and the capHeight zone when it draws none of T, H, E, Z, O, C, Q, S: the letters the ttfautohint manual lists for those zones, without which ttfautohint's output on the corpus leaves those heights unsnapped. The reference app autohinting such a font would settle it. |
| `autohint.tt.option.windowsCompatibility` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The built-in autohinter's Windows compatibility adds zones one unit high at usWinAscent and −usWinDescent, which snap extremes there to the nearest row rather than keeping them below the clipping line; a hinted export of a glyph that reaches usWinAscent, rendered under GDI, would settle it. |
| `autohint.tt.option.info` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | With FontCraft's own TrueType autohinter (decided in M10 § 7, question 2) the name strings are left alone unless detailed or plain info is asked for; the reference app's choice between its default and No Autohint Info would settle it. |
| `autohint.tt.controlInstructions.syntax` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The built-in autohinter turns the y shift of a touch correction into a vertical Delta before IUP, one pixel up or down for a shift of at least half a pixel; point shifts and every x shift move the point after IUP by the shift in eighths of a pixel. Stored by autohint.tt.run or hint.tt.autohintGlyph, point shifts become Deltas as touch shifts do and x shifts are reported. The external program applies them as its manual describes. |
| `autohint.tt.controlInstructions.syntax` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The built-in autohinter treats each left or right point as a one-point segment from its x plus the left offset to its x plus the right offset: snapped to a zone it lies in, else a vertical Stem from the nearest flat run above or below that overlaps the segment within 1.5 times the glyph's widest horizontal stem, else rounded to the grid. The manual's own example (a stem formed with a neighbouring segment) is the case it covers; ttfautohint's output on a glyph with a left or right point would settle the rest. |
| `autohint.tt.controlInstructions.syntax` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The built-in autohinter keeps nodir points out of every instruction, and reads the style forms (script feature @ glyphs, script feature width) and leaves them out. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.defaultFromAlignmentZones` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Zones derived from alignment zones are named after their metric (ascender, capHeight, xHeight, baseline, descender, or a named metric's name), the master's other zones zone 1, zone 2…; Get PS zones in the reference app would show its names. |
| `hinting.tt.param.ttfZones` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | No source holds a TTFZones value, so its entries are read as {name, position, size, align, deltas = {column: {PPM: shift}}, filter = predicate text}, the stem form of the corpus's TTFStems extended, and a new delta cell is written as 1 (up) or -1 (down); a file saved by the reference app with zones, an alignment, a delta and a filter would show the real keys. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.name` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | TT zones and TT stems are matched across masters by name; a master that lacks a name uses the main master's value for it; adding or renaming a zone or stem acts on every master; names must be unique and a duplicate is refused; the reference app would settle all four. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.align` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An alignment to a missing zone, or the link that closes a cycle, is left out with a warning and that zone is rounded on its own; the reference's editor may refuse such links instead. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.align` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An aligned zone's own deltas are applied after the alignment; a reference export with deltas on both zones would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.zones.deltas` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The delta column that applies is the master id in a master's build and the instance name in an instance's; the reference's stored delta tables would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.stems.deltas` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | GDI ClearType is recognised as ClearType on with symmetric smoothing off (GETINFO selectors 64 and 2048), so monochrome rendering counts as not GDI; rendering in Windows GDI with and without ClearType would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.param.overshootSuppressionBelowPpm` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Absent TTFOvershootSuppressionBelowPPM: no size-based suppression beyond what the zones do on their own; a reference export without the parameter would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.param.zoneRoundingThreshold` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A 'positive zone' is read as a zone with a positive size (a top zone), so bottom zones get no threshold; rounding a negative-position top zone in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.param.zoneRoundingThreshold` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The threshold is clamped to plus or minus one pixel; a reference export with a larger value would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.param.minimumDistance` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The minimum distance also applies to Round up and Round down and to stems without a stem definition, and is clamped to 0 to 8 px; a reference export with a stem rounded to zero would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.param.controlValueProgram` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The cvt Table parameter an opened font gets holds the values as text separated by commas, as the handbook gives its type as text; a .glyphs file saved by the reference app after opening a hinted TrueType font would show its form. |
| `hinting.tt.param.controlValueProgram` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | No corpus source holds the prep or fpgm Table Assembly parameter, so FontCraft writes and reads them in its own assembly syntax (one instruction per line, mnemonics with bracketed flags as fontTools prints them, push values on the push's line; fontTools' layout with the values on the following lines and /* */ comments is read too); a file saved by the reference app after opening a hinted TrueType font would show its syntax, and the reader would then accept both. |
| `hinting.tt.param.controlValueProgram` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When a master carries any of the three parameters, manual instructions are not compiled, since they call functions of FontCraft's own fpgm, and a warning says to remove the parameters (the handbook says to remove them before instructing afresh); a reference export of a font with both would show what it does. |
| `hinting.tt.import.glyphPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Imported glyph programs are kept per layer as assembly text with a fingerprint of the outline; they are written back while the outline is unchanged and the layer has no editor instructions, editor instructions win, and a changed outline drops the program with a warning; opening a hinted TTF in the reference app would settle how it stores them. |
| `hinting.tt.import.glyphPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | A composite glyph's fingerprint covers its own components and transforms, not the outlines of the glyphs they point to, so editing a base glyph keeps the composite's program; editing the base of a hinted composite in the reference app and exporting would show whether it keeps that program. |
| `hinting.tt.import.glyphPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Glyph programs are kept only when the font has a cvt, fpgm or prep table, since export writes them only beside those tables; a reference export of a font opened with glyph programs and none of the tables would show otherwise. |
| `hinting.tt.gasp.flagMapping` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Without a gasp Table parameter a font with TrueType instructions gets three records: up to 8 PPM DOGRAY + SYMMETRIC_SMOOTHING, up to 20 GRIDFIT + SYMMETRIC_GRIDFIT, up to 65535 all four bits; an unhinted font gets no gasp table, as fontc and fontmake write none; an export from the reference app without the parameter would settle both. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.masterOutline` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Extra nodes can be selected with a selection rectangle in the Instructor, as nodes can; the reference app would show whether a rectangle takes them. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.masterOutline` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Two segments that cross more than once give one extra node, the crossing nearest to where their chords cross, since an instruction names an extra node by its two segments only and export moves it to that crossing; instructing a curve that crosses a straight edge twice in the reference app would show which crossings it offers. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.instanceOutline` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The previewed instance is held by the Instructor and chosen with the `instance` parameter of hint.tt.showPixelPreview, since the Preview Area's instance menu (preview.setInstance) does not exist yet; the blue outline is the instance compiled as TrueType and drawn unhinted, so it shows the exported quadratic outline; the reference app would show whether its blue outline is the cubic instance. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.gridFittedOutline` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The grid-fitted outline comes from skrifa's TrueType interpreter (FreeType's v40 interpreter in Rust), not from the Windows rasterizer the handbook names (decided in M10 § 7, question 4); comparing red outlines with the reference app at a few sizes would show the difference. |
| `hinting.tt.tool.pixelBackdrop` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | The backdrop shows grey coverage of the grid-fitted outline, sampled 6 times across each pixel in the two ClearType intents and once down it in GDI ClearType, not coloured subpixels; screenshots of the reference app's backdrop in each intent would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.hintOrder` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Instructions cannot be reordered: the list order is creation order, and deleting and re-adding is the way to move one to the end; the reference app would show whether a reorder exists. |
| `hinting.tt.touchedPoints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A Delta instruction leaves its point untouched in the model, since it moves the point only at the sizes it lists; the reference app would settle whether a point with only a Delta can still take a Snap. |
| `hinting.tt.instructions.addFromSelection` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Dragging an instruction badge onto another node moves the instruction's target point there, and the drag is not saved as a badge position; the reference app would settle what the drag changes. |
| `hinting.tt.instructions.badgeColours` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Delta badges are orange, a colour no other instruction uses; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.instructions.badgeColours` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Badges are plain discs placed beside the point (left of it for vertical instructions, below it for horizontal ones) or on the arrow of Stem and Shift; the reference app would show their shape, placement and icons. |
| `hinting.tt.snap.zoneAuto` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | In horizontal mode Auto ignores zones and rounds to the nearest pixel edge, since zones are vertical; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.snap.zoneAuto` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Round up and Round down on a Snap inside a zone take the zone's row like Round, since the handbook ties zone snapping to the zone choice only; the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.snap.zoneNamed` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A point that lies inside its named zone is snapped to the zone's row instead of being kept at a rounded distance; the reference app would settle it by snapping an overshoot point with that zone named. |
| `hinting.tt.stem.originInZoneSnaps` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An untouched Stem origin outside every zone is rounded to the nearest pixel, since the handbook says a Stem rounds both untouched points; the reference app would settle whether it follows the instruction's rounding mode instead. |
| `hinting.tt.delta.restrictions` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Deltas run at their place in the list, before the final interpolation, so on an untouched point a Delta moves it from its unhinted position at that size; the reference app would settle it by adding a Delta to an untouched point and comparing that size with its neighbours. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Path > Remove Overlap in the editor applies the documented export rule to instructions on removed nodes and on intersections, and PostScript ends on removed nodes become numbers; running Remove Overlap on an instructed glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | At export, the points a removed node can move to are the on-curve points no source node lies at, which includes on-curve points the cubic-to-quadratic conversion added; they lie on the source segment as crossing points do, so they are not told apart. Exporting an instructed cubic glyph with Remove Overlaps in the reference app and reading where the instruction lands would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | At export, an off-curve node whose segment lost an end to overlap removal has no point, and an instruction on it is left out with a warning; exporting such a glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
