| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | An outline edit other than Remove Overlap removes an instruction whose node is gone instead of moving it; deleting an instructed node in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.overlapRemovalMovesHints` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | When upkeep removes one Stem instruction of a triple hint, the other two stay as plain Stem instructions; deleting a node of a triple hint in the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.variable.extraNodeHintsIgnored` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A variable export drops the instructions on extra nodes with one warning per glyph, the other instructions of the glyph kept; a variable export from the reference app would show whether it warns and whether instructions that depend on the dropped ones change. |
| `hinting.tt.variable.deltasIgnored` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Zone and stem deltas reach a variable font only for the default master's column; per-instance Delta instructions are dropped as the handbook says; a variable export from the reference app would settle the zone and stem part. |
| `hinting.tt.variable.cvar` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | Per-master zones and stems reach a variable font as cvt values of the default master plus cvar deltas; a variable export from the reference app would settle it. |
| `hinting.tt.export.generatedPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | The fpgm, prep and cvt layout is FontCraft's own, written from the TrueType instruction set specification; byte parity with the reference app is not a goal and nothing from its output is copied. |
| `hinting.tt.export.generatedPrograms` (behavior) | M10 | inferred | – | A font with TrueType programs sets head flags bits 2 (instructions depend on point size) and 3 (integer PPM); a reference export of an instructed font would show its head flags. |
| `hinting.tt.export.instanceValues` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | Composite glyphs get no glyph program of their own: their components' programs apply; Alternate and Intermediate layers that replace the main master's layer carry their own instructions, as the PostScript chapter says for hints; a reference export would settle both. |
| `hinting.tt.export.compileError` (behavior) | M10 | handbook | – | A glyph whose instructions fail to compile is exported unhinted and the export goes on, with a warning naming the glyph; a reference export of a glyph with a broken instruction would show whether it stops instead. |
| `dialog.ttfZones` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | Each edit takes effect at once as its own undo step, and Done closes the editor; the reference app's sheet would show whether its edits wait until it closes. |
| `dialog.ttfZones` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The plus button adds `zone N` at position 0, 16 units per 1000 units per em high, to every master; the reference app would show what a new zone holds. |
| `dialog.ttfZones` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | A row is selected by a click on its number; the reference app would show how its list selects a row. |
| `dialog.ttfZones` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The glyph filter offers every Smart Filter rule, not only Name, Category, Subcategory and Script, and a stored filter reopens as one Custom rule holding its predicate; the reference app's filter sheet would show its rule list. |
| `dialog.ttDeltas` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The table has one row per PPM from 8 to 80, the waterfall's range; its columns are the masters for zone and stem deltas and the static instances for Delta instructions; the reference app would settle both. |
| `dialog.ttDeltas` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | A font without static instances gets one column per master in the Delta table of an instruction, and instances or masters at one location share a column; the reference app would show what it offers then. |
| `dialog.ttDeltas` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | One table serves every selected Delta instruction: a click sets that cell on all of them, and a cell where they differ shows a dash whose first click sets one pixel up; the reference app would show whether the table opens for several instructions. |
| `dialog.ttfStems` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The plus button adds `stem N`, an X stem 80 units per 1000 units per em wide, to every master; the reference app would show what a new stem holds. |
| `infoBox.ttInstructor` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The five rounding modes are text buttons (Round, Up, Down, None, GDI) that act on the selected Snap, Stem, Shift and Interpolate instructions and leave selected Delta instructions alone; the reference app would show its icons and what a mixed selection does. |
| `infoBox.ttInstructor` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | With several instructions selected, a pop-up or rounding button shows the value they share and nothing when they differ; the reference app would show how it marks a mixed selection. |
| `infoBox.ttInstructor` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The zone pop-up lists the TT zones whose filter takes the glyph, only for vertical Snaps, and the stem pop-up the TT stems that suit the direction of the selected Stems; the reference app would show whether it lists every zone and stem. |
| `preview.ttHintingWaterfall` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | Each waterfall row is one PPM from 8 to 80 with the tab's text set in the current instance at that size; the reference app would settle spacing and labels. |
| `preview.ttHintingWaterfall` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The rows set the tab's master, since choosing a preview instance (preview.setInstance) is not built yet; the reference app's instance pop-up would settle what the waterfall follows when an instance is picked. |
| `preview.ttHintingWaterfall` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | A row holds at most 32 of the tab's glyphs, centred on the edited one, and leaves out glyphs that do not export; a long text in the reference app would settle how much of the line it shows. |
| `preview.ttHintingWaterfall` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | The rows are rebuilt at most four times a second while the font changes and not while the mouse button is down; the reference app would settle whether its waterfall follows a node drag live. |
| `dialog.ttPreviewAddress` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | Closing the dialog stops the server; the reference app would settle it. |
| `dialog.ttPreviewAddress` (panel) | M10 | handbook | – | An Only this computer checkbox serves the page on loopback instead of the local network, and Update serves the font as it is now at the same address; the reference dialog shows neither as far as the handbook says, and seeing it would settle whether to keep them. |
| `trueTypeInstructor` (tool) | M10 | handbook | – | The tool sits after the Measurement and Thickness tools in the toolbar and node selection works as with the Select tool (click, Shift+click, rubber band, Select All on the layer); the custom-parameter descriptions give its shortcut as I; the reference app would settle the rest. |
| `trueTypeInstructor` (tool) | M10 | handbook | – | S pairs the selected points along the stems: for vertical hinting by x, then y, each pair from the lower point (the origin) to the upper one; for horizontal hinting by y, then x, from left to right; the reference app would show how it pairs four or more points. |
| `trueTypeInstructor` (tool) | M10 | handbook | – | Arrow keys and Return do nothing in the Instructor, and Delete and Backspace delete only the selected instructions, so the outline cannot be edited by accident; the reference app would settle whether they move or delete nodes there. |

## `15-color-fonts.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `colorPalette.edit` | M11 | handbook | yes | The editor edits the font-level Color Palettes only; a master's own palettes are edited with the colorPalette commands and their `master` parameter. Settled by clicking a master's Color Palettes value in the reference. |
| `colorPalette.addColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new row is opaque black (#000000FF) in every palette unless `color` or `colors` is given. Settled by pressing plus in the reference's palette editor. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Removing a row renumbers the layers that use higher rows and refuses while a layer uses the row. Settled by removing a used row in the reference's editor and reading the layers afterwards. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Palette colours of shapes (fills, strokes, gradient stops, shadows) count as uses of a row and are renumbered with the palette layers. Settled by removing a row a full-colour layer's gradient uses in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | The last colour row can be removed, which leaves every palette empty. Settled by removing every colour in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.removeColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Palette colours of shapes in a layer's background count as uses of a row too: they hold it and are renumbered with the foreground. Settled by removing a row only a background shape uses in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.addPalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new palette is a copy of palette 0 (`copyFrom` defaults to 0). Settled by Add Palette in the reference's editor. |
| `colorPalette.removePalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | The last remaining palette cannot be removed; the command is refused. Settled by removing every palette in the reference's editor. |
| `layer.convertToColorPalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | Without `index` the layer gets colour index 0, and a layer that already is a palette layer keeps its index. Settled by choosing Color Palette on a new layer in the reference. |
| `layer.convertToColorPalette` | M11 | handbook | yes | A master layer cannot become a colour layer, and converting a colour layer to another kind replaces its kind. Settled by the Layers palette context menu on a master layer and on a colour layer. |
| `layer.setColorPaletteIndex` | M11 | handbook | yes | The headless command accepts any index up to 65,533, also one beyond the rows of the font's palettes, so layers can be set up before the palettes grow. Settled by opening a file whose colorPalette index is beyond its palettes in the reference. |
| `layer.convertToIColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | Without `size` a new iColor layer gets 128 pixels per em. Settled by the label of a new iColor layer in the reference. |
| `layer.setIColorSize` | M11 | handbook | yes | The layer's placed image is rescaled about its bottom-left corner so it keeps unitsPerEm ÷ size units per pixel. Settled by the size change in the observe step. |
| `layer.setIColorImage` | M11 | inferred | yes | Only the master layer's paths set the bounds, not its components. Settled by dropping an image on an iColor layer of a glyph made of components in the reference. |
| `layer.setIColorImage` | M11 | inferred | yes | The image is placed with its bottom-left corner at the bottom-left of the master layer's path bounds (the origin when the master layer has no paths) and scaled to unitsPerEm ÷ size units per pixel, so the iColor size alone sets its scale. Settled by dropping an image on an iColor layer in the reference. |
| `layer.setSvgImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | The SVG's viewBox is placed with its bottom-left corner at the layer origin, one SVG user unit per font unit, y pointing up. Settled by dropping an SVG file on an svg layer in the reference. |
| `attributes.addAttribute` | M11 | handbook | yes | Shadow and Inner Shadow have blur, offset and colour fields, as the file format stores them; Pattern Image has an image reference. Settled by adding each in the reference's inspector. |
| `attributes.addAttribute` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new Shadow or Inner Shadow is black at 50 % opacity with blur 10 and offset (10, −10) font units; Pattern Image keeps only a file reference, in a FontCraft key. Settled by adding each in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `attributes.setStrokeWidth` | M11 | handbook | yes | A stroke added to a path without one is opaque black. Settled by entering a stroke width in the reference's Attributes inspector. |
| `attributes.setStrokeAlignment` | M11 | handbook | yes | The left button is inside and the right button outside, for paths in the usual direction. Settled by choosing each in the reference and reading `strokePos` in the saved file. |
| `attributes.removeStroke` | M11 | handbook | yes | The removed stroke's colour is transparent black. Settled by reading `strokeColor` in a file saved after the minus button. |
| `attributes.removeGradient` | M11 | inferred | yes | The Gradient row has a minus button, like the Stroke and Fill rows, that removes the gradient. Settled by looking at the Gradient row of the reference's Attributes inspector. |
| `attributes.addGradient` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new gradient is linear, from the path's fill colour at offset 0 to the same colour fully transparent at offset 1, running from the left middle to the right middle of the path's bounds. Settled by adding a gradient in the reference. |
| `attributes.addGradient` | M11 | handbook | yes | Conic gradients and the extend mode, which the version 4 file format has and the handbook does not describe, are accepted as parameters; the inspector shows only linear and radial. Settled by looking for them in the reference's inspector. |
| `attributes.addGradient` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new radial gradient is centred in the path's bounds and runs from radius 0 to half the bounds' width; a new conic gradient is centred and turns 360°. Settled by switching a new gradient to radial in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `attributes.setGradientType` | M11 | handbook | yes | Switching the type places the gradient as a new gradient of that type and keeps its stops and extend mode. Settled by switching a moved gradient between linear and radial in the reference. |
| `attributes.addGradientStop` | M11 | handbook | yes | A new stop takes the colour of the gradient at its offset. Settled by clicking the gradient bar in the reference. |
| `attributes.setGradientStopColor` | M11 | handbook | yes | A stop can be moved along the bar, which `offset` does. Settled by dragging a stop knob sideways in the reference. |
| `attributes.setGradientPosition` | M11 | handbook | yes | Without `end`, the end moves by as much as the start, so the gradient moves as a whole. Settled by dragging the start knob of a linear gradient in the reference's Edit View. |
| `filter.glyphAsImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | Glyph as Image PDFs hold outlines, not text: outlines and one-colour fills are vector paths, gradients and the image-only effects are images. Settled by exporting a full-colour glyph as PDF and reading the page's content. |
| `filter.glyphAsImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | It takes the formats and options of `glyph.exportArtwork`, SVG included, and writes one file per glyph into a folder. Settled by opening Filter › Glyph as Image in the reference and noting its formats and options. |
| `filter.glyphAsImage` | M11 | handbook | yes | A palette or full-colour layer draws all the glyph's colour layers of that master, as the compiled font shows the glyph, with black as the text colour; a master layer draws its outline in black. Settled by running it on one palette layer of a layered glyph. |
| `color.fourKinds` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph may mix kinds: its palette and full-colour layers feed COLR, its iColor layers sbix and its svg layer the SVG table, each written by its own rule; an svg layer wins over a conversion by Color Layers to SVG for that glyph. Settled by exporting a glyph with a palette layer and an svg layer from the reference. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Other glyphs are drawn with the colour layers of the current master, in palette 0. Settled by selecting a colour layer in a tab of several colour glyphs in the reference. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The edited glyph is drawn in colour too, all its colour layers of the master in paint order behind the edited outline, its active iColor or svg layer itself when that is the active layer. Settled by editing a palette layer of a glyph with several palette layers in the reference. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Another glyph of the tab shows the colour kind of the active layer when it has it, else palette and full-colour layers, then its svg layer, then its iColor layer of the active layer's size or else its largest. Settled by a tab of glyphs with mixed colour kinds in the reference. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A palette index the palette does not have is drawn in the text colour. Settled by setting a palette layer's index past the last row in the reference. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An svg layer's SVG filters are drawn in Edit View, as the compiled font shows them; a file without filters keeps the plainer drawing of paths and strokes. Settled by selecting an svg layer whose file has a drop shadow in the reference and looking at the canvas. |
| `color.preview.editViewOtherGlyphs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Edit View runs an svg file's filters only on the part of the drawing within the canvas and a 256-pixel margin, so a blur reaching further than the margin fades towards the margin's edge, and says at the canvas's bottom left why a file is drawn without some or all of its filters. Settled by zooming into an svg layer with a large blur in the reference and scrolling it to the canvas edge. |
| `color.preview.previewArea` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The colour glyphs are not inverted by Invert, and the text colour row draws in the preview's text colour. Settled by inverting the Preview Area of a palette font in the reference. |
| `color.preview.previewArea` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A preview showing an instance or all instances draws every glyph in its text colour, since the instance has no selected layer. Settled by choosing an instance in the Preview Area of a palette font in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph in more than one colour table is drawn from COLR, else SVG, else sbix, the order Edit View picks a glyph's colour layers in. Settled by exporting a glyph with palette, svg and iColor layers and looking at it in the reference's Text Preview. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The sbix image is drawn from the smallest strike at least as large as the pixel size, else the largest, with its bottom left at the record's origin offsets from the glyph origin and no side bearing added. Settled by previewing an iColor font with two sizes at several text sizes in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Colour glyphs are drawn with their origin on a whole pixel, so a glyph can sit up to half a pixel from its outline position. Settled by comparing small colour text with plain text in the reference's Text Preview. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A colour glyph larger than 4,096 pixels on a side, or than 2,097,152 pixels, is drawn as its outline. Settled by previewing a colour font at the largest text size in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph with a CBDT image and no other colour is drawn from the CBDT strike nearest the pixel size by the sbix rule, its top left at the image's bearings. Settled only outside the reference, which does not export CBDT; compare with a system text engine on a nanoemoji-built CBDT font. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An SVG document's embedded image that cannot be decoded, or that would take its document past 4,194,304 decoded pixels, is left out and the rest of the document drawn. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph with a damaged embedded PNG in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An SVG mask's luminance is taken from its sRGB values with the weights 0.2125, 0.7154 and 0.0721, without converting to linear RGB first. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph masked by a mid-grey rectangle in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An SVG document fitted to the em square with preserveAspectRatio slice is clipped to the em square unless its root's overflow (its style property, else its attribute) is visible or auto; a document fitted with meet is not clipped. Settled by previewing such a glyph whose content overflows the em in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | SVG filters work on 8-bit premultiplied pixels in linear RGB unless color-interpolation-filters says sRGB; blur deviations and offsets scale by the lengths of the transform's axes, so a rotated element's blur is not rotated, and offsets round to whole pixels. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph with a blur and an offset shadow in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An SVG element whose filters use feImage, a transfer function of more than 1,024 values or a convolution kernel more than 16 cells on a side, hold more than 32 primitives (each feMerge input counting as one), nest inside 4 filtered elements, could paint more than 1,048,576 sprite pixels (what the element draws grown by its blurs, offsets, dilations, convolutions and displacements, or the whole filter region when a flood, tile, turbulence or light paints where its input is clear), or would take the glyph past its work budget is drawn unfiltered with a warning, and the glyph is still drawn in colour; blurs past a deviation of 256 pixels blur by 256, dilations and erosions past a radius of 256 pixels reach 256, and turbulence runs at most 10 octaves. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph with feImage in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | feTurbulence takes the noise at each pixel's top left corner in user space; feConvolveMatrix adds its bias by the Filter Effects formula (the bias to the alpha, the bias times that alpha to each premultiplied colour, or the bias to straight colour with preserveAlpha), ignores kernelUnitLength and reads its kernel cells as sprite pixels; feMorphology radii and feDisplacementMap moves round to whole pixels. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph with each primitive at two text sizes in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | feDisplacementMap converts only its map (in2) to the color-interpolation-filters space and moves the in image's pixels in the space they are in, as the Filter Effects specification says. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph displaced by a gradient map in linearRGB and in sRGB in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A negative diffuseConstant or specularConstant lights nothing, so the lit result is black; the specification calls such a value an error. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph lit with diffuseConstant=-1 in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Lighting takes light positions through the filter's transform, z scaled by the square root of its determinant, uses the Filter Effects specification's Sobel kernels with its edge kernels, clamps specularExponent to 1 to 128 and gives a spot light's cone a hard edge. Settled by previewing an SVG glyph lit by a point and a spot light in the reference. |
| `color.preview.textPreview` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An SVG glyph whose masks and clip paths nest more than 8 layers deep, or that uses more than 4,096 pattern tiles, is drawn as its outline. Settled by previewing such a glyph in the reference. |
| `color.preview.paletteDrivesFontView` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Cells use palette 0 of the master's palettes, as Edit View does. Settled by the palette font with two palettes in Font View in the reference. |
| `color.preview.paletteDrivesFontView` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Of several iColor layers, a cell draws the largest size. Settled by a glyph with iColor 64 and iColor 128 layers in Font View in the reference. |
| `color.preview.paletteDrivesFontView` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | In a layered font, a cell stacks every master that has a Master Color, the first on top, whichever master Font View shows; a master without one shows plain outlines. Settled by the layered font in Font View in the reference. |
| `color.layered.masterColor` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The other glyphs of the tab are filled in the same masters' colours as the edited glyph. Settled by typing several glyphs in a tab of a layered font with all masters shown in the reference. |
| `color.layered.masterColorDark` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The app's dark theme stands for the system's dark appearance. Settled by switching the reference's appearance setting that keeps window content light, and looking at the masters. |
| `color.layered.editLikeMasters` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The masters shown are the active one and those whose layer eye is on in the edited glyph, for every glyph of the tab. Settled by showing a second master on one glyph of a tab in the reference and looking at the other glyphs. |
| `color.layered.toSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The SVG document stacks the masters as Color Layers to COLR does, each filled with its Master Color, opacity included. Settled by exporting the layered test font with Color Layers to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.layered.toColr` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The masters with a Master Color become palette layers of one glyph in master order, the last master at the bottom and the first on top (as Edit View stacks them), each in a palette entry made from its Master Color; the fallback outline is the instance's own master. Settled by exporting the layered test font with Color Layers to COLR from the reference. |
| `color.layered.toColr` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The Master Colors become palette rows after the font's own palettes, the same rows in every palette, numbered in the order the layers first use them; without palettes they are the one palette. Settled by reading CPAL of the layered test font exported from the reference. |
| `color.layered.toColr` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph that has palette or full-colour layers of its own keeps them; only glyphs without such layers are converted from the masters, and a master layer without outlines adds no layer. Settled by exporting a layered font in which one glyph also has palette layers. |
| `color.palette.multiplePalettes` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | CPAL is version 1 only when a palette has a name or a background style or a colour has a name, and version 0 otherwise, as both reference compilers write it; a font the reference exports from a version 4 file with named palettes would settle it. |
| `color.palette.perMaster` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A palette command given `master` edits that master's own Color Palettes, and creates it from the font's palettes when the master has none. Settled by checking whether the reference offers the palette editor on a master's Color Palettes and what it starts with. |
| `color.export.colrFromPaletteLayers` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The first palette layer in the file paints at the bottom, as both oracles write it. Settled by exporting the palette test font from the reference and comparing its layer records with the Layers palette order. |
| `color.export.colorPaletteForCpal` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Every palette is written, with the chosen palette moved to position 0, so the font keeps its alternatives and renders the chosen one by default. Settled by exporting the palette test font with Color Palette for CPAL = 1 from the reference. |
| `color.export.colorPaletteForCpal` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A palette number the font does not have leaves the palettes in their stored order, with an export warning. Settled by exporting the palette test font with Color Palette for CPAL = 5 from the reference. |
| `color.fullColor.layerNaming` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Glyphs 2 files mark full-colour and svg layers by the names `Color` and `svg` alone, as the labels read. Settled by saving a colour font from the reference app as a Glyphs 2 file. |
| `color.fullColor.defaultGrayFill` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The gray is 50 % gray, opaque (#808080FF), written to the path's fill colour when the path is made. Settled by reading the colour picker of a new shape in the reference. |
| `color.fullColor.gradientTypes` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A radial gradient with an end radius of 0 reaches from its centre to the farthest corner of the shape's bounds, which is how both oracles draw a version 3 `circle` gradient; a version 4 file writes such a gradient without `endRadius`. Settled by saving a version 3 radial gradient from the reference app as version 4. |
| `color.fullColor.gradientCanvasKnobs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A linear gradient's knob moves its own end and keeps the other; the radial knob moves the start circle and the end circle with it; a conic gradient has no knob. Settled by dragging each knob in the reference and reading the saved positions. |
| `color.fullColor.gradientCanvasKnobs` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A knob lies above the path's nodes, so a press on a knob drags the gradient even where a node lies under it. Settled by placing a knob on a node in the reference and dragging there. |
| `color.fullColor.imageOnlyEffects` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A Shadow is the path's silhouette (fill and stroke) moved by the offset (font units, y up) and blurred by a Gaussian whose standard deviation is half the blur value, in the shadow colour, drawn just below the path and above the paths before it. Settled by exporting a square with a blur-0 shadow and a square with a blur-20 shadow through Glyph as Image and measuring the PNGs. |
| `color.fullColor.imageOnlyEffects` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An Inner Shadow is the outside of the path moved by the offset and blurred the same way, kept inside the path, over the fill and the pattern and below the stroke. Settled by exporting a square with an inner shadow through Glyph as Image. |
| `color.fullColor.imageOnlyEffects` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A Pattern Image is tiled from the glyph origin at one image pixel per font unit (a PDF by its points), over the path's fill, which shows where the image is transparent; an image that cannot be read is left out with a warning. Settled by exporting a path with a small pattern image through Glyph as Image. |
| `color.fullColor.imageOnlyEffects` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A picture is widened to take in the shadows: the offset plus one and a half times the blur past the silhouette. Settled by exporting a glyph whose shadow reaches past the advance width. |
| `color.fullColor.imageOnlyEffects` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Effects of paths inside a component that refers to another colour glyph are not drawn. Settled by exporting such a component through Glyph as Image. |
| `color.export.fullColorToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | One uncompressed SVG document per glyph whose group has the id `glyph<ID>` and a transform that flips y at the baseline; centred strokes are SVG strokes, inside and outside strokes are expanded to outlines. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font with Color Layers to SVG from the reference and reading the documents. |
| `color.export.fullColorToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A conic gradient, which SVG cannot draw, is written in the colour of its first stop, with a warning. Settled by exporting a conic gradient with Color Layers to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.export.fullColorToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph whose document cannot be made (a palette row beyond the palette, a shape that cannot be drawn) is left out of the SVG table with a warning and keeps its master outline. Settled by exporting a palette layer whose index is beyond the palette with Color Layers to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.export.fullColorToColrV1` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A full-colour Color layer exports as COLR version 1 without any parameter, as both oracles do; the handbook names only SVG for these layers. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font from the reference with no parameters. |
| `color.export.fullColorToColrV1` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A path on a full-colour layer with neither a fill colour nor a gradient, and not a stroke alone, is painted 50 % gray, the colour a new shape gets, and a fill or stroke colour with zero opacity paints nothing; fontc refuses such a path and fontmake strokes it 1 unit wide. Settled by exporting a full-colour path without paint attributes from the reference. |
| `color.export.colrGlyphComponent` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | The reference app writes a component of a colour glyph as a PaintColrGlyph of that glyph, as glyphsLib does, rather than as its outline. Settled by exporting a full-colour glyph holding a moved component of another full-colour glyph from the reference and reading its COLR. |
| `color.export.colrGlyphComponent` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Shapes keep their order on the layer: glyphsLib puts a layer's paths below its components, FontCraft keeps the order of the shapes, as fontc does for master layers. Settled by exporting a full-colour layer with a component below a path from the reference. |
| `color.export.colrGlyphComponent` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A component whose transform differs between masters is written with the default master's transform; fontmake would write a variable transform. Settled by a variable full-colour font with a moved colour component in the reference. |
| `color.export.colrGlyphComponent` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A component of a colour glyph that Remove Glyphs or Keep Glyphs leaves out is drawn as its outline in its own layer glyph, with a warning, rather than stopping the export. Settled by exporting such a font from the reference with the referenced glyph removed. |
| `color.export.colrGlyphComponent` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Color Layers to SVG writes a component of a colour glyph as a copy of that glyph's drawing moved by the component's transform, as the COLR reference draws it. Settled by exporting the same font with Color Layers to SVG from the reference and reading the SVG document of the referring glyph. |
| `color.export.colrGlyphComponent` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Copying referenced drawings into one SVG document stops after 65 536 paints, the limit Edit View draws with; the references met after that are drawn as their component's outline in grey, with a warning, rather than leaving the glyph without a document. Settled by exporting from the reference a glyph whose colour components nest and fan out to many thousands of copies with Color Layers to SVG. |
| `color.export.colrVersionChoice` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | Solid-only full-colour layers export as COLR version 0, as fontc does, not version 1 as fontmake does. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font with only solid fills from the reference. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | Gradient geometry and stop colours vary when they differ between masters, as fontmake writes them. Settled by exporting the two-master palette font and a two-master gradient font as variable fonts from the reference. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A master whose colour layers differ from the default master's in kind, count, palette rows, colours of their own or stop counts leaves the glyph's paints static towards that master, with a warning, and its layer glyph outlines take the default master's there; fontmake refuses such a font. Settled by exporting a variable font from the reference with one master missing a colour layer. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A layer glyph whose layers are not compatible between the masters keeps the default master's outline everywhere, with a warning. Settled by exporting a variable font from the reference whose colour layers differ in point count between the masters. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | A variable font is written without sbix, SVG and CBDT tables, with a warning. Settled by exporting a two-master iColor font and a two-master svg-layer font as variable fonts from the reference. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | An interpolated static instance takes the origin master's iColor and svg layers, with a warning naming the master. Settled by exporting an instance between the masters of a two-master iColor font from the reference. |
| `color.export.variableColr` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | The paint attributes of an interpolated instance's colour layers (colours, gradient positions and stops) are mixed with the master weights when every master with a weight has the layer; otherwise they are the heaviest master's. Settled by exporting an instance halfway between two masters whose gradient positions differ. |
| `color.export.strokeInColr` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A stroke becomes its own layer glyph painted in the stroke colour, above the path's fill, expanded with the path's stroke position, caps and joins. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font from the reference. |
| `color.sbix.bitmapFormats` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | PNG and JPEG data are stored as read, with the graphic types `png ` and `jpg `; TIFF is converted to PNG. Settled by exporting the sbix test font from the reference and reading the graphic types. |
| `color.sbix.imagePerSize` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | When a glyph has two iColor layers of one size for a master, the first in the file is exported and the second is reported in a warning. Settled by exporting a glyph with two iColor 64 layers from the reference. |
| `color.sbix.sameAspectRatio` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Unequal ratios are exported as they are, with a warning from export and from `color.describeExport`. Settled by exporting the non-square image in the sbix test font from the reference. |
| `color.sbix.sameAspectRatio` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Two images count as the same ratio when the smaller one's width is within one pixel of the larger one's ratio at the smaller one's height. Settled by exporting images that differ by one pixel from the reference. |
| `color.sbix.iColorNaming` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Glyphs 2 files mark sbix layers by the name `iColor n` alone, as the label reads. Settled by opening a Glyphs 2 sbix file from the reference app. |
| `color.sbix.sizeScalesImage` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | An image scaled, rotated or flipped on its iColor layer is exported at its pixel size, with a warning, and its box's bottom-left corner gives the origin offsets. Settled by scaling an image on an iColor layer in the reference, exporting, and reading sbix. |
| `color.sbix.sizeScalesImage` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Edit View draws an iColor layer's image opaque, as the glyph it is, not at a tracing image's 50 % opacity. Settled by selecting an iColor layer in the reference and comparing the image with the file. |
| `color.layers.widthFollowsMaster` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Palette and full-colour layers follow the master's width as iColor and svg layers do. Settled by changing a master width in the reference with a palette layer present. |
| `color.export.sbixAutomatic` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A glyph whose image file is missing at export is left out of that strike, with a warning naming the glyph and the file. Settled by exporting the sbix test font from the reference with one file moved away. |
| `color.export.sbixAutomatic` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The origin offsets are the bottom-left corner of the image's box on the iColor layer, in strike pixels from the glyph origin, rounded; the advance width comes from the master layer. Settled by reading sbix from the reference export of a glyph whose image was moved. |
| `color.export.sbixAutomatic` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The whole image file is written; a crop set on an iColor layer's image is not applied. Settled by cropping an image on an iColor layer in the reference and exporting. |
| `color.export.sbixToSvg` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | Each bitmap becomes an `<image>` element with embedded PNG data, placed where the image sits on its iColor layer. Settled by exporting the sbix test font with SBIX to SVG from the reference. |
| `color.export.cbdtNotInReference` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Each iColor size is one CBLC strike holding one index subtable per run of consecutive glyph ids, where nanoemoji writes a separate strike per run; a renderer test with glyphs that are not consecutive would settle which renderers draw. |
| `color.export.cbdtNotInReference` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A strike's line metrics span its bitmaps (the highest top as ascender, the lowest bottom as descender, the widest advance as widthMax), where nanoemoji derives them from the font's ascender and descender; comparing line spacing on Android at the strike size would settle it. |
| `color.export.cbdtNotInReference` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | JPEG and TIFF images are converted to PNG, because glyph bitmap format 17 holds PNG only; nanoemoji makes its bitmaps from SVG and never meets them. |
| `color.export.cbdtNotInReference` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | CBDT export gives no warning when one glyph's images differ in width ÷ height, since each CBDT strike is drawn on its own and the handbook's aspect rule is about sbix; nothing in a reference decides this. |
| `color.svg.layerHoldsFile` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | A compressed SVG file (svgz), a file with a link outside itself (an image file, another SVG, a style sheet), or one nested more than 256 elements deep is refused with a warning, and the glyph has no SVG document. Settled by dropping such files on an svg layer in the reference and exporting. |
| `color.svg.imageTransform` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The placed SVG's transform is written as a group transform inside the glyph's document, combined with the y flip at the baseline; the SVG's own content is kept as usvg normalises it. Settled by exporting the SVG test font with a moved and scaled svg layer from the reference. |
| `color.svg.imageTransform` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The SVG file's bottom left sits at the image origin on the layer, one SVG user unit per font unit before the image transform, and content outside the file's width and height is not clipped. Settled by placing a file with content outside its view box on an svg layer in the reference and reading the exported document. |
| `color.export.exportSvgTable` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The SVG table is written whenever svg layers exist or a conversion asks for it, without Export SVG Table, as the appendix says; the parameter set off removes it. Settled by exporting the SVG test font from the reference with no parameters. |
| `color.export.exportSvgTable` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | When no glyph's SVG document can be made, the font gets no SVG table, with a warning, rather than an empty one. Settled by exporting from the reference a font whose only svg layer names a missing file. |
| `color.export.perInstance` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | The switches are read from the font's parameters, then the master's, then the instance's, the later winning, as every other export parameter is read. Settled by setting Export COLR Table off on the font and on in an instance in the reference and exporting that instance. |
| `color.export.glyphFilterTokens` (behavior) | M11 | handbook | – | `isAnyColorGlyph` also holds for a glyph whose only colour layer is an svg layer, which the handbook's list (COLR/CPAL, sbix, full colour) leaves out. Settled by exporting a font with Remove Glyphs `isAnyColorGlyph=1` and a glyph that has only an svg layer. |
| `color.open.compiledColorTables` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A CPAL version 1 table with palette types or labels opens as the version 4 Color Palettes dictionary, the only form that holds them, even in a document that is later saved in another format. Settled by opening, in the reference, a font whose CPAL has palette types and labels and reading the saved parameter. |
| `color.open.compiledColorTables` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Opening a compiled colour font recovers palettes and vector colour layers and reports bitmap and SVG glyphs as lost. Settled by opening a COLR font in the reference. |
| `color.open.compiledColorTables` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | A COLR version 1 glyph whose paints are all opaque solid palette colours opens as palette layers; any other version 1 glyph opens as one full-colour layer. Settled by opening a COLR version 1 font with solid and gradient glyphs in the reference. |
| `color.open.compiledColorTables` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Colours of full-colour layers open as palette references, so palette rows an export added for RGBA colours stay rows of every palette. Settled by opening, in the reference, a COLR version 1 font exported from full-colour layers with RGBA fills. |
| `color.open.compiledColorTables` (behavior) | M11 | inferred | – | Gradient positions open as fractions of the curve bounds of the layer glyph's outline. Settled by reopening a font exported from a full-colour layer with a gradient and comparing the gradient knobs. |
| `dialog.colorPalettes` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | A click on a row's number selects the row that minus removes, and a click on a palette's heading opens a menu with Remove Palette. Settled by removing a row and a palette in the reference's editor. |
| `palette.layers.colorLayerEntry` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | Double-clicking the bold kind label of an iColor row edits its size, while double-clicking the name still renames the layer. Settled by double-clicking an iColor entry in the reference. |
| `palette.layers.paletteColorChooser` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | The chooser and the Edit View preview always use palette 0. Settled by opening the chooser in a font with two palettes in the reference. |
| `inspector.attributes` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | The stroke alignment buttons read Centre, Left and Right, and shadows show as read-only lines (blur and offset) because no command removes them yet. Settled by adding a shadow in the reference's inspector. |
| `inspector.attributes` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | With several paths selected the bar shows the first path's gradient and the other controls show a value only when every path has it. Settled by selecting two paths with different gradients in the reference. |
| `inspector.attributes.gradientBar` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | A knob released more than two bar heights above or below the bar removes its stop. Settled by dragging a stop away in the reference and noting the distance. |
| `dialog.masterColorPicker` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | The colour is stored as the text `R,G,B,A` with components from 0 to 255 (the picker also reads `#RRGGBBAA` and a list of numbers). Settled by saving a font with a Master Color from the reference and reading the stored value. |
| `dialog.masterColorPicker` (panel) | M11 | handbook | – | Opacity is the alpha slider of the colour picker. Settled by opening the picker in the reference. |

## `16-settings.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `app.settings` | M14 | handbook | yes | Settings… is the last item of the Edit menu on every platform, because FontCraft draws its own menu bar and has no application menu; the reference puts it in the application menu on macOS. |
| `app.settings` | M14 | handbook | yes | The window reopens on the section last shown; the handbook does not say which section it opens on. |
| `settings.get` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own command; the reference has no headless access to settings, so there is nothing to settle. |
| `settings.set` | M4 | inferred | yes | Our own command; the reference has no headless access to settings, so there is nothing to settle. |
| `sharing.answerDevice` | M13 | inferred | yes | A refused device is turned away until the preview is turned off; it is not remembered across launches. |
| `settings.menuItemNameOnOlderSystem` (behavior) | M14 | handbook | – | One label and place on every platform, Settings… in the Edit menu, as many Linux and Windows applications do; a native macOS menu bar would move it to the application menu. |
| `sharing.confirmConnectionOnHost` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | Devices are known by their IP address, so a device that gets a new address must be confirmed again; what the reference keys them by is not documented. |
| `sharing.viewerShowsActiveEditView` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | The page reloads every second. |
| `sharing.viewerShowsActiveEditView` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | The tab is drawn filled on one line, as the preview line draws it, not with nodes, metrics or line breaks. |
| `sharing.viewerShowsActiveEditView` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | The viewer follows tab switches. |
| `addons.repo.httpBasicAuth` (behavior) | M13 | handbook | – | Credentials are taken from the URL itself and stored with the setting, not in the operating system's keychain; the reference's storage is not described. |
| `window.settings` (panel) | M14 | handbook | – | Sections that are not built yet stay in the switcher, greyed with the reason, so the order matches the reference; the reference has no such state. |
| `settings.userSettings` (panel) | M14 | handbook | – | Every checkbox is off by default; the handbook gives no defaults. |
| `settings.sharing` (panel) | M13 | handbook | – | The section shows the page's address, because there is no companion app that finds the computer by itself. |
| `settings.sharing` (panel) | M13 | handbook | – | Reset shows while at least one device is trusted. |
| `settings.addons` (panel) | M13 | handbook | – | The section is called Extensions, because FontCraft has no add-on kinds that need Python; the reference calls it Addons. |
| `appearance.displayUnicodeValue` (setting) | M4 | handbook | – | On shows the code point, off the small marker, as the handbook's wording implies. Settled by observation. |
| `appearance.displayUnicodeValue` (setting) | M4 | handbook | – | Off by default (the small marker), as the handbook gives no default. Settled by observing a fresh install. |
| `appearance.handleSize` (setting) | M14 | handbook | – | Three steps, small, regular and large, at 0.75, 1 and 1.5 times the regular sizes; the handbook lists no steps. Settled by opening the reference's Appearance settings and measuring a node at each step. |
| `appearance.handleSize` (setting) | M14 | handbook | – | The appearance colour settings default to the canvas colours FontCraft drew before they existed, the same value for light and dark; the handbook states no defaults. Settled by reading the reference's colour wells after Standard. |
| `user.disableLocalization` (setting) | M14 | handbook | – | Disable Localization is stored and does nothing, because FontCraft has no translations yet. |
| `user.useVersions` (setting) | M14 | handbook | – | Use Versions is stored and does nothing, because the system's version browser is a macOS service FontCraft does not use. |
| `user.sendCrashReports` (setting) | M14 | handbook | – | Send Crash Reports and its two contact fields are stored and do nothing, because FontCraft has no crash-report service. |
| `sampleStrings.groups` (setting) | M5 | handbook | – | The built-in default holds groups of our own choosing: spacing strings built around n, o, H and O with placeholders, figures, and traditional pangrams for Latin, Greek and Cyrillic; no source lists the reference defaults we may copy. |
| `sharing.enableExternalPreview` (setting) | M13 | handbook | – | Off by default; the handbook does not say. |
| `sharing.enableExternalPreview` (setting) | M13 | handbook | – | The port and the random path are new each time the preview is turned on. |
| `addons.useSystemConsoleForScriptOutput` (setting) | M13 | handbook | – | With the setting on, the log still shows in the Command Console as well as on standard error, where the handbook says the reference sends it to the system console instead; keeping both loses nothing and the switch stays off by default. |
| `addons.alternatePluginRepos` (setting) | M13 | handbook | – | The per-index automatic-updates switch is kept in the same text as a `manual ` prefix, so the list stays one setting; the reference has no such switch. |
| `export.ttfAutohintBinary` (setting) | M10 | inferred | – | A document may only ask for the user's trusted ttfautohint program, unlike the reference app, which runs the program the parameter names; we chose this for safety because a document from elsewhere could otherwise run any program on the user's machine. |

## `17-extensions.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `window.pluginManager` | M13 | inferred | yes | The window and its menu item are called Extension Manager, since it lists modules and macros rather than plug-ins; the reference calls it Plugin Manager. |
| `pluginManager.filterByInstallState` | M13 | inferred | yes | The catalogue opens showing every extension (All), and the choice is not remembered across launches; watching the reference app open the Plugin Manager would settle both. |
| `pluginManager.filterByType` | M13 | inferred | yes | The type buttons pick one type at a time, with an All button to show every type; the handbook names the buttons but not whether several can be on together or how all types are shown again. |
| `pluginManager.search` | M13 | inferred | yes | The search ignores case and matches inside words, and also matches the id; the reference's matching is not documented. |
| `pluginManager.install` | M13 | inferred | yes | An install from an index entry whose checksum does not match the downloaded bytes is refused and nothing is written; the reference's handling of a failed download is not documented. |
| `pluginManager.install` | M13 | inferred | yes | A macro installed from an index is saved in the Macros folder as `<id>.fcmacro`, so the listing and Remove find it by the entry's id; the reference's scripts keep their repository's file names. |
| `pluginManager.remove` | M13 | inferred | yes | A removed module or macro file is moved into a `Removed` folder beside the Modules and Macros folders rather than deleted, so a removal can be undone by hand; the reference's Remove is not documented in that detail. |
| `pluginManager.enlargePreview` | M13 | inferred | yes | The enlarged screenshot opens in its own window and closes from that window or by running the command without an id; the reference app's full-size view would settle where it appears and how it closes. |
| `pluginManager.openWebsite` | M13 | inferred | yes | The Website button shows on every index entry and the command fails with a message when the entry has no `homepage`; the reference app with an entry that lacks a website would settle whether the link is hidden instead. |
| `pluginManager.openDonationPage` | M13 | inferred | yes | One Donate button stands for every donation service, opening the entry's single `funding` link; an index entry in the reference format that lists several services would settle whether one button is enough. |
| `script.run` | M13 | inferred | yes | A macro runs as one undo step that is undone as a whole, like command.batch; the reference undoes script edits per glyph and does not say how a script's changes group. |
| `script.run` | M13 | inferred | yes | One top-level macro run executes at most 1,000,000 steps, the steps of the macros it runs included, and reads at most 256 MiB of macro files; past either it fails as a whole. Our own limit; no source describes one for scripts in the reference. |
| `script.rerunLast` | M13 | inferred | yes | The label of the item at the foot of the Script menu is Re-run Last Script followed by the script's title; the handbook gives no label. |
| `script.openScriptsFolder` | M13 | inferred | yes | The macros folder is created when missing and its path returned, and sessions without one (headless runs, the web app) have the command disabled; the reference app's behaviour when the Scripts folder is missing would settle the first part. |
| `script.reloadScripts` | M13 | inferred | yes | It is always shown in the Script menu and returns the new list, since the folder is also watched; files that cannot be read stay listed with their error. Holding Option over the reference app's Script menu settles only where the item shows. |
| `window.macroPanel` | M13 | inferred | yes | A run-style line with several commands runs them as one transaction and one undo step, and may name engine commands only, so a failing line changes nothing; the command-line tool runs them one by one and simply does not save after a failure. |
| `window.macroPanel` | M13 | inferred | yes | Clear empties the console's pane but keeps the session's command log, which other readers use; the reference's clear behaviour is not described. |
| `window.macroPanel` | M13 | inferred | yes | The history lives for the session only and is not saved between launches; watching whether the reference keeps typed text after a relaunch would settle it. |
| `plugin.installFromFile` | M13 | inferred | yes | A module keeps the file name it had (or `<id>.wasm`); installing the same module again only turns it on, and a different module with an installed id needs `replace`; the reference's handling of a second copy is not documented. |
| `plugin.runFilter` | M13 | inferred | yes | Without `layer` or `layers` the module runs on every master layer of each glyph; a locked glyph among `glyphs` refuses the whole run rather than being skipped. |
| `plugin.runFilter` | M13 | inferred | yes | With `selectionOnly`, only the paths that hold a selected node take the module's result, and the module must return the layer's shapes in their order; the reference leaves selection handling to each plug-in. |
| `view.showReporter` | M13 | inferred | yes | Reporter entries are listed at the foot of the View menu in the order of their names, below a separator; the handbook does not give the order. |
| `view.showReporter` | M13 | inferred | yes | Reporters draw only the glyph being edited (with `active` true), not the other glyphs of the Edit View text; observing a reporter plug-in on a line of several glyphs would settle it. |
| `view.showReporter` | M13 | inferred | yes | A reporter that fails is not run again until its layer, scale or module changes, and its error is written at the top left of the canvas; the reference's handling of a failing reporter is not documented. |
| `view.showReporter` | M13 | inferred | yes | Reporters run with their default parameters; there is no form for reporter parameters. |
| `script.recordMacro` | M13 | inferred | yes | A recording also stops taking steps once their parameters reach 64 MiB as JSON, and says so as for the step limit. Our own limit: the reference has no recorder. |
| `pluginManager.scriptsLoadWithoutRelaunch` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Folders are checked by comparing file names, sizes and modification times when the window regains focus and every 5 seconds, rather than with an operating-system file watcher, so the same code works on every platform; a reference behaviour is not involved. |
| `pluginManager.automaticUpdates` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Automatic updates can be turned off per index, and are then offered with an Update button; the reference installs them automatically and does not say whether that can be turned off (open question in docs/spec/17-extensions.md). |
| `pluginManager.automaticUpdates` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Any version other than the installed one counts as an update, so an index can also roll a module back; the reference's version comparison is not documented. |
| `pluginManager.repositories` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | When two indexes list the same id, the index listed first in the settings wins; the reference does not say how it merges entries. |
| `script.menu.mirrorsFolder` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Script menu items are sorted by title, case-insensitively and with numbers in numeric order, folders before items; the handbook does not give the order. |
| `script.file.pyExtension` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Macro files end in `.fcmacro` and hold JSON; nothing in the reference decides it (the format is ours, open decision D8). |
| `script.importExistingPython` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | The repository ships no Python client package, only documented examples, because the code base is Rust only; nothing in the reference decides it. |
| `plugin.kind.palette` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A filter module's Filter menu item runs it at once with its default parameters; other values are given in its Palette section or by agents in plugin.runFilter's `params`. |
| `plugin.pluginsFolder` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | The folders are `Macros` and `Modules` in FontCraft's settings folder (the per-user application data folder); the reference's folder names and location are not given in the handbook (open question). |
| `plugin.noManualMove` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Each module file's switch is kept in a hidden `.switches.json` in the Modules folder; a manual copy is recorded as off the first time it is seen, so at the next launch it is off but no longer marked new. |
| `plugin.kind.check` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Module checks run under every profile that runs layer checks (outlines, universal, strict, and custom profiles with outline checks) and not under opentype; user feedback on which profiles should include them would settle it. |
| `plugin.kind.check` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | A module that fails on a layer is listed once as `check.plugin.<module id>` with status `error` and is not run on the remaining layers of that audit run, so a slow or broken module costs one time-out per run; user feedback would settle it. |
| `plugin.kind.check` (behavior) | M13 | inferred | – | Check modules run with their manifest's parameter defaults, since audit.run has no way to set module parameters; a request for per-run module parameters would settle it. |
| `window.pluginManager` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | Screenshots are shown only when the entry's Preview button is pressed, not in the list, so opening the window downloads no images; the reference shows preview images in the list. |
| `window.pluginManager` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | Entries are listed in index order (first index first), then installed modules no index lists, then macros of the Macros folder; the reference's order is not documented. |
| `window.pluginManager` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | The type buttons are Filters, Reporters, Checks and Macros in place of Plugins, Scripts and Modules, because FontCraft has no Python modules; settled by nothing in the reference (our own extension model, decision 0005). |
| `window.macroPanel` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | The console's input is one line with a history, not a multi-line editor, because commands are one line of JSON; the reference's panel layout is not described (open question). |
| `window.macroPanel` (panel) | M13 | inferred | – | Tab takes the first completion suggestion and Up and Down stay with the history, because the history is the row's documented use of those keys; nothing describes completion keys in the reference. |

## `18-custom-parameters.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `customParameters.shortAndLongNames` (behavior) | M7 | handbook | – | A parameter added under another spelling is stored under the catalogue's spelling (the short name), and set and remove find rows of either spelling; the saved file of the observe step settles which spelling the reference app keeps. |
| `customParameters.unknownKept` (behavior) | M7 | inferred | – | Unknown parameters are shown and kept, not hidden or dropped; opening a file with an unknown parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Add Class` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | A leading @ on the class name is dropped, and the white space after the semicolon is not part of the code (glyphsLib's split); exporting with the parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Add Prefix` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | A prefix name may hold spaces, as prefix names in the Features tab are free text; adding `Language Systems; languagesystem DFLT dflt;` in the reference app settles it. |
| `blueShift` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | Negative and fractional values are refused, as the handbook gives an integer; typing -1 in the reference app settles it. |
| `CJK Grid` (parameter) | M3 | handbook | – | A grid has 1 to 100 rows or columns; the handbook gives no range. |
| `CJK Guide` (parameter) | M3 | handbook | – | An inset of 50 percent or more, which leaves no square, is refused; the handbook gives no range. |
| `codePageRanges` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Any other number is refused; the reference app's code page dialog shows which values it stores. |
| `Color Palette for CPAL` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | An index past the palettes warns and keeps the value, since the palettes may be added later; setting 5 on a font with two palettes in the reference app settles it. |
| `Color Palettes` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | A version 4 Color Palettes dictionary saved as format version 2 or 3 keeps only its colours, as lists, rather than being written as a dictionary those versions do not document. Settled by saving a version 4 file with named palettes as version 3 in the reference and reading the parameter. |
| `Color Palettes` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | An edited Color Palettes keeps the form it was stored in; a new one is written as version 3 integer lists, or as the version 4 dictionary in a document read from version 4. Settled by editing the palettes in the reference and reading the saved file. |
| `Color Palettes` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | A CMYK palette colour is converted to RGB without a colour profile, as (1 − c)(1 − k) and so on. Settled by exporting a CMYK palette from the reference and reading CPAL. |
| `Compatible name Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | When on, every name record is also written on the Macintosh platform (Roman encoding, English), with name ID 1 holding the family and style name together and ID 2 the style name, the layout older Macintosh applications group by; exporting with the parameter in the reference app and reading the name table settles it. |
| `Compatible name Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Compatible name Table writes its Macintosh records even when Export Mac Name Table Entries is absent, and none when that parameter is present and off; both parameters together in the reference app settle it. |
| `compatibleFullNames` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Without the field no name ID 18 is written, as both oracles do, though the handbook says the reference derives it from family and style name; an export in the reference app settles whether it writes ID 18 by default. |
| `Decompose Components` (parameter) | M6 | handbook | – | A decomposed glyph keeps its own contours first, then its components' contours in the order the compiler decomposes a glyph that mixes contours and components (fontc's order); a composite exported by the reference app with the parameter on would settle it. |
| `Decompose Glyphs` (parameter) | M6 | handbook | – | A text value is read as glyph names separated by commas or spaces, besides the documented list; the reference app with a text value would settle it. |
| `Default Layer Width` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | Entries are looked up in the master's parameters before the font's, as for the other per-script parameters; the reference app with the parameter on a master would settle it. |
| `Default Layer Width` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | A value that is not a number from 0 to 65535 is ignored; the reference app with such a value would settle it. |
| `Default Layer Width` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | The CJK scripts are han, hangul, kana and bopomofo; a new layer of a glyph of each script in the reference app would settle it. |
| `Elidable STAT Axis Value Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Only an axis tag is accepted when the parameter is added or set, as the handbook describes the value; a style name such as `Regular`, which some sources hold, is refused. Typing `Regular` as the value in the reference app settles whether it accepts a name. |
| `Export Folder` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Nested folders separated by `/` are accepted and a path that leaves the export folder is refused; typing `a/b` and `../a` in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Export Glyphs accepts the names, wildcards and queries of Remove Glyphs and applies after Rename Glyphs, to the names glyphs are exported under; exporting a renamed glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export kern Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The table is version 0 with one format 0 subtable per run of at most 10,920 pairs (the subtable's 16-bit length), class pairs expanded to every member pair, exceptions overriding, zero pairs left out, only the default (horizontal) kerning of the exported master; pairs past 65,535 in total are dropped with a warning; an export with the parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export kern Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | When the table is full, the pairs kept are those found first in lookup order (glyph–glyph exceptions, then glyph–group, group–glyph, group–group), so exceptions survive; an export of a font past the limit in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export kern Table` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A CFF export with the parameter on writes no kern table and reports it in a warning; an OTF export with the parameter in the reference app settles it. |
| `Export Mac Name Table Entries` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Macintosh records are written only for name IDs whose text the Mac Roman encoding can hold; an export from the reference app read with ttx settles which IDs it writes. |
| `Export STAT Table` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The parameter is read from the variable font setting first and from the font when the setting has none; exporting a font with it only in Font Info settles whether the reference reads it there. |
| `Feature for Feature Variations` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | A value that is not a feature tag puts the substitutions in `rvrn` with a warning, as fontc does; exporting with such a value from the reference app would settle it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Locked glyphs and locked paths are filtered at export like any other, since a lock guards editing; a Filter on a locked glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Before decomposing, each composite takes the anchors its components pass on, so mark attachment is kept; reading the mark feature of a filtered export from the reference app settles it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The filter runs on every glyph of the instance with its components decomposed first, so a TrueType export of an instance with a Filter has no composites; a Transformations Filter on an instance with a composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `Filter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A Filter on a master applies to every export of that master; on the font it applies to every export, which fontmake ignores but no source forbids; the reference app's picker settles where it may be added. |
| `fsType` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Only one of the usage levels 1 (restricted), 2 (preview and print) and 3 (editable) may be set, as the handbook says of restricted and the OS/2 specification of all three; ticking two levels in the reference app's dialog settles it. |
| `fsType` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Bit 9 (bitmap embedding only) is offered though the handbook names only installable, restricted, editable, preview and print, and no subsetting; the reference app's dialog settles it. |
| `gasp Table` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | Flags above 15, which the table does not define, are refused rather than dropped at export; the reference app's gasp editor settles it. |
| `Ignore Vertical Hints` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | A horizontal Stem instruction set to No Stem stays under Ignore Vertical Hints, since it is linked to no stem definition; the reference app would settle it. |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The path is relative to the host file's folder unless absolute; the imported glyphs are read when the host opens and before each export, shown locked, never saved into the host; a missing or unreadable file is a warning on opening and at export, not an error; the reference app settles the path form and the missing-file case. |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A linked glyph's layers go to the host master with the same id, else the one at the same position, and layers of other masters are left out; the linked file's kerning, features and groups are not imported; the reference app settles both with font B (m7-two) linked into font A (m7-one). |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A linked file with other units per em than the host is used unscaled, with a warning; linking a 2048-unit file into a 1000-unit font in the reference app settles it. |
| `Import Font` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The linked glyphs are locked, so Scale Font to Units per Em and adding, duplicating or removing a master are refused while any are present, naming the locked glyph; the reference app settles whether it scales or extends linked glyphs instead. |
| `Import Master` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The value is the other file's path, a semicolon and the master's name or id, or a dictionary with `Path` and `Master` (the form Import Font's samples suggest), resolved as Import Font resolves paths; with no master named, the file's first master; the reference app settles the value syntax. |
| `Import Master` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The linked master keeps its id unless the host uses it (then it gets a fresh one), takes its axis values by axis tag, its metric and stem values by definition, its layers for every glyph the host has and its kerning; its layers can be edited but are not saved into the host; the reference app settles whether it is read-only. |
| `Instance Preview` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The value lists glyph names separated by spaces or line breaks, at most 64 drawn; names the font lacks are skipped; a disabled parameter is ignored; the reference app settles separators and missing names. |
| `InterpolationWeightY` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Applied to the axis tagged wght (ignored without one) and to y coordinates of outlines, component offsets, anchors, vertical metrics, horizontal stems and alignment zones; settle with an instance that sets it in the reference app. |
| `Italic Style Linking` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | On links the value at 0 on the ital axis (the font's own or the table-only one) to 1 when it has no link; off removes every link on that axis; read from the variable font setting, else from the font. Exporting an upright and an italic family with the parameter on and off and reading STAT settles it. |
| `italicAngle` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Angles of 90 degrees or more either way are refused; the handbook gives no range. |
| `Keep Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | When both Keep Glyphs and Remove Glyphs are present, Keep Glyphs applies first and Remove Glyphs then removes from what is kept, with a warning; the reference app settles it. |
| `Keep Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | .notdef and space are kept whatever the list says; the reference app settles it. |
| `Keep UI-Font Bounding Box` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | The parameter has no effect because the generated cjct never stacks conjuncts; Update in the reference app on an Oriya font with and without the parameter would show what it changes. |
| `Link Metrics With First Master` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | Spacing and kerning commands refuse to edit a linked master and name the master it follows; no source says what the reference does with such an edit. |
| `Local Interpolation` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | `Include:` or `include :` is refused, since the interpolation would not find the glyph list; typing them in the reference app settles whether it reads them. |
| `Local Interpolation` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The listed glyphs are interpolated at the given location with every other part of the instance unchanged, including their widths; settle with an instance that sets it in the reference app. |
| `MakeOTF Compatibility Mode` (parameter) | M12 | handbook | – | The parameter changes only how substitutions are grouped into lookups, the one layout rule the project compile has of its own; exporting feature code with mixed single and ligature substitutions in the reference app with it on and off would settle what else it changes. |
| `MakeOTF Compatibility Mode` (parameter) | M12 | handbook | – | Pair kerning classes keep the ordinary numbering (the largest first class in class 0), where makeotf numbers classes in the order the code names them and puts the first one in class 0; comparing the reference app's class kerning with the parameter on and off would settle whether it follows makeotf there. |
| `Master Background Color` (parameter) | M14 | handbook | – | The background and stroke colours take Master Color's forms (`R,G,B[,A]` or `#RRGGBB[AA]`); the handbook gives no syntax for them, and a value saved by the reference app settles it. |
| `Optical Size` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | The values are whole decipoints from 0 to 65535 and the code is a `size` feature added at the end of the list, replacing the code of a `size` feature the font already has; exporting with the parameter in the reference app and reading GPOS settles it. |
| `Optical Size` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | The menu name is written for Windows (English) and, when it is plain ASCII, for the Mac (Roman, English); a subfamily of 0 writes no range and no name, as the feature file specification asks; the exported name table of the reference app settles it. |
| `Optimize Variable Deltas` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The handbook drops the deltas of a contour whose nodes all move half a unit or less; FontCraft drops the deltas IUP can infer within half a unit, as both oracles do. A variable font exported by the reference app with the parameter on and off would settle it. |
| `panose` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Digits above 15 are refused, following the handbook's sixteen variations per category; a value of 16 typed into a copy of a file and opened in the reference app settles it. |
