| `Point To Unit Ratio` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The copied SVG gives coordinates in points, font units divided by the ratio, and declares its width and height in pt; without the parameter the SVG keeps one font unit per user unit with no unit on its size; copying into a drawing application from the reference app settles the scale. |
| `Point To Unit Ratio` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The master's parameter wins over the font's, and a ratio that is not a number from 0.001 to 1,000,000 is ignored; copying from a font with the parameter on a master in the reference app settles it. |
| `Point To Unit Ratio` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Only the SVG flavour is scaled: the clipboard fragment that edit.paste reads keeps font units, so copy and paste inside the app keeps the size; pasting a drawing copied from the reference app back into it settles what its own paste does. |
| `Post Table Type` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Only 2 and 3 are accepted, the two versions the handbook names; versions 1, 2.5 and 4 are refused. Typing 4 in the reference app settles it. |
| `postscriptFontName` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A name over 63 bytes is refused (the OpenType limit) while the handbook only recommends under 30 characters; a 40-character name in the reference app settles whether it warns. |
| `PreFilter` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | On a variable font setting PreFilter is skipped with a warning, not an error; the reference app settles it. |
| `Prevent Name ID` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Name IDs 1, 2, 3, 5 and 6 can be prevented like any other, with a warning that the font may not install; preventing ID 1 in the reference app settles whether it refuses. |
| `Prevent Name ID` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The parameters of the font, the master and the instance add up, as Name Table Entry does; an instance with one ID and a font with another, exported from the reference app, settles it. |
| `Preview Descender` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | The default reads winDescent, not the winAscent the handbook names, because that text reads as a misprint; the reference app with a font whose winAscent and winDescent differ settles it. |
| `Preview Descender` (parameter) | M5 | handbook | – | A value of either sign is a distance below the baseline; the reference app with a positive and a negative value settles it. |
| `Reencode Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Reencode Glyphs applies after Rename Glyphs, to the names glyphs are exported under, and when two lines give the same code point the later line wins; the reference app settles both. |
| `Remove Features` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | Besides a list, one text with the names separated by commas or line breaks is accepted, and every entry of a listed name is left out; the reference app's parameter editor settles which forms it stores. |
| `Remove Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | A removed glyph that a kept composite uses is decomposed into that composite, as non-exported glyphs are (M2 preflight); removing the base of a composite in the reference app settles it. |
| `Remove Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Queries compare text values exactly except for `*`, are case-sensitive, and an unknown key is a warning that matches nothing; `unicode` compares hexadecimal text in either case, and a glyph without the property compares as empty text; the reference app settles case, empty values and unknown keys. |
| `Remove Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Remove and Keep Glyphs apply after Rename Glyphs, to the names glyphs are exported under, as the handbook's recipe for replacing a glyph needs and as fontmake does; the reference app settles it. |
| `Rename Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | An entry naming a glyph the font lacks renames the existing glyph to the new name, keeping its code points, with a warning; an entry whose old name is missing is skipped with a warning; the reference app settles both. |
| `Rename Glyphs` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | Code points and production names stay with the names while the drawing, export flag and kerning go with the glyph, and composites keep the drawing they used (fontmake rewrites component references), although the handbook's Decompose Glyphs entry suggests that composites show the swapped drawing; check 38 in the reference app settles it. |
| `Replace Feature` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | Only the first feature of that tag is replaced, its labels are kept and it counts as hand-written from then on, so a replaced `kern` still gets the generated kerning after it; exporting a font with a stylistic-set name and a replaced `kern` in the reference app settles it. |
| `Replace Feature` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | A Replace parameter that names no entry does nothing, without a message, as the handbook limits these parameters to existing entries and glyphsLib skips them; the reference app's export report settles it. |
| `Replace Prefix` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | A prefix name may hold spaces, since it is written as in the Features tab, where prefix names are free text; replacing a prefix called `Language Systems` in the reference app settles it. |
| `ROS` (parameter) | M12 | handbook | – | Any well-formed Registry-Ordering-Supplement is accepted, not only the six the handbook lists; entering another value in the reference app would settle whether it is refused. |
| `ROS` (parameter) | M12 | handbook | – | On a source that already holds CID-keyed data, the parameter's collection replaces the stored one and the stored CID map and sub-fonts are kept, with a warning when they differ; no source settles it, as the reference app has no stored CID-keyed data. |
| `ROS` (parameter) | M12 | handbook | – | Any well-formed Registry-Ordering-Supplement is accepted, not only the six public ones the handbook lists; choosing a value in the reference app shows whether it offers a menu or a text field. |
| `SBIX to SVG` (parameter) | M11 | handbook | – | The value is taken as a whole number from 0 to 65535; the handbook does not say what the number means. |
| `Scale to UPM` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The instance's value wins over the master's and the font's; a value that is not a whole number from 16 to 16384 is skipped with a warning, and a scaling that cannot be done refuses the export; a Scale to UPM on a master and another on its instance in the reference app settle the first part. |
| `styleMapStyleNames` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | The value must be regular, italic, bold or bold italic in any case, as the UFO key it comes from; another value is ignored with a warning; the reference app settles it. |
| `TTFAutohint binary path` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | A failing external autohinter keeps the built-in autohinter's font with a warning instead of refusing the export; the reference app with a binary path that does not exist would show what it does. |
| `TTFAutohint binary path` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | The parameter only selects the program the user trusted in the settings, and the options are limited to ttfautohint's option flags; this departs from the reference app, which runs whatever the parameter names, because a document from elsewhere could otherwise run any program with any arguments. |
| `TTFAutohint options` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | No corpus source holds the parameter; FontCraft stores and reads it as ttfautohint command-line options in one string, which is how fontmake reads it from an instance (split on spaces); a file saved by the reference app with the options sheet filled in would show the real form. |
| `TTFDontPreserveDiagonals` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | Diagonals are preserved for a moved point with exactly one flat neighbour and one straight slanted neighbour that runs at least a quarter of its length along the hinting direction; the reference app would settle which points it applies to, by comparing an exported A's crossbar points at small sizes. |
| `TTFFamilyZonesThreshold` (parameter) | M10 | handbook | – | Family zones apply up to and including the threshold PPM, are matched to the master's zones by index, are left out with a warning when the counts differ, and are not applied without the parameter; a reference export with family zones at the threshold size would settle it. |
| `unicodeRanges` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | Bits 123 to 127, which the OS/2 table reserves, are refused; the reference app's ranges dialog shows whether it offers them. |
| `Update Features` (parameter) | M8 | handbook | – | Export regenerates only the automatic entries the font already has and adds none of the entries Update would add; an export of a font whose Features list lacks an automatic feature its glyphs call for would settle it. |
| `Use Arabic Presentation Form Unicodes` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | An exported glyph without code points gets the legacy code point the glyph database records for its name (`unicodeLegacy`: beh-ar.init FE91, lam_alef-ar FEFB) when it lies in FB50–FDFF or FE70–FEFF and no other glyph has it; glyphs with code points keep them; only cmap changes; custom glyph data is not read for it; an export from the reference app settles which glyphs it covers. |
| `Variable Font Family Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Looked up on the variable font setting first and on the font second; an export from the reference app with the parameter on each would settle it. |
| `Variable Font File Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Without it the file is named as glyphsLib names it: the font's family name, a hyphen and the setting's name unless that is Regular, then `VF`, spaces removed (the default font of a source without settings: family name and `VF`); an export from the reference app would settle it. |
| `Variable Font Optimize Deltas` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Treated as a second name of Optimize Variable Deltas, as the appendix describes both alike; settle by setting one of them off in the reference app and comparing the exported gvar. |
| `Variable Font Origin` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | A value naming a missing master warns and keeps the value, since the master may be added or renamed later; typing an unknown name in the reference app settles it. |
| `Variable Font Style Name` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Read as a style name, not a family name as its description says: on a setting or the font the variable font's default style, on a static instance its `fvar` name; settle by exporting with it set in each place. |
| `Variable SubfamilyName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Without it the variable font's default style name is the origin master's name, as the oracles build it from the default master; settle by exporting F4 (three named masters) without the parameter and reading name IDs 2 and 17. |
| `variablePostscriptFontName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | On a setting without it, the setting's PostScript Font Name property is used next; settle by exporting a setting with only that property set. |
| `variablePostscriptFontName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | A static export of an instance that has it takes it as name ID 6, ahead of postscriptFontName, as fontmake reads it; exporting such an instance as a static font in the reference app settles it. |
| `variableStyleName` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Read as a style name, not a family name as its description says: on a setting or the font the variable font's default style, on a static instance its `fvar` name; settle by exporting with it set in each place. |
| `variableStyleNames` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Only the default-language text is written to `fvar`; settle by exporting an instance with two languages and reading the name records of its `fvar` entry. |
| `Variation Font Origin` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Treated as an older name of Variable Font Origin naming the master by name, as glyphsLib reads it; settle in the reference app. |
| `variationsPostScriptNamePrefix` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | Without it no name ID 25 is written, as both oracles do; the typographic family fallback the description names is what a consumer computes. Settle by exporting without it and listing the name records. |
| `variationsPostScriptNamePrefix` (parameter) | M9 | handbook | – | The prefix is limited to 63 letters and digits; the handbook gives the characters but no length. |
| `vendorID` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | An id of one to three characters is accepted (export pads it with spaces) and an empty one means none; entering `ABC` in the reference app settles it. |
| `versionString` (parameter) | M7 | handbook | – | `%d` takes the major version and `%03d` (or any printf integer form) the minor, in that order; more than two `%` forms leave the text as written with a warning; the reference app settles other forms. |
| `Webfont Formats` (parameter) | M2 | handbook | – | A format matches in any case and is stored as the handbook spells it (`woff2` becomes `WOFF2`); a lowercase entry in the reference app settles it. |

## `20-cli.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `font.compile` | M2 | docs | yes | Several masters with an axis and no formats build a variable TTF, as fontc does; fontmake builds static masters there. A static-only parameter, a project compile or a .otf destination file keeps the static default. |
| `font.compile` | M2 | docs | yes | With familyName the instances of other families are listed as skipped; fontmake leaves them out silently. |
| `font.compilePlan` | M2 | inferred | yes | The features section is given for static outputs only: a variable output's feature code would need the variable layout stage, which the plan does not run. |
| `font.dumpTables` | M12 | docs | yes | Colour images (CBDT formats 17 to 19) are written raw with bitmapFormat bitwise, where fontTools 4.66.1 fails on them; a later fontTools that writes them would settle it. |
| `font.dumpTables` | M12 | docs | yes | With bitmapFormat extfile each image path in the dump is relative to the dump's folder (bitmaps/strike<N>/<file>), where ttx makes it relative to the folder ttx ran in; the two agree when ttx runs in the dump's folder, and tables.importTtx reads both. |
| `font.dumpTables` | M12 | docs | yes | With noClobber an existing image file is refused as an existing dump is, and nothing is written; ttx replaces image files. A user who keeps image folders between dumps would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | A variable font converted to the outline flavour it already has (TrueType to ttf, CFF2 to otf-cff2 or cff2) keeps its variations when no location is given; tx writes only static fonts, so user feedback would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | cid.keyed names the one Font DICT after the font's PostScript name and leaves the post table as it is; a makeotf -cn build of the same font would settle both. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | cid.keyed draws seac glyphs out (base hints kept when the glyph has none of its own, accent hints dropped), as CFF to CFF2 does, where makeotf -cn refuses or keeps seac is not documented; a makeotf -cn build of a seac font would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | A glyph whose CFF or CFF2 overlaps are merged loses its stem hints and its points move to the one-unit grid; tx +V output for an overlapping hinted glyph would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | A single /CID item naming a CID the font lacks is refused, as a missing name is, while a CID range keeps the CIDs the font has; tx's output for a missing CID would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | fdSelect takes a list of Font DICTs and accepts CFF2 fonts, where tx -fd takes one index and documents only CID-keyed fonts; tx -fd on a CFF2 font would settle the second part. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | CFF to CFF2 writes the glyph names into post format 2 (fontTools leaves post as it was, usually format 3, so the names are lost); comparing with a reference app's CFF2 export would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | CFF2 to CFF is name-keyed when post carries names and the table has one Font DICT, and CID-keyed Adobe-Identity-0 otherwise; fontTools always writes the CID-keyed form, so a reference user's expectation would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | TrueType to CFF merges quadratic runs with fontTools' qu2cu within one font unit (all curves cubic); tx -cff merges by its own rule, which is not documented and strays further from the source on some glyphs, so a documented tx rule would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | CFF to CFF2 writes a seac glyph's base and accent into its charstring, keeping the base's hints when the glyph has none of its own and dropping the accent's; fontTools leaves the seac operands in place, so the glyph draws nothing, and a reference app's CFF2 export of a seac font would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | TrueType to CFF names the glyphs of a font without post names glyphNNNNN; tx names them from the cmap with Adobe Glyph List names and g<gid> otherwise, by a table it does not document, so tx's name table would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | The OpenType tables around a Type 1 or bare CFF input follow makeotf's metric rules but are not compared with makeotf output (fsType 4 when the font has no FSType, vendor ID NONE, sub- and superscript sizes 650/600 and offsets 75/350 per 1000 units, strikeout at half the x-height, name records for Windows only, timestamps 0); a makeotf build of the same Type 1 font with no feature file would settle them. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | A Type 1 glyph whose hint replacement sets the CFF format cannot hold (two overlapping stems in one set, or a stem out of range) loses its hints with a note, where tx writes them with a warning; tx's output for such a glyph would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | A Type 1 font without .notdef gets an empty one at glyph 0; tx's output for such a font would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | The CFF Top DICT built from a TrueType font takes Notice from name ID 7, Copyright from name ID 0, FullName from ID 4, FamilyName from ID 16 or 1 and version from head.fontRevision; a comparison with tx and makeotf output would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | embedMinimal (tx +E) removes version, Notice, Copyright, FullName, FamilyName and Weight from the Top DICT, the keys tx's help calls purely descriptive without listing them; tx -cff +E output would settle the list. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | warnHints reports a glyph that draws a path without hstem, vstem or hint-mask stems (tx +H), and a glyph whose hint mask repeats the previous one (our reading of tx +d's duplicate hintsubs), as one line per font listing glyph ids; tx's stderr on the same font would settle the wording and the cases. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | keepGlyphOrder changes only Type 1 inputs, whose default order is tx's (.notdef, standard-string names, the rest); a compiled font always keeps its glyph order, since its other tables depend on it, where tx -cff without +b may sort glyphs by a rule it does not document; tx -cff output from an OTF would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | allowMissingNotdef makes the first glyph of a Type 1 font without .notdef glyph 0, named .notdef as every CFF glyph 0 is; tx -cff -amnd output would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | decid names glyphs cid and the decimal CID without padding (cid42), as tx's help describes, where FontCraft's own CID names elsewhere are padded to five digits; tx -decid output would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | decid takes the Font DICT's FontMatrix into the Top DICT when the Top DICT has none or the identity, and refuses two different scaling matrices; tx -decid on a font with a Font DICT FontMatrix would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | decid with fd re-encodes the width of every glyph moved to another Private DICT so its advance is kept; tx -decid -usefd output would settle whether tx does the same. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | optimizeWidths false leaves defaultWidthX and nominalWidthX at 0 in a table built from TrueType and changes nothing in a table passed through; tx -cff -W output would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | keepUniqueId defaults to true, as tx's -t1 help lists +q among the defaults; whether tx -cff keeps UniqueID is not documented, and tx -cff output from a Type 1 font with UniqueID would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | nameBy postscript-name names an output after name ID 6 cleaned to a CFF FontName (Untitled when empty), where tx -A takes the source font's FontName; the two differ only for fonts whose names disagree. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | An SVG font's font-face keeps the writer's family name and vertical metrics (ascent, descent, x-height, cap-height), where tx writes the PostScript name as font-family with the underline position and thickness; and the copyright comments tx writes are not written; a user of SVG fonts would settle which matters. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | A standalone SVG font is an SVG 1.1 document with the svg namespace and the font inside defs, where tx -sa writes an svg element without namespace after an SVG 1.0 DOCTYPE; browsers need the namespace, and tx output in use elsewhere would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | An SVG font keeps up to three decimals in coordinates and writes the hkern pairs the font's kerning names, where tx writes no kerning; tx output for a font with fractional points would settle the rounding. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | Private-use code points for glyphs without one skip code points the font already maps; tx output for a font that maps U+E000 would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | recalcBounds false changes only TTX inputs and rebuilt tables; a conversion to another outline flavour recalculates the bounds as AFDKO otf2ttf does, and a user asking for stated bounds there would settle it. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | optimizeFor speed writes CFF without subroutines and every gvar delta (font.optimize's speed preset); fontTools' speed mode also packs glyf coordinates and gvar runs differently, which is not built, and a speed measure in a text engine would settle whether it matters. |
| `font.convert` | M12 | docs | yes | ufo.structure zip names the archive's one folder after the output's stem with .ufo, as fontTools' UFO writer does; a UFOZ from fontmake would confirm it. |
| `font.subset` | M12 | docs | yes | keep.random draws from the glyphs the other selections leave, after the exclusions, Font DICTs and GOADB; tx -p draws from every glyph of the font and takes no other selection, so user feedback would settle the combination. |
| `font.transform` | M12 | docs | yes | A composite whose component glyph is transformed keeps showing it as before when the composite itself is not selected: the component's transform becomes the inverse of the matrix; a user transforming part of a font would settle whether the composite should follow its component instead. |
| `font.transform` | M12 | docs | yes | scaleEm multiplies units per em by the absolute value of the matrix's a and changes nothing else, as rotatefont's -fm changes only the FontMatrix; a TrueType user would settle whether metrics should follow. |
| `collection.unpack` | M12 | docs | yes | A member whose file name another member already takes gets the collection name plus -fontN, N its own index or the next number no other member uses; otc2otf on such a collection would settle it. |
| `collection.unpack` | M12 | docs | yes | When a later file cannot be written, the fonts this call created before it are removed and the error says so; no reference tool documents a partial failure. |
| `woff.decompress` | M12 | docs | yes | extractMetadata and extractPrivateData that name the input, the font output or each other (compared as resolved paths) are refused before anything is written; no reference tool documents the case. |
| `outlines.correctDirection` | M12 | inferred | yes | Without flavour a source gets the PostScript convention (the editor's) and a TrueType font the TrueType one; a reference run on a source drawn for TrueType outlines would settle the source default. |
| `outlines.correctDirection` | M12 | inferred | yes | Composites are left alone, since their direction comes from their bases; no reference documents whole-font direction fixing of composites. |
| `outlines.addExtremes` | M12 | inferred | yes | minSegment accepts only 3 (the editor's fixed threshold) or 0 (every extreme forced); a reference run with other values would settle whether other thresholds are wanted. |
| `outlines.roundCoordinates` | M12 | inferred | yes | Halfway values round away from zero to the grid; fontTools' otRound rounds them up, which differs only for negative halves on a grid coarser than 1 unit. |
| `outlines.roundCoordinates` | M12 | inferred | yes | On sources advance widths stay, as in the editor's rounding command; on compiled fonts they are rounded with the points. |
| `outlines.convertCurves` | M12 | inferred | yes | A compiled font's contours are never reversed, since glyf contours already run the TrueType way; a cubic glyf font with PostScript-direction contours would show whether reversing should follow keepDirection there too. |
| `outlines.componentize` | M12 | inferred | yes | detect prefers components with more contours and, of two equal outlines, makes the later glyph refer to the earlier; no reference tool detects components, so this stands until a use case asks otherwise. |
| `outlines.componentize` | M12 | inferred | yes | detect sets USE_MY_METRICS on the first component when its advance equals the glyph's, the rule ttfcomponentizer applies to the source's advances. |
| `outlines.decompose` | M12 | docs | yes | Several inputs whose outputs resolve to one file, or an output that would replace another input, are refused before anything is written; no reference tool documents the case. |
| `font.proof` | M12 | docs | yes | The HTML specimen has no script: feature switches are checkboxes and each axis is a row of stops (nine even values plus the default) that CSS turns into font settings, so the axes are not free sliders. Settled by the owner if a scripted slider is wanted. |
| `layout.decompileFeatures` | M12 | docs | yes | A lookup a feature uses is named feature_script_language_index after the first language system that uses it, and any other lookup GSUB_index or GPOS_index; the round-trip measure on the corpus would show whether stabler names are needed. |
| `layout.decompileFeatures` | M12 | docs | yes | Glyph sets of two or more glyphs used at least twice become named classes (@first_last); sets used once stay inline. Reading the decompiled text of a few corpus fonts would settle the threshold. |
| `layout.decompileFeatures` | M12 | docs | yes | Mark classes are named @MC_lookup_subtable_class, one set per subtable, so a mark that has different anchors in two subtables of one lookup never collides. |
| `layout.lintFeatures` | M12 | inferred | yes | Without a glyph set the compiler's messages about glyphs missing from the font are left out and hyphenated names are read as ranges; addfeatures always checks against a font. A request to check names against the code's own classes would change it. |
| `source.merge` | M12 | inferred | yes | A merged designspace is refused when it would be written beside BASE without inPlace, because its UFOs would replace BASE's; a user workflow that wants that would settle it. |
| `tableViewer.show` | M12 | inferred | yes | The Table Viewer opens from the Window menu with no shortcut; the companion tool is a separate application, so nothing settles a menu place. |
| `cli.global.logging` (behavior) | M0 | docs | – | The levels are error, warning, note, info and debug, with note (files written, steps skipped) shown by default between warnings and info; the references use the Python and Rust logging levels, which have no note, so user feedback would settle it. |
| `cli.global.logging` (behavior) | M0 | docs | – | A line reads `fontcraft-cli: LEVEL: INPUT: message`, in the style of compiler diagnostics; no reference fixes a format, so user feedback would settle it. |
| `cli.global.logging` (behavior) | M0 | docs | – | --log-file creates or truncates the file instead of appending to it; a user who wants a running log would settle it. |
| `cli.global.logging` (behavior) | M0 | docs | – | A per-module --log level applies to the libraries' messages only; the command line's own messages follow the main level. fontc's module filters would settle whether both should follow it. |
| `cli.global.color` (behavior) | M0 | docs | – | --color auto also turns colour off for TERM=dumb, and an empty NO_COLOR counts as unset, as the NO_COLOR convention says; fonttools diff's own rule would settle it. |
| `cli.global.timing` (behavior) | M2 | docs | – | The stages are the engine commands and file opens, not finer steps inside the compiler; fontmake's and fontc's stage lists would settle a finer split. |
| `cli.global.help` (behavior) | M0 | docs | – | -h prints the full reference like --help rather than a shorter summary; the AFDKO -u and -h split would settle whether users want both. |
| `cli.global.abbreviations` (behavior) | M12 | inferred | – | A subcommand or long option may be shortened to any prefix that names exactly one subcommand, or one option of that subcommand; an ambiguous prefix is a usage error listing the candidates. No source fixes the rule; a user complaint about a clash would settle it. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | A source with several masters and no --format builds a variable TTF, as fontc does; fontmake builds static otf and ttf masters there. Any option that only static fonts take turns the default back to a static TTF. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | A --instances value holding a regular expression character is an expression, any other a comma-separated list of names, so the older list form keeps working; fontmake's -i takes only an expression. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --name-by filename names a static font without a fileName parameter by its PostScript name; fontmake names it FamilyName-StyleName with spaces removed (glyphsLib build_ufo_path), which differs only when the instance sets its own PostScript name. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --output-layout flat puts TrueType and CFF outlines into ttf and otf subfolders when a run builds both, so their WOFF files do not overwrite each other. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --interpolate-binary-layout resolves its folder (default master_ttf or master_otf) against the source's folder; fontmake resolves it against the working folder, which is the same folder when fontmake runs beside the source. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | With --interpolate-binary-layout the instances are compiled with the automatic features as usual, and fontmake compiles them without (it turns its feature writers off); the difference shows only in a GDEF or GSUB the default master lacks, which a fontmake run on masters without GSUB and with feature code would show. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | An instance compiled with --interpolate-binary-layout from masters without GPOS has no GPOS; fontmake stops with an error there, so nothing settles a better choice. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --interpolate-binary-layout takes the next argument as its folder only when it is not an option and does not end in a source extension (.glyphs, .designspace, .ufo, a font…); fontmake's parser takes any next argument that is not an option. Nothing in fontmake's documentation says which callers rely on that. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | The file exports (file.exportStatic, file.exportAll) share the compile's naming, so a .glyphs source without a family name exports to Unnamedfont-Regular too; the reference app's export of such a file has not been observed. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --feature-writer takes short names (kern, mark, curs, gdef) or ufo2ft class names with an optional mode, not arbitrary Python class specs; fontmake loads any class with any keyword arguments. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --no-generate-gdef leaves the GDEF generator out of the writer list; in fontmake it stops glyphsLib from writing a GDEF block into the master UFOs' features, after which ufo2ft's GDEF writer still runs, so a fontmake build may still get generated glyph classes. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --debug-feature-file holds the code of the first font built; fontmake writes the file for each master or font it compiles, so the last one remains. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --debug-feature-file holds the assembled code without the generated kerning and mark lookups, which are merged as lookups; fontmake's file has them written out as feature code. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --mti-source uses one entry of the plist for the whole build (the default master's, or the only one); fontmake gives each master UFO its own entry and merges the masters' tables for a variable font. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --mti-source resolves `# N` glyph references against the exported glyphs in source order; fontmake resolves them against each master UFO's glyph order. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --feature-names-override is accepted and changes nothing: every compile lets the feature code's name records replace generated ones, as fontc does; makeotf replaces menu names only with -overrideMenuNames and never IDs 2 and 6. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --stub-gsub writes the empty GSUB in any compile; makeotf's -fs applies to its project workflow. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --glyphs takes a comma-separated list of glyph names; fontc's -g takes a regular expression, and no source defines a SELECTOR syntax for compile. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --glyphs keeps the components' glyphs but not the glyphs reached only through substitutions; a fontc -g build of a font with a ligature settles whether its closure follows GSUB. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --notdef allow-missing builds as auto does, because every FontCraft font gets a .notdef as glyph 0; makeotf without -amnd stops on a source without .notdef, which FontCraft never does. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --optimize-widths is off by default, keeping the source's defaultWidthX and nominalWidthX; the reference's default is on, and comparing a fontmake build's widths on a corpus font settles whether it changes the output. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --exclude-table refuses head, maxp, hhea, hmtx, glyf, loca, CFF and CFF2, without which a font cannot be read; no source says which tables the option may drop. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --tables writes the raw tables beside the fonts, which are written too; fontc writes only the raw tables, which a fontc build with --tables would show. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --warnings all prints the same warnings as default; the notes the reference adds for 'all' have not been listed. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --autohint and --no-autohint set the export's Autohint option, which an instance's Autohint parameter still overrides; fontmake's -A turns autohinting off whatever the source says. |
| `compile` (cli) | M2 | docs | – | --bump-version without a value adds 0.005, as makeotf's -rev; a value N adds N thousandths and X.YYY sets the version, which no source documents for the compile. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A table view, @font or @glyphs given with --format ttx (the default) writes text as if --format text were given; a user of the reference command line would settle whether that should be refused instead. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Where ttxn stops with a Python error (format-3 anchors, GPOS chaining contexts of format 2, --no-version on a Windows name ID 5), the normalised dump goes on: the anchor prints its coordinates, the rule prints in the GSUB form, and name ID 5 keeps the text before its first semicolon; a fixed ttxn would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | @glyphs names a TrueType glyph by its post name where tx printed g<index> for an unencoded glyph of a font built with fontTools; reading when tx ignores post names would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | In @glyphs, two off-curve TrueType points in a row become one cubic with handles four-thirds along the quadratic handles and every TrueType coordinate is rounded to an integer, as tx printed them on two corpus glyphs and one test glyph; more glyphs with long off-curve runs would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Vertical ghost stems of width -20 and -21 print as rightedge and leftedge by analogy with the horizontal ones, and a CFF2 font's @font says CFF2 (CID-keyed); no corpus font has either, and running tx on one would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without -o or --output-dir the dump goes to standard output, where ttx writes FONT.ttx beside the input; a user of the reference command line would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The JSON form is the TTX element tree, each element an object with name, attributes, text and children; no reference tool writes JSON, so readers of it settle the shape. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The hex form lists each table with a header line, then offset, 16 bytes in two groups of eight and their ASCII, as hexdump -C does; no reference tool settles it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A dump past 1 GiB of text (the JSON form counting its element tree) stops with an error, and a simple glyph whose contour end points decrease is written as hex with the decompilation error, where ttx would write repeated points; a font that needs more would settle the limit. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A coverage or class definition table whose ranges list more than 10,000,000 glyphs, or layout subtables nested so deep that their elements would pass 64 levels (extension lookups pointing at extension lookups), make the table be written as hex with the decompilation error, where ttx writes the glyphs in full and fails on the nesting; a font that needs more would settle the limits. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A component bitmap (EBDT image formats 8 and 9) is written as ebdtComponent elements with the glyph name and the x and y offsets, which is what the ttx 4.66 code means to write but stops with an error on; a fixed ttx would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A bitmap data table holding a glyph that cannot be read is written as hex with the decompilation error, and a trak table in a font without a name table is written without the name comments, where ttx stops with an error on both; a fixed ttx would settle them. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A COLR clip list or an AAT lookup whose glyph ranges add up to more than 10,000,000 glyphs, a bitmap location table listing more than 10,000,000 glyphs, or a trak table with more than 10,000,000 per-size values make the table be written as hex with the decompilation error, where ttx writes it in full; a font that needs more would settle the limits. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The spot listings name glyphs as fontTools does (post names, CFF charset names, \CID for CID-keyed fonts); for a font without glyph names spot prints glyph indices where FontCraft prints the names fontTools makes up; a user of the listings would settle which form they want. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A listing header of a table read from a WOFF or WOFF2 file, or a CFF literal view of one, gives offset 0, since the table has no place in an sfnt file; spot and tx read only sfnt and bare CFF files, so nothing settles it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | kern=pairs lists the pairs a format 0 subtable counts and leaves out the sentinel pair spot prints after them when the subtable has room for one (spot reads it as data); subtables of formats 2 and 3 print nothing. A spot that does not crash on the test table would settle the sentinel. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | cmap listings of format 2 and 13 subtables print the mappings as fontTools reads them (glyph 0 left out), and formats 8 and 10 print only their header lines, where spot stops with an error on 13, 8 and 10; spot on a format 2 font would settle that one. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | In the hmtx listings a font with fewer side bearings than glyphs stops at the last one the table holds, where spot reads past the table. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --unicode-data takes the names only from the file given: without one the cmap comments are left out, where ttx writes the names of Python's own Unicode database, and unicodeData true is refused because no names are bundled; bundling a name table would settle whether the comments should be written by default. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Lines of a UnicodeData file that ttx stops on with a Python error (no semicolon, an empty name, a first field that is not hex) are skipped; a ttx that reads such files would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --names, --goadb, --fd, --exclude-fd and --flatten-seac apply to @glyphs only and are refused with the TTX dump, other views and --normalize; tx -gf also renames the glyphs of its other outputs, which dump does not write. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --glyphs and --exclude-glyphs select @glyphs by the names shown after --goadb maps them; tx run with -gf and -g together would settle which names -g matches. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --fd takes several font dicts where tx -fd takes one, and takes CFF2 fonts as well as CID-keyed CFF ones; tx -fd on a CFF2 font would settle the second. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --flatten-seac shows the base glyph's hints even when the seac glyph has stems of its own, and the seac form leaves such stems out; tx -dump -6 with and without -d on a seac glyph with its own stems would settle it. |
| `dump` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | An avar version 2 table without a variation store prints its segment maps and no mappings, where fontTools stops with an error. |
| `info` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | The text layout (path line, indented key: value lines, field=value lines for records, yes/no for flags) is ours; no reference prints this summary, so a user preference would settle it. |
| `tables list` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | The header line above the rows (path, format, sfnt version, table count) is ours; ttx -l prints a Listing table info line and spot -T the offset table fields. |
| `tables copy` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Copying a table the target already has replaces it without a prompt, as tables add does; a test against the reference workflow (sfntedit extract then add) shows the same result. |
| `tables import` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without -o or --in-place the result is written beside the input as FONT.edited.EXT, as the other tables actions do; the reference tool writes FONT#1.EXT beside the input, and a user of the reference command line would settle which to follow. |
| `tables import` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A bitmap data table is read only with its location table in the same dump, and the other way round (fontTools would compile the missing one from the font being merged into); a dump that holds both settles nothing, and an import of one alone into a font that has the other is refused. |
| `tables import` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | An image file (extfileimagedata) is looked for relative to the folder of the dump file that names it, then by the last three parts of its path (bitmaps/strike<N>/<file>) below that folder; fontTools opens the path as given, from the folder it runs in. An absolute path, or one that climbs out with .., is used only through those three parts. A user who keeps dumps and images in other layouts would settle it. |
| `tables import` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A row or bitwise image wider than 255 pixels, with more than 255 rows or a bit depth outside 1 to 32 is refused, where fontTools takes any size; the glyph metrics hold widths and heights up to 255, so no dump ttx writes passes these limits. |
| `tables import` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Row and bitwise images of the colour formats 17, 18 and 19 are refused; fontTools fails on them too. |
| `glyphs list` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | spot prints the whole -n listing on one line; ours wraps it at 100 characters, with the same items. A user who pipes the listing into a line-based tool would settle it. |
| `glyphs list` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | spot adds @GID to the names of TrueType glyphs unless -ngid is given; ours never does, since the index is already in the item. |
| `glyphs list` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | A glyph with several code points prints them joined by + (U+0041+U+0391), where tx -dump -4 prints the first only; a script reading tx's rows would settle it. |
| `glyphs list` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | kind is composite for a glyph with any component, simple for outlines only, empty otherwise. |
| `glyphs list` (cli) | M1 | docs | – | advance and bbox print as integers when whole, bbox as x0 y0 x1 y1, inside the braces of the --columns row. |
| `glyphs rename` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Names a GOADB or map lists that the font lacks are reported as missing, not refused, since one GOADB serves a whole family. Settled by running tx -gf with a GOADB that names an absent glyph. |
| `glyphs reorder` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --sort unicode puts encoded glyphs first by their lowest code point and keeps the unencoded ones after them in their old order. Settled by tx's +b ordering on a font with unencoded glyphs. |
| `glyphs reorder` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --sort size sorts by the byte size of each glyph's outline data (glyf entry or charstring), smallest first. Settled by finding a reference tool that documents a size order. |
| `glyphs reorder` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A post version 3 TrueType font, which stores no names, gets the names glyphNNNNN; fontTools derives names from cmap instead. Settled by comparing glyph orders with fontTools on such a font. |
| `metrics` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The text form adds statistics, font-statistics and kerning sections in the same bracketed style as the glyph lines; a comparison with a future tx release or user feedback would settle the layout. |
| `metrics` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Glyph names come from post, then the CFF charset, then uniXXXX from the cmap, then glyphNNNNN, with #n added to repeats as fontTools does; tx's own fallback names (g1, g2) were not followed. |
| `metrics` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With several fonts on the command line the text forms print a '## FONT' line before each report. |
| `convert` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --zopfli is accepted and has no effect with a plain or WOFF2 result, as the reference converter compresses only WOFF with zopfli; a WOFF result is refused rather than written with zlib, since no zopfli compressor is built in and no dependency was added for one. A user who wants zopfli output would settle whether a fallback to zlib with a warning is better. |
| `convert` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With IN OUT.woff or OUT.woff2 the output keeps the input's outline flavour; the reference tools have no such two-argument form, so user feedback would settle it. |
| `convert` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A conversion to CFF whose glyphs would hold more than 16,777,216 points together (8,388,608 on wasm), components counted once for every use, a conversion to TrueType that would make more than 67,108,864 points, or a CFF or CFF2 table whose expanded charstrings would take more than 1 GiB of memory (512 MiB on wasm), is refused with a message, where the reference tools convert it; a font that needs more would settle the limits. |
| `convert` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --glyphs or --subset-to-goadb an sfnt result keeps its other tables, subset as the subset command subsets them with every feature, script, name record and cmap subtable kept and no layout closure (composite components still come along); tx writes only the font program, so user feedback would settle it. |
| `convert` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --glyphs together with --subset-to-goadb keeps the glyphs both select, as tx did on one corpus font; a second font with other GOADB columns would confirm it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --random uses our own generator (SplitMix64, seeded from the glyph names unless --seed is given), so its choice differs from tx -p's for the same font; the share and the rounding follow tx. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The subset always keeps .notdef (unless --no-notdef-glyph), where tx -p and -gx can leave it out; the closure of the subsetter is kept as it is. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Arguments after FONT that end in .ttf, .otf, .woff, .woff2, .ttc or .otc are further inputs rather than glyph names; neither reference takes several fonts, so user feedback would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A source subset has no layout closure: glyphs only feature rules reach are removed with those rules, where fontmake's --subset (pyftsubset on the compiled font) keeps them; user feedback would settle whether sources should close over their feature code too. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Feature code of a source is pruned token by token: a rule with a removed glyph outside a class goes whole, also a mark-attachment rule whose other mark classes stay; a compile of the pruned code against fontmake's subset of the compiled font would show where the two differ. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Contextual kerning entries of a source are kept as they are, with a warning, because no document describes their syntax. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A designspace subset keeps the feature code of non-default UFOs as it is; only the default source's feature code is pruned. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --subset-to-goadb is accepted with --goadb and changes nothing, matching the spelling of convert and compile; tx needs -gs to subset, so user feedback would settle whether --goadb alone should only check the file. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A variation index that names no delta set becomes no variation in the subset (pyftsubset stops with an error); a damaged corpus font would show what users expect. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | An sbix dupe or flip bitmap whose source glyph left the subset is dropped (pyftsubset stops with an error); a font that reuses bitmaps across glyphs would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A CBLC index subtable of format 2 left with gaps is written as format 5 (pyftsubset stops with an error); a bitmap font with constant-size strikes would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without --pretty-svg, SVG documents keep the source text of what they keep, without blank text (pyftsubset writes them again through its XML library, so quoting and empty-element forms can differ); comparing documents after canonical XML on the corpus would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Where unused CFF subroutines cannot be removed safely (a subroutine number not written just before its call, a subroutine with a hint mask reached with different stem counts, or a global subroutine whose local calls would be renumbered differently in different Font DICTs), all subroutines are kept as they are (pyftsubset stops with an error); a font with such charstrings would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Unused CFF subroutines stay in the subset (pyftsubset removes them); outlines are the same, the file is larger. Comparing sizes on the corpus would show whether pruning is worth porting. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Feature, script and language tags shorter than four characters are padded with spaces before they are compared (pyftsubset pads scripts and languages only); a font with a short feature tag would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | References to lookups or features past the end of their lists, which make pyftsubset stop with an error, are dropped; a damaged corpus font would show what users expect. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A VARC component keeps the values that follow its reserved flag bits (pyftsubset drops the values and keeps the bits, so its output reads wrongly); a VARC font that sets a reserved bit would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The glyphs a VARC glyph's components name are kept even when only a glyf composite reaches that glyph (pyftsubset closes over VARC before glyf and then cannot write the table); a font whose glyf composites use VARC glyphs would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A composite bitmap (image format 8 or 9) has its component ids renumbered to the output glyph order and loses the components not in the output (an index subtable of format 2 or 5 whose images then differ in length is written as format 1); pyftsubset copies its bytes unchanged, so its ids still count in the input order (the same bytes under --retain-gids). A bitmap font with composites, subset by both, would show whether anyone relies on the copied ids. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A compressed SVG document is compressed again with our deflate at its best level, so its bytes, and in rare cases whether the gzip form is the smaller, differ from pyftsubset's zlib output; the documents inflate to the same text. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --recalc-bounds the glyph boxes are the ones each glyf glyph stores (pyftsubset recomputes every glyph's box before using it); a font whose stored boxes are wrong would show a difference. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A table that indexes glyphs, added to --no-subset-tables, is subset all the same with a warning (pyftsubset leaves it unsubset, which either writes it again by glyph name or fails); a run of pyftsubset with such a list on a corpus font would settle what users expect. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --recalc-max-context leaves an OS/2 table older than version 2, which has no usMaxContext field, as it is (pyftsubset stops with an error on such a font, so there is no reference output); a user who needs the field on such a font would show whether raising the version is wanted. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without --canonical-order the tables keep the input's order (pyftsubset writes them in its table-dependency order); the order of table data does not change how the font works. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A WOFF2 output uses the default transforms (glyf and loca) whatever a WOFF2 input used (pyftsubset reuses the input's choice); comparing outputs on a corpus WOFF2 with the hmtx transform would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --with-zopfli with a WOFF output is refused, since FontCraft has no zopfli compressor (pyftsubset compresses with zopfli); with WOFF2 or plain output it is ignored, as in pyftsubset. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --pretty-svg, whitespace written as character references, comments and processing instructions holding characters past ASCII, and references to entities other than the five predefined ones keep their source text (lxml may decode, drop or escape them); a corpus SVG font with such documents, subset by both, would settle it. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --OPTION? given without a FONT prints its lines and exits with status 0 (pyftsubset then prints its usage and exits with status 1); a user scripting the query would show which is wanted. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --OPTION? answers for every option, --gids, --no-subset-tables and our own included (pyftsubset treats `--gids?` as a glyph name and rejects `--no-subset-tables?`); --layout-features? lists the 64 tags without the four internal pseudo-tags pyftsubset shows, and --harfbuzz-repacker? prints True by default (pyftsubset prints None until the option is given). |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --show-defaults prints one `Current setting` line per option under our option names (name-ids, not name-IDs), since pyftsubset has no such option; a user of the listing would show whether `--OPTION=VALUE` lines, which can be read back with @FILE, serve better. |
| `subset` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --xml with --json is refused rather than printing both; nothing in pyftsubset settles it, since it has no JSON output. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A UFO instance writes an instance parameter without a font-information key to the lib as a parameter of the UFO's master, which FontCraft reads back and applies when the instance UFO is compiled; glyphsLib writes it under a lib prefix of its own for instances, which nothing reads back. Compiling both instance UFOs with a Remove Glyphs parameter would show the difference. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Two instance outputs of a source whose paths differ only in case count as one file, the later kept, also on a file system that tells case apart (font.compile compares its outputs the same way); fontmake compares nothing and lets the file system decide, which a run on a case-sensitive file system with two such names would show. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --layout-only resolves a relative --master-finder result against the designspace's folder; fontTools resolves it against the working folder. Running fontmake --interpolate-binary-layout from another folder would show which callers depend on it. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --layout-only names an instance's file after its PostScript name (else its file name's stem), beside the designspace or in --output-dir; the reference CLI builds only locations. Comparing with fontmake's instance_ttf names would settle it. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --named matches its pattern anywhere in the subfamily or PostScript name of an fvar named instance; checking makeinstancesufo and fontmake on a designspace with similar instance names would settle the matching rule. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A named instance without a PostScript name is written as <input stem>-<subfamily without spaces>.<ext>; the reference tools name such files from the family and style names, which a run on such a font would show. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A source instance at a location is named after the location (wght380), which sets its style name and file names; makeinstancesufo and fontmake only build the instances a designspace names, so no reference settles it. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without a location or a selection, a source gives every exporting static instance and --all adds the inactive ones; running makeinstancesufo on a .glyphs-derived designspace with an inactive instance would settle the default. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | For a source, overlaps are kept by default in compiled instances too (font.compile removes them by default); fontmake -i removes them, which a comparison of the outlines would show matters. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A CFF2 instance keeps each charstring's operators with the blends resolved; fontTools also respecialises them (hlineto, hvcurveto …), which changes the bytes and not the outlines; a byte comparison of the CFF2 tables on corpus fonts would show whether matching it is worth porting the specialiser here. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A downgraded CFF instance has its head and hhea bounds and its Top DICT FontBBox recomputed; fontTools keeps the input's head and hhea values when it downgrades (it saves without recalculation) and writes no FontBBox. Comparing the two on a CFF2 corpus font shows the difference. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A downgraded CFF table states CIDCount when the font has more than 8,720 glyphs, which fontTools never writes; a font that large would show whether other tools need it. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A downgrade refuses a hint operator with more than 48 operands, which a CFF stack cannot hold; fontTools writes it unchanged. A font with more than 24 stems in one operator would settle it. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A format 3 BASE coordinate with a hinting device (not a variation index) is left as it is; fontTools stops on it with an assertion. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | For --update-name-table, a STAT table of version 1.0 (no elided fallback name) uses name ID 2 in its place, and a name missing on one platform is left out when names are joined; fontTools stops in both cases. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A CFF2 table whose charstrings, subroutine calls followed, would take more than 1 GiB of memory as operators and blended values (512 MiB on wasm) is refused with a message; a font that needs more would settle the limit. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A CFF2 path operator with an operand count the specification does not allow is refused; running fontTools' instancer on such a table would show whether it passes the operator through. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A variable COLR table is instanced (fields take their rounded default deltas, the store goes in a static instance) although fontTools' instancer leaves COLR unchanged; rendering the instance and the variable font at the same location in a COLRv1 renderer would confirm the values. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A VARC table whose axis index lists are renumbered keeps the old lists as unused bytes and gets the new ones appended, packed in runs of up to 64 values; fontTools rewrites the table and cannot read the corpus VARC fonts, so a newer fontTools would settle the layout. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | In an avar version 2 partial instance, variation regions the new ranges cannot reach are kept; fontTools removes them, which changes the size and no value. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --interpolate-binary-layout resolves its folder (default master_ttf or master_otf) against the source's folder; fontmake resolves it against the working folder, which is the same folder when fontmake runs beside the source. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | An instance built with --interpolate-binary-layout from masters without GPOS has no GPOS; fontmake stops with an error there. A run of fontmake on such masters shows only the error, so nothing settles a better choice. |
| `instance` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --interpolate-binary-layout the instance is compiled with the automatic features as usual, and fontmake compiles it without them; the difference shows only in a GDEF or GSUB the default master lacks, which a fontmake run on masters without GSUB and with feature code would show. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With a map, the result's glyph order is .notdef first, then each font's glyphs in its map's order (all glyphs of a font without a map, in font order); comparing with mergefonts' output order would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --layout drop still unites the cmaps (first font wins, no locl lookups), so a TrueType result stays usable; mergefonts writes no cmap at all. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A glyph merge takes head, hhea, vhea, OS/2, name, post and the font programs from the first font unchanged, apart from the glyph count, the bounding box, the hhea extents and the loca format. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | When not every font has vmtx and vhea, the result has neither (with a warning); fontTools fails on such input. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The merged CFF table is subroutinised again, where fontTools leaves it without subroutines; a user who needs pyftmerge's exact bytes would settle whether to leave it expanded. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Equal BASE baseline tag lists are kept once and a script several fonts list keeps the first font's record, where fontTools 4.66 concatenates both into an invalid table; a fontTools release that fixes its BASE merge would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | BASE tables whose baseline tag lists differ are refused, where fontTools concatenates the lists so that scripts have fewer coordinates than tags; a fontTools fix would settle the rule. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --duplicates first, last or error, a MATH or BASE entry for a glyph two fonts share keeps the first font's entry; a run of the reference tools on such fonts cannot settle it, as pyftmerge has no such option. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without a timestamp the library keeps the first font's creation time and the latest modification time; the command sets both to SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH or the current time, as fontTools does. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Variable fonts merge as variable fonts only when all are variable with equal axes and equal avar tables, and are refused otherwise; fontTools drops the variation tables instead, so a future fontTools rule for variable merging would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The merged fvar keeps the first font's axis records (flags and axis name IDs) and adds a later font's named instance only when its coordinates are new; a fontTools rule for variable merging would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | MVAR is the first font's, with a warning when another font's differs, as the OS/2 and hhea fields it varies mostly come from the first font; a fontTools rule for variable merging would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | STAT and cvar are the first font's, as the name and cvt tables they refer to are; a fontTools rule for variable merging would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | HVAR or VVAR is left out, with a warning, when one font lacks it (the advances then vary through gvar's phantom points), and a side-bearing or origin map is kept only when every font has it; a fontTools rule for variable merging would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Two identical glyphs are shared under --duplicates first, last or error only when their gvar data is identical too; HVAR deltas are not compared, as gvar's phantom points carry the same advances. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | When the result is static (--drop-tables fvar, or no font is variable) the GDEF variation stores are dropped and every variation-index device goes, so GPOS values, anchors and ligature carets keep their default values, as a full instance at the default location does; fontTools refuses fonts with GDEF stores, so a user who merges such fonts would settle whether they should be refused instead. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without a cidfontinfo file, a CID-keyed result from a name-keyed first font is Adobe-Identity-0 and takes the first font's name and Top DICT values, with a warning; a CID-keyed first font gives its own ROS; running mergefonts with numeric map names and no -cid would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The cidfontinfo AdobeCopyright and Trademark go into the Top DICT Notice, joined by a space, and version into the version string; no CIDFontVersion is written; tx -dump of mergefonts' -cid output would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | In a CID-keyed result each font's map header names the Font DICTs made from that font and sets their LanguageGroup, and --ps-name and --language-group replace the first font's header; in a name-keyed result only the first font's header counts; the mergefonts help says only that the header overrides values of the first source font's dictionary, so its -cid output would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The result's Font DICTs are ordered by input font, then by that font's Font DICT index; mergefonts' output would settle it. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --hint-dict-from takes the hint values of the hint font's first Private DICT and its underline position and thickness, for any number of inputs and for CID-keyed results too (one hint dictionary for every Font DICT); mergefonts' -hints works only on two Type 1 fonts and names only 'global metrics and hint data'. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --fd and --exclude-fd keep no glyph specially: .notdef is dropped with its Font DICT, and the CID 0 check then asks for it from another font; mergefonts' -gx never excludes .notdef. |
| `merge` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A Type 1 first font gives a bare CFF result (with a note), as FontCraft writes no Type 1; mergefonts writes Type 1. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --glyphs and --fd choose the glyphs a matrix transforms and leave the others in the font, where rotatefont writes only the chosen glyphs; a user who expects the subset would settle it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A Private DICT changes when any glyph using it is transformed, even if others using it are not; a font mixing transformed and untouched glyphs in one Font DICT would settle it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Renamed glyphs from a glyph table keep their cmap entries; rotatefont removes their codes from the CFF Encoding, which an OpenType font does not use; a vertical-text user would settle it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A matrix with b = 0 and d < 0 writes each kept horizontal stem with its edges swapped so its width stays positive; rotatefont writes them in reverse order; running such a font through a rasterizer would settle it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A turn combined with a mirror (a = d = 0, b and c of one sign) drops every hint; rotatefont stops with a program error there. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On a variable TrueType font, HVAR is dropped whenever the advance deltas change other than by one positive horizontal scale for every glyph; rebuilding HVAR from the phantom points would keep it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A negative advance width, which rotatefont writes into CFF, is set to 0 with a warning because hmtx cannot hold it; a font that relies on such widths would settle it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On a TrueType font a matrix strips the instructions of every changed glyph, drops fpgm, prep, cvt and cvar when no glyph keeps instructions, and drops hdmx, LTSH and VDMX whenever a glyph or width changed; rotatefont writes only CFF, so a TrueType user would settle it. |
| `transform` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A matrix leaves vmtx, post.italicAngle, the hhea caret slope, kerning and GPOS as they are, as rotatefont leaves everything but outlines and widths; a user of slanted output would settle whether the slant should reach italicAngle. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --for speed expands CFF subroutines and writes every gvar delta, the parts of a font whose reading cost the reference tools document; the glyf side of ttx --optimize-font-speed belongs to convert --optimize-for speed, and a reference user's timing would settle what else speed should change. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Naming any optimisation runs only the named ones, and naming none runs every built lossless one; each reference tool does one optimisation, so a user who combines them would settle it. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A step whose result is not smaller is dropped and the table kept as it was; fontmake keeps compreffor's output whatever its size, and comparing sizes on the corpus would show whether that ever matters. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A glyph keeps its implied on-curve points when something numbers them: its instructions, a composite that has instructions or places a component by point numbers, a GPOS anchor on a contour point, or GDEF attachment points or carets on contour points; fontTools drops them whatever numbers them, and a hinted variable font run through both would settle whether anyone relies on that. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On a variable font an on-curve point is dropped only when it is halfway between its neighbours in the default glyph and in the glyph at each distinct peak of the font's gvar tuples; fontTools tests the masters the font was built from, which a compiled font no longer has, so the crosscheck against its instancer settles how often the two differ. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The default run includes gvar delta optimisation, the GDEF variation store and COLR layer reuse, and leaves implied on-curve points alone as fontmake and fontTools do unless asked; a user who combines the reference tools would settle whether that default is right. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --varstore is applied only when GDEF comes out smaller; fonttools varLib.varStore always writes its result, and the crosscheck on the corpus shows where the two differ. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --gpos-compaction keeps a class-kerning subtable as it is when a pair in its class-0 column (second glyphs in no class) has a value, and drops the values of classes with no glyphs, where fontTools stops with an error on both; a font that has either would show whether keeping is the better answer. |
| `optimize` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --gpos-compaction writes GPOS with write-fonts' packer instead of fontTools' overflow handling, so a table that overflows can come out split differently and with another size; the crosscheck compares subtables and classes, and a large kerning table that overflows would settle it. |
| `collection pack` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A --share-table that names a font past the inputs, or a font without that table, is refused; otf2otc ignores it silently. A user report of a script that relies on otf2otc's behaviour would settle it. |
| `collection unpack` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | When two members have the same PostScript name, the later ones are named STEM-fontN; otc2otf writes them to the same path, so the last one wins. A user report would settle whether the reference behaviour is wanted. |
| `diff` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without --all a table lists at most 1000 removed or added lines, or 4096 hex rows; none of the reference tools caps its output, so the cap is ours. |
| `diff` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | In compiler mode the index sorting, name-ID resolution and the removal of a one-sided name ID 25 apply to both fonts alike, where ttx_diff applies some only to the fontmake side; comparing verdicts on corpus pairs would show whether this matters. |
| `diff` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The off-by-one budget is counted on B's coordinates (ttx_diff's fontmake side) and A's values are set to B's; ttx_diff's own pairing of the two sides is not symmetric either. |
| `diff` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The hex detail prints offset, bytes and ASCII per 16-byte row as a unified diff; the exact layout of sfntdiff -d 2 has not been compared. |
| `outlines remove-overlaps` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On CFF fonts --keep-hinting changes nothing: glyphs that do not change always keep their hints, as tx +V leaves them, while fontTools without --keep-hinting removes the hints of the whole font; a tx +V run on a hinted OTF settles which reference to follow. |
| `outlines remove-overlaps` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On CFF fonts head, hhea and hmtx keep their bytes after a merge (merging rarely moves the bounds; dropped small contours can); fontTools recalculates head bounds on saving, so a comparison of the head table after fontTools removeOverlaps on a corpus OTF would settle it. |
| `outlines remove-overlaps` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --min-area (default 25) only drops contours of glyphs whose overlaps were merged, so glyphs without overlaps match the fontTools result; checkoutlinesufo's behaviour on tiny contours of unmerged glyphs would settle it. |
| `outlines remove-overlaps` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --keep-contour-order has no effect: the editor's merge always keeps the order and start points of the contours it can match; checkoutlinesufo --ignore-contour-order output on a corpus font would show whether the difference matters. |
| `outlines direction` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | On CFF fonts a glyph whose contours were reversed or restarted loses its hints, as every changed CFF glyph does here; keeping the stems (they stay valid when no point moves) would need the hint masks moved, and a tx run that reverses contours would show what the reference keeps. |
| `outlines direction` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | The default flavour of a source is the PostScript convention (the editor's); a source written for TrueType outlines would need --flavour truetype. |
| `outlines extremes` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | On CFF fonts a glyph that gains extreme points loses its hints, as checkoutlinesufo drops the hints of glyphs it changes; checkoutlinesufo -e on a hinted OTF would confirm it. |
| `outlines round` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Halfway values round away from zero to the grid (Rust's rounding); fontTools' otRound rounds them up, which only differs for negative halves on a grid coarser than 1 unit. |
| `outlines round` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | On CFF fonts widths stay as they are (they are whole numbers in practice) and a rounded glyph loses its hints; the reference tools round while writing charstrings and never as a separate step, so no source settles it. |
| `outlines to-quadratic` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | A compiled font's contours are never reversed, since glyf contours already run the TrueType way; a cubic glyf font with PostScript-direction contours would show whether reversing should follow --keep-direction there too. |
| `outlines componentize` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | --detect prefers components with more contours and, of two equal outlines, makes the later glyph refer to the earlier; no reference tool detects components, so this stands until a use case asks otherwise. |
| `outlines componentize` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | --detect sets USE_MY_METRICS on the first component when its advance equals the glyph's, the rule ttfcomponentizer applies to the source's advances. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | `--gasp smooth` writes one range up to 0xFFFF ppem with flags 0x000F, the value the font-quality checks expect on an unhinted font; the reference app's export of an unhinted TrueType font would settle it. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A WOFF, WOFF2 or collection input gives a plain font file of the chosen face; keeping the container would need a decision once collections and WOFF writing are commands. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With `--keep-font-hints` the TrueType hdmx, VDMX and LTSH tables still go, since they record the hinted glyphs; only fpgm, prep, cvt, cvar and the maxp limits stay. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On a source, dehint keeps the masters' zones and stems and the font-wide TrueType data (zones, stems, font programs, settings), which also serve the design, and warns when --keep-font-hints, --hinting-tables, --keep-flex or --gasp is given; no reference tool removes hints from sources, so a request from users would settle it. |
| `dehint` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | On a source, dehint removes TrueType instructions from the main master only, where the TrueType autohinter and the Instructor store them; a source with instructions on other masters, made in the reference app, would show whether they live there too. |
| `check family` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | -l has the long name --log-output, since --log and --log-file are the global logging options; the reference has only the short form. |
