| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A palette cell is one glyph size wide and tall, plus two label lines (name, then code point and advance) at a tenth of the glyph size; the glyph is scaled so the font's ascender-to-descender height fills the cell. Settled by measuring a fontplot page of the reference script on our own font. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | An explicit --glyphs list is shown in --sort order (glyph order by default), not in the order given. Settled by running the reference script with an out-of-order -g list. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A waterfall without --text shows the characters of the selected glyphs, split into groups that fit one line at the largest size; each group is set at every size before the next group starts. Settled by a waterfallplot run of the reference script. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Pages of an SVG proof are numbered with at least three digits after the name (A.proof-001.svg); a one-page proof keeps the plain name. Nothing in the references fixes it. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A PDF waterfall draws its text as outlines (glyphs from cmap, advances at the proof's location, GPOS or kern pair kerning) instead of embedding the font, so the viewer's rasteriser and hinting are not what is seen. Settled by the owner if the PDF should embed the font as the reference's waterfallplot does. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | PDF waterfall kerning reads the kern feature of the default language system of latn, else DFLT, else the first script that has one, and the kern table only when GPOS has no kern feature; device and variation deltas are left out. Settled by comparing a waterfallplot page of the reference script on a kerned font of ours. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A character the font does not map is drawn as .notdef with its advance in a PDF waterfall. Settled by a waterfallplot run with text the font lacks. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | PDF labels and titles use the standard Helvetica font in WinAnsi encoding; characters outside it print as ?. Settled by reading the label font of a proofpdf page. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A tile of boxes, hints and digi is one em wide plus the glyph padding (and room for hint labels when hints are drawn), as tall as the font's ascender-to-descender band or the em box, with the metrics text under it. Settled by measuring a hintplot and a digiplot page of the reference script on our own font. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without --baseline the em box's bottom edge is at 0, or at -0.12 em for a CID-keyed font and for digi (the reference's -120 scaled to the units per em); the BASE table is not read. Settled by a hintplot run on a 2048-unit font and on one with a BASE table. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A glyph without hint replacement prints no 'in groups' line, and --hint-groups hides its stems unless group 0 is named. Settled by a hintplot -select_hints run on a glyph without replacement. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Hints and alignment zones come only from a CFF table; TrueType and CFF2 fonts show none. Settled by running hintplot on a TrueType font. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The compare layout puts each glyph's origin at the left of its one-em column and its baseline one glyph size below the row top, and labels rows with the font's index in the sorted set. Settled by measuring a fontsetplot page of the reference script. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The palette of palette+outline fits 320 glyphs per page, as the reference's linked palette, rather than the overview palette's 156. Settled by counting a tx -pdf -1 palette page. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | In SVG, where each page is its own file, a palette+outline cell names its outline page (data-link-page) instead of linking to it. Nothing in the references fixes it. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --list-settings lists only the settings FontCraft draws; the reference's font, margin, error-log and metadata-placement parameters are refused by --set. Settled by the owner if more are wanted. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --metrics on digi changes both copies. Settled by a digiplot -vh run of the reference script. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Each --show and --hide element switches a group of the reference script's settings (sidebearings both the horizontal and vertical lines, path-counts the contour and segment counts); they apply after the layout's preset and --black and before --metrics and --set, so --set has the last word. Nothing in the references fixes the names or the order. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The cmap sheet reads the font's best Unicode mapping once, where the reference proofs every cmap subtable in turn, and prints the code in hex (the reference's cmap=9); the decimal variant (cmap=10) is not offered. Settled by the owner if per-subtable sheets are wanted. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The vorg sheet shows the glyphs with a VORG record among those selected, and every selected glyph when there are none (a TrueType font or a VORG without records), with the origin from vmtx or the em box. Settled by running the reference's VORG proof on a font without records. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A feature sheet shows each lookup once per feature with every language system that uses it, where the reference repeats it for each script and language. Settled by the owner if the repetition is wanted. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Contextual, chained, reverse, cursive and mark lookups are named in the feature sheet but not drawn, and a class pair subtable is drawn as every pair of its class members without the second glyph's class 0, at most 50,000 rules per sheet. Settled by running the reference's feature proof on a font with contextual and class kerning. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --glyphs, a feature sheet keeps the rules whose first glyph is selected. Settled by running the reference's feature proof with -g. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --no-header removes the title line but keeps its room at the top of the page, so the layout of the rest does not move. Settled by comparing a spot -d page with one without -d. |
| `proof` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The text sheet breaks lines at the right margin glyph by glyph, not at spaces, at 48 points unless --size is given, and draws a character the font does not map as .notdef. Settled by the owner. |
| `features list` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | The text layout is ours (one line per table and feature, then an indented script/language line each, lookups after a colon; --by script swaps the two levels), since no reference prints this list in one format; JSON is the stable form. A request for spot's or comparefamily's layout would change it. |
| `features compile` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without -o the result goes beside the font as NAME.features.EXT, where feaLib's command line writes NAME#1.EXT; a request for the reference's naming would change it. |
| `features compile` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With --debug-lookups, feature code converted from FontDame or VOLT is named in the Debg table by the --emit-fea file, or <features> (feaLib's name for code without a file) when there is none; a request to name the original source would change it. |
| `features compile` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | A Debg table already in the font is replaced whole, where feaLib replaces only its com.github.fonttools.feaLib entry and keeps other entries; a font carrying another tool's entries would settle it. |
| `features lint` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Without --font or --glyph-order the compiler's messages about glyphs missing from the font are left out and hyphenated names are read as ranges; addfeatures always checks against a font. A request to check names against the code's own classes would change it. |
| `features lint` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | --warnings all adds one kind of note so far, a glyph that one mark-to-base or mark-to-ligature subtable lists both as a mark and as a base; running addfeatures -V on the same code would list the other notes worth adding. |
| `features convert` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | FontDame backtrack sequences (glyph, class and coverage rules, reverse chaining) are read nearest glyph first, as the binary stores them, and reversed for feature code; a FontDame file with a multi-glyph backtrack and the table the reference tool compiles from it would settle it. |
| `variations build-avar` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without a designspace the font's own version 1 avar is read back as user-space maps and built again (fontTools' deprecated varLib.avar prints the maps instead); a version 2 avar is refused. Comparing with a user's expectations of the reference tool would settle it. |
| `variations plan-avar` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | --plot writes an SVG chart of the normalised maps to FILE, where fontTools opens a window; comparing with how users of the reference tool use the chart would settle it. |
| `variations build-hvar` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Without --replace an existing HVAR or VVAR is kept and the run fails when nothing is left to build, where fontTools always replaces HVAR; a user's expectations of the reference tool would settle it. |
| `variations plot` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | Each master gets a panel of its own, where fontTools 4.66 draws every support on one chart (it computes a grid it does not use); the reference tool's intent, seen in its history or its users' plots, would settle it. |
| `variations plot` (cli) | M12 | docs | – | With more than two axes, --axes picks the plotted ones and masters that vary on other axes are left out, where fontTools refuses; comparing with users' needs would settle it. |
| `run` (cli) | M0 | inferred | – | A failure after some steps succeeded exits 5, as a batch with some failed inputs does; tx -s and sfntedit -X stop with their own error, so users' scripts would settle it. |
| `run` (cli) | M0 | inferred | – | With --keep-going, --save and --export are skipped when any step failed; no reference has the option, so use would settle it. |
| `run` (cli) | M0 | inferred | – | A script step that runs a subcommand and finds something (exit status 1) counts as failed; users' scripts would settle whether it should go on. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | `--read-only` admits only commands that declare themselves queries; `--allow` and `--deny` take shell-style patterns over command ids, deny winning. No reference has such options; settled by the sibling apps' conventions. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | File access is unrestricted by default; `--automation-read-root` and `--automation-write-root` each take one folder, grant read and write access separately, and are an error with `--connect`. No reference has such options; settled by the sibling apps' conventions. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | Macro, recent-document and URL commands are refused while a root, `--read-only`, `--allow` or `--deny` is set, because their files and steps are not in their parameters; agents' needs would settle whether a checked macro runner is worth building. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | No log notifications are sent until the client calls logging/setLevel; the protocol leaves the default to the server, and client feedback would settle it. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | The five prompts are start_family, add_master, build_accented_glyphs, kern_pair_list and prepare_release, with the steps of docs/mcp.md; agents' use of them would settle the wording. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | The HTTP transport listens on 127.0.0.1:7986 by default, next to the app's control port 7985; the sibling apps' choice, once one serves HTTP, would settle it. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | All HTTP sessions share one backend and so one document, each with its own log level, and a 65th session ends the one unused for longest; agents' use of several sessions would settle it. |
| `mcp` (cli) | M13 | inferred | – | The HTTP transport admits a request with an Origin header only when it is the server's own origin, refusing every web page; a browser-based MCP client that needs access would settle it. |
| `bench` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Bench's options come before the timed subcommand and everything from the subcommand on is timed, so a subcommand's own options (such as --json) never clash with bench's; the reference benchmark modules take no such command line, so use will settle it. |
| `tables show` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Records are expanded three levels deep unless --depth says otherwise; a user's need to see deeper by default would settle it. |
| `sources diff` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Masters and instances are matched by axis location first and by name second, and glyphs by name; a renamed glyph is reported as a rename when its outlines and code points are unchanged. A comparison with the companion tool on two versions of one font would settle it. |
| `sources diff` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | A change id is the part name and a hash of the change's place and both values (`glyphs:3f2a1c9b04de`), so it is stable between runs and changes when either side changes; the companion tool's own way of naming changes would settle it. |
| `sources diff` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Kerning groups are compared per glyph under the kerning part, and the non-kerning glyph groups of a UFO under the features part; how the companion tool groups them would settle it. |
| `sources merge` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Merging is two-way only (base and other), with no common ancestor; a three-way merge needs version history, which FontCraft does not keep. A user workflow that needs three-way merging would settle it. |
| `sources merge` (cli) | M12 | inferred | – | Without -o or --in-place the merge is refused rather than written beside BASE under a derived name; a user who expects a default output name would settle it. |
| `sourceDiff.show` | M13 | inferred | no | Compare with File… sits in the File menu with no shortcut; nothing in the reference app settles a menu place. |
| `window.tableViewer` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | The table list is at the left and the selected table's view at the right, with the hex pane under the tree; a look at the companion tool's window would settle the layout. |
| `window.tableViewer` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | Differing tables are marked in the list by a byte comparison, and the field-level differences of one table are shown as a line comparison of its decoded text in a Differences view rather than as marks on tree records; the companion tool's comparison display would settle it. |
| `window.tableViewer` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | The search keeps the matching records with the records above them, and matches only records already read; the companion tool's search would settle whether it reads the whole table. |

## `21-qa-checks.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `audit.run` | M3 | docs | yes | A source's instances are compiled as TrueType for the binary checks whatever outline format the source exports, and at most 32 of them; a run of the reference auditors on a source would settle whether the CFF flavour or every instance should be read. |
| `audit.run` | M3 | docs | yes | On a source, the family checks receive every instance compiled from it, the variable font included; checks that should compare only the static instances would need the reference tools' behaviour on sources to settle it. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | An outline fix on a composite glyph only reports unless `safeOnly` is false (21-qa question 4). |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | The fixes of M12 run in the three stages and the order of docs/spec/21-qa.md § 5 › Fix calls (glyph data, then layers, then kerning, feature code and hints); no reference tool fixes all of these, so a case where the order changes the result would settle it. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | On a family of files, syncFamilyValues takes the widest vertical metrics and the underline values of the font the check compares the others with, as the source fix does with masters; a reference fixer's output on such a family would settle it. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | alignHandles turns both handles onto the line that bisects their two directions, keeping their lengths; a type designer's review of fixed kinks would settle whether one handle should stay put instead. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | setGdefClasses makes a glyph that must not be a mark a Symbol when the glyph database classes it as a mark; a review of the fixed glyphs' categories would settle it. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | cleanKerning leaves a tabular glyph that is kerned through a group shared with other glyphs in the report, because removing the group pair would change their kerning; an exception pair of zero is the other choice, which a review of fixed fonts would settle. |
| `audit.fix` | M3 | docs | yes | A compiled font fixed with inPlace through a symbolic link replaces the file the link points to and keeps the link, with that file's permissions; fixing a linked font with the reference tools would settle whether the link should be replaced instead. |
| `audit.profile.save` | M12 | inferred | yes | Without store the profile goes to the user's settings rather than the project; a user report would settle which store people expect by default. |
| `audit.profile.delete` | M12 | inferred | yes | Deleting a profile that another profile of its store includes is refused rather than removing the include; a user report would settle it. |
| `audit.profile.setDefault` | M12 | inferred | yes | target defaults to the document, so a source carries its own default; a user report would settle whether the application default is expected instead. |
| `audit.ignoreIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | An entry made from a result keeps table-level keys but not outline addresses, which change with every edit; a user report would settle whether point-level entries are wanted. |
| `audit.unignoreIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | A call that matches no entry is refused rather than doing nothing, so a mistyped check id is noticed. |
| `audit.listIgnored` | M12 | inferred | yes | Whether an issue still occurs is judged only against the last kept run, with null when that run did not include the check, rather than running the check again. |
| `audit.export` | M12 | docs | yes | Without `format`, the format follows the extension of `path` (.json, .txt, .md, .html) and is JSON for any other; a look at how the command line is used would settle it. |
| `audit.export` | M12 | docs | yes | A result document can be passed in as `results` instead of naming a target, so a run on files can be exported without being kept; settled once the run on compiled fonts keeps its results. |
| `audit.export` | M12 | docs | yes | The Markdown and HTML reports end with the checks that were skipped or failed to run, with their reasons; the reference tools' reports would settle whether to list them. |
| `audit.start` | M12 | inferred | yes | A run in steps examines the font as it was when it started, so edits made between steps make its results stale rather than changing them; people using the panel would settle whether a run should restart instead. |
| `audit.step` | M12 | inferred | yes | A step examines 256 layers unless the call says otherwise; measuring the panel's frame time on a large font would settle the number. |
| `audit.checkOutlines` | M12 | docs | yes | A fix of a file given by path is refused; the command line opens the source, fixes it and saves it, so the engine command only fixes the open document. |
| `audit.checkOutlines` | M12 | docs | yes | The clean-up keeps node types, smooth flags and node names; the reference redraws the glyph through a segment pen, which loses smooth flags and point names. Running it on a UFO with smooth points would show whether that matters to users. |
| `audit.checkOutlines` | M12 | docs | yes | Only paths are cleaned up and reordered; components keep their places in the shape list and their offsets, fractional ones included. The reference decomposes components before testing, so a fixed glyph never has any. |
| `audit.checkOutlines` | M12 | docs | yes | `quiet` drops the results list and keeps the counts; the reference's quiet mode still prints how many glyphs it skipped, which the summary covers. |
| `audit.checkInterpolation` | M12 | docs | yes | `showAll` lists the glyphs without problems in `glyphsWithoutProblems` instead of one line per glyph as the reference prints; its JSON output would settle the shape. |
| `audit.compareFamily` | M12 | docs | yes | The label is Check Family and the results go to the Font Audit panel, never to the Compare Family window, so the two features with similar names stay apart; the id stays for the command line (21-qa.md question 13). |
| `audit.compareFamily` | M12 | docs | yes | `reports` only adds the tables; the reference's -rm also turns on its menu-name checks, which run here whenever their test numbers are selected. Running the reference with and without -rm on our fonts would settle whether the checks should follow the option. |
| `audit.report.metrics` | M12 | docs | yes | The underline thickness, which comparefamily does not print, is listed after the underline position and sorted the same way; the row's summary asks for it. |
| `audit.report.panose` | M12 | docs | yes | Two misspelled value names of comparefamily's tables are spelled correctly (Borders, Literals); a reader comparing text output would notice the difference. |
| `audit.showPanel` | M12 | inferred | yes | The Font Audit panel opens from the Window menu with no shortcut, because the reference app has no audit panel; a later decision on shortcuts would settle it (21-qa.md question 1). |
| `audit.showPanel` | M12 | inferred | yes | The panel opens on the target This Font with the document's default profile; nothing in the reference app settles it. |
| `audit.panel.run` | M12 | inferred | yes | Family of Open Fonts reads the saved files, not unsaved edits; an engine target for several open documents would settle it. |
| `audit.panel.cancel` | M12 | inferred | yes | Cancel lists the partial results rather than going back to the earlier list; people using the panel would settle which they expect. |
| `audit.panel.previewFix` | M12 | inferred | yes | A preview runs only when asked (Preview Fix), not on every selection change, because a dry run re-checks the layers; measuring a dry run on a large selection would settle whether it can follow the selection. |
| `audit.panel.exportReport` | M12 | inferred | yes | The export row offers HTML first, as the most readable report; people using the panel would settle the default. |
| `audit.revealIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | Reveal reuses a tab that already edits the glyph instead of always opening a new one as the export results dialog's Show button does, so stepping through results does not pile up tabs; watching Show in the reference app with a tab already open settles how it treats an open tab. |
| `audit.revealIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | Font Info opens on the tab that holds the field without focusing the field; a Font Info command that focuses a field would settle it. |
| `audit.nextIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | No menu item and no shortcut: the panel's arrow buttons and the Edit View marker badges run it, because the reference app has no audit panel (21-qa.md question 1). |
| `audit.previousIssue` | M12 | inferred | yes | No menu item and no shortcut: the panel's arrow buttons and the Edit View marker badges run it, because the reference app has no audit panel (21-qa.md question 1). |
| `audit.toggleLiveAudit` | M3 | inferred | yes | Live audit is off when the app starts; the default is ours. |
| `audit.toggleMarkers` | M3 | inferred | yes | Markers are on when the app starts; the default is ours. |
| `audit.filterResults` | M12 | inferred | yes | The severity filter is three choices (all, warnings and failures, failures) rather than a toggle per severity, because the command takes a minimum severity; a later design review would settle it. |
| `audit.editProfile` | M12 | inferred | yes | The reference app has no audit profile editor, so the dialog is our design: it opens on the panel's profile, else the default one; people using it would settle whether that is the profile they expect. |
| `audit.closeProfileDialog` | M12 | inferred | yes | Saving a draft with changed project values is one undo step (a command.batch of audit.config.set and audit.profile.save); the reference app has no such dialog, so people using it would settle it. |
| `path.eraseOpenCorners` | M12 | inferred | yes | A corner counts only when the crossing lies in the later half of the first segment and the earlier half of the second, so the part dropped is the smaller one; watching the reference's Erase Open Corners on a corner with a long loop would settle it. |
| `path.eraseOpenCorners` | M12 | inferred | yes | The new corner node and the handles of the split curves are rounded to the font's grid; checking the coordinates the reference writes after erasing a corner on a curve would settle it. |
| `component.flatten` | M12 | inferred | yes | The flattened components keep the inner components' settings but have automatic alignment off, so the drawn outline cannot move; comparing with the reference's flatten filter output on an automatically aligned composite would settle it. |
| `tables.recalculate` | M12 | inferred | yes | A monospaced font gets PANOSE proportion 9 (family kind 2) or spacing 3 (kinds 3 and 5), and a proportional font with proportion 9 gets 0; comparing with what a reference fixer writes on the same fonts would settle it. |
| `tables.recalculate` | M12 | inferred | yes | Code page bits 30 and 31 keep their stored values while the others are recomputed from the cmap, since the check does not compare them either; a reference fixer's output would settle it. |
| `tables.recalculate` | M12 | inferred | yes | Smart dropout control is appended to the end of an existing prep program; a hinting tool's fix of the same font would settle whether it goes first. |
| `tables.cleanNameRecords` | M12 | inferred | yes | The PostScript name records take the CFF font name when the font has one, else the Windows English record; a reference fixer's choice on a font whose names disagree would settle it. |
| `tables.rebuildCmap` | M12 | inferred | yes | Macintosh and other non-Unicode subtables are left out of the rebuilt table, as current compilers write it; a reference fixer's output on a font with a Mac Roman subtable would settle it. |
| `tables.recompileCff` | M12 | inferred | yes | UIDBase stays in a CID-keyed font, which the cffUniqueIds check also leaves alone; a reference fixer's output on a CID-keyed font would settle it. |
| `tables.recompileCff` | M12 | inferred | yes | The accent of a seac glyph loses its hints and the base keeps them only when the glyph has none of its own, as in the CFF2 conversion; comparing with the reference's decomposition of seac glyphs would settle whether hints should be merged. |
| `tables.recompileCff` | M12 | inferred | yes | The table is subroutinised again even when the input had no subroutines; a reference fixer's output would settle whether the input's subroutine choice should be kept. |
| `tables.cleanInstances` | M12 | inferred | yes | Instances are sorted by the other axes in axis order, then by weight, so weights rise within each group; a reference fixer's order on a two-axis font would settle it. |
| `tables.cleanInstances` | M12 | inferred | yes | When some records lack a PostScript name (0xFFFF), the PostScript name field is removed from every record rather than names invented; a reference fixer's output would settle it. |
| `font.info.syncVerticalMetrics` | M12 | inferred | yes | The line gap moves into the ascenders so the hhea line height stays as it was; a reference tool that recommends vertical metrics would settle it. |
| `font.info.syncVerticalMetrics` | M12 | inferred | yes | winAscent and winDescent take the extremes of all masters, so every instance between them is covered; a reference tool's recommendation would settle it. |
| `font.instance.syncStyleLinking` | M12 | handbook | yes | An italic style whose Bold flag was set by hand keeps its flag and its link, since the handbook allows linking bolds to other styles; nothing in the reference settles it. |
| `font.instance.syncStyleLinking` | M12 | handbook | yes | A link is made only to a style the font exports as a static instance; otherwise the link stays as it was. |
| `font.info.syncFamilyValues` | M12 | inferred | yes | The first master stands for the regular face whose underline values the family takes; a family-level recommendation in a reference tool would settle it. |
| `font.info.cleanNames` | M12 | inferred | yes | Line breaks become spaces in every name, the licence text included, because check.names.lineBreaks reports them anywhere; `keepLineBreaks` keeps them. |
| `audit.glyphHashCache` (behavior) | M3 | docs | – | Only the layer checks are cached: several glyph checks read glyphs no component names (kerning groups, metrics keys), and the reference's hash map covers only its outline checks; a profile of long runs would show whether glyph checks need a cache with their own dependencies. |
| `audit.glyphHashCache` (behavior) | M3 | docs | – | Edit-tab texts and the font note are left out of what the cache compares, since no layer check reads them. |
| `audit.glyphSelectionSyntax` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | A piece that is a glyph name is read as a name even when it holds a hyphen, and `/N` names the glyph `cidNNNNN` (five digits) before glyph index N; a CID-keyed source in the reference tool would settle it. |
| `audit.outlineFixOrder` (behavior) | M3 | docs | – | The findings of binary checks are not computed again between layer steps; their calls (widths, anchors, emptying a glyph) do not depend on point addresses. Comparing with a run that recompiles before each step would settle whether it matters. |
| `audit.familyGrouping` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | On a compiled font without its source the style comes from name IDs 2 and 17 and the fsSelection bits, never from the file name; only check.files.canonicalFileName reads the file name (21-qa.md question 9). |
| `audit.validatorKnownIssues` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | W2106 (OS/2 version below 3) is treated as wrong for OS/2 version 2 and later, and a ssNN tag counts as registered for 01 to 20 and cvNN for 01 to 99; a run of the validator on fonts with those values would settle it. |
| `audit.exitStatus` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | Results hidden by --min-severity still make the exit status 1 when they reach --fail-on; the reference tools have no separate display threshold, so nothing settles it. |
| `check.outlines.missingExtremes` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `minDistance` of 1 unit is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.almostStraightCurve` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxDeviation` of 2 units is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.almostOrthogonalHandle` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The defaults `maxAngle` 2° and `maxOffset` 2 units are first guesses to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.nearCoincidentPoints` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `minDistance` of 1 unit is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.coordinatesOutOfRange` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxEmMultiple` of 4 em is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.nearAlignmentZone` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `margin` of 2 units is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.kinkedSmoothNode` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxAngle` of 1° is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.outlines.kinkedSmoothNode` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | When one side of the smooth node is a straight segment, the fix turns only the handle onto the line's direction and leaves the line; running the reference's fix on such a node would settle it. |
| `check.outlines.unbalancedHandles` (check) | M3 | inferred | – | The default `maxRatio` of 4 is a first guess to tune on real fonts (21-qa question 5). |
| `check.components.nested` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Only glyphs that export are checked, since the reference check reads the compiled font; checking the exported TrueType font with the reference check would settle it. |
| `check.components.transformed` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A source does not say whether its TrueType export will be hinted, so a mirrored component (negative determinant) is a failure and any other transform a warning; the reference check makes both failures on hinted fonts and only mirroring on unhinted ones. |
| `check.anchors.unknownName` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | Anchors on corner, cap, segment and brush component glyphs are not checked, because their roles are not documented; observing which anchor names those glyphs use in the reference app would settle it. |
| `check.glyphset.notdef` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A font that stores no glyph names has .notdef as glyph 0, as the reference reads such fonts; a font with stored names whose .notdef is missing is settled by the reference on a TTF with a version 3 post table. |
| `check.glyphset.notdef` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A cmap entry pointing at glyph 0 means "no glyph" in cmap, so only a .notdef placed elsewhere is reported as having a code point; the reference on a font mapping a character to glyph 0 would settle it. |
| `check.glyphset.minimumCoverage` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We read only the built-in glyph sets and skip a value that is a file path; a project that keeps its own glyph-set file would settle how files are named and read. |
| `check.glyphset.duplicateCodepoint` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | The finding sits on the later glyph in glyph order and names the others; the reference app's export message for two glyphs with one code point would settle it. |
| `check.glyphset.nameCodepointMismatch` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | Only exporting glyphs are checked, and a glyph with no code point whose name implies one is reported too; the reference app's glyph info on such glyphs would settle it. |
| `check.metrics.monospaceFlags` (check) | M12 | docs | – | One rule decides monospacing: at least 80% of the ASCII glyphs share one width, as the conformance checker of the opentype profile does; comparefamily's all-glyph hint is not reported separately (21-qa.md question 8). |
| `check.metrics.verticalMetricsScheme` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The `scheme` parameter defaults to the Google Fonts scheme (0); no reference names a default, so the owner's choice of house scheme would settle it. |
| `check.metrics.baseGlyphWidth` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Glyphs that GDEF classes as marks are left out by glyph id; FontBakery compares glyph ids with mark glyph names, so its own mark exclusion never applies. A font with a zero-width enclosing mark (Me) not in GDEF would settle whether the reference warns. |
| `check.metrics.whitespaceWidths` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The conventional advances of the other space characters come from a vendor profile's check and are reported as warnings under this universal check rather than as a separate strict check; running the reference conformance checker's vendor profile on the corpus would show whether failures there are common enough to deserve their own check. |
| `check.metrics.tabularWidths` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The tnum targets are found by applying the feature's single substitutions directly instead of shaping, so contextual or default-feature substitutions are ignored; comparing with the reference checker on corpus fonts whose tnum feature is contextual would settle it. |
| `check.metrics.fixedFractionWidths` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The en dash is held to half the em like the dot leaders, although comparefamily's typo means it never checks it; running comparefamily with the typo fixed on the Oswald families would show whether the rule misfires. |
| `check.metrics.accentedWidths` (check) | M12 | docs | – | When the accented glyph has no width key, audit.fix keys its width to its first component's glyph, standing in for the base glyph the check derives from the name; a fix run in the reference on a glyph whose first component is not its base would settle it. |
| `check.names.lineBreaks` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We treat a carriage return as a line break as well as a line feed; a FontBakery run on a name with a lone carriage return would show whether the verdicts differ on purpose. |
| `check.names.versionString` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The house-rule messages (Version prefix, three decimals, above 1.000, the word Development) are informational by default, so the opentype verdict equals FontBakery's opentype/font_version; a profile can raise them. Comparing verdicts on corpus fonts with short version strings would show whether that suits users. |
| `check.names.subfamilyMatchesStyleBits` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We read the style bits from OS/2 fsSelection rather than head.macStyle; a comparefamily run on a font whose two bit sets disagree would show the difference. |
| `check.names.recommendedStyleLinking` (check) | M12 | handbook | – | The check is in the strict profile, as an informational lint: the handbook calls linking a non-bold style as a bold allowed, so the universal profile would flag a legitimate choice; a reference report on such a family would settle it. |
| `check.names.recommendedStyleLinking` (check) | M12 | handbook | – | Styles are matched by their style names (Regular, Bold, Italic, Bold Italic, NAME Italic), not by weight class; a family named in another language would settle whether weight classes should be read too. |
| `check.names.macRecordsComplete` (check) | M12 | docs | – | With keepMacNames set, a font with no Macintosh records at all gets every missing record reported, where comparefamily checks Mac records only when the font has some; the project setting says the records are wanted. |
| `check.names.legalAndCredits` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We ask for Macintosh designer-note and URL records only in fonts that keep Mac records, since check.names.noMacRecords asks for none; a comparefamily run on a font without Mac records shows the reference's stricter rule. |
| `check.names.legalAndCredits` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We compare the URL with defaultUrl exactly once white space at either end is trimmed; a comparefamily run with a URL that differs only in case or a trailing slash would settle it. |
| `check.tables.lowestRecPpem` (check) | M12 | docs | – | We report head.lowestRecPPEM below 6 pixels per em; the validator note names 3 as the value it questions but gives no limit, so a survey of shipped TrueType fonts would settle it. |
| `check.layout.defaultLanguageSystems` (check) | M12 | docs | – | latn/dflt is asked for only in fonts whose character map holds a basic Latin letter (A-Z, a-z); the legacy family comparison asks for it in every font with features, and its findings on non-Latin fonts would settle it. |
| `check.layout.smallCapsGlyphs` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The letters smcp must cover are the mapped lowercase letters with an uppercase partner (uppercase with a lowercase partner for c2sc), limited to the scripts of the letters the feature does cover; the reference checker only reports substituted glyphs that do not exist (also reported here), and its results on fonts with partial small caps would settle it. |
| `check.variations.pathCount` (check) | M12 | docs | – | An intermediate layer is compared with the nearest master layer of its glyph; running the reference tool on a designspace that holds intermediate layers as sparse sources would settle whether its pairs agree. |
| `check.variations.instanceOutsideAxisRange` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | Instances that do not export are left out; whether the reference app's own audit counts them would be settled by an inactive instance placed beyond the masters. |
| `check.variations.statItalAxis` (check) | M12 | docs | – | One font at a time, an italic variable font fails without an ital axis in STAT and a missing upright sibling is not reported; FontBakery pairs the fonts of a family. Comparing verdicts on corpus families with an upright and an italic variable font would settle whether the family checks must cover the pairing. |
| `check.variations.statValueQuality` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The values an axis must name are the OS/2 weight classes (100 to 900) and width classes (50 to 200) inside the fvar range; the vendor check takes them from its own axis registry, with names and flags. Comparing verdicts with that check on the corpus variable fonts would settle it. |
| `check.variations.staticInstantiation` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Instancing is tested by computing every glyph's outline and advance at the middle of each axis and reading the variation tables, not by running an instancer; comparing verdicts with FontBakery on fonts whose instancing fails would settle whether this catches the same faults. |
| `check.hinting.zonesOverlapInSource` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A zone counts as holding a metric line when the master's position of any metric except the italic angle, or 0, lies inside it or on its edge; how the reference app judges zones that serve no metric would settle it. |
| `check.hinting.stemsUndefined` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A direction counts as undefined when none of the master's stems of that direction has a positive value; stems of value 0 count as missing. How the reference autohinter treats a zero stem would settle it. |
| `check.kerning.kernTable` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The fix only removes the table (tables.delete); regenerating a format 0 table from GPOS for encoded glyphs is not built, and nothing says which of the two a user expects first. |
| `check.kerning.tabularKerning` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Without a tnum feature every glyph except marks is checked against the figures; FontBakery also adds marks whose Unicode category is a number or a symbol, which a comparison on fonts with such marks would settle. |
| `check.kerning.tabularKerning` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The fix removes a stored pair only when every glyph pair it kerns is reported, and never splits a group or adds a zero exception; no reference tool fixes this result, so a user report of a font where that leaves kerning in place would settle it. |
| `check.kerning.groupsDifferBetweenMasters` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Only kerning groups (public.kern1 and public.kern2) are compared, member order ignored, one result per differing group; FontBakery compares every group of the file and reports once per source. |
| `check.kerning.missingGlyphReference` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A group side with no member on the side facing the pair counts as dangling, since it is what removing the group's glyphs leaves; observing the reference app's export would settle whether it treats it the same. |
| `check.kerning.redundantPairs` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A pair's fallback is the next pair that applies without it, in the order glyph-glyph, glyph-group, group-glyph, group-group, as the UFO specification orders them; the reference app's kerning window would settle whether it orders exceptions the same. |
| `check.kerning.excessivePair` (check) | M12 | inferred | – | A group side stands for its narrowest glyph in that master, glyphs without width are left out, and vertical kerning is not checked; nothing documents the reference's rule. |
| `check.family.uniqueStyleLinking` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Bold and italic come from OS/2 fsSelection, or head macStyle without an OS/2 table (the legacy family comparison reads macStyle); comparing the two tools' results on the legacy corpus families would settle it. |
| `check.family.consistentFamilyName` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The per-language comparison sits behind a `strict` threshold (default 0) so the opentype profile keeps the basic rule; the measure against the legacy comparison runs with strict 1. |
| `check.family.panoseFamilyType` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The legacy rules (all zero, first four only) sit behind a `strict` threshold (default 0) because the check's home is the opentype profile; the measure against the legacy comparison runs with strict 1. |
| `check.family.superfamilyVerticalMetrics` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The sibling families are the typographic families among the fonts given in one run, since a family check sees fonts, not folders; the folder listing becomes one informational result naming the families. |
| `check.family.layoutConsistency` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Fonts are compared within typographic families rather than across every font given, which is the same when one family is checked; running both tools on a folder holding two families would show any difference. |
| `check.family.glyphWidthRatio` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Glyph names come from the post table or the CFF charset; fonts without names get made-up names per glyph id, which compare by glyph order; checking a post format 3 family against the reference would settle it. |
| `check.family.familyBlues` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The zone limits are those of the CFF specification (14 values for FamilyBlues, 10 for FamilyOtherBlues); the reference allows 14 for both, so a family with 11 to 14 FamilyOtherBlues values would show the difference. |
| `check.family.italicsHaveUprights` (check) | M12 | docs | – | An italic font's partner is an upright font of the same typographic family with the same weight and width classes (any upright variable font for an italic variable font), read from the style bits instead of the file names the reference reads; running both on a family with a missing upright would settle it. |
| `check.family.tabularFigureWidths` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Inconsistent widths fail, as in the stricter of the two reference checks, while the suffix rule and the half-width note follow the other; comparing both reference checks on the corpus families would settle it. |
| `check.files.fileSize` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A font compiled in memory is measured by its uncompressed sfnt bytes, so a WOFF2 export can warn here and pass in FontBakery; measuring the compressed output would settle it. |
| `check.files.sanitiser` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Unsorted, duplicated, misaligned, overlapping and out-of-bounds tables are failures and wrong search fields a warning, as we read the sanitiser's published behaviour; running the sanitiser on fonts built with each fault would settle it. |
| `check.files.roundTrip` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A table that reads back with different bytes is not reported, because FontBakery reports only errors of the dump and the read; comparing verdicts on the corpus would settle it. |
| `check.files.rasterises` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Every glyph is drawn, where FontBakery loads one character with FreeType, so this check can fail where FontBakery passes; the verdict measure on the corpus would show how often. |
| `check.files.externalValidator` (check) | M12 | docs | – | One face audited alone counts as part of a family, so the allowance for a slanted face without the italic bit (italicAngleWithoutItalicBit) never applies here; running the validator on a single slanted face and comparing would settle it. |
| `check.files.canonicalFileName` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The family comes from name ID 16, else 1, and the style from ID 17, else 2, with spaces removed, and an empty style counts as Regular; axisregistry's build_filename would settle it. |
| `check.files.canonicalFileName` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A .woff or .woff2 file keeps its extension; any other file is expected to end in .otf for CFF outlines and .ttf otherwise. |
| `check.sources.consistentGlyphSet` (check) | M12 | docs | – | The check runs on every source with several masters, not only on designspace documents as in FontBakery; for .glyphs sources it can repeat a missing-glyph finding of the variations checks. |
| `check.sources.requiredInfoFields` (check) | M12 | docs | – | A master's metric counts as unset only when its value list ends before it; a value stated as 0 between others cannot be told from an unset one. |
| `check.sources.recommendedInfoFields` (check) | M12 | docs | – | Underline thickness and position and the version are never reported: the model gives them the reference's defaults, so they always have a value. |
| `check.sources.unnecessaryInfoFields` (check) | M12 | docs | – | versionString and uniqueID are reported in .glyphs sources too, since they compile to the same name records as the UFO keys FontBakery names. |
| `check.cid.definedTwiceOrMissing` (check) | M13 | docs | – | Two glyphs mapped to one CID count as the CID defined twice, as a glyph in two sub-fonts does; a glyph with a CID in no sub-font is reported with its own code, because the source formats can say that and the reference's sub-font model cannot. |
| `check.cid.definedTwiceOrMissing` (check) | M13 | docs | – | Missing CIDs are reported as runs between CID 0 and the highest CID that has a glyph, one result per run; a CID mapped to a name the font has no glyph for counts as missing. |
| `audit.panel.fix` | M12 | inferred | no | Fix runs the selected rows' fixes with safeOnly false, since the person chose them; watching how people use the panel would settle it. |
| `audit.panel.ignore` | M12 | inferred | no | Ignoring several rows at once is one undo step (a command.batch); nothing settles it. |
| `window.fontAudit` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | Fix runs the selected rows' fixes with safeOnly false, since the person chose them, while Fix All Safe keeps safeOnly true; watching how people use the panel would settle it. |
| `window.fontAudit` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | Ignoring several rows at once is one undo step (a command.batch); nothing settles it. |
| `window.fontAudit` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | The panel sits at the top right of the window and lists sixteen rows before it scrolls; a layout review would settle it. |
| `dialog.auditProfile` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | A built-in profile opens as a read-only draft that includes it, and Duplicate turns that into a user profile `<id>-copy` that includes the built-in one instead of listing its checks; nothing settles it. |
| `dialog.auditProfile` (panel) | M12 | inferred | – | Project values are edited in the profile dialog although they belong to the document, not to the profile, because the inventory row lists them there; a design review would settle it. |

## `30-formats.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ufo.openTypeCategories` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | glyphsLib classifies after propagating anchors into composites; a layer without anchors of its own counts its components' attaching anchors, which gives the same class for the usual accented composites. Settled by comparing with glyphsLib's UFOs of the corpus. |
| `ufo.openTypeCategories` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | Custom glyph data files do not change the classes, as glyphsLib reads only its built-in data here. Settled by a reference export with a custom GlyphData file. |
| `roundtrip.editsAreLocal` (behavior) | M1 | inferred | – | Version 2 settings keys at the top level of a version 3 file move into the `settings` dictionary once any setting is edited, since the dictionary written after them would override them on reading; a file from the reference app with such keys, saved after an edit, would settle what it writes. |
| `roundtrip.editsAreLocal` (behavior) | M1 | inferred | – | Node flags are read for `R`, `C` and `X` in any order (the first of `R` and `C` wins); when an edit changes the lock or orientation, the fields are written in the writer's order followed by the letters the model does not read, in their order. A file from the reference app with such flags would settle what it keeps. |
| `roundtrip.binaryPassThroughTables` (behavior) | M12 | inferred | – | Stored tables are copied only when the whole glyph order is unchanged, since each of them indexes glyphs by id; settled by opening a TrueType font with an LTSH or hdmx table in the reference app, exporting it unchanged and checking whether the table is still there. |
| `glyphs3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | A gray colour `(gray, alpha)` is read as an RGBA colour with equal components, and the shape remembers which of its colours the file wrote as gray so that they are written as gray again. Settled by watching whether the reference app writes a colour picked from the gray swatches as gray. |
| `glyphs3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | A version 3 `strokePos` stored as text that is not a plain number stays a stored value, because it cannot be converted to the version 4 convention. Settled by finding a file whose stroke position is an expression. |
| `ufo3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | A full-colour layer read from a colour layer list is named `Color`, the name the reference gives a new full-colour layer. Settled by opening a UFO whose lib has a colorLayers paint in the reference, if it reads one. |
| `ufo3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | glyphsLib does not fold `<glyph>.color<i>` glyphs back into colour layers when it reads a UFO; the reader folds them so a saved source opens as it was. Settled by opening such a UFO in the reference, if it reads the list. |
| `ufo3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | A full-colour layer whose runs glyphsLib would not split the same way (paths and components mixed) is split as the compiler merges runs, in shape order; glyphsLib puts paths before components. Settled by comparing glyphsLib's minimal UFOs of such a source. |
| `ufo3` (format) | M1 | docs | – | Colours of the list's full-colour layers that are not palette entries are added to the end of every palette, painted with alpha 1 and their own alpha in the palette, as the compiler adds them; glyphsLib adds them the same way but also puts the alpha in the paint. Settled by comparing the COLR of the reference's UFO export of a full-colour font with fontmake's build of it. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A font-info value that ufoLib cannot convert (an unknown `fontStyle`, `widthName` or `msCharSet`, or a value of the wrong type) is dropped and the file opens; ufoLib and norad refuse the file instead. A UFO 1 from the tools of that time with such a value would settle whether refusing is better. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The feature code is joined as defcon joins it (a line break between the classes and each feature, tags sorted when the lib has no order); norad leaves out the line breaks between features. Both texts compile the same; a UFO 1 whose features only work with one of the two joins would settle it. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A tag the feature order lists twice is written once, so a crafted order cannot multiply the feature code; defcon writes it each time. |
| `ufo1` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Font-info keys a UFO 1 does not define are dropped, as ufoLib drops them, so the saved UFO 3 holds only valid keys. |
| `variable-ttf.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | A variable font beyond the limits (more than 256 avar points on an axis, more than 2,000,000 layers to make) opens as its default instance with the reason among the warnings rather than being refused; settled by checking what users expect when a huge variable font opens. |
| `variable-ttf.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | A peak becomes a master when every coordinate is -1, 0 or 1, or when at least half of the glyphs with variations use it; any other peak becomes an intermediate layer of the glyphs that use it, on the nearest master. Settled by opening fonts built from sources with known masters and intermediate layers and comparing. |
| `variable-ttf.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | A master takes the name of the named instance at its location, else its axis names and design coordinates; an intermediate layer is named by its coordinates in braces. |
| `cff2.open` (format) | M9 | inferred | – | Every region of the CFF2 variation store becomes a master, since the charstrings do not say which glyphs use which region without evaluating them. Settled by opening CFF2 fonts built from sources with intermediate layers and comparing. |
| `cff2.export` (format) | M9 | docs | – | A glyph whose masters' PostScript hints differ in structure, or that varies with an intermediate layer, is written without hints and with a warning, where fontTools' merge of hinted masters refuses the whole font; a variable CFF2 export from another tool chain that hints such glyphs would settle what to write instead. |
| `ttc.export` (format) | M12 | docs | – | With `collection: true`, a destination file ending in .ttf or .otf is refused instead of receiving collection bytes under a single-font name; a user's expectation, or a reference tool that writes collections, would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Vertical metrics are measured on the glyphs d, H, x and p (falling back to the FontBBox), since a Type 1 font has no such fields; the reference app's import of a .pfb would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Ghost hints become TopGhost and BottomGhost hints with place (edge, 0), the edge being y for a top ghost and y + dy for a bottom ghost as tx reports them; the reference app's import of a hinted .pfb would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Standard stems are named hStem0, hStem1… and vStem0…; the reference app's import of a .pfb would show its names. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A font whose glyphs together hold more than 20,000,000 hint and path operations, or take more than 500,000,000 charstring steps to run, is refused rather than read in part; no limit of the reference readers is documented, and real fonts stay far below both. A font past them in the reference app would settle it. |
| `type1` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A repeated glyph name gets .1, .2…; when the name is too long for its suffix, it is cut to 247 bytes first so the result stays within 255. Importing a font with repeated long names into the reference app would settle it. |
| `afm` (format) | M5 | docs | – | A glyph's code is its first Unicode value when that is at most 255, else -1; Adobe's note means a code in the font's encoding. An AFM exported by the reference would settle it. |
| `pfm` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A word before the pair list equal to `etmKernPairs` is a count and is skipped (and gives the count when `etmKernPairs` is 0); the DDK describes no such word, but files made for PostScript printers are said to have it. A PFM file from a font vendor would settle it. |
| `pfm` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Character codes of a font whose `dfCharSet` is not 2 (symbol) are read as Windows-1252; a symbol font's code c is looked up as U+F000 + c, then as c. PFM files of symbol fonts from a vendor would settle it. |
| `pfm` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Kerning amounts are in `etmMasterUnits` per em (1,000 when the field is 0, as the DDK says PostScript drivers assume) and are scaled to the font's units per em, rounded half up. |
| `cff` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A font whose glyphs together hold more than 20,000,000 hint and path operations, or take more than 500,000,000 charstring steps to run, is refused rather than read in part; no limit of the reference readers is documented, and real fonts stay far below both. A font past them in the reference app would settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | An open subpath in a glyph's path data is read as closed, since SVG fonts fill every subpath; no reference reader was available to settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A glyph without glyph-name is named from its characters through the glyph database (uniXXXX when it cannot be loaded), ligatures joined with underscores; a reference importer of SVG fonts would settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | Only the first missing-glyph element is read, as .notdef; later ones are left out with a warning. A reference importer of SVG fonts would settle it. |
| `svg-font` (format) | M12 | docs | – | A repeated glyph name gets .1, .2…; when the name is too long for its suffix, it is cut to 247 bytes first so the result stays within 255. Importing a font with repeated long names into the reference app would settle it. |
| `bdf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Glyph codes count as Unicode code points when `CHARSET_REGISTRY` is ISO10646, or ISO8859 with `CHARSET_ENCODING` 1, or missing; other registries match glyphs by name only. Importing a BDF in another registry into the reference app would settle it. |
| `bdf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph with no `DWIDTH` and no font-wide default advances by its bitmap's right edge, with a warning. The note requires `DWIDTH`, so only a malformed file reaches this. |
| `bdf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Grey BDF is read from and written as `STARTFONT 2.3` with bits per pixel as the fourth `SIZE` value; TN 5005 covers 1 bit per pixel only. A grey BDF exported by the reference app would settle it. |
| `bdf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A bitmap font opened on its own gets 1,000 units per em, the default of a new font, with widths and vertical metrics scaled from pixels and rounded to whole units; no handbook page covers opening bitmap fonts. Opening a BDF in the secondary reference would settle the units per em it picks. |
| `bdf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A bitmap font opened on its own gets no `.notdef` and no outlines; its glyphs are the file's bitmaps only. Opening a BDF in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bdf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | When a bitmap font names two glyphs alike, the later one is renamed `.1`, `.2`…; when two glyphs share a code, the later one opens without a code point but its bitmap keeps the code. A BDF with repeated names and codes opened in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `pcf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A PCF file without a glyph names table names its glyphs `glyph0`, `glyph1`…; opening such a file in the reference app would show what it calls them. |
| `pcf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | When several codes of the encodings table point at one glyph, the glyph keeps the lowest and a warning counts the rest; the reference may add copies instead. A PCF with a shared glyph opened in the reference app would settle it. |
| `pcf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | `.pcf.gz` is written as one gzip member at the best compression, with no file name and time 0. Whether the reference writes `.pcf.gz` at all is not documented; a font folder written by it would settle it. |
| `fnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Glyphs read from an FNT file are named `uniXXXX` from their code point in the ANSI character set, and `charNNN` from their 8-bit code in any other; opening an FNT in the secondary reference would show the names it picks. |
| `fnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Codes whose table entry repeats the default character's width and offset are read as missing, not as copies of it, and the writer fills codes without a glyph that way; an FNT written by the secondary reference with a gap in its codes would settle it. |
| `fnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Pixels per em are the cell height minus the internal leading, and the writer sets the internal leading to the cell height minus the strike's pixels per em; the format states only point size and resolution. An FNT the secondary reference writes from a known strike would settle it. |
| `fnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Character sets other than ANSI are kept as raw 8-bit codes under registry `MICROSOFT`; a strike in another X registry is written with the ANSI character set and its codes unchanged. A non-ANSI FNT exported by the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `fnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | `WEIGHT_NAME` maps to and from the Windows weight through Thin 100, ExtraLight 200, Light 300, Medium or Regular 400, SemiBold 600, Bold 700, ExtraBold 800 and Black 900, and reading picks the nearest name. |
| `fon` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Opening a FON file gives one font whose glyphs come from its first font, with one strike per font; the secondary reference may open each font as its own document. Opening a two-size FON in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `fon` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The writer uses the smallest resource alignment, from 16 bytes up, that addresses every resource, a module name made from the face name, and resource flags moveable and preloaded for the directory and moveable, pure and discardable for the fonts; a FON written by the secondary reference would settle these. |
| `pk` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A PK or GF strike is `design size × vertical pixels per point` pixels per em, rounded, and its resolution is the pixels per point times 72.27 (TeX points per inch), rounded; the format states no pixel size. Opening a PK file in the secondary reference would show the size it gives the strike. |
| `pk` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A PK or GF glyph is named after its code: the character itself for an ASCII letter or digit, else `char` and the decimal code; the file has no names. Importing a PK font into the secondary reference would show the names it picks. |
| `pk` (format) | M13 | docs | – | PK and GF codes are in the TeX font's own encoding (OT1, T1 and others; code 123 is an en dash in cmr10), so a PK or GF strike is marked `CHARSET_REGISTRY` `TeX`, `CHARSET_ENCODING` `FontSpecific` and its bitmaps match glyphs by name only, and opening one gives glyphs without code points; letters and digits still match by their names. A PK written from a Unicode strike reads back the same way. Importing a cmr10 PK into the secondary reference would show whether it maps TeX codes to Unicode. |
| `pk` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Writing PK leaves out bitmaps without a code and bitmaps whose code an earlier bitmap has, and gives the file a design size that reads back at the strike's pixel size. |
| `gf` (format) | M13 | docs | – | GF codes are in the METAFONT program's own encoding, not Unicode, so a GF strike is marked `CHARSET_REGISTRY` `TeX`, `CHARSET_ENCODING` `FontSpecific` and its bitmaps match glyphs by name only, as for `pk`. Importing a GF font into the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `nfnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Only the first font family of a Mac file, and only its plain style, is read; a family's bold or italic strikes are separate fonts, not strikes. Opening a Mac suitcase with several styles in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `nfnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A Mac strike without a font family record is taken to be its ascent plus descent pixels per em, with a warning; only the family record states the point size of an `NFNT`. |
| `nfnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Mac codes below 32 map to the same code points; a font that draws symbols there (such as command-key glyphs) gets control-character code points for them. |
| `nfnt` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Blank rows above and below a Mac character's bitmap are trimmed so its box is tight, as BDF and PCF boxes are; the record keeps every character as tall as the font rectangle. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A contour is closed when its last point equals its first and open otherwise; the page does not say how an open contour is written. A file from the originating editor with an open path would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | In a quadratic layer a `c` line is read as a quadratic segment when both controls are the same point or are exactly two thirds of the way to one point; the page does not say how quadratic splines are written. A quadratic file from the editor would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | An on-curve point flagged 0x80 that lies halfway between two control points is dropped, so runs of quadratic control points come back. A TrueType-derived file from the editor would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The point flags of a closed contour's start come from its `m` line, not from the line that returns to it. A file with a smooth start point would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | `Refer` and `Kerns2` glyph numbers are the third number of a glyph's `Encoding` line (its original position), or its position in the file when that is missing; a reference that matches no glyph falls back to its code point. Files with reordered glyphs would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph without a `Width` line is as wide as the em. A file with such a glyph would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Anchor names follow the reference's convention: the class name for base and base-mark anchors, `_` before it for marks, `_1`, `_2`… after it for ligature components, `entry` and `exit` for cursive anchors. The page names only the classes. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Pair positioning is lookup type 258 (0x102), read from the page's example; subtables no lookup declares count as kerning. A file with several lookup types would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Glyph pairs take precedence over class pairs, and an earlier class table over a later one, as in the model; in OpenType the order of subtables decides. A file with overlapping subtables would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A kerning class becomes a group named after its first glyph; a glyph already in a group on that side kerns by name instead. Files with a glyph in classes of two tables would settle it. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Cap height and x-height, which the format does not store, are the tops of H and x, or 70% and 50% of the em; overshoots are zero. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | Hhea, typo and win values marked as offsets from a computed value are not kept, because the page does not say how the value is computed. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | The digits after the point in `Version` are thousandths (`1.5` is version 1.500). |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A TrueType instruction block (`TtTable`, `TtInstrs`) ends at a line starting `EndTt` or at the first line that is neither a mnemonic nor numbers; the page names no end line. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph layer the font does not declare (the multi-layered Type 3 form) is merged into the master layer. |
| `sfd` (format) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph listed in more than one class on one side of a class kerning table belongs to the first of them; the later listings are skipped and reported. A table from the originating editor with such a repeat would settle which class it keeps. |
| `pdf-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | The frame for placement is the crop box, else the media box, with one point per unit and its top edge at the given top, matching EPS and SVG import; importing a PDF of known geometry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `pdf-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | A hairline stroke (line width 0) is given a stroke width of one unit; a reference import of a hairline would settle it. |
| `pdf-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | A path both filled and stroked keeps its stroke width and is marked as filled; a reference import of such a path would show which style it gets. |
| `pdf-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Paths that only clip, or end without painting, are left out and counted as dropped, as for EPS; a reference import of a file with a clipping path would settle it. |
| `pdf-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Only the first page is read, and its /Rotate is applied before placing; importing a two-page, rotated PDF in the reference app would settle both. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Unscaled placement keeps one point per unit and puts the top edge of the bounding box (else of the drawing) at the given top, matching how SVG files are placed; importing an EPS of known geometry in the reference app would settle it. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Paths that are only used to clip, or ended without painting, are left out and counted as dropped; the reference may import every path, which importing a file with a clipping path and an unpainted path would settle. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Arcs (arc, arcn, arct), text and images make the file fail with a message rather than being skipped; an EPS with an arc or a text object imported in the reference app would show whether it skips them or converts them. |
| `eps-outline` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | A path filled inside gsave/grestore and stroked again afterwards is imported once; a reference import of such a file would settle it. |
| `image-jpeg` (format) | M11 | handbook | – | A converted image keeps its file name with a `png` extension, numbered when that name is taken, and the original name is not kept. Settled by exporting a font with a JPEG image to UFO from the reference and looking at its images folder and GLIF image element. |
| `image-pdf` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | The page is drawn on a transparent background, not white, so the glyph shows through around the artwork; placing a PDF in the reference app would show which it uses. |
| `image-pdf` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | Text and images inside the PDF are not drawn; the reference draws them through the operating system, and a PDF with text placed in the reference app would show the difference. |
| `image-pdf` (format) | M13 | handbook | – | A placed PDF goes into a bitmap strike only for a glyph without paths and components, as the image of an empty glyph is always shown, and other placed images stay out; the reference has no strikes, so this is our choice. |
| `proof-html` (format) | M12 | inferred | – | The default sample is an English pangram with digits when the font maps all of its characters, else the first 64 characters of the character set. Settled by the owner. |

## `40-tables.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `compile.instanceInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | Copied from fontmake's instancer: a glyph with no layer in the origin master is left out of the instance; a glyph whose layer is empty in some masters but drawn at the origin interpolates without the empty ones; outlines are not rounded before the compile; a glyph that does not export and cannot interpolate keeps its nearest layer instead of failing the instance. Settle each with an instance export from the reference app. |
| `compile.instanceLayoutInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A variable font's kerning groups are the font's glyph groups in every master; a UFO master whose own groups differ is read with the glyph groups. A designspace whose masters' groups differ would settle it against fontmake. |
| `compile.instanceLayoutInterpolation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | When a master's mark rules differ from the origin's (an anchor missing in one master), the mark positions keep the origin's values with a warning instead of failing the export. An export from the reference app of such a font would settle it. |
| `compile.removeOverlapsStatic` (behavior) | M2 | docs | – | A composite glyph keeps its components at export, so components that overlap each other keep their overlaps, following fontmake; how the reference app exports such a glyph is not documented. |
| `compile.dropImpliedOnCurves` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | Implied on-curve points are dropped by default, as fontc writes glyphs, where fontmake keeps them; settled by exporting a TrueType font from the reference app with a smooth node that lies halfway between the quadratic control points around it and counting the glyph's points. |
| `compile.dropImpliedOnCurves` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | With implied points kept, an on-curve node of a quadratic source contour that lies exactly halfway between its off-curve neighbours is written as implied, because the model's quadratic segments do not tell such a node from an implied point; settled by the same export of a TrueType source with such a node. |
| `compile.glyphsOutlinePreSteps` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | A glyph whose anchor propagation fails keeps its own anchors and the build goes on with a warning; exporting a source with a component cycle from the reference app settles what it does. |
| `compile.productionNames` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | Use Custom Naming (disablesNiceNames) keeps the source names at export, as the handbook's Don't use Production Names entry says it has the same effect; the reference compilers ignore it. Settled by exporting a font with Use Custom Naming on from the reference app. |
| `compile.productionNameSanitising` (behavior) | M4 | docs | – | A name that is empty after cleaning, or longer than the 255 bytes post can store, becomes glyphNNNNN (by glyph id) with a warning; fontc would write the empty or long name. Settled only by a reference that writes such a font. |
| `compile.cmapLegacyOptions` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | The stub keeps a format 14 subtable of Unicode Variation Sequences, as it keeps the Macintosh subtable; settled by a makeotf build with -stubCmap4 and -ci. |
| `compile.styleLinking` (behavior) | M7 | docs | – | Bold or Italic set on a designspace instance whose stated style-map style is outside the four (which reads as neither) wins over that style: it compiles with the flags' bits and name, and the designspace is written with the flags' style. No source covers editing such an instance; the reference app's handling of a stated style it does not know would settle it. |
| `compile.styleLinking` (behavior) | M7 | docs | – | A single-source designspace whose instance states no style-map names takes ufo2ft's fallbacks from the instance's style, where fontmake keeps the source UFO's style-map names; a fontmake build of such a source with style-map names in its UFO would settle it. |
| `compile.fontMenuNameDb` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | An entry with the older c= key is read with c= taken as the compatible family name, with a warning; compiling a c= entry with makeotf would settle what the older scheme writes. |
| `compile.fontMenuNameDb` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A FontMenuNameDB without the font's PostScript name falls back to the source's family and style names, with a warning; makeotf says it derives the names from the PostScript name, which compiling such a file would show. |
| `compile.nameFeaOverrides` (behavior) | M8 | docs | – | In a variable font, whose axis and instance names are allocated after the layout stage, a code name whose ID meets one of them is left out with a warning, so the axis names stay right; allocating the variable names before the layout stage, as fontc does, would settle it. |
| `compile.os2WeightAdvice` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | The project compile checks only the lower limit (a weight class below 250), since it builds one font at a time; checking the bold partner's range needs the family, which a family-level check would settle. |
| `compile.os2FsSelection` (behavior) | M2 | observed | – | A project file's kSetfsSelectionBitsOn or kSetfsSelectionBitsOff may hold a list as makeotf writes one (['7', '8']), which makeotf 5.0.1 cannot read back (its wrapper stops with a Python error); FontCraft takes the bits it lists. A makeotf release that reads its own lists would settle it. |
