| `compile.feaTableOverrides` (behavior) | M8 | docs | – | An `OS/2` block with only one optical size writes the other as 0, the zero feaLib leaves in the fields a raised version adds; a fontmake build of a source with only `UpperOpSize` would settle it. |
| `compile.kernGroupReconciliation` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A static instance interpolates kerning as fontmake's instancer does (fontMath): a pair one master lacks takes the value that master's groups give it (the pair, the second side's group, the first side's group, both groups, else zero: the compiled kern lookup's order, where fontMath tries the first side's group before the second); a master read from its own UFO uses that file's groups; a pair that comes out zero is dropped unless one side is a glyph in a group; values are rounded half up. The common refinement of the groups is for variable fonts (M9.9); settle by comparing an instance of a source whose masters group glyphs differently with fontmake. |
| `compile.kernFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | Where the featureWriters setting asks for what fontc refuses to build (ignoreMarks off, a quantization other than 1, kernFeatureWriter2), FontCraft writes the kerning with the standard settings and warns; a build with fontmake would settle what the setting does there. |
| `compile.kernFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M5 | docs | – | The featureWriters setting is read from the font's user data, then from the built master's; fontc reads only a .glyphs font's user data, while glyphsLib also carries a master's into its UFO lib. |
| `compile.markFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | A top-level include in the feature code keeps all five generated features out until M8, as it keeps the generated kerning out, since the included file may hold any of them; compiling the included file (M8) settles it. |
| `compile.markFeatureWriter` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | Anchors whose names the reference compilers refuse (`_`, `_top_1`, a number 0), whose ligature number is above 1,024, or whose positions do not fit 16 bits are left out with a warning, where fontc stops the build; an export of such anchors from the reference app would settle it. |
| `compile.gdefClasses` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | A static master's classes are worked out from that master's anchors only, as fontmake does for each master's UFO, where fontc looks at the anchors of every master; a source whose masters differ in anchor names would settle it. |
| `compile.gdefLigatureCarets` (behavior) | M6 | docs | – | Carets at the same position are all kept, as fontc keeps them, where ufo2ft keeps one; a ligature with two carets at one position in the reference app's export would settle it. |
| `compile.bracketLayerGlyphs` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | When the font already has a glyph named like an alternate glyph (`a.BRACKET.varAlt01`), the alternate takes the next free number instead of replacing or clashing with it; settle by exporting such a font from the reference app and reading the glyph order. |
| `compile.bracketLayerGlyphs` (behavior) | M9 | docs | – | A kern pair on a glyph with alternate layers is copied to its alternate glyphs the way glyphsLib copies it, not to every combination of alternates as fontc does; settle by exporting a variable font with a pair between two glyphs that both have alternate layers and reading its kerning at a location inside both ranges. |
| `compile.cffSubroutinisation` (behavior) | M2 | docs | – | A CFF2 subroutine may hold some of the operands of a blend while its caller holds the rest; whether cffsubr ever does the same is not checked, and a dump of a CFF2 font it subroutinised would settle it. |
| `compile.cidKeyedFonts` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A sub-font with a PrivateDict takes only the values it gives, CFF defaults for the rest, and one without a PrivateDict takes the font's hinting values; a makeotf build of a UFO with a sub-font that lacks a PrivateDict, or one that sets only BlueValues, would settle it. |
| `compile.cidKeyedFonts` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A glyph in no FDArraySelect group is written with sub-font 0, with a warning; a makeotf build of a UFO with such a glyph would settle it. |
| `compile.cidKeyedFonts` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A glyph the CID map leaves out, and a second glyph on a CID already taken, follow the highest CID in glyph order, with a warning, as export.cidMapping describes the reference app; a makeotf build of such a UFO would settle what makeotf does. |
| `compile.cidKeyedFonts` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | The Name INDEX holds the CID font name when the source gives one, else the PostScript name; a makeotf build of a UFO whose CIDFontName differs from its PostScript name would settle it. |
| `compile.cidCmapResources` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A glyph name in a Unicode Variation Sequences file is matched against the source's glyph names, not the names a GlyphOrderAndAliasDB gives; a makeotf release build with renaming and -ci would settle which names it reads. |
| `compile.cidCmapResources` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | Of two lines for the same character and selector the first counts; a makeotf build of such a file would settle it. |
| `compile.cidCmapResources` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | Without a Unicode CMap the source glyphs' own code points are encoded instead of refusing the build as makeotf does; a CID-keyed export from the reference app without the collection's data would settle what it encodes. |
| `compile.cidCmapResources` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | The Macintosh subtable's language field holds the -cl value as given, 0 by default; a makeotf build with -cl would settle it. |
| `compile.cidCmapResources` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | Without -cm the stub Macintosh subtable takes the -cs script, else the ordering's; only a Latin ordering (Roman, 0) was observed, so a makeotf build of a Japan1 font without -cm would settle it. |
| `compile.makeotfOptionPrecedence` (behavior) | M12 | docs | – | makeotf 5.0.1 stops with a Python error on a project file whose kSetOS2Version, kSetfsSelectionBitsOff, MacCmapScriptID or MacCmapLanguageID holds a number (its wrapper passes the number on unconverted, read from its source, not run); FontCraft takes the number as the key documents it. A makeotf release that reads them would settle it. |
| `compile.makeotfMetrics` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A negative advance in the source is written as 0 with a warning, where makeotf writes it wrapped to a large unsigned number; a font whose layout depends on that value would settle whether to follow it. |
| `compile.makeotfMetrics` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A project compile with autohinting turned on writes the autohinted glyphs with integer coordinates, as every autohinted CFF glyph is written; makeotf does not autohint, so only a request for both would settle it. |
| `compile.makeotfMetrics` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | A CID-keyed project compile keeps the integer coordinates of every CID-keyed build; comparing makeotf's CID-keyed output point by point would settle it. |
| `compile.kernClassOptimisation` (behavior) | M12 | observed | – | The project compile keeps the size order of class pair subtables instead of makeotf's order of naming, and leaves out the empty class record -skco adds; neither changes a pair's value, and an application that reads class 0 differently would be the reason to follow makeotf. |
| `compile.colrGradientGeometry` (behavior) | M11 | docs | – | The ellipse angle of a version 4 radial gradient is not written: COLR circles cannot be squashed without a transform paint. Settled by exporting a radial gradient on a wide path from the reference and reading its paint. |
| `CFF2` (table) | M9 | docs | – | Charstrings are not specialised or subroutinised; byte size against fontmake's output would show whether that is worth doing. |
| `gasp` (table) | M10 | docs | – | A CFF export gets no gasp table even with the parameter, as ufo2ft writes gasp for TrueType output only and the handbook describes the parameter for TrueType fonts; a CFF export from the reference app with the parameter would settle it. |
| `gasp` (table) | M10 | docs | – | A gasp Table parameter with no usable range counts as no source, so a hinted font gets the default ranges; an export from the reference app with an empty parameter would settle it. |
| `DSIG` (table) | M12 | docs | – | The stub DSIG is off by default, as fontmake and fontc build fonts; settled by checking whether the reference app's export writes a DSIG table. |
| `EBDT` (table) | M12 | docs | – | A composite image's component is drawn with its offset measured from the composite's top-left corner, rows counting down, clipped to the composite's box, the darker pixel winning where components overlap; the OpenType text says only that the offsets give the component's left and top. A font with composite bitmaps opened in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `EBDT` (table) | M12 | docs | – | A glyph listed twice in one strike keeps its first image; fontTools keeps the last. A font with a glyph in two index subtables opened in the secondary reference would settle it. |

## `90-secondary.toml`

| Row | Milestone | Status | Live | Guess |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `view.showTunniLines` | M13 | docs | yes | Tunni lines show for cubic segments only; quadratic segments get none. Watching the reference app on a font with quadratic curves would settle it. |
| `path.selectTunniLines` | M13 | docs | yes | A segment counts as inside the selection when one of its handles is selected, both its end nodes are, or the segment itself is, as for Fit Curve; FontLab does not define it, and selecting nodes in FontLab with Tunni lines on would settle it. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | Where a segment's handles are parallel or diverge, so they have no intersection, tension is shown as a dash and Set Curve Tension leaves the segment unchanged with a warning; FontLab does not say (open question 9 in docs/spec/90-secondary-references.md). |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | A zero-length handle has no direction, so its segment counts as having no Tunni point and is left unchanged too; running the command on such a segment in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | With no segments named the command acts on every curve segment of the layer, and it also takes a list of glyphs; FontLab documents only the selection. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | An absolute tension must be 1 to 100 percent and a relative step keeps each handle within 1 to 100; FontLab gives no range, and entering larger values in its Node panel would settle it. |
| `path.setCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | A relative step is added to each handle's own tension, so unequal handles stay unequal; FontLab does not say whether it balances them first. |
| `view.showCurveTension` | M13 | docs | yes | Each handle's tension prints as a whole percentage beside the handle's end, and the lengths-and-angles readout is not drawn with it. A screenshot of the reference app with the option on would settle both. |
| `path.harmonizeNodes` | M13 | docs | yes | At an inflection (the far handles on opposite sides of the tangent) the two curvatures are made equal in size, since equal signed curvature is impossible there; running Harmonize on an S-curve in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.harmonizeNodes` | M13 | docs | yes | A smooth node with a straight side, or a selected node that is not between two curve segments, is left unchanged and listed in the result; FontLab does not say. |
| `path.harmonizeHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | G2 keeps the sum of the two handle lengths at the node and only divides it differently; FontLab does not say which lengths it keeps, and comparing handle lengths before and after Harmonize Handles in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.harmonizeHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | G3 changes the lengths of the two neighbouring handles along their own directions, by the smallest change found, so the neighbouring nodes keep their tangents; FontLab does not describe Superharmonize's method. |
| `path.harmonizeHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | With no node selected both orders act on every smooth node of the layer, as Harmonize Nodes does; FontLab documents only the selection. |
| `path.togglePowerNudge` | M13 | docs | yes | From each moved node the walk goes both ways along the contour over unselected smooth nodes that are not extremes (a handle horizontal or vertical) and stops at the first corner node, extreme or locked node, which stays fixed; FontLab does not document how it sorts fixed from servant points, and moving one node of a contour with intermediate smooth nodes in FontLab would settle it. |
| `path.togglePowerNudge` | M13 | docs | yes | A servant moves by the drag times its position between the fixed node and the moved node on each axis (by length along the contour where the two share that coordinate), its handles with it; between two moved nodes, or towards an open end, servants move by the whole drag; FontLab says only that servants move proportionally. |
| `path.togglePowerNudge` | M13 | docs | yes | Power Nudge acts on plain moves only: duplicating drags and nudge drags (Alt) move no servants. |
| `path.setServantNode` | M13 | docs | yes | axis both declares a servant on both axes; FontLab offers only X-Servant and Y-Servant, and turning both on for one node in FontLab would show whether that is possible. |
| `path.setServantNode` | M13 | docs | yes | A declared servant follows only when it is reached from a moved node over other servants (or Power Nudge's smooth nodes), not from anywhere on the contour; FontLab does not say which edits count as neighbouring. |
| `path.makeInkTrap` | M13 | docs | yes | The two copies move half the width each, perpendicular to the corner's bisector, with the copy ending the incoming segment on that segment's side, and their neighbouring handles move with them; the reference only says the nodes move apart 'intelligently', so watching it trap an inside and an outside corner would settle it. |
| `path.makeInkTrap` | M13 | docs | yes | Without an inkTrapWidth parameter the width is 2% of the em; the reference's default for a new font would settle it. |
| `path.loopCorner` | M13 | docs | yes | Each segment goes on past the corner by `size`, which defaults to half the master's first vertical stem (4% of the em without stems); the reference does not say how far its loops reach, so measuring one would settle it. |
| `path.replaceWithCorner` | M13 | docs | yes | One segment or gap per path per call, and neighbours whose extensions meet behind them are refused rather than shortened; trying both in the reference would settle it. |
| `path.alignToMask` | M13 | docs | yes | Points snap to background nodes and handles alike, and a moved on-curve node takes its unselected handles along; aligning a curve's node to a background node in the reference would settle it. |
| `path.collapsePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | The centre is the middle of the selected nodes' bounding box, rounded to the grid, and at least two on-curve nodes are needed; collapsing three uneven nodes in the reference would settle it. |
| `path.scaleToSameSize` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph counts as uppercase when its case says so or one of its code points is an uppercase letter; trying a glyph without code points in the reference would settle it. |
| `edit.storeSelection` | M13 | docs | yes | Selected anchors and components are stored with the nodes, by anchor name and shape index; FontLab documents node selections only, and its panel on a selection with a component would settle it. |
| `edit.storeSelection` | M13 | docs | yes | Storing an empty selection is refused; FontLab's Add button with nothing selected would settle it. |
| `edit.getStoredSelections` | M13 | inferred | yes | A query that maps stored selections onto any layer is our addition for agents and scripts; FontLab has no such command. |
| `edit.recallSelection` | M13 | docs | yes | On a layer where only part of a stored selection resolves (an incompatible master), the parts that resolve are selected and the reply counts the rest as missing; recalling where nothing resolves is refused. FontLab's panel on an incompatible master would settle it. |
| `view.lockOutline` | M13 | docs | yes | The lock drops the pointer and key edits whose command changes paths, nodes or components, and a drag that moves nodes and anchors together is refused whole. The reference app with a mixed selection would settle it. |
| `view.lockOutline` | M13 | docs | yes | Menu shortcuts run commands by name, so they still edit the outline while it is locked. The reference app's Delete key and transform shortcuts while locked would settle it. |
| `view.showStemWidths` | M13 | docs | yes | The detection range is a view setting with the documented defaults, not stored in the font; the reference's per-font storage would settle where it lives. |
| `view.showStemWidths` | M13 | docs | yes | Diagonal stems are compared with the vertical stem values; watching a slanted stem's label colour in the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.showCounterWidths` | M13 | docs | yes | A counter is measured between neighbouring stems only, at the first place along their overlap that is white; the reference app's placement of the label would settle it. |
| `view.showCornerAngles` | M13 | docs | yes | The angle is the one between the two segments, so the inner and outer corners of a crossbar both read 90; whether the reference shows the ink side's angle (270 on a concave corner) is open. |
| `view.showCornerAngles` | M13 | docs | yes | The zoom at which labels appear is our choice. |
| `view.showQuickMeasurement` | M13 | docs | yes | With Shift, the axis is the one closer to the unrestricted measurement's direction. |
| `autometer.measure` | M13 | inferred | yes | A stem counts from 20 to 250 units wide and as uncommon within 5 units of a master stem value by default; measuring a thin hairline and a heavy stem in the reference app with its default detection range would settle it. |
| `autometer.measure` | M13 | inferred | yes | Diagonal stems compare with the vertical stem values; measuring a slanted stem next to declared vertical stems in the reference app would settle it. |
| `autometer.quickMeasure` | M13 | inferred | yes | Without an axis the distance runs from the nearest outline point through the given point to the next segment beyond; Quick Measurement over a bowl in the reference app would settle whether it measures along the normal instead. |
| `autometer.setStemWidth` | M13 | inferred | yes | Both edges move by half the change unless one edge lies on a metric line, which stays; editing a stem number of a stem standing on the baseline in the reference app would settle it. |
| `font.recalculateMeasurements` | M13 | docs | yes | The sample glyphs (x, H, d, p, o, O, n, H, e) and taking the most frequent stem width are our choice; the reference documents neither. |
| `font.recalculateMeasurements` | M13 | docs | yes | Stem values the font has no stem definition for are skipped, not added. |
| `view.showDetailsAcrossGlyphs` | M13 | docs | yes | The other glyphs on the edited glyph's line (at most 200) get their nodes, handles and the display aids that are on (indexes, Tunni lines, tensions, lengths, beam values); selection and metric-line highlights stay with the edited glyph. The reference app on a word would settle which details it draws. |
| `view.suggestHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | Every suggestion switch starts off; the reference's defaults are not documented, and a fresh install of the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.suggestHandles` | M13 | docs | yes | Nodes, handles, guides and metric lines within reach win over a suggestion, and a suggestion wins over alignment with another node; dragging a handle near both in the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.suggestLines` | M13 | docs | yes | A segment or handle counts as nearby when one of its ends is within 200 screen points of the pointer; the reference does not give a reach, and dragging a node at growing distances from a segment in the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.suggestLines` | M13 | docs | yes | A diagonal guide's perpendicular passes through the guide's own point; the reference does not say where, and dragging a node across a diagonal guide's surroundings would settle it. |
| `view.suggestLines` | M13 | docs | yes | Centrelines come from pairs of straight vertical or horizontal edges whose runs overlap; hints and thick guides add none of their own (a thick guide's centreline is the guide, which plain snapping covers); checking hinted glyphs and thick guides in the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.suggestDistance` | M13 | docs | yes | The distance across an edge running up and down is x, across one running left and right is y, and an edge at an angle gets \|nx\|·x + \|ny\|·y for its normal (nx, ny); parallels go round corners; drawing a diagonal stem with different x and y values in the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.suggestStems` | M13 | docs | yes | Only straight edges that run up and down or left and right are matched, against straight edges; round and diagonal stems get no suggestion; dragging a bowl's side and a diagonal in the reference app would settle it. |
| `guide.makePowerGuide` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `nodes`, every on-curve node of an unlocked path within 0.5 units of the guide is linked; off-curve nodes are not linked and move with the on-curve node before them, else after them. |
| `guide.makePowerGuide` | M13 | docs | yes | Links name nodes by index, so an edit that renumbers nodes leaves a link on another node; a link that no longer resolves is ignored. |
| `guide.makePowerGuide` | M13 | docs | yes | Moving a power guide is one undo step for every glyph it reshapes, and locked glyphs keep their outline while the rest move, with a warning naming them (open question 3). |
| `guide.addPowerGuides` | M13 | docs | yes | Diagonal stems get no power guides; the manual names horizontal and vertical stems only. |
| `guide.unlinkPowerGuides` | M13 | docs | yes | Unlinking with nothing to unlink fails with a message instead of adding an empty undo step. |
| `guide.movePowerGuide` | M13 | docs | yes | Unlinked nodes beyond the outermost power guides stay where they are; the manual speaks only of nodes between power guides. |
| `guide.movePowerGuide` | M13 | docs | yes | Links to guides that are not parallel to the moved one play no part in the move, so nodes held by them move like unlinked nodes. |
| `guide.setThickness` | M13 | docs | yes | A positive thickness puts the second edge on the left of the guide's direction, a negative one on the right. |
| `guide.addMarkers` | M13 | docs | yes | Marker positions are measured along the guide from its knob, in the guide's direction; `clear: true` removes them all. |
| `component.copyTransformation` | M13 | docs | yes | A target entry without `glyph` or `layer` means the source glyph and the source layer's id; the reference copies to whatever is selected, so it has no such default. |
| `component.copyTransformation` | M13 | docs | yes | An automatically aligned target takes the scale, rotation and slant but keeps the position alignment gives it on the aligned axes; the reference has no automatic alignment, so nothing settles this but use. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A match needs every point of the other glyph's outline within 0.5 unit after the best translation, or after a translation and flip when `flips` is on; FontLab gives no tolerance. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Only glyphs whose layer has closed paths and no components serve as bases; running it in the reference app on a font whose base glyphs are themselves composites would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Bases are tried from the most nodes down, and a base must come before the target in an order of nodes, then combining marks (a name whose part before the first suffix ends in 'comb' or 'cmb', unless the glyph database calls it an enclosing mark), then fewer suffixes, then glyph order (no cycles), so of two glyphs with the same outline the combining mark, or the name without a suffix, is the base of the other, and largeCircle is the base of enclosingcirclecomb; comparing the reference app's choice between identical outlines (acute and acutecomb, colon and colon.tf, largeCircle and enclosingcirclecomb) would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Of bases with the same outline that match the same paths, in order: one that is not a look-alike of a basic Latin letter among them, that is, not a glyph that detection found to be a copy of that letter or found the letter to be a copy of (A rather than Alpha in Alphatonos, though Alpha is in the recipe and the name, whichever of A and Alpha comes first in the font); one whose code point is in the target's canonical decomposition or that the glyph database's recipe for the target names; the one whose name without suffixes and 'comb' or 'cmb' spells the longest part of the target's name, reading 'sub' as 'below' (circumflexsubcomb in dcircumflexbelow); one whose suffix gives it the target's case (.uc, .case and .tonos upper, .lc lower, .sc, .c2sc and .smcp small capitals; uni1FFE.tonos rather than dasia in uni1F09), then one of no other case (the case from a suffix, else the glyph database); fewer suffixes the target lacks (one rather than one.tf in one.circled); a combining mark; the shortest shift (two.numr rather than two.dnom in a fraction). Running the reference app on flattened fonts with such pairs would settle each step. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph that detection itself finds made of two or more glyphs (a copy of such a glyph counts too) is not used as a base for part of a larger glyph, unless it is a combining mark or a mark by the glyph database (stacked marks such as circumflexacutecomb and psilivaria are often built from other marks), or it holds a mark, or is a copy of a glyph that holds one, and the glyph database gives it no canonical decomposition (i, carondot.lc, and icyr drawn as a copy of i); it still serves a glyph whose whole outline it is. Running the reference app on a flattened font with Abreve and Abrevedotbelow, and i and tworoman, would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `layer` or `layers` every master layer is searched, each on its own, and a glyph whose masters got different components is warned about; the reference app's behaviour on a multi-master font would settle it. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A mirrored match is a mirrored copy drawn in the opposite direction, as a mirrored component draws once decomposed; the offset is rounded to the font's grid when the match still holds. |
| `font.detectComposites` | M13 | docs | yes | A detected component keeps the default automatic alignment when aligning puts it where the outline was (within the tolerance) and leaves the width as it is; alignment is turned off on the components it would move, and on all new components of a layer whose width it would change, as the primary reference's Import Metrics does for metrics that alignment would change. Checking the alignment setting of components the reference app detected, with and without matching anchors, would settle it. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | The first glyph in font order whose same-id layer has only closed paths equal to the selection wins, within 0.5 unit after a translation (and a flip with `flips`); selecting an outline shared by two glyphs in the reference app would settle which one it picks. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | No matching glyph is an error that changes nothing; the reference app's message or silence when nothing matches would settle it. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | The new component keeps the default automatic alignment unless aligning would move it beyond the tolerance or change the width, and then alignment is off on it; alignment is judged on the layer as it ends up, so a component next to remaining paths keeps the default. Checking the alignment setting of a component the reference app detected would settle it. |
| `path.detectComponent` | M13 | docs | yes | Hints keep their nodes: the ends of PostScript hints on the replaced paths become numbers at their places, TrueType instructions and corner components on them are removed and reported; a hinted glyph in the reference app settles it. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | `masters` defaults to every master, which covers the reference's companion action that copies the recipe to all masters; seeing whether that action is a command of its own would settle it. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `recipe`, a glyph keeps the recipe one of its layers has, else takes the components its name decomposes into by Create Composite's rules; the reference's default recipe is not documented. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | An automatic layer holds only the recipe's components: paths or components added by hand are replaced at the next rebuild; whether the reference lets one draw on an automatic layer would settle it. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | A recipe whose components contain the glyph is refused, and a layer whose recipe becomes circular later stays empty. |
| `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` | M13 | docs | yes | Recipe parts are glyph names only, without placement suffixes such as `@anchor`. |
| `anchor.removeExpressions` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyph` or `glyphs` it acts on every glyph, and locked glyphs are skipped and listed; the reference acts on the selection. |
| `anchor.setExpressions` | M13 | docs | yes | Expressions are set by a command of their own, where the reference types them into the anchor's coordinate fields; a syntax error refuses the command. |
| `dialog.actions` | M13 | docs | yes | Actions sits in the Script menu beside the action-set commands, without a shortcut; FontLab's menu place and shortcut are not documented in the sources read. |
| `script.runActionSet` | M13 | docs | yes | Action sets and macros are one file format and one command; FontLab keeps action sets in its Actions folder apart from scripts, a split FontCraft does not copy. |
| `script.saveActionSet` | M13 | docs | yes | An existing file is replaced only when `replace` is true, otherwise the save is refused; FontLab's documentation does not say what saving over an existing set does. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | Both edges of a stem move by half the change, so the stem keeps its centre line; the secondary reference running a rule on a stem away from the sidebearings would settle it. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | A horizontal stem's edge on the baseline, x-height, cap height, ascender or descender stays and the other edge takes the whole change; the secondary reference on a serif sitting on the baseline would settle it. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | A round stem (the sides of o) is adjusted by moving the extreme nodes on both sides with their handles; one without a node at either measured point is left alone. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | A rule that would make a stem narrower than 1 unit makes the whole command fail; the secondary reference with an offset larger than a stem would settle it. |
| `filter.adjustStems` | M13 | docs | yes | One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | Method optical is FontCraft's own (open decision D11, docs/plans/M13.md § 3): for each side, the side profile sampled every `step` units (default 5) over a band from the baseline less its overshoot to the reference height plus its overshoot is clipped to `depth` inward from its extreme; the distance from the advance edge to the extreme plus a weight times the clipped profile's mean depth equals `area`. Neither FontLab nor FontForge documents its method; comparing the results on a font spaced in either app would show how far they differ. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | The clipping depth defaults to 10% of the x-height: on the corpus measure (docs/plans/M13.md § 5) shares from 5% to 10% did about equally well and 15% or more did worse. Spacing a test font in FontLab with its defaults would show whether its depth is similar. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | The mean depth is taken over the rows with ink rather than over the whole band, so a glyph with ink in part of the band (a period, a hyphen) is not pushed away by the empty rows; FontLab's and FontForge's pages say nothing about such glyphs. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | The area and the depth weight of a group are calibrated on the font's own n, o and v (capitals and figures: H, O and V) as they stand: a least-squares line through their six sides, white outside the extreme against mean depth, gives the weight as its slope (held between 0 and 2) and the area as its value at depth 0, so a straight, a round and a diagonal side keep about their spacing. With only straight controls the weight is 1. A group without its control glyphs takes the other group's; a master without any has weight 1 and is refused unless `area` is given, which replaces the area and keeps the weight. On the corpus measure at the same depth, fitting the weight lowered Inter's worst median error from 89% to 75% against a weight fixed at 1, with Oswald's about the same (12%). FontForge asks for a separation instead; it accepts `separation` as twice the area. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | Capitals and figures (case upper or category Number) use the cap-height band, everything else the x-height band. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyphs`, every exported letter and figure is spaced; without `masters`, every master. Masters that take their widths from another are skipped. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | Sidebearings are rounded to whole units, then held between `min` and `max`. Method custom defaults to 5% of the em. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | A side whose field holds a metrics key keeps its value, and a layer with a width key or under automatic alignment is skipped, so keys stay in sync. FontLab's page does not say what auto-spacing does with its own metrics expressions. |
| `spacing.autoSpace` | M13 | docs | yes | `beam` applies to tight and custom: both sidebearings are measured where the outline meets the beam. Optical refuses it, as the profiles cover the whole band. |
| `view.showMetricsBeam` | M13 | docs | yes | The beam is dragged in whole units, only on the line itself (within half the node hit tolerance) and only outside the advance boxes of the edited glyph and the other glyphs on its line, so nodes and marquees near it stay with the tools; the reference apps' pages do not say where the line can be grabbed. |
| `view.showMetricsBeam` | M13 | docs | yes | The beam is one height per font, shared by Edit View and Space Center, and sidebearing commands take it as an optional `beam` parameter; FontLab's and RoboFont's pages do not say whether it is per font or per window. |
| `view.showMetricsBeam` | M13 | docs | yes | The beam starts at half the x-height of the tab's master, like the measurement line, and giving only a height also shows it. The reference app's first use of the metrics line would settle the start height. |
| `spacing.centerInWidth` | M13 | docs | yes | `ratio` is left ÷ right as a number, or text `a:b`; 1:2 matches FontForge's Thirds in Width. The new left sidebearing is rounded to the font's grid, and metrics keys stay (possibly out of sync), as with the spacing shortcuts. Running Center in Width in FontForge on an outline with an odd amount of space would show its rounding. |
| `spacing.centerInWidth` | M13 | docs | yes | Layers under automatic alignment are skipped, as alignment sets their position; empty layers are skipped. |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | The proposed value is the target separation minus the pair's optical gap, rounded to the nearest 5 units by default; the target separation defaults to the optical gap of a reference pair (n n for lowercase, H H for capitals) without kerning. The optical gap is taken between the left glyph's right profile and the right glyph's left profile over auto-spacing's bands, each clipped to `depth` (default 30% of the x-height) inward from its extreme, as the power mean of order −2 of the row distances (each at least 1 unit), so the rows where the glyphs come closest count most. On the corpus measure (docs/plans/M13.md § 5) the depth of 15% that auto-spacing first used agreed in sign with Inter's kerning in 74% of pairs, 25% to 30% in about 85%, and the power mean added one to two points and raised the correlation. FontLab documents neither K1 nor K2 (open decision D11). |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | A pair mixing a capital or figure with any other glyph is measured over the x-height band, and its target is the mean of the two glyphs' reference gaps (n n and H H); comparing proposals for To, Av and the like with a font kerned in the reference app would settle it. |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | A group side is measured on one member: the glyph named like the group, else the group's first member in glyph order; FontLab's page does not say how class pairs are measured. |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | With `touching`, the gap and the reference gap are both measured at the outlines' closest approach, as Kern to Distance measures them; FontForge's Lookups page names the Touching option without describing its measure in detail. |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | A value of 0, a value smaller in size than `minKern`, or a positive value with `onlyCloser` stores nothing; when `keepExisting` is false such a value also removes the stored pair. `minKern` defaults to 0. |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | `keepExisting` (default true) leaves every pair whose sides are stored already in that master, whatever its value; pairs that apply through groups do not count as stored. |
| `kerning.autoKern` | M13 | docs | yes | `apply: false` returns the proposals without storing them, for the pair review windows. |
| `kerning.kernToDistance` | M13 | docs | yes | The gap is the smallest horizontal distance between the two outlines at any height, measured on the decomposed and overlap-removed outlines; FontLab says only that the pair's minimum horizontal gap becomes the value. |
| `kerning.kernToDistance` | M13 | docs | yes | The smallest distance is found on rows at most one unit apart, then refined between the neighbouring rows by a golden-section search on the outlines' exact crossings; the value is rounded to a whole unit. |
| `kerning.kernToDistance` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `pairs` or glyph lists, every pair stored in the master is set; FontLab applies the command to the pairs selected in its window. |
| `kerning.kernToDistance` | M13 | docs | yes | A group side is measured on the member named like the group, else its first member, not on every member; a pair whose value is 0 is stored only where another pair would otherwise apply. |
| `kerning.auditExceptions` | M13 | docs | yes | A class pair is a stored pair with at least one group side; a glyph pair that a glyph–glyph pair covers is not measured, as its value is not the class value. |
| `kerning.auditExceptions` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `pairs` or glyph lists, every class pair stored in the master is expanded to its members; FontLab applies the command to the pairs selected in its window. |
| `kerning.auditExceptions` | M13 | docs | yes | The default threshold is 0, so only outlines that overlap are reported; touching outlines (distance 0) are not. |
| `kerning.auditExceptions` | M13 | docs | yes | `fix` adds a glyph–glyph exception set to the threshold minus the distance without kerning, rounded up to a whole unit; it never adds a glyph–group exception. |
| `kerning.removePairsByRule` | M13 | docs | yes | An exception is a stored pair with a glyph side whose glyph has a kerning group on the side facing the pair; its class value is the value that applies once it is removed (glyph–group, then group–glyph, then group–group; 0 when none applies). FontLab does not define either term on its page. |
| `kerning.removePairsByRule` | M13 | docs | yes | Every pair is judged on the kerning as it stood before the call, so removing a small group pair does not change which exceptions are removed. |
| `kerning.removePairsByRule` | M13 | docs | yes | `glyphs` keeps the pairs that name one of the glyphs, or a group one of them belongs to on the side facing the pair. |
| `kerning.matchAcrossMasters` | M13 | docs | yes | A master that lacks a pair gets the values most of the glyph pairs it decides have there (ties to the first in glyph order), 0 when it decides none; FontLab says only that missing pairs get zero values, which is the same where nothing applied before. |
| `kerning.matchAcrossMasters` | M13 | docs | yes | Class kerning is not rebuilt with Compress afterwards: Compress works one master at a time and could leave the masters with different pairs again. The union keeps every master's own classes, and only the exceptions a filled pair needs are added. |
| `kerning.matchAcrossMasters` | M13 | docs | yes | `mainMaster` decides only the order and spelling of added pairs (its own pairs first); by default it is the font's default master. |
| `kerning.matchAcrossMasters` | M13 | docs | yes | Masters that take their kerning from another are skipped; at least two masters with kerning of their own are needed. |
| `kerning.matchAcrossMasters` | M13 | docs | yes | Every direction (left to right, right to left, vertical) is matched; FontLab's page does not mention directions. |
| `kerning.loadPairList` | M13 | docs | yes | Lines starting with # or %% are skipped, and the two characters \n break a phrase into lines; the documentation does not describe comments or multi-line rows, and a sample list exported from the reference app would settle it. |
| `kerning.loadPairList` | M13 | docs | yes | A word that is neither a glyph name nor an @group is read as characters, and a single character in a pair as the glyph that encodes it; loading a list of plain words in the reference app would settle it. |
| `kerning.loadPairList` | M13 | docs | yes | The built-in lists are our own: about thirty Latin pairs and seven spacing strings. |
| `kerning.stepPairList` | M13 | docs | yes | A pair goes in place of the pair at the current glyph and keeps the rest of the tab's text, while a phrase replaces the whole text; watching the reference app step from a pair to a phrase would settle it. |
| `kerning.stepPairList` | M13 | docs | yes | Next on the last row and previous on the first leave the list where it is instead of wrapping round. |
| `kerning.autoKernPairList` | M13 | docs | yes | The values are proposed for review and stored only on Apply, refused when the font changed in between; the reference applies them at once as far as its documentation says. |
| `glyph.matchMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | Match Masters never adds or removes nodes: a layer whose paths differ in node structure from the reference's is reported and left alone; settled by watching the reference app on a glyph with a missing node. |
| `glyph.matchMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | Without `strong`, a layer already compatible with the reference is left alone; `strong` matches it too (start nodes and orders that interpolate but do not correspond). Settled by running both modes in the reference app on a glyph whose master starts at a different corner. |
| `glyph.matchMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | Paths pair up by the centroid of their on-curve nodes and their area, both measured in the layer's node box scaled to a unit square; settled by scrambling the contours of a glyph with similar contours in the reference app. |
| `glyph.relocateStartPoints` | M9 | docs | yes | The bottom-left of the glyph is the bottom-left corner of the bounding box of the layer's paths (components left out), and nearest is the straight-line distance, ties going to the lower, then the leftmost node; settled by running the command in the reference app on an O with a counter. |
| `font.mergeFontsToMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | The axes come from the weight and width classes, with the weight class value and the width class percentage as design coordinates; the secondary reference's result on fonts with weight classes 300, 400 and 700 would settle it. |
| `font.mergeFontsToMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | Each font also becomes a static instance at its master, so the merged font still exports every input; the secondary reference's instance list after a merge would settle it. |
| `font.mergeFontsToMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | Family name, features and the rest of Font Info are the first font's; the secondary reference's result on fonts with different family names would settle it. |
| `font.mergeFontsToMasters` | M9 | docs | yes | A glyph only some fonts have is kept, without layers for the other masters; the secondary reference's result on fonts with different glyph sets would settle it. |
| `font.addVariation` | M9 | docs | yes | The other masters sit at 0 on a new axis, the value Add Axis gives them; the secondary reference's coordinates after Add Variation would settle it. |
| `font.addVariation` | M9 | docs | yes | The copy is named after the original, the axis and the location (`Regular Width 75`); the secondary reference's name for the new master would settle it. |
| `font.addVariation` | M9 | docs | yes | The commands to run are listed in the call rather than read from a saved action set; a documented action set file format would settle whether a file should be read as well. |
| `glyph.replaceWithInstance` | M9 | docs | yes | Without a glyph list every glyph with a layer for the master is adjusted, and the master defaults to the first; the secondary reference's dialog defaults would settle it. |
| `glyph.replaceWithInstance` | M9 | docs | yes | Interpolated coordinates are not rounded; the secondary reference's result between odd coordinates would settle it. |
| `font.setMasterLocations` | M9 | docs | yes | Coordinates between table entries are placed proportionally, and those beyond the ends follow the nearest pair; the secondary reference's result on an instance the table does not list would settle it. |
| `font.setMasterLocations` | M9 | docs | yes | Moving the whole font also moves the virtual masters, the special layers and the internal side of Axis Mappings, while a list of masters or instances moves only those; the secondary reference's result on a font with intermediate layers would settle it. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | The result opens as a new untitled document and no source changes; watching the reference's command would settle whether it fills the current font instead. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | Sources are open documents only, as Add Other Font takes them; whether the reference also reads files that are not open is not documented. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | In colorLayers output each glyph's master layer holds every source's outlines, as the drawing shown where colour is not supported; the reference's choice (bottom source only, or all) would settle it. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph the bottom source lacks is added, with its record and width from the lowest source that has it and no kerning groups; the reference may keep only the bottom source's glyphs. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | The bottom source's scale also scales the widths, vertical metrics and kerning; its offset moves only the outlines. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | Components are always resolved to paths, so a scaled or moved source draws correctly; the reference may keep components where the transform allows it. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | In masters output each master is named after its source's family and master name, and colour and opacity are not stored, since a master has no colour. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | The new font keeps the bottom source's family name and drops its axes and instances. |
| `font.overlayFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | `color` defaults to opaque black and `output` has no default. |
| `color.generateDarkPalette` | M13 | docs | yes | The dark colour of each entry inverts its OKLab lightness (L' = 1 - L) and keeps its hue and alpha, losing chroma, not hue, when the result falls outside sRGB; the reference's palettes for a set of known colours would settle it. |
| `color.generateDarkPalette` | M13 | docs | yes | The new palette is named after its source with " Dark" added ("Dark" when the source has no name), and only the new palette is marked; the source palette gets no `light` style. Settled by the names and CPAL palette types of a font the reference exports with a dark palette. |
| `color.generateDarkPalette` | M13 | docs | yes | A Color Palettes value in the list forms (versions 2 and 3) is converted to the version 4 dictionary, the only form that records background styles; settled by what the reference keeps when it saves such a font in an older format. |
| `file.exportWithProfiles` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `fonts` the active document is exported, not every open one; the reference's Export Fonts As dialog would settle the default. |
| `file.exportWithProfiles` | M13 | docs | yes | A profile's subfolder may not climb out of the destination with `..`, for any caller; the reference's profile settings would settle whether it can. |
| `file.exportWithProfiles` | M13 | docs | yes | Two profiles that would write the same file refuse the whole export instead of keeping one; the reference's behaviour with two profiles sharing a folder would settle it. |
| `file.saveExportProfile` | M13 | docs | yes | A copy made with basedOn writes into a subfolder named after the copy unless `settings` names a folder; the reference's Duplicate would settle it. |
| `file.exportWindowContents` | M13 | docs | yes | Only Edit View tabs are exported; the reference's Font View and Preview panel exports are not followed. Exporting a Font View window in the reference would settle whether it is offered there. |
| `file.exportWindowContents` | M13 | docs | yes | The page spans every glyph's advance from the master's descender to its ascender, widened to the outlines, with a tenth of the em around it; an export in the reference would settle the frame. |
| `file.exportWindowContents` | M13 | docs | yes | One point per font unit by default (size defaults to the units per em), scaled down so no side passes 14400 points. |
| `file.exportWindowContents` | M13 | docs | yes | SVG draws each glyph with a light fill and a thin contour, and nodes, handles and guides on every glyph unless `edited` names some; metric lines, anchors, open paths and the canvas background are left out. |
| `file.exportWindowContents` | M13 | docs | yes | Feature substitutions active in the tab are not applied: the text is laid out by code point. |
| `file.exportWindowContents` | M13 | docs | yes | Global guides are drawn at every edited glyph's origin, whatever their glyph filter says. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | SVG uses the io-svg writer, PNG the render crate and PDF a minimal writer of our own; FontLab's options beyond the three formats are not followed. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | A picture spans the advance width and the master's descender to ascender, widened to what it draws (the outline, or the colour layers and their shadows) where that reaches further, so pictures of one font line up; exporting a few glyphs in the reference would settle the frame. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | SVG and PDF use one user unit or point per font unit (a PDF is scaled down to at most 14400 points a side); PNG has a transparent background at 256 pixels per em by default, outlines in black. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | SVG pictures are the outline in black even for colour layers; PDF pictures draw gradients and the image-only effects as images at `pixelsPerEm`. Exporting a full-colour glyph in each format from the reference would settle both. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | Characters a file name cannot hold become `_`, and names that differ only in case get `_2`, `_3`; the reference's file names for such glyphs would settle it. |
| `glyph.exportArtwork` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyphs` every glyph of the font is exported; the shell passes the selection. |
| `view.filterByEncodingFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Encoding files are read in three line forms (`name slot`, `0xSlot 0xCodePoint`, and one name or code point for the next slot) with `%%FONTLAB ENCODING:` and `%%FONTLAB CODEPAGE:` titles; FontLab's own encoding and code page files would settle the forms it accepts. |
| `view.filterByEncodingFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `path`, `name` picks a built-in code page (ASCII, Latin 1, Windows 1252, Mac OS Roman); with `path` it names the filter. FontLab's list of bundled encodings would settle which ones ship. |
| `view.filterByEncodingFile` | M13 | docs | yes | A code-point slot the font has no glyph for lists the glyph data's name for that code point, and running the command again with the same name replaces the list filter instead of adding a second one. |
| `font.compareInTable` | M13 | docs | yes | Each font is read in its first master, and without `fonts` every open document is compared; FontLab's Font window comparison mode would settle which master and fonts it uses. |
| `font.compareInTable` | M13 | docs | yes | Measured values are rounded to hundredths of a unit before rows are marked as differing; the reference's own comparison display would settle the precision. |
| `path.findOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | Matching is point for point only, as FontForge documents; shapes that are equal with another point structure do not match (open question 12). |
| `path.findOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | Matches are found up to translation, and with allowFlipped up to mirroring and half turns; allowRotated and allowScaled are refused until the matcher supports them, which a matcher with rotation and scale would settle. |
| `path.findOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | A pattern glyph gives its paths per layer id, so each master is searched with its own pattern; the reference's single pattern pane would settle it. |
| `path.findOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | With endpointsLoose, an end node matches when the segment to it points the pattern's way within the tolerance at the pattern's length and is at least as long; the start end node needs the same on-curve status, the last the same kind. Observing the reference on a serif with longer stems would settle it. |
| `path.replaceOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | For an open pattern, the replacement's first node keeps the kind and smoothness of the matched run's first node, and an end node that lands within the tolerance and a grid step of the run's end node takes its exact position so the contour stays joined; observing a replacement with slightly different ends would settle it. |
| `path.replaceOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | With endpointsLoose, the replacement's end nodes take the positions of the contour's own end nodes, so stems keep their length; the reference's documentation does not say. |
| `path.replaceOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | Closed patterns: the replacement paths take the place of the first matched contour and its attributes, and an empty replacement removes the matches; the reference's documentation does not say. |
| `path.replaceOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | A replacement under a mirroring match is reversed so its direction stays correct, as a mirrored component decomposed with corrected direction would be. |
| `path.replaceOutlinePattern` | M13 | docs | yes | Hints on a path a closed pattern replaces lose their nodes (a PostScript end becomes a number, a TrueType instruction or corner component is removed and reported), other paths' hints follow them by path or shape index, and hints on a contour an open pattern edits follow their nodes as after any node edit; the reference's documentation does not mention hints. |
| `path.findIntersections` | M13 | docs | yes | Crossings of a path with itself count too, and touching curves get a node like crossing ones; the manual speaks only of overlapping contours. |
| `path.findIntersections` | M13 | docs | yes | Locked paths get no nodes but still cross the others; the manual does not mention locks. |
| `path.makeParallel` | M13 | docs | yes | With two lines, the moved line keeps its length and turns about its fixed end to the nearer of the two parallel directions; the manual only says the lines become parallel. |
| `path.spacePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | The lowest and highest points stay and the others keep their order; the manual only says the points are spaced regularly. |
| `path.spacePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | Regions join points whose coordinates along the spacing axis are at most `within` apart (default 100 units), and the centres of the regions' extents are spaced evenly; the manual gives no distance and no reference point. |
| `path.spacePoints` | M13 | docs | yes | An on-curve node's handles move with it unless they are selected too. |
| `path.roundToCluster` | M13 | docs | yes | x and y are clustered on their own, every node and handle included; a cluster grows while values are at most `within` (default 1 unit) above its lowest, and takes the value most of its members have (ties: nearest the mean, then the lower). The manual gives no distance and no rule for the shared value. |
| `path.makeArc` | M13 | docs | yes | Of the many ellipses through the two nodes with the path's directions there, FontCraft takes the quarter ellipse framed by the two tangent lines (a quarter circle when they meet square at equal distances), and half an ellipse when the directions are opposite; the manual does not say which ellipse it picks beyond preferring one with a node on an axis. |
| `path.makeArc` | M13 | docs | yes | At an open end without a neighbouring segment the segment's own direction is used, and the result is always a cubic segment. |
| `path.insertNodeAt` | M13 | docs | yes | A node goes in at every place the segment crosses the coordinate, and a segment that does not reach it is refused; the manual does not cover several or no crossings. |
| `path.nameContour` | M13 | docs | yes | An empty name removes the name; the manual does not say how a name is cleared. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | The offset moves nodes and handles without adding nodes, so curves are only approximately parallel to the original; an outline comparison against the secondary reference would settle how close it must be. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Method auto picks cjk for CJK ideographs, Hangul and kana and lcg for everything else; the secondary reference's documentation on auto would settle it. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Default zones: bottom at the baseline, top at the x-height for lowercase glyphs (by code point, else by the first letter of the name) and the cap height for the rest, serif height 0; the secondary reference's dialog defaults would settle them. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Counters auto means retain with lcg and squish with cjk; retain moves each vertical stem right by the amount times its index so the counters keep their widths, and the advance width grows by the amount times (stems − 1). |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Squish keeps the advance width, so the outline grows into both sidebearings by half the amount each. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Cleanup (Remove Overlap after the change) is off by default. |
| `filter.changeWeight` | M13 | docs | yes | One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | The counter add applies only to counters between two stems; the parts of the outline left of the first stem and right of the last (arms, serifs) only scale. The secondary reference on a T would settle it. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph with no vertical stem scales as one piece by the counter scale. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | Counters scaled below zero width become zero width. |
| `filter.condenseExtend` | M13 | docs | yes | One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on. |
| `filter.changeXHeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Above the x-height band the outline stretches so the master's ascender stays where it is, and nothing above the ascender moves; the secondary reference on b or h would settle whether ascenders move instead. |
| `filter.changeXHeight` | M13 | docs | yes | Below the serif height nothing moves (descenders included) and the band within the serif height of the x-height shifts rigidly. |
| `filter.changeXHeight` | M13 | docs | yes | The master's x-height metric is not changed; the command changes outlines only. |
| `glyph.addSmallCaps` | M13 | docs | yes | Petite caps use the suffix .pc, as the handbook's naming rules expect, where the reference uses its own extension; making petite caps with the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.addSmallCaps` | M13 | docs | yes | Symbols take the same suffix as the letters (nine.sc), where the reference documents an old-style figure extension; the reference's naming for other symbols would settle it. |
| `glyph.addSmallCaps` | M13 | docs | yes | Horizontal stems scale by the vertical stem ratio unless stemScale.h is given, because there is no lowercase horizontal stem to measure against; the reference's default horizontal stem value would settle it. |
| `glyph.addSmallCaps` | M13 | docs | yes | Without glyphs, every glyph with a character is considered and those that give no small cap are left out quietly; the reference always works on the Font View selection. |
| `glyph.addSmallCaps` | M13 | docs | yes | An automatic feature with the tag is regenerated rather than edited, so substitutions to a suffix the feature generator does not know are not written there; a test with the reference's automatic features would settle whether it should warn. |
| `glyph.addSubSuperscripts` | M13 | docs | yes | The raise of 450 units is read per 1000 units of the em and scaled to the font's units per em; running the command on a 2048-unit font in the reference would settle it. |
| `glyph.addSubSuperscripts` | M13 | docs | yes | subs and sinf default to a drop of 150 units per 1000 and dnom to no shift, where the reference has one raise for every feature; the reference documents no subscript default. |
| `glyph.addSubSuperscripts` | M13 | docs | yes | Without glyphs, the figures zero to nine are used, as in the reference's example; the reference always works on the Font View selection. |
| `glyph.addSubSuperscripts` | M13 | docs | yes | A suffix without a leading dot gets one (sups gives one.sups); the reference's handling of a bare extension would settle it. |
| `filter.nonLinearTransform` | M13 | docs | yes | The expressions use the shared expression grammar (M13.16) with the names x, y, width, xHeight, capHeight, ascender, descender, unitsPerEm and pi; FontForge's own grammar is not followed. Settled by user demand for its syntax. |
| `filter.nonLinearTransform` | M13 | docs | yes | A segment is split in half, at most 10 times, until its mapped curve is within the tolerance (default 0.5 units) of the mapped outline at a quarter, half and three quarters, and a bent straight segment becomes a curve; an affine expression only moves points. Settled by comparing results with the reference on our own fonts. |
| `filter.nonLinearTransform` | M13 | docs | yes | Unlocked anchors move with the outline; locked paths and anchors, components and images stay. Settled by running the reference on a glyph with anchors and components. |
| `glyph.autotraceImage` | M13 | docs | yes | A pixel is ink when its darkness, laid on white, is above the threshold (default 0.5); transparent pixels count as white. Settled by tracing a grey and a semi-transparent scan in a reference app that has an autotracer. |
| `glyph.autotraceImage` | M13 | docs | yes | Smoothness runs from 0 to 1 (default 0.25): a vertex is a corner when the path turns by more than 30° + 120° × smoothness there, and curves stay within 0.3 + 1.2 × smoothness pixels of the boundary. Settled by comparing corner counts with a reference tracer on our own scans. |
| `glyph.autotraceImage` | M13 | docs | yes | Ink pixels that touch only at a corner make separate contours, and contours under 2.5 square pixels (specks) are dropped. Settled by tracing a checkerboard and a dotted scan in a reference tracer. |
| `glyph.autotraceImage` | M13 | docs | yes | Traced contours are added after the layer's existing shapes, and the image stays unless `removeImage` is true; a locked image is never removed. Settled by autotracing a glyph that already has paths in a reference app. |
| `glyph.autotraceImage` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyphs`, every glyph whose layer has an image is traced. Settled by running the reference command with no glyphs selected. |
| `font.compareGlyphs` | M12 | docs | yes | A fontRef is an open document's title or path; anything else is read as a file path. The secondary reference's documentation of its font selector would settle it. |
| `font.compareGlyphs` | M12 | docs | yes | Near matching without a tolerance uses 1 unit, and giving a tolerance alone turns near matching on; the secondary reference's default would settle it. |
| `font.compareGlyphs` | M12 | docs | yes | differencesToBackground replaces the shapes of the background of A's matched master layer with B's shapes and keeps the background's other contents; the secondary reference's behaviour on a glyph with a background would settle it. |
| `font.compareLayers` | M13 | docs | yes | Two layers match when their advance widths, their paths (closed or open, node count, each node's kind and position) and their components (glyph and placement) agree in order; anchors, hints and images are not compared. Watching the secondary reference compare layers with different anchors would settle it. |
| `font.compareLayers` | M13 | docs | yes | The tolerance applies on each axis to node positions, advance widths and component offsets, and defaults to 0; component scale, rotation and slant must match exactly. The secondary reference's dialog would settle it. |
| `font.interpolateFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | Fonts that differ anywhere are refused with the first difference, rather than leaving out the glyphs that do not match; the secondary reference's handling of a partly compatible pair would settle it. |
| `font.interpolateFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | The new font keeps only the master layer of each glyph, and its family name, Font Info, features and other data are the first font's; the secondary reference's result on two fonts with different names and extra layers would settle it. |
| `font.interpolateFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | Interpolated numbers are not rounded; the secondary reference's result at 50 between odd coordinates would settle it. |
| `font.interpolateFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | A kerning pair only one font has counts as 0 in the other; the secondary reference's result on fonts with different pairs would settle it. |
| `font.interpolateFonts` | M13 | docs | yes | The menu bar has a Font menu, between Edit and Glyph, holding this and the other font-wide commands whose rows give the menu path Font › …; the primary reference has no Font menu, and the menu stays until these rows' menu paths are settled. |
| `glyph.listDependencies` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyphs that use the glyph through other composites are listed too, with their depth and the glyph they reach it through, nearest first; the reference's documentation does not say whether nested references are shown. |
| `file.revertGlyph` | M13 | docs | yes | Without glyphs, every glyph that differs from the saved file is reverted; glyphs added since the save are left alone and naming one is refused. Reverting a glyph added since the save in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `file.revertGlyph` | M13 | docs | yes | With layers, a layer added since the save is removed and a deleted one is put back. The secondary reference reverts whole glyphs only, so nothing there settles it. |
| `edit.copyMetrics` | M13 | docs | yes | FontCraft copies straight from `from` to the glyphs in `to` in one command, layer by layer with the same layer id (the first master by default); without `to` it only returns the values. FontForge copies to a clipboard and pastes later; commands take explicit targets, so the two steps are one. |
| `edit.copyMetrics` | M13 | docs | yes | The values apply in the order width, lsb, rsb, vertical advance, so copying lsb and rsb together decides the width. A copied value removes the metrics key its field showed, as typing a number does. |
| `edit.copyLayerToLayer` | M13 | docs | yes | The mode defaults to replace, and replace keeps the target's images; widths, anchors and the background are not copied. The secondary reference's dialog defaults would settle it. |
| `edit.copyLayerToLayer` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph without one of the two layers is skipped and listed rather than refusing the command. The secondary reference has font-wide layers, so nothing there settles it. |
| `edit.pasteAfter` | M13 | docs | yes | Only whole copied glyphs paste after; a drawing fragment copied from a layer has no advance width and is refused. Pasting a selection from an outline window in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `edit.pasteAfter` | M13 | docs | yes | Paths and components are pasted; anchors are not, so a word glyph does not get several anchors of one name. The secondary reference's result with anchored glyphs would settle it. |
| `edit.pasteAfter` | M13 | docs | yes | Each copied glyph gives its layer of the target's master (by id, else by name, else the master shown when copying), as edit.paste matches masters. |
| `hint.tt.editInstructions` | M13 | docs | yes | The command has no menu item, since the app has no Hints menu; it runs from the command search, the console and agents. Adding a Hints menu would settle where it goes. |
| `hint.tt.setAssembly` | M13 | docs | yes | A call's function number is known only when a push sits just before the call, as hand-written programs put it; other calls only need the font programs to define some function. A program that computes its numbers would need the interpreter to check, and nothing in the secondary reference says it checks at all. |
| `hint.tt.setAssembly` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `master`, fpgm and prep text goes to every master and the font-level parameter is removed, as opening a hinted font stores them on every master; the secondary reference has a single font, so nothing settles it there. |
| `hint.tt.setAssembly` | M13 | docs | yes | Without the cvt Table, prep Table Assembly and fpgm Table Assembly parameters, a glyph program that needs font programs is left out rather than written against FontCraft's own fpgm and cvt; an export from the secondary reference of a glyph program that calls a function, with no font programs set, would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.setAssembly` | M13 | docs | yes | Text that assembles to no bytes removes the program instead of storing an empty one; an empty program and none export the same. |
| `hint.tt.setAssembly` | M13 | docs | yes | Arguments written after an instruction are pushed in the order written, so the last ends on top of the stack; the secondary reference's documentation does not give the order, and its dialog would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.debugStep` | M13 | docs | yes | A step is one instruction of the glyph program or of a function it calls, and the font and control value programs run without stops; the secondary reference's debugger would settle whether it steps through prep as well. |
| `hint.tt.debugStep` | M13 | docs | yes | Breakpoints are byte offsets in the glyph program only, not inside functions; the secondary reference's breakpoint behaviour in fpgm would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.debugStep` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyphs made of components are refused with a message; the secondary reference's debugger on a composite glyph would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.suggestDeltas` | M13 | docs | yes | An edge closer than `within` (default 0.1 pixel) to a pixel centre, measured along one axis, is what needs a delta; the secondary reference's suggestions on a glyph with edges at half pixels would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.suggestDeltas` | M13 | docs | yes | Where the interpreter is in backward compatibility mode (the smooth intents unless prep turns it off) only vertical deltas are suggested, as horizontal moves are ignored there; the secondary reference's suggestions on such a font would settle it. |
| `hint.tt.suggestDeltas` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyphs made of components are skipped and listed as skipped; the secondary reference's handling of composites would settle it. |
| `hint.ps.reviewHints` | M13 | docs | yes | The command has no menu item, since the app has no Hints menu; it runs from the command search, the console and agents. Adding a Hints menu would settle where it goes. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A strike to remove given without a depth removes every depth of that size, and one to add without a depth is black and white; the secondary reference's dialog lists sizes with their depth, so its behaviour for a bare size would settle it. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | Adding a strike the font already has is refused rather than ignored. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | Rasterising draws the first master's exported glyphs only; glyphs that are not exported get no bitmap. Rasterising a font with an unexported glyph in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | At 1 bit a pixel is on when the outline covers at least half of it, with no grid-fitting or hinting; the secondary reference's rasteriser may differ at the edges. Rasterising a glyph with a half-covered row in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | Each bitmap spans the outline's box rounded out to whole pixels, so an empty glyph has a 0 × 0 bitmap that keeps its advance. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph without outlines draws its placed PDF, as dark as the page is, with transparent counting as white; neither reference rasterises placed artwork into strikes, so this is our choice. |
| `bitmap.setStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A placed PDF too large for a bitmap is left out with a warning rather than refusing the strike, since a template image is often larger than the glyph. |
| `bitmap.regenerateGlyphs` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `glyphs` every exported glyph is drawn again, so a strike that covered only some glyphs gains bitmaps for the rest; the secondary reference acts on the font view selection. Regenerating with nothing selected in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bitmap.regenerateGlyphs` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph that is not exported is skipped and listed, as when a strike is first rasterised. |
| `bitmap.regenerateGlyphs` | M13 | docs | yes | A regenerated bitmap keeps the code its file gave the old one, so a strike in a non-Unicode encoding still exports with its codes. |
| `bitmap.removeGlyphs` | M13 | docs | yes | References to a removed bitmap from other glyphs of the same strike are removed with it, and those glyphs are listed in the result, rather than refusing the removal. Removing a referenced bitmap in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bitmap.removeGlyphs` | M13 | docs | yes | A call that would remove nothing (none of the glyphs has a bitmap in the chosen strikes) is refused rather than reported as an empty change. |
| `bitmap.setPixels` | M13 | inferred | yes | Clearing pixels never shrinks the bitmap's box; only `rows` set a smaller one. The secondary reference's saved BDF after clearing an edge row would settle whether it trims. |
| `bitmap.setPixels` | M13 | inferred | yes | `origin` is the bitmap's bottom-left corner relative to the glyph origin, the BDF bounding box offset, not a point inside the bitmap. |
| `bitmap.setPixels` | M13 | inferred | yes | A `strike` without `depth` names the strike of that size when the font has only one; with several depths of the size the call is refused. |
| `bitmap.transform` | M13 | docs | yes | Skew is horizontal only and counted from the baseline: a row moves floor(height × dx ÷ dy) pixels right, where height is how far its bottom edge lies above the baseline, so rows below the baseline move left. Skewing a glyph with a descender in the secondary reference's bitmap view would settle the direction and the rounding. |
| `bitmap.transform` | M13 | docs | yes | Flips mirror the bitmap in its own box, which stays where it was, rather than about the advance's centre. Flipping an off-centre bitmap in the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bitmap.transform` | M13 | docs | yes | A quarter turn keeps the centre of the bitmap's box, rounding the new corner down; the secondary reference may turn about the glyph origin instead. |
| `bitmap.transform` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph built from references has them drawn into its pixels before it is transformed, so it loses its references; transforming only its own pixels would move them apart from the drawn parts. |
| `bitmap.importStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A bitmap goes to the glyph whose code point is its code when the file's codes are Unicode, else to the glyph of its name; the summary names both keys but not their order. Importing a BDF whose names and codes disagree into the secondary reference would settle it. |
| `bitmap.importStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | Bitmaps that match no glyph are left out and listed; no glyph is created for them. Importing a BDF with an extra glyph into a font without it, in the secondary reference, would settle it. |
| `bitmap.importStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A file none of whose strikes has a bitmap that matches a glyph is refused, changing nothing; a strike that matches nothing in a file whose other strikes match (such as a symbol font in a FON file) is left out with a warning. |
| `bitmap.importStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | When the files of one import hold two strikes of one size and depth, the first is added and the second left out with a warning, as opening the file does; the two are not merged. Importing a FON with a regular and a bold font of one size into the secondary reference would settle it. |
