| `bitmap.importStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | Importing a strike whose size and depth the font has replaces the strike's name, metrics and properties and the bitmaps of the glyphs the file holds, and keeps the strike's other bitmaps. |
| `bitmap.importStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A strike keeps each bitmap's code from the file and writes it back on export, so a strike in a non-Unicode encoding survives a round trip. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | Files written to a folder are named after the family and the strike, `Family-12.bdf`, or `Family-12@4.bdf` for a grey strike; the secondary reference's file names for several strikes would settle it. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A strike with no stated point size or resolution is written at 72 dpi, so its point size equals its pixel size, and one with no name takes the family name. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A bitmap without a stored code takes the first code point of its glyph when the strike's codes are Unicode. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | An `.otb` keeps every glyph of the compiled font, and its `loca` has one zero entry per glyph plus one; the secondary reference's page says `maxp` counts the bitmap glyphs and `loca` holds one entry. We keep a `loca` that validators and every reader accept. Opening an `.otb` the secondary reference writes in ttx would settle it. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | The line metrics give the strike's ascent and descent (from the bitmaps when both are 0), the widest advance as `widthMax`, an upright caret (1/0), and the extremes of the bitmaps with pixels; the vertical line metrics are zero. An embedded strike the secondary reference writes would settle it. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | A bitmap that does not fit the embedded formats (over 255 pixels, offsets beyond -128..127) refuses the export instead of being left out. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | `fon` puts every chosen strike into one file, as Windows font files hold one face at several sizes, while `fnt` writes one file per strike; the secondary reference's Generate Font output for several strikes would settle it. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | `fnt` and `fon` leave out glyphs without an 8-bit code and pixels outside the advance, listing them in `warnings`, rather than refusing the export; the secondary reference's behaviour on such a strike would settle it. |
| `bitmap.exportStrikes` | M13 | docs | yes | `embedded` without `font` compiles the open font as TrueType with the default export settings; the secondary reference embeds strikes as part of its Generate Fonts dialog. |
| `bitmap.setStrikeProperties` | M13 | docs | yes | A property name given twice is refused, since a BDF file names each property once. |
| `bitmap.setStrikeProperties` | M13 | docs | yes | Changing the strike's ascent or descent does not edit a `FONT_ASCENT` or `FONT_DESCENT` entry in `properties`; the two are set separately. The secondary reference's BDF Info dialog after a change of line metrics would settle whether it keeps them in step. |
| `kerning.buildGroups` | M13 | docs | yes | Two glyphs fall in one group while the root mean square of their side profiles' differences over the band stays under maxDifference; FontForge does not describe its grouping rule. |
| `kerning.buildGroups` | M13 | docs | yes | Each profile is compared as its depth inward from the side's extreme, clipped like auto-spacing's; rows inside the x-height (cap height) where only one glyph has ink count as the full clipping depth, rows in the overshoot zones where only one has ink are left out. Over several masters the largest difference counts. |
| `kerning.buildGroups` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyphs are taken in glyph order; each joins the group whose members all lie within maxDifference (the closest such group), else starts one, and lowercase never shares a group with capitals and figures. |
| `kerning.buildGroups` | M13 | docs | yes | A group of two or more glyphs is named after its first member (`.2`, `.3`… when another glyph keeps that name); a glyph left alone gets no group and keeps what it has. |
| `kerning.buildGroups` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `replaceExisting`, glyphs that have a group on a side keep it and are left out of that side; `apply` defaults to false, so the call proposes until told to apply. |
| `kerning.verticalFromHorizontal` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph's vertical alternate is the glyph named with the `.vert` suffix, the suffix the primary reference's automatic `vert` feature uses; FontForge takes the `vrt2` substitution, which FontCraft does not read from feature code. |
| `kerning.verticalFromHorizontal` | M13 | docs | yes | A group side stands for each of its members that has an alternate, and each vertical pair gets the kerning that applies between the two horizontal glyphs; the first glyph's alternate is the top one. |
| `kerning.verticalFromHorizontal` | M13 | docs | yes | Vertical kerning is cleared only in the masters the call names; masters that take their kerning from another are skipped. |
| `font.setMathData` | M13 | docs | yes | A new math.ic or math.ta anchor is placed at height 0, and an existing one keeps its height; the plugin that defined these anchors would settle where it puts them. |
| `font.setMathData` | M13 | docs | yes | Math kerning anchors are named with the corner's prefix and a number from 1 (math.tr1, math.tr2); ufo2ft reads any name with the prefix. |
| `font.setMathData` | M13 | docs | yes | A master without constants gets an empty set when math data is set, so the font has a MATH table: ufo2ft builds MATH only when the font lib holds a math key. |
| `font.setBaselines` | M13 | docs | yes | The baselines are stored as feature code rather than in custom parameters or user data, so the reference compilers build the same BASE table from the source; a reference app that edits BASE would settle where it keeps them. |
| `font.renameGlyphsByNamelist` | M13 | docs | yes | An unencoded glyph named after a renamed glyph plus a suffix is renamed with it (uni0041.sc to A.sc); the reference's documentation does not say, and renaming such a font in it would settle it. |
| `font.renameGlyphsByNamelist` | M13 | docs | yes | Renamed glyphs keep their code points and their places in the glyph order, and when a code point is named twice in a list the first name counts. |
| `font.renameGlyphsByNamelist` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph with several code points is renamed by its first code point only, and keeps its name when the list does not name that one; renaming such a glyph in the reference would settle it. |
| `font.renameGlyphsByNamelist` | M13 | docs | yes | With updateReferences (the default) components, metrics keys, kerning, groups and the glyphOrder parameter follow the new names, as Rename Glyph does. |
| `cid.convertToCid` | M13 | docs | yes | Without a CMap file only the Identity ordering is accepted, with .notdef at CID 0 and the other exporting glyphs from 1 in glyph order; bundled data for the public collections (M7's ROS row) would allow the others. |
| `cid.convertToCid` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyphs the CMap does not reach, and a second glyph on a CID already taken, get CIDs after the highest mapped one in glyph order, as the reference's export appends glyphs its ROS does not cover; converting such a font in the reference would settle the order. |
| `cid.convertToCid` | M13 | docs | yes | Non-exporting glyphs get no CID, and glyph names and the glyph order do not change. |
| `cid.convertToCid` | M13 | docs | yes | The CID font name and the single sub-font's name are the family name without spaces and PostScript delimiters. |
| `cid.convertToCid` | M13 | docs | yes | CMap codes are read as UTF-8 or UTF-16 when the CMap's name says so and as the code point otherwise; CMaps whose name does not start with Uni are refused, since glyphs are encoded by code point. |
| `cid.flatten` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyphs without a CID follow the glyphs in CID order, in their old order. |
| `cid.flatten` | M13 | docs | yes | With a CMap, a glyph whose CID it reaches gets exactly the code points the CMap maps to that CID, replacing its own, and other glyphs lose those code points; the documentation does not say whether existing code points stay. |
| `cid.editSubfonts` | M13 | docs | yes | An inserted font's glyphs whose names the font already has get .001, .002… (references inside the inserted font follow), code points the font already uses are dropped, and exporting glyphs take CIDs after the highest; the documentation does not say how name and code point clashes are resolved. |
| `cid.editSubfonts` | M13 | docs | yes | An inserted font needs as many masters as the font, matched in order. |
| `cid.editSubfonts` | M13 | docs | yes | Removing a sub-font removes its glyphs, and is refused when glyphs that stay use one of them as a component, or when it is the last sub-font. |
| `cid.editSubfonts` | M13 | docs | yes | A new sub-font is named after the inserted font's family name without spaces, or FD followed by its index for a blank one, with -1, -2… when taken. |
| `cid.editSubfonts` | M13 | docs | yes | postscriptOtherBlues, the family blues, the stem snaps and postscriptForceBold use the same naming as the keys seen in tx output; a CID-keyed font with those values converted by tx would settle it. |
| `file.importMetricsFile` | M13 | docs | yes | `replace` gives a pair the master already has the file's value and keeps every other pair; `addMissing` adds only pairs the master lacks; the reference documentation does not say what happens to existing pairs. Merging a file into a font that already kerns one of its pairs in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importMetricsFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Without `masters`, the pairs go into the first master only, as other import commands do; the reference has one master per font. |
| `file.importMetricsFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Pairs that name a glyph (AFM) or a character code (PFM) the font lacks are left out and listed, not stored; importing a file with such a pair in the reference app would settle it. |
| `file.importMetricsFile` | M13 | docs | yes | Ligature data in an AFM file is not imported: ligature features come from glyph names; the reference merges it into its ligature tables. |
| `window.spaceCenter` | M13 | docs | yes | A sample string's `/Placeholder` becomes `/?`, the glyph being edited, in the Space Center; the reference's Space Center reads its own sample strings, and choosing one with a placeholder there would settle it. |
| `window.spaceCenter` | M13 | docs | yes | The waterfall's sizes are 9, 10, 12, 14, 18, 24, 36, 48, 72 and 96 points up to the window's size, then the window's size; the reference app's waterfall would settle the series. |
| `window.spaceCenter` | M13 | docs | yes | In a waterfall the matrix keeps one column per glyph of the text and a click selects the glyph in every size; the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.setSpacingCutOff` | M13 | docs | yes | The band is drawn on every line across the whole preview width; the reference app would settle whether it stops at the text's ends. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | Right to left uses the font's right-to-left kerning, as an Edit View tab set right to left does; the reference's kerning is not split by direction, and a font with right-to-left pairs would settle it. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | Upside down turns the preview half a circle, as Text Preview's Flip does, rather than mirroring it top to bottom; the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | With the preview upside down the keys and drags keep their meaning for the glyph (Right still grows the right margin); the reference app would settle whether they follow the screen instead. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | The beam shares its height with Edit View's metrics beam and is switched on separately; the reference app would settle whether its Space Center beam has its own height. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | The margins on the beam are labelled on rows whose em is at least four label sizes, so small waterfall lines show only the beam; the reference app would settle it. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | Tracking adds its units between neighbouring glyphs after the lines are broken, so lines wrap as without tracking; the reference app's tracking near the window's edge would settle it. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | Guides are drawn within each glyph's advance box, the master's and the glyph's own; the reference app would settle whether a horizontal guide runs across the whole line. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | Overlay layers are offered by master name in the menu, and the command also takes layer names or ids; the reference's layer list would settle it. |
| `view.setSpaceCenterDisplay` | M13 | docs | yes | Line space multiplies the master's line height, from 0.5 to 4; the reference app's control would settle the range. |
| `view.setPreferredSuffix` | M13 | docs | yes | The suffix replaces control glyphs as well as the text's glyphs; the reference app would settle it. |
| `guide.setAppearance` | M13 | docs | yes | Magnetism is a distance in screen points from 0 to 100, 0 meaning not magnetic, as the manual's number field suggests. |
| `view.showIndexes` | M13 | docs | yes | Nodes and segments are numbered within their path, paths and components by their shape index in the layer, anchors by their index in the layer, so the numbers match the `[shape, node]` addresses commands take. The reference apps' numbering on a glyph with components would settle it. |
| `view.showIndexes` | M13 | docs | yes | Without parameters the command turns all five kinds off when any is on, else turns all five on. The reference app has one switch per kind, so nothing there settles it. |
| `view.showCurveLength` | M13 | docs | yes | The length prints with one decimal at the middle of every segment that has handles; straight segments get none. A screenshot of the reference app with the option on would settle it. |
| `file.reviewExternalChanges` | M13 | docs | yes | The document's own unsaved edits are kept and never listed, so Update All merges rather than reloads; the manual speaks of taking the incoming change, and a document with unsaved edits in the reference settles whether it keeps them. |
| `file.reviewExternalChanges` | M13 | docs | yes | Font-level data is grouped into nine parts (info, axes, masters, instances, kerning, features, groups, glyph order, other), each accepted whole; the manual mentions font-level data without saying how finely it is split. |
| `file.reviewExternalChanges` | M13 | docs | yes | Glyph data apart from the layers (code points, export, kerning groups, metadata) is one item per glyph, beside one item per changed layer; changing a glyph's code point in a UFO while it is open in the reference settles how it is listed. |
| `font.buildFromConstructions` | M13 | docs | yes | Components a rule places are stored with automatic alignment off, so the realignment after every edit keeps the rule's positions; the original runs where there is no automatic alignment, so nothing there settles it. |
| `font.buildFromConstructions` | M13 | docs | yes | Built glyphs get a colour label only when a rule, the file's `# MarkGlyphs:` line or `markColor` gives one; the extension's sheet marks them red by default. Building with the extension's first-use settings would show whether that default stands. |
| `font.buildFromConstructions` | M13 | docs | yes | A rule without a `#` note leaves an existing glyph's note as it is; the extension writes the rule's note, empty or not. |
| `font.buildFromConstructions` | M13 | docs | yes | A new glyph gets a layer for every master; `masters` limits only which master layers of existing glyphs are rebuilt. |
| `font.buildFromConstructions` | M13 | docs | yes | Kerning applied with a backslash also moves the marks placed over the kerned base; the original measures them from where the unkerned base would be, which looks like an oversight. |
| `font.setCrossAxisMappings` | M13 | docs | yes | Locations are design coordinates, as the designspace format stores them, not user coordinates; the secondary reference's fields on a font whose axes have a user-to-design map would settle it. |
| `view.setReferenceFont` | M13 | docs | yes | The language code picks a glyph named like the found one plus .locl and the code in capitals (A.loclTRK for TRK); applying the reference font's own locl substitutions through the shaper would settle what the reference app does. |
| `view.setReferenceFont` | M13 | docs | yes | Adding a path selects that font and turns the display on; the reference app's panel would settle whether adding also shows it. |
| `view.setReferenceFont` | M13 | docs | yes | Any font the open path reads (sources too, not only compiled fonts) can be a reference, drawn from its first master. |
| `font.setStatusDefinitions` | M13 | docs | yes | The definitions are stored in the font's user data as a list of code, name, colour and default flag; Fontra's storage is not documented. |
| `font.setStatusDefinitions` | M13 | docs | yes | Two definitions with the same code or the same name, or more than one default, are refused; checking the reference's Status definitions panel would settle it. |
| `font.setStatusDefinitions` | M13 | docs | yes | Redefining the list keeps every glyph's stored code, even one the new list lacks (such glyphs show no tint); renaming or deleting a status in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.setStatus` | M13 | docs | yes | FontCraft stores the status per glyph, in the glyph's user data, so `.glyphs` and UFO keep it; per-source statuses would move it to layer user data. The reference documentation does not say. |
| `glyph.setStatus` | M13 | docs | yes | A glyph without a stored status is shown in the default status; setting a status on a new glyph in the reference app would settle it. |
| `glyph.setStatus` | M13 | docs | yes | The tint fills the advance box from the descender to the ascender at a quarter of the status colour's opacity, and shows whenever the glyph is in a defined status (no display toggle yet); the reference's Development status color option would settle the look and the default. |
| `tunni.dragLineSymmetric` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Both handles gain the same share of their own distance to the Tunni point, the share being how far the pointer moves across the chord over the Tunni point's height above it, so the Tunni line follows the pointer; FontLab does not say how the two lengths relate, and dragging an unbalanced segment's Tunni line in FontLab would settle it. |
| `tunni.dragLineSymmetric` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A Tunni line counts as selected when both its handles are selected, so clicking a Tunni line selects its two handles and Shift-click toggles them; FontLab keeps a separate Tunni line selection, and selecting a line there and then a node would show whether the two interact. |
| `tunni.dragLineSymmetric` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The control dot is drawn and hit at the Tunni point itself (where the handle lines meet); the FontLab manual does not say where on the line the dot sits, and a screenshot of FontLab with Tunni lines on would settle it. |
| `tunni.dragLineSymmetric` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A dragged handle stays between 1 unit long and four times its node's distance to the Tunni point; FontLab documents no limit. |
| `tunni.dragPointAsymmetric` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Alt-dragging a Tunni line moves the handle whose end is nearer the press; dragging the dot moves the handle whose direction is nearer the drag's first movement; FontLab says only that one handle moves, and trying both in FontLab would settle which. |
| `nodes.slideAlongCurve` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Only an open end slides past the end; FontLab also extends at a sharp node, which needs a curve where FontCraft has a corner, and sliding past a sharp node in FontLab would show what it adds. |
| `nodes.slideAlongCurve` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | At a corner node, or next to a line, the other segment keeps its far handle and its near handle moves with the node, so that segment changes shape; FontLab says the shape is preserved without saying how in this case. |
| `drag.leverPrecision` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The drag follows 1/ratio of the pointer's movement over the first 50 screen points and the pointer's full speed beyond; FontLab says only that short drags are slowed and longer ones gradually match the pointer, and timing a long lever drag in FontLab would settle the curve. |
| `autometer.editStemNumerically` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Both edges move by half the change (as Adjust Stems does); the reference may move one edge only. |
| `autometer.editStemNumerically` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The irregular-stem tolerance is 5 units by default. |
| `recipe.directives` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A recipe has one line of components; directives may follow it on that line or stand on lines of their own, several `#!` lines are joined with line breaks, and the note is written when the recipe is set, not at every rebuild. |
| `recipe.directives` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | An unknown `#?` keyword is refused, and `noanchors` is matched without regard to case; the reference's handling is not documented. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | FontCraft's variable names are ascender, descender, xHeight, capHeight, baseline and width, plus `anchor:<name>.x`, `guide:<name>`, `zone:<name>` and `param:<name>` references; the reference's spellings (x_height, caps_height) are read as aliases. Nothing settles it in the primary reference, which has no anchor expressions. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | An expression that names something missing, divides by zero or gives a value that is not finite leaves the anchor where it was and is reported in the command's warnings; the reference's handling is not documented. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | When expressions are worked out again after other commands, failures leave the anchors in place without a warning; `anchor.setExpressions` reports them. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Results are rounded to the font's grid, as typed anchor positions are. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | An anchor with expressions that is moved by hand goes back to its expressions' values at once; removing the expressions frees it. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Anchors are worked out once per change, in layer order, so `anchor:NAME` sees the new position of an anchor before it and the old position of one after it. |
| `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | `guide:NAME` is the y of a horizontal guide and the x of a vertical one (`.x` and `.y` pick either), looked up on the layer and then on the master; `zone:NAME` is a master metric by name, then by kind; `param:NAME` is a master number value, then a numeric font custom parameter. |
| `kerning.representativeGlyph` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Members are stepped in glyph order and wrap round from the last to the first. |
| `kerning.representativeGlyph` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The keys work only while the Pairs and Phrases list has the keyboard, so they never clash with the Edit View's own keys; elsewhere the command runs from Font › Kerning or the control channel. |
| `features.opticalBoundsFromGuides` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph without outlines or components (a space) takes no global offset, only its own guides; settled by exporting a font with a non-zero offset and a space glyph from the reference app. |
| `features.opticalBoundsFromGuides` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A guide is read where it crosses the baseline, so a slanted guide in an italic works; guides closer than 30° to horizontal are not read; settled by exporting a glyph with a slanted `ol` guide. |
| `features.opticalBoundsFromGuides` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Bounds are rounded half up to whole units, and a bound beyond a 16-bit value leaves the glyph out with a warning; settled by exporting a guide at a fractional position. |
| `features.opticalBoundsFromGuides` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Only glyph-layer guides count, not master-wide guides named ol or or; settled by adding a master guide named ol in the reference app and exporting. |
| `features.opticalBoundsFromGuides` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | In a variable font a bound that differs between masters is written as a variable value, one per master; settled by exporting a variable font with different offsets per master. |
| `features.opticalBoundsFromGuides` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A hand-written lfbd or rtbd feature, enabled or not, replaces the generated one; settled by exporting a font that has its own lfbd feature. |
| `bitmap.widthOnlyInBitmapFonts` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A font has outlines when any glyph has a path or component on any layer; a font opened from a bitmap file has none. |
| `bitmap.widthOnlyInBitmapFonts` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The advance follows the outline when a bitmap is edited or regenerated, not at once when the glyph's width changes; the secondary reference's bitmap view after a width change would settle it. |
| `bitmap.references` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Where a reference overlaps the glyph's own pixels or another reference, the darker level wins. |
| `bitmap.references` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | References are drawn in on export rather than written as composite bitmaps (`EBDT` formats 8 and 9). |
| `bitmap.references` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A reference names a glyph by name, and a reference to a bitmap that is later removed draws nothing until it is removed too. |
| `spaceCenter.inputSyntax` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Space Center takes the reference spellings /name, /name@layer, /?, /!, /:group and \n unchanged; Edit View's text mode keeps the primary reference's escapes only. |
| `spaceCenter.inputSyntax` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph name after `/` ends at the next `/`, space, backslash or line break, and one space after any slash token is dropped, as in Edit View text; the reference app would settle whether `/? ` keeps its space. |
| `spaceCenter.inputSyntax` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | `/?` in Font View stands for the first selected glyph; the reference app would settle it. |
| `spaceCenter.inputSyntax` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | `/:group` takes every glyph whose left or right kerning group has that name, in glyph order; the reference's groups are not split by side, and a font with both kinds would settle it. |
| `spaceCenter.inputSyntax` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph asked for in two layers in one text is drawn from the last layer named, because the layout takes one layer per glyph name; the reference app would settle it. |
| `spaceCenter.matrixEntry` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A matrix entry's result rounds halves up, as metrics keys do; entering a fraction in the reference would settle it. |
| `spaceCenter.matrixEntry` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Up and Down in a matrix cell set the cell's value plus or minus the step as one command; the reference's undo history after several steps would settle it. |
| `spaceCenter.pointerAndKeys` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Each side's arrow moves its edge that way: Right grows the right margin and Left shrinks it, Alt+Left grows the left margin and Alt+Right shrinks it, as the primary reference's text-mode spacing shortcuts do; the reference app would settle the direction of the Alt keys. |
| `spaceCenter.pointerAndKeys` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A press within the selection-ring radius of a glyph's left or right edge drags that margin, anywhere else inside its box drags the outline; the reference's grab distance would settle it. |
| `spaceCenter.pointerAndKeys` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Ctrl+Cmd with Shift shifts the outline by 10; the reference gives no 100 step for the outline, and pressing Ctrl+Cmd+Shift there would settle it. |
| `io.watchSourceOnDisk` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Every source with a file is watched, single-file sources (.glyphs, .fontcraft, .designspace) and compiled fonts included, not only file-per-glyph sources; the manual names UFOs only. |
| `io.watchSourceOnDisk` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A file that is only touched (same content) refreshes the stamp without a prompt; touching an open UFO in the reference settles it. |
| `io.watchSourceOnDisk` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A document whose file was deleted on disk raises no prompt; the next save writes the file again. Deleting an open UFO in the reference settles it. |
| `constructions.ruleGrammar` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A part whose suffixed name the font lacks falls back to the name before the first dot wherever it appears, as the README says; the library itself does this only for parts with a position. |
| `constructions.ruleGrammar` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | Variable values may use other variables (at most 16 deep) and a `#` ends a value; the original replaces variables in one pass and keeps a trailing comment in the value, which breaks its own README example. |
| `constructions.ruleGrammar` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A code point or colour that cannot be read makes the rule an error; the original drops it silently. |
| `constructions.ruleGrammar` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | A number before a position name works with + and * (`10+top`), and position names may hold dots (`top.alt`); the original fails on both. |
| `drag.ghostOutline` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The grey outline shows only the shapes the running interaction changed, and it also shows under a dialog's live preview, which runs as the same kind of interaction. The reference app during a dialog preview would settle the second part. |
| `drag.ghostOutline` (behavior) | M13 | docs | – | The dotted cross-hair through the pointer is not offered. The reference app's appearance settings would settle whether it is worth a switch. |
| `view.showCurvature` | M13 | inferred | no | The comb sits on the outside of each curve and its length scales with curvature times a user factor, not with the square root of curvature or with zoom. |
| `view.showCurvature` | M13 | inferred | no | Combs show for cubic and quadratic segments alike, and on every master layer in the edit view text, not only the active glyph. |
| `app.commandPalette` | M14 | docs | no | The palette lists the commands that have a menu row; the others take parameters and are reached through the Command Console. The secondary reference's list would settle it. |
| `app.commandPalette` | M14 | docs | no | The shortcut Cmd+Shift+P comes from the secondary reference; the reference app has none. |
| `dialog.auditKerningExceptions` | M13 | docs | no | The audit runs once with the default settings as the dialog opens; FontLab's page does not say whether its dialog lists pairs before a button is pressed. |
| `dialog.matchKerning` | M13 | docs | no | The summary gives the pairs filled, the exceptions added and each master's pair count before and after; FontLab's page names no summary. |
| `spaceCenter.setValue` | M13 | docs | no | A matrix entry is its own command that evaluates the text and then runs the spacing setter as a second step; the reference's undo history after one entry would settle whether it is one step. |
| `palette.selections` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The section sits after Transformations in the Palette and shows only the edited glyph's entries; FontLab's panel placement would settle it. |
| `palette.selections` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Add with a name that already exists replaces that entry rather than refusing; observing the panel's Add on a duplicate name would settle it. |
| `palette.measurements` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The section shows the current master only, not a table of all masters, to fit the Palette's width. |
| `dialog.actions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Each action's parameter form is the command's own dialog when it has one, and otherwise a JSON editor checked against the command's params doc; FontLab's dialog lists only its own actions, each with a form. |
| `dialog.actions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Visible layers are the active layer plus every layer marked visible in any glyph of the scope, sent as one `layers` list, so a glyph without one of those layers skips it; how FontLab picks visible layers per glyph would settle it. |
| `dialog.actions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A command that takes only one `layer` runs once per layer of the scope, and one that takes `allMasters` gets it when the scope is every master; FontLab's actions all take a layer scope of their own. |
| `dialog.actions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Set steps take the scope through the macro arguments `glyphs` and `layers` when the command takes a `layers` list; for other commands the layer is fixed when the step is added. Running a saved FontLab action set on another layer scope would settle it. |
| `dialog.actions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The scope starts as the selected glyphs and the active layer; FontLab's starting scope is not documented. |
| `palette.pairsAndPhrases` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Automatic mode shows the pairs list while the showing tab is in text mode and the phrases list otherwise; the reference switches by its kerning tool, which FontCraft does not have. |
| `palette.pairsAndPhrases` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A loaded list lasts for the session and is not stored with the window state. |
| `palette.pairsAndPhrases` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Pair rows show the value stored for exactly that pair in the showing tab's master, not the value that applies through groups. |
| `dialog.exportProfiles` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The built-in profiles are OpenType PS (CFF, plain), OpenType TT (TrueType, plain) and Web (TrueType, WOFF2 and WOFF), each writing into a subfolder named after it; the reference's full built-in list would settle it. |
| `dialog.exportProfiles` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | There is no tick for which profiles the export dialog shows: Export Fonts As lists every profile; the reference's dialog would settle whether hidden profiles are needed. |
| `dialog.exportWindowContents` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A small dialog with the file and the scale comes before the save chooser, and the format follows the file's extension (.pdf or .svg); exporting in the reference would show whether it asks anything besides the file. |
| `dialog.findOutline` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The pattern and the replacement are drawn in glyphs of their own and named in the dialog, in place of the reference's two small outline editors; drawing panes inside the dialog would settle it. |
| `dialog.findOutline` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Restricting to selected glyphs is the selection itself: the selected glyphs, or every glyph when none is selected; an explicit checkbox would settle it. |
| `dialog.ttInstructions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Parse stores the text at once (one undo step) instead of waiting for the dialog to close; watching the secondary reference's dialog would show whether its changes apply only on OK. |
| `dialog.ttInstructions` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The dialog has a Cancel button that drops the edited text and shows the listing again; the documentation names Edit and Parse only. |
| `window.ttDebugger` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The window lays out the instruction list, glyph pane and inspector side by side in one window, rather than in separate panes; the secondary reference's debugger window would settle it. |
| `window.ttDebugger` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Changing the glyph, size or intent starts again at the first instruction instead of keeping the step; the secondary reference's behaviour when the size changes mid-session would settle it. |
| `dialog.reviewHints` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The position field is the hint's origin edge and the width its signed distance to the other edge, as the hint badges show them; ghost hints show an empty width. The secondary reference's dialog would show whether it uses base and width the same way. |
| `dialog.reviewHints` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Previous and Next stop at the first and last hint of the direction instead of wrapping round; the secondary reference's dialog would settle it. |
| `dialog.reviewHints` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Opening the dialog turns on View › Show Hints so the reviewed hint is visible. |
| `window.kerningPairsPreview` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Pairs are drawn as outlines, one pair per row, in left-to-right text only; vertical and right-to-left pairs are not listed. |
| `window.kerningPairsPreview` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Changed values are those that differ from the values the window found when it opened. |
| `window.kerningPairsPreview` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Double-clicking a pair shows it in Edit View, which stands in for the enlarged view. |
| `window.kerningPairsPreview` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The selected pair's value changes in a value field and with −10, −1, +1 and +10 buttons; dragging the second glyph, which the reference describes, waits for a draggable glyph run in the UI framework. |
| `window.kerningPairsPreview` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A changed value says what it was in its detail line, and a pair new since the window opened says new. |
| `window.kerningPairsPreview` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Sorting by value orders by the size of the value, largest first, ties in stored order; typing a character scrolls to the first pair whose sorted-by glyph encodes it, else whose name starts with it. |
| `dialog.kerningGroupsEditor` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The stacked view aligns the members on their origin for a left group and on their advance width for a right group, where the kerning applies. |
| `dialog.kerningGroupsEditor` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Propose (kerning.buildGroups without applying) and Apply are our additions, so built groups are reviewed before they are stored; a proposal made before the font changed is refused. |
| `dialog.kerningGroupsEditor` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The editor and the Kerning Pairs Preview are panels of the window's dock rather than floating windows; a dock panel can be floated. |
| `dialog.kerningGroupsEditor` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The stacked view shows the member cells under the stacked drawing, so glyphs can still be dropped onto the group. |
| `dialog.mathTable` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The dialog edits the first master and one glyph at a time, with the data as text fields rather than a table per glyph; the reference's math dialog would settle the layout. |
| `window.spaceCenter` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A new Space Center shows `hamburgefontsiv` at 72 points; the reference's default text and size would settle it. |
| `window.spaceCenter` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The Space Center draws the showing Edit View tab's master, else the first master; how the reference picks the master of a multi-master font would settle it. |
| `window.spaceCenter` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Matrix columns are the text's own glyphs; control glyphs get none. Opening a text with control glyphs in the reference would settle it. |
| `dialog.externalChanges` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Every item starts ticked, so Accept Selected with no change of the ticks takes everything; the reference's manual does not say which state the ticks start in, and opening the window there settles it. |
| `dialog.externalChanges` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The stored-text view on double-click shows the glyph in FontCraft's native JSON form rather than as a .glif file, since the comparison is between models; the reference shows the file text, and a file-per-glyph text view would follow it more closely. |
| `window.crossAxisMappings` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Edits collect in a draft that Apply writes, rather than each edit changing the font at once; watching the secondary reference's undo after editing a card would settle it. |
| `window.crossAxisMappings` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A new mapping starts with no axis ticked and each value at the default master's location; the secondary reference's new card would settle it. |
| `palette.referenceFont` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Fonts are added by typing or pasting a path; dropping files onto the section is not built. The reference app's drop behaviour would settle whether anything else happens on drop. |
| `palette.referenceFont` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The list keeps at most 16 fonts and loads only the selected one; the reference app does not document a limit. |
| `palette.relatedGlyphs` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | A glyph's base name is the part of its name before the first dot, and alternates are the other glyphs with the same base name; the reference's documentation does not define the suffix rule. |
| `palette.relatedGlyphs` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The used-as-component section lists direct users only; Show Dependencies lists the nested ones. |
| `palette.relatedGlyphs` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | The decomposition sections use Unicode's canonical decompositions, one level and full together, so U+1EA4 lists both U+00C2 and U+0041, and characters the font has no glyph for are listed dimmed. |
| `palette.relatedGlyphs` (panel) | M13 | docs | – | Clicking an entry opens the glyph in a new tab; the reference's documentation does not say whether it replaces the current glyph instead. |
| `dialog.adjustByVariation` (panel) | M9 | docs | – | The dialog starts relative, at offset 0 on every axis, on the first master; the secondary reference's dialog would settle its defaults. |
| `dialog.setMasterLocations` (panel) | M9 | docs | – | The table is typed as text pairs rather than edited as rows; the secondary reference's dialog would show its layout. |
| `dialog.addVariation` (panel) | M9 | docs | – | The tag starts at the first registered axis the font lacks; the secondary reference's dialog would settle its default. |
| `dialog.mergeFontsToMasters` (panel) | M9 | docs | – | Every open font starts ticked; the secondary reference's dialog would settle its defaults. |
| `curveTension` (parameter) | M13 | docs | – | A master without the parameter, or with a value outside 1 to 100, uses 55 percent, close to a circle's tension; FontLab's default value is not documented and a new FontLab font would show it. |
| `suggestDistance` (parameter) | M13 | docs | – | Read as a dictionary {x, y} where a missing key is 0, an array of two numbers, or one number for both; a source file from the reference app that sets it would settle the stored form. |
| `rapid` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | With no sub-mode latched, a plain click adds a smooth curve node, Alt+click a corner and Cmd+Alt+click a tangent node; the reference's documentation of the tool's modifier keys would settle it. |
| `rapid` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | A segment's tension point sits halfway between its two handles and is dragged towards or away from the Tunni point; a click on it without dragging returns the segment to the master's tension. Watching the reference's tension points would settle it. |
| `rapid` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | A segment whose handle lines do not meet ahead of both nodes (an S-curve) gets handles of the tension times the chord length over √2, a quarter circle's proportion; the reference's handles on an S-shaped run of clicks would settle it. |
| `thickness` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | A drag with no modifier changes the width on the dragged side only, at the dragged node or at every picked control point; the release notes list only the modified drags, and watching the tool would settle it. |
| `thickness` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Shift+drag changes the path's stroke width by twice the drag distance and adds the drag distance to both sides of every node with its own widths, so the modulation is kept; the release notes do not say what happens to nodes with their own widths. |
| `thickness` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Shift+Alt+drag acts on the whole path: it changes the stroke position and moves the per-node widths of that path sideways by the same amount, each side kept at 0 or more. |
| `thickness` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Between two nodes the left and right widths change linearly with the curve parameter; the release notes do not describe the interpolation. |
| `thickness` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Once any node has its own widths the pen is round (the stroke height is not used), and round-inset and axis-aligned caps end the stroke as butt caps. |
| `thickness` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Delete clears the picked control points' per-node widths as Backspace does. |
| `magnet` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | A Shift+drag between two nodes adds a horizontal hint when the nodes are further apart vertically than horizontally, else a vertical one; watching the tool would settle it. |
| `magnet` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Nothing changes while a node is dragged: a line runs from the node to the pointer and the link is made on release; the node itself does not move. |
| `spiro` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Plain click adds a G4 point, Shift+click G2, Alt+click a corner, Cmd+click a left point and Cmd+Shift+click a right point when no kind is latched; the reference's point tools have no modifier keys, so any mapping is ours. |
| `spiro` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | At a G2 point the second derivative of curvature is zero on both sides, at a corner (and an open contour's end) the first two derivatives are zero on each side, and a curve meeting a straight line is tangent to it with a zero second derivative; the thesis's exact end conditions, compared on a shared test contour, would settle it. |
| `spiro` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | A left point is followed by the straight line and a right point preceded by it; drawing a left point and a right point in the reference app would settle it. |
| `spiro` (tool) | M13 | docs | – | Spiro points are not kept with the path: once added, the contour is ordinary cubic segments, so there is no spiro mode to toggle back. A contour key in the stored data would be needed to keep them. |
